# Harry Potter and the Order of the Phoenix — Trivia Fact Sheet
(Notes compiled sequentially while reading book5.txt; chapters covered so far are complete and detailed. This file is being built incrementally.)

## Chapter 1: Dudley Demented

**Plot events (in order):**
- Hot, drought-stricken summer in Little Whinging; hosepipe ban in effect; Harry has been at the Dursleys' for a month, hiding in Aunt Petunia's flower bed under the living-room window to eavesdrop on the Muggle news, hoping for a sign Voldemort's return has become public.
- Harry overhears Vernon and Petunia talking about Dudley being "at the Polkisses'" for tea; Harry knows Dudley's gang actually spends evenings vandalizing the park, smoking, and throwing stones at cars/children.
- News items heard: stranded holidaymakers due to a Spanish baggage-handlers' strike (second week), drought in the Southeast, a helicopter nearly crashing in a field in Surrey, a famous actress's divorce, and finally "Bungy the budgie," who lives at the Five Feathers pub in Barnsley and has learned to water-ski (reporter: Mary Dorkins).
- A loud crack (later revealed as Mundungus Fletcher Disapparating) startles Harry; he hits his head on the window pulling out his wand; Uncle Vernon throttles him briefly until an "invisible force" shocks Vernon into letting go.
- Vernon covers for the noise by telling neighbor "Mrs. Number Seven" it was a car backfiring.
- Harry recalls his frustration: a month of isolation, no real news from Ron/Hermione/Sirius's letters, having thrown away their Honeydukes chocolate birthday presents unopened (regretted it after eating Petunia's wilting salad).
- Harry walks to the children's playground; sits on a swing; watches Dudley's gang (named: Malcolm, Piers, Gordon) cross the park; Dudley bullied/boxed a ten-year-old named Mark Evans two nights before.
- Dudley is revealed to have become Junior Heavyweight Inter-School Boxing Champion of the Southeast (a year of dieting plus boxing training); his opponent for the title was actually sixteen years old and was knocked out for twenty minutes (per Dudley's boast).
- Harry taunts Dudley on the walk home through the alleyway linking Magnolia Crescent and Wisteria Walk (same alley where Harry first saw Sirius in book 3). Dudley reveals he heard Harry talking/moaning in his sleep about "Cedric" — proof Dudley overheard Harry's nightmares about Cedric Diggory's death.
- Harry draws his wand on Dudley in anger; at that moment the night goes pitch black and freezing — two Dementors arrive in the alley.
- Dudley panics, punches Harry in the head knocking his wand away, then runs and collides with a Dementor.
- Harry re-lights his wand with "Lumos," finds the dementor looming over him, casts "Expecto Patronum" — fails twice (weak silvery wisps) while hearing Voldemort's remembered voice ("Bow to death, Harry...") in his head.
- Thinking of Ron and Hermione, Harry successfully casts a full Patronus — a silver stag — which drives off the first dementor, then rescues Dudley from a second dementor about to perform the "Dementor's Kiss."
- Normality (moon, stars, streetlamps, traffic sound) returns instantly once the dementors flee.
- Mrs. Figg, the "batty," cat-loving old neighbor from Wisteria Walk, arrives, revealing she knows what dementors are and curses "Mundungus Fletcher" for abandoning his guard post.

**Named characters:** Harry Potter, Vernon Dursley, Petunia Dursley, Dudley Dursley, Arabella Figg (named fully later), Mundungus Fletcher (mentioned/implied, named next chapter), Ron Weasley (mentioned), Hermione Granger (mentioned), Sirius Black (mentioned, letters), Voldemort/Lord Voldemort (referenced), Cedric Diggory (referenced, murdered), Dobby (house-elf, Harry wonders if the crack was Dobby), Dudley's gang members named: Malcolm, Piers (Piers Polkiss), Gordon; Mark Evans (boy Dudley beat up); Mary Dorkins (TV reporter, Muggle news); Bungy (the water-skiing budgie).

**Named places/objects/spells/creatures:**
- Places: Privet Drive, Little Whinging, Wisteria Walk, Magnolia Crescent, Magnolia Road, the play park, Azkaban (wizard prison), Five Feathers pub (Barnsley).
- Objects: Harry's wand, Invisibility Cloak (not used here), hydrangea bush, begonias (Petunia's flowers).
- Spells: Lumos (wandlight), Expecto Patronum (Patronus Charm).
- Creatures: Dementors (two of them), a cat (unidentified, flees at the crack).
- Food/sweets: Honeydukes chocolates (birthday gifts thrown away), Fruit 'N Bran breakfast cereal (jingle on TV), wilting salad (dinner).

**Specific numbers/quantities:**
- Harry has been at Privet Drive for a month (four weeks) since summer holidays began.
- Dudley's boxing opponent was (per Dudley) sixteen years old, knocked out for twenty minutes; Dudley is Junior Heavyweight Inter-School Boxing Champion of the Southeast.
- Mark Evans beaten up "two nights ago."
- Sirius served twelve years in Azkaban (recalled fact).
- Two dementors attack in the alley.
- Harry's Patronus fails on first two attempts, succeeds on the third with full corporeal stag.

**Small/obscure details:**
- Harry has grown a lot recently and looks "pinched, slightly unhealthy."
- His jeans are torn, T-shirt baggy and faded, trainer soles peeling.
- Uncle Vernon's walrus mustache is a recurring visual motif.
- Harry scavenges newspapers from bins to find news.
- Harry's scar occasionally prickles but he dismisses it as unremarkable now that everyone knows Voldemort is back.
- The narrow alleyway between Magnolia Crescent and Wisteria Walk is the same spot Harry first met Sirius (established in Prisoner of Azkaban).
- Dudley hums tunelessly and is called "Big D," "Ickle Diddykins," "popkin," and "Dinky Diddydums" by his mother.
- Mrs. Figg appears wearing a hairnet, tartan carpet slippers, and carrying a string shopping bag that clanks (full of cat food cans).

## Chapter 2: A Peck of Owls

**Plot events (in order):**
- Mrs. Figg reveals she is a Squib (not a witch), stationed by Dumbledore to watch Harry without telling him; her cat "Mr. Tibbles" served as a lookout.
- Mundungus Fletcher Disapparates in, reeking of drink and stale tobacco, carrying a bundle that is an Invisibility Cloak (implied stolen/traded); he'd left his guard post to pursue "a very good business opportunity" — a batch of cauldrons that "fell off the back of a broom" (stolen goods). Mrs. Figg beats him with her cat-food-laden string bag and sends him to report to Dumbledore.
- Mrs. Figg helps Harry haul the barely-conscious Dudley home, warns him to keep his wand out "never mind the Statute of Secrecy," mentions the Reasonable Restriction of Underage Sorcery.
- Harry learns Mrs. Figg has been watching him on Dumbledore's orders since before he knew about the wizarding world, pretending to be a boring old woman so the Dursleys wouldn't stop his visits.
- Dudley vomits on the doormat; Aunt Petunia panics, wants to call police; Dudley eventually mumbles "Him," blaming Harry.
- A screech owl delivers an official Ministry letter (delivered at 9 PM-ish): Harry is informed he performed the Patronus Charm at "twenty-three minutes past nine" in a Muggle-inhabited area in front of a Muggle, resulting in expulsion from Hogwarts and his wand to be destroyed; he is also summoned to a disciplinary hearing at the Ministry of Magic at 9 A.M. on August 12th, citing a prior official warning under Section 13 of the International Confederation of Wizards' Statute of Secrecy (reference to the Hopping Pot/flying car incident from book 2).
- A second owl (barn owl) brings a hurried note from Ron Weasley telling Harry not to leave the house, not to do magic, not to surrender his wand — signed implicitly by Ron (mentions "Dumbledore's just arrived at the Ministry").
- Uncle Vernon rages about the owls; Harry explains dementors to the Dursleys; Aunt Petunia shockingly reveals she knows what dementors are — she overheard her sister (Lily) mention them years ago. This is the first time Petunia acknowledges knowing about the magical world/her sister directly.
- Uncle Vernon works out that "Lord Voldemort" (recognizes the name as the one who murdered Harry's parents) might be sending dementors after Harry, and orders Harry out of the house ("OUT! OUT!"), listing grievances: owls, exploding puddings, "Dudley's tail" (book 1), Marge "bobbing around on the ceiling" (book 3), the flying Ford Anglia (book 2).
- A third owl brings a second official Ministry letter: the decision to destroy Harry's wand is rescinded; he may keep his wand until the hearing on 12 August; the question of expulsion will also be decided then — he is "suspended" pending inquiry.
- A fourth owl arrives via the kitchen fireplace (soot/dust) — this is Sirius's reply, brief and unhelpful ("Arthur's just told us what happened. Don't leave the house again, whatever you do.").
- A fifth owl delivers a scarlet Howler addressed to Aunt Petunia personally. When opened it bursts into flame and booms "REMEMBER MY LAST, PETUNIA." Petunia is deeply shaken and, in a abrupt reversal, insists Harry must stay ("the neighbors will talk"), overruling Vernon.
- Chapter ends with Petunia ordering Harry to bed, refusing to explain the Howler.

**Named characters:** Harry Potter, Mrs. (Arabella) Figg, Mr. Tibbles (her cat, mentioned by name), Mundungus Fletcher, Vernon Dursley, Petunia Dursley, Dudley Dursley, Lily Potter (referenced indirectly as "my mum"), Ron Weasley (letter), Arthur Weasley (referenced — "Arthur's just told us"), Sirius Black (letter), Dumbledore (referenced), Lord Voldemort (named and discussed openly with the Dursleys for the first time), Aunt Marge (referenced, book 3 events), Mrs. Polkiss (referenced).

**Named places/objects/spells/creatures:**
- Ministry of Magic, Hogwarts School of Witchcraft and Wizardry, Azkaban.
- Improper Use of Magic Office (sender of official letters).
- Objects: Invisibility Cloak (Mundungus has it — implication he may have taken/borrowed Harry's or his own), Howler (scarlet envelope), parchment letters, dead frog (Hedwig's prey, mentioned in ch 3 actually — note: appears late ch2/ch3 boundary).
- Underage Sorcery Decree ("Decree for the Reasonable Restriction of Underage Sorcery"), International Confederation of Wizards' Statute of Secrecy (Section 13).

**Specific numbers/quantities:**
- Patronus Charm performed at 9:23 p.m.
- Disciplinary hearing: 9 A.M., August 12th.
- Five owls arrive at the Dursleys' house in total during this chapter.
- Harry already has one prior official warning (for the Hover Charm incident on Dobby's pudding, from book 2 — implied).

**Small/obscure details:**
- Mundungus's appearance: squat, unshaven, short bandy legs, long straggly ginger hair, bloodshot baggy eyes "like a basset hound," tattered overcoat.
- Mrs. Figg whacks Mundungus with her string bag full of cat food cans.
- The fridge in the Dursleys' kitchen springs open from Vernon's fist-pound, spilling Dudley's "low-fat snacks."
- Aunt Petunia is described with "horsey teeth" and a "horsey face."
- Vernon's "well-worn vein in his temple" pulses when angry (recurring motif).
- Dudley describes the dementor experience: "all dark... heard things inside my head... horrible, cold, really cold... felt as if he'd never be happy again."

## Chapter 3: The Advance Guard

**Plot events (in order):**
- Harry writes identical hurried notes to Sirius, Ron, and Hermione about the dementor attack and his expulsion, sends them via Hedwig.
- No reply for four days; Harry is confined to his room, fed through a cat-flap Vernon installed "three summers ago"; grows increasingly anxious about the upcoming hearing and the possibility of Azkaban or having his wand snapped.
- On the fourth night, the Dursleys go out (lured away under false pretenses, revealed next scene) leaving Harry alone and locked in his room.
- Harry hears a crash downstairs; his bedroom door's lock clicks open on its own; he goes to the landing and finds eight or nine wizards in the dark hallway below.
- The group: Mad-Eye Moody (growls, suspicious, tests Harry by asking the form of his Patronus — a stag — to confirm his identity, referencing the impostor "Moody" (Barty Crouch Jr.) from the previous year who tried to kill Harry), Remus Lupin (looking tired, more gray hair, patched robes), Nymphadora Tonks (insists on being called "Tonks"; violet spiky hair she can change at will — she's a Metamorphmagus; young Auror, graduated training about a year ago, nearly failed Stealth and Tracking, got top marks in Concealment and Disguise), Kingsley Shacklebolt (tall, bald, black wizard, gold hoop earring, deep voice, senior Auror), Elphias Doge (wheezy-voiced, silver-haired wizard, notes Harry has "Lily's eyes"), Dedalus Diggle (excitable, drops his top hat twice, previously met Harry in book 1), Emmeline Vance (stately witch, emerald-green shawl), Sturgis Podmore (square-jawed, straw-colored hair), Hestia Jones (pink-cheeked, black-haired witch).
- Tonks reveals she lured the Dursleys out of the house by sending a fake Muggle-post letter claiming they'd been shortlisted for the "All-England Best-Kept Suburban Lawn Competition."
- Moody explains they're Harry's "guard" — numerous Order members volunteered to escort him; they are headed to new secret Order headquarters, not the Burrow ("too risky").
- They refuse to discuss anything "sensitive" (i.e., Voldemort) inside Privet Drive for security.
- Moody removes his own magical eye to clean it in a glass of water (disgusting per Tonks) — explains he wants "360 degrees visibility" on the journey.
- They'll travel by broomstick since Harry is underage (can't Apparate), the Floo Network is being watched, and an unauthorized Portkey is too risky.
- Tonks helps Harry pack (messily, via a sweeping spell), comments on his Firebolt broomstick (hers is a Comet Two Sixty), casts Scourgify to clean Hedwig's cage.
- Moody casts the Disillusionment Charm on Harry (described like an egg cracking on his head, cold trickle sensation, makes him blend chameleon-like into surroundings) before departure.
- Flying formation ordered by Moody: Tonks in front, Lupin below, Moody behind, others circling; instructions to keep flying/not break ranks even if someone is killed — Kingsley reassures "No one's going to die."
- They fly for a long time (altering course for secrecy), nearly going through clouds, cold enough that Harry recalls a Quidditch match played in a storm against Hufflepuff in third year.
- Moody uses a silver Put-Outer (Deluminator, borrowed from Dumbledore) to extinguish Muggle streetlamps in the destination square before landing.
- They arrive at a grimy London square; Moody hands Harry a note written in narrow handwriting: "The headquarters of the Order of the Phoenix may be found at number twelve, Grimmauld Place, London," then burns it.

**Named characters:** Harry Potter, Mad-Eye (Alastor) Moody, Remus Lupin, Nymphadora Tonks, Kingsley Shacklebolt, Elphias Doge, Dedalus Diggle, Emmeline Vance, Sturgis Podmore, Hestia Jones, Hedwig (owl), Sirius Black (referenced), Ron Weasley (referenced), Hermione Granger (referenced), Dumbledore (referenced — owns the Put-Outer, is "sorting it out" at the Ministry), Arthur Weasley (referenced, "has his best interests at heart"), Barty Crouch Jr. (referenced indirectly as "an impostor" who posed as Moody).

**Named places/objects/spells/creatures:**
- Privet Drive, London (grimy square — Grimmauld Place, revealed next chapter).
- Objects: Hedwig's cage, Harry's Firebolt (broom, gift from Sirius), Tonks's Comet Two Sixty, the Put-Outer/Deluminator (silver cigarette-lighter device, "borrowed from Dumbledore"), Harry's school trunk.
- Spells/charms: Disillusionment Charm, Scourgify (cleaning charm), Locomotor Trunk (levitation spell on luggage), wand-lit "Lumos."
- Vehicle/transport: broomsticks (no Apparition for underage, Floo Network watched, no Portkey).

**Specific numbers/quantities:**
- Four days pass with no reply from Hedwig before the guard arrives.
- Eight or nine wizards/witches in the rescue party (explicitly noted as "eight or nine of them").
- Harry hasn't combed his hair for four days.
- Tonks qualified as an Auror "a year ago."
- The journey takes roughly described as "felt like an hour at least."
- Cat-flap installed "three summers ago."

**Small/obscure details:**
- Moody's magical eye is electric blue, the other is small dark and beady; he has a chunk missing from his nose.
- Tonks's hair changes from violet to "bubble-gum pink" when she decides purple doesn't suit her.
- Tonks's mother apparently has a knack for packing (makes socks fold themselves) that Tonks hasn't mastered.
- Harry recalls the third-year Quidditch match against Hufflepuff played in a storm as his previous coldest broom experience.
- Vernon's reaction to the fake lawn competition letter is anticipated by Harry with amusement.
- The group lands in a small square with broken windows, peeling paint, rubbish bags — a rundown, poor-looking street (contrasts with the hidden grandeur to come).
- A stereo thuds loudly from a nearby Muggle house the entire time, oblivious to the magic happening outside.

## Chapter 4: Number Twelve, Grimmauld Place

**Plot events (in order):**
- Harry reads the hidden note (headquarters is at number twelve, Grimmauld Place) and watches a house magically expand into view between numbers eleven and thirteen (a Muggle-repelled/Unplottable house) — Fidelius Charm-protected (implied; secret-keeper confirmed later as Dumbledore).
- They enter; the hallway is dark, dusty, smells of damp and rot; gas lamps light up; serpent-shaped door knocker, chandelier, and candelabra noted; portraits hang crooked; something scuttles behind the baseboard.
- Mrs. Weasley greets them, looking thinner and paler than before; says a meeting is in progress and Harry/the kids must wait upstairs.
- They pass moth-eaten curtains hiding something, and an umbrella stand made from a severed troll's leg, and a row of mounted, shrunken house-elf heads on plaques going up the staircase.
- Harry reaches the bedroom with Ron and Hermione; Hermione hugs him; Ron's tiny owl Pigwidgeon flies around. Hedwig is already there waiting (delivered earlier).
- Harry explodes in anger at Ron and Hermione for being kept together all summer while he was isolated and uninformed; he shouts about saving the Sorcerer's Stone, defeating Riddle (Chamber of Secrets), surviving the Triwizard graveyard, seeing Voldemort return.
- Ron and Hermione explain: Dumbledore swore them to secrecy; they themselves aren't allowed into Order meetings (too young); Fred and George invented "Extendable Ears" to eavesdrop, but Mrs. Weasley confiscated some after finding out.
- They reveal: some Order members tail known Death Eaters, some recruit new members, some are on "guard duty" (Harry realizes this meant guarding him).
- Hermione and Ron describe spending the summer decontaminating the derelict house (cleaning kitchen, bedrooms).
- Fred and George Apparate in (having just passed their Apparition tests, "with distinction") holding an Extendable Ear, trying to listen to the Order meeting; Ginny arrives and reports Tonks taught her how to detect an Imperturbable Charm (chuck stuff at a door — if it bounces off, the door's been charmed); the kitchen door has indeed been Imperturbed, foiling the Ears.
- They mention Snape is downstairs giving a "top secret" report to the meeting; Ron and Fred clearly dislike Snape despite him being "on our side now" (per Hermione); Ginny notes "Bill doesn't like him either."
- Learn Bill Weasley now works a desk job in Britain (was in Egypt) to be in the Order; flirting with Fleur Delacour, who now works at Gringotts to improve her English — Bill giving her "private lessons." Charlie Weasley is in the Order too, still in Romania, recruiting foreign wizards for Dumbledore.
- Major family subplot revealed: Percy Weasley was promoted to Junior Assistant to Minister Fudge; this followed the Barty Crouch Sr. scandal (Percy failed to notice his boss was being controlled/impersonated last year). Arthur suspects Fudge wants Percy to spy on the Weasleys/Dumbledore. Percy had a huge row with Arthur, accused him of lacking ambition and poverty, declared loyalty to the Ministry over family, said Harry's word alone wasn't good enough evidence, and moved out to live in London alone.
- Hermione explains the Daily Prophet has been subtly mocking Harry in small, recurring jabs (building on Rita Skeeter's past stories, though Rita herself is bound by a promise/blackmail not to write — referencing Chamber events book4), portraying him as an attention-seeking, deluded "tragic hero" type; no mention at all of the dementor attack or his Statute of Secrecy violation — suspected to be saved for when he's expelled.
- Mrs. Weasley interrupts to call them to dinner; Ginny blames the Dungbombs outside the kitchen door on Crookshanks (Hermione's cat).
- Harry learns about Kreacher, the ancient house-elf living in the house, whose life ambition is to have his head mounted on a plaque like his mother's; Hermione defends him and plugs her "S.P.E.W." (Society for the Promotion of Elfish Welfare) cause, which Ron mocks as "spew."
- On the way down, they spy over the banister: the hallway is full of Order members; Snape is in the center of the group, dark greasy hair, prominent nose — Harry is curious what Snape does for the Order.
- Tonks trips over the troll-leg umbrella stand (loud crash), triggering the screeching portrait behind the hallway curtains: Sirius's mother, Mrs. Black, screams insults ("Filth! Scum! By-products of dirt and vileness! Half-breeds, mutants, freaks...") about blood purity; other portraits wake and shriek too. Lupin and Mrs. Weasley fight to close the curtains; Sirius finally storms out and forces them shut, shouting at "the horrible old hag." The portrait recognizes and curses Sirius as "blood traitor, abomination, shame of my flesh."
- Chapter ends: Sirius turns to Harry: "Hello, Harry, I see you've met my mother."

**Named characters:** Harry Potter, Alastor Moody, Remus Lupin, Hermione Granger, Ron Weasley, Fred and George Weasley (twins, newly of age, passed Apparition test "with distinction"), Ginny Weasley, Molly Weasley, Pigwidgeon (Ron's small owl), Hedwig, Severus Snape (seen, not spoken to), Bill Weasley (eldest Weasley son, works at Gringotts in Egypt, now desk job for Order, long hair in ponytail), Fleur Delacour (now working at Gringotts to improve English), Charlie Weasley (in Romania, recruiting for the Order), Percy Weasley (promoted to Junior Assistant to the Minister; estranged from family), Cornelius Fudge (Minister of Magic, promoting Percy, distrustful of Dumbledore), Barty Crouch (referenced — Percy's old boss, "went off his rocker" cover story), Rita Skeeter (referenced, bound to silence), Dumbledore (referenced, founder/leader of the Order), Kreacher (house-elf, introduced by name), Sirius Black (appears at chapter's end), Mrs. Black (Sirius's mother, portrait), Crookshanks (Hermione's cat, blamed for Dungbombs), Tonks (trips over umbrella stand), Voldemort (referenced).

**Named places/objects/spells/creatures:**
- Number Twelve, Grimmauld Place, London — Headquarters of the Order of the Phoenix (Black family's ancestral home).
- Objects: umbrella stand made from a severed troll's leg, shrunken house-elf heads mounted on plaques along the staircase, serpent-shaped door knocker and doorknobs, cobwebby chandelier and serpent candelabra, blank canvas portrait that snickers (someone lurking unseen inside it), moth-eaten curtains concealing Mrs. Black's portrait.
- Dungbombs (Weasley twins' product), Extendable Ears (Fred and George's invention for eavesdropping).
- Imperturbable Charm (seals a door against sound/intrusion).
- The Society for the Promotion of Elfish Welfare (S.P.E.W.), Hermione's organization.
- Department of International Magical Cooperation (where Percy worked, Ministry).

**Specific numbers/quantities:**
- Twenty-ish Order members the kids have met so far ("about twenty of them," per Ron).
- Percy is "only a year out of Hogwarts" when promoted.
- Bill "gathering up twelve scrolls at once" (next chapter overlap, actually ch5 — ignore).

**Small/obscure details:**
- Fred holds a long, flesh-colored string (the Extendable Ear).
- Fred/George comment "Time is Galleons" joking about Apparition vs. walking down stairs.
- Hermione's hands (like Ron's) are marked with half-healed cuts from Hedwig's beak (from delivering Harry's angry letters).
- The room Harry/Ron share has a blank canvas portrait that seems to have a hidden, sniggering occupant.
- Ginny calls the Dungbombs Crookshanks's doing (lie/joke) — Mrs. Weasley suspects Kreacher instead.

## Chapter 5: The Order of the Phoenix

**Plot events (in order):**
- Sirius reveals Grimmauld Place was his parents' house; as "the last Black left" he inherited it and offered it to Dumbledore as HQ — "about the only useful thing I've been able to do." His tone is bitter.
- Mrs. Black's portrait has a Permanent Sticking Charm so it can't be removed from the wall.
- Downstairs basement kitchen: cavernous, stone-walled, lit mainly by a big fire; Mr. Weasley (thin, balding, red-haired, horn-rimmed glasses) and Bill Weasley (long hair in ponytail) are rolling up building plans/parchment at the table — Harry glimpses what looks like a building's floor plan before Mrs. Weasley hides it.
- Bill jokes about Mad-Eye possibly routing them "via Greenland." Tonks knocks a candle onto parchment; Mrs. Weasley repairs it with her wand.
- Bill vanishes leftover scrolls with "Evanesco."
- Mundungus Fletcher is revealed asleep in a heap of rags at the table; wakes up, mumbles agreement to nothing, greets Harry.
- Mrs. Weasley organizes dinner; enchanted knives chop food on their own; a cauldron of stew cooks over the fire.
- Crookshanks (Hermione's half-Kneazle ginger cat) brushes Harry's legs then jumps onto Sirius's lap.
- Sirius, sarcastic about his month of house arrest, explains: the Ministry is still after him; Voldemort now knows (via Wormtail/Peter Pettigrew) that Sirius is an Animagus (a dog, "Padfoot"), ruining his disguise; so he's stuck doing nothing for the Order but listening to Snape's reports and being nagged about cleaning.
- Sirius explains no one has lived in the house for ten years, since his mother died, except for the half-mad Kreacher who's stopped cleaning.
- Mundungus examines a goblet, asking if it's solid silver; Sirius identifies it as "finest fifteenth-century goblin-wrought silver, embossed with the Black family crest" — Mundungus eyes it covetously, suggesting the crest could be removed.
- Fred and George magically fling a cauldron of stew, a flagon of butterbeer, and a breadboard/knife across the table to "save time"; the knife lands quivering where Sirius's hand had just been. Mrs. Weasley explodes at them for reckless magic use now that they're of age.
- Mrs. Weasley almost brings up Percy again mid-rant but stops herself; awkward silence; Bill quickly changes subject to eating.
- Tonks entertains Hermione and Ginny by morphing her nose at the table (into a Snape-like beak, a button-mushroom shape, a hairy-nostriled shape, then — on request — a pig snout resembling Dudley).
- Mr. Weasley, Bill, and Lupin discuss goblin politics: goblins are undecided about Voldemort's return; Bill (who works for Gringotts, dealing with goblins professionally) says goblin loyalties are uncertain, noting Voldemort murdered a goblin family near Nottingham in the first war. Lupin warns goblins might be tempted by promises of rights "denied to them for centuries." Bill mentions a goblin named Ragnok who is still angry about "the Bagman business" (Ludo Bagman, from Goblet of Fire, owing goblins gold).
- Mundungus tells a long rambling anecdote about swindling a man named Will over stolen toads (originally stolen from "Warty Harris"), causing Fred, George, and Ron to roar with laughter; Mrs. Weasley is openly disapproving of Mundungus's criminal past and presence at dinner.
- Sirius privately tells Harry that Mundungus is in the Order because he's "useful" — knows all the crooks, is loyal to Dumbledore (who once helped him out of trouble), and "hears things we don't," despite Mrs. Weasley's disapproval (she hasn't forgiven him for abandoning his watch over Harry).
- Three helpings of rhubarb crumble and custard later, Mrs. Weasley tries to send everyone to bed, but Sirius provokes a confrontation by inviting Harry to ask about Voldemort.
- A major row erupts: Mrs. Weasley insists Harry (only fifteen) is too young to be briefed; Sirius argues Harry has "a right to know," having witnessed Voldemort's return and endured more than most Order members; Fred and George protest they're kept in the dark too despite being of age; Mrs. Weasley invokes "Dumbledore's instruction" not to tell Harry "more than he needs to know"; Mr. Weasley sides partly with Sirius (position has changed now Harry's at HQ); Lupin backs telling Harry "the general picture."
- Mrs. Weasley says Harry is "as good as" her son; Sirius retorts "He's got me," and Mrs. Weasley cuttingly notes Sirius's twelve years in Azkaban made real fathering difficult — Sirius nearly explodes; Lupin intervenes ("Molly, you're not the only person who cares about Harry... Sirius, sit down").
- Harry is allowed to ask questions; Mrs. Weasley sends Ginny, Ron, Hermione to bed (Fred and George insist they're of age and may stay); Ron argues Harry will tell him everything anyway; Harry agrees.
- Information revealed to Harry (with Sirius, Lupin, Bill, Tonks, Mr. Weasley, Mundungus present): Voldemort hasn't killed anyone obviously since his return because he doesn't want attention — his "comeback" was botched because Harry survived and alerted Dumbledore immediately (via the graveyard events of Goblet of Fire); thanks to that, Dumbledore reconvened the Order of the Phoenix about an hour after Voldemort's return.
- The Order's current main goals: (1) prevent Voldemort from rebuilding his forces (recruiting Death Eaters, Dark creatures, giants), (2) try to convince the wizarding world and the Ministry that Voldemort is really back.
- Minister Cornelius Fudge refuses to believe Voldemort is back, partly out of fear that Dumbledore is plotting to seize his job (context: Dumbledore was favored for Minister when Millicent Bagnold retired but never applied; Fudge became Minister instead and has grown possessive of power, now views Dumbledore as a rival stirring trouble).
- The Ministry is pressuring the Daily Prophet to suppress any "Dumbledore's rumor-mongering" stories, leaving the wizarding public largely unaware and vulnerable to Imperius Curse infiltration.
- Sirius can't help publicize Voldemort's return since he's a wanted "mass murderer" with a ten-thousand-Galleon Ministry bounty on his head; Lupin is marginalized as a werewolf; Tonks and Arthur would lose their Ministry jobs if they spoke out, and they're valuable as inside spies, since Voldemort certainly has his own Ministry spies.
- Kingsley Shacklebolt, in charge of the official Sirius Black manhunt, has been misdirecting the Ministry by feeding them false leads that Sirius is in Tibet.
- Dumbledore himself has been undermined: the Prophet falsely reported he was voted out as Chairman of the International Confederation of Wizards (actually Ministry wizards ousted him after his speech about Voldemort's return); he's also been demoted from Chief Warlock of the Wizengamot (the Wizard High Court); the Ministry is discussing stripping his Order of Merlin, First Class. Bill jokes Dumbledore only cares about staying on Chocolate Frog Cards.
- Harry asks what else Voldemort is after; Sirius/Lupin reveal (reluctantly) that Voldemort seeks "a weapon" he didn't have last time — something obtainable "only by stealth" — details withheld.
- Harry asks if it's worse than the Avada Kedavra curse; Mrs. Weasley cuts off the conversation and insists on bedtime for the kids. Harry demands to join the Order and fight; Lupin refuses, stating membership requires being of age/out of school, given the unknown dangers involved.

**Named characters:** Harry Potter, Sirius Black, Mrs. Black (portrait, implied), Remus Lupin, Molly Weasley, Arthur Weasley, Bill Weasley, Mundungus Fletcher, Nymphadora Tonks, Ron Weasley, Hermione Granger, Fred & George Weasley, Ginny Weasley, Crookshanks, Kingsley Shacklebolt (referenced), Cornelius Fudge, Lord Voldemort, Peter Pettigrew/"Wormtail" (referenced — told Voldemort about Sirius's Animagus form), Millicent Bagnold (former Minister of Magic, referenced), Ragnok (a goblin, referenced), Ludo Bagman (referenced, "the Bagman business"), Warty Harris and "Will" (minor rogues' gallery names in Mundungus's anecdote), Dumbledore (discussed extensively), James Potter (referenced, Harry's resemblance), Lily Potter (referenced indirectly).

**Named places/objects/spells/creatures:**
- The basement kitchen of Grimmauld Place; the drawing room (mentioned, has a boggart-containing writing desk and doxy-infested curtains).
- Objects: fifteenth-century solid silver goblet embossed with the Black family crest; butterbeer (flagon); rhubarb crumble and custard (dessert, three helpings); Buckbeak (the hippogriff, fed upstairs — mentioned next chapter overlap).
- Spells: Evanesco (vanishing spell), charm to repair parchment (unnamed), Stunning Spell (Mrs. Weasley stuns shrieking portraits).
- The Order of the Phoenix (secret society founded by Dumbledore to fight Voldemort).
- The Wizengamot (Wizard High Court) — Dumbledore demoted from Chief Warlock.
- International Confederation of Wizards — Dumbledore removed from Chairmanship (falsely reported reason).
- Order of Merlin, First Class — Dumbledore's honor, threatened with removal.
- Imperius Curse (referenced as a Death Eater recruitment/control tool).
- Avada Kedavra (the Killing Curse, referenced).
- Cruciatus Curse (referenced, Voldemort's torture spell).
- Animagus (Sirius's dog form, "Padfoot," compromised as disguise).

**Specific numbers/quantities:**
- Sirius has been "stuck inside" for a month.
- No one has lived in the house for ten years (since Mrs. Black died), except Kreacher.
- Ministry bounty on Sirius: ten thousand Galleons.
- The Order reconvened "about an hour" after Voldemort's return.
- Nearly fourteen years since the Ministry last had to cope with Voldemort-level trouble (i.e., since his first fall).
- Percy is "a year out of Hogwarts" (consistent with ch4).
- Harry is fifteen years old (explicitly stated by Mrs. Weasley).
- Goblet of fifteenth-century silver.
- Three helpings of rhubarb crumble and custard eaten by Harry.

**Small/obscure details:**
- Harry's jeans are "once Dudley's" and now uncomfortably tight in the waist after the meal.
- Mundungus speaks in a thick Cockney-ish dialect ("'ere," "'arry," dropped h's).
- Mundungus's pipe produces "great billowing clouds of greenish smoke" smelling like "burning socks."
- The drawing room's writing desk rattles — suspected boggart; Mad-Eye is to check it before release.
- The curtains in the drawing room are infested with doxies (to be tackled "tomorrow").
- Mrs. Weasley throws Sirius a "very nasty look" after the Mundungus toad story, implying she blames Sirius for keeping such company.
- Tonks's nose-morphing is described as "regular mealtime entertainment" for Ginny and Hermione.

## Chapter 6: The Noble and Most Ancient House of Black

**Plot events so far (in progress):**
- Mrs. Weasley sends the kids to bed with no talking allowed; predicts (correctly, per Fred) that Ginny is secretly awake waiting for gossip from Hermione.
- Harry and Ron's bedroom: a blank portrait on the wall breathes as if sleeping (an unseen inhabitant). Ron bolts the door because of Kreacher, who wandered into the room at 3 a.m. on Ron's first night there.
- Ron and Harry discuss the dinner conversation quietly; Harry pointedly uses Voldemort's actual name, prompting Ron's reflexive flinch; notes Sirius and Lupin always use the real name.
- Fred and George Apparate directly into the bedroom (startling Ron, landing partly on his knees) to keep discussing "the weapon" Sirius mentioned — they speculate wildly: something that kills many at once, or an especially painful method; Harry notes Voldemort already has the Cruciatus Curse for pain. They joke that Dumbledore might be hiding it at Hogwarts (as with the Sorcerer's Stone), and George jokes about Ginny's dangerous Bat-Bogey Hex as a counterexample that size isn't everything.
- They're interrupted by Mrs. Weasley's footsteps; Fred and George Disapparate out just in time; she listens at the door, then checks on Fred and George's room too.
- Harry eventually falls asleep and has a confused dream involving Hagrid and "many-legged creatures...with cannons for heads," mentioning "studying weapons this term."
- Morning: George wakes Harry and Ron, saying Mrs. Weasley needs them in the drawing room — more doxies than expected, plus a nest of dead "puskeins" found under the sofa.
- In the drawing room (long, high-ceilinged, olive-green walls, dirty tapestries, dusty carpet, moss-green velvet curtains "buzzing" with doxies): Mrs. Weasley, Hermione, Ginny, Fred, George are already working, masked with cloths, armed with bottles of black liquid labeled "Doxycide." Harry and Ron are told to mask up and spray too.
- Mrs. Weasley complains about the infestation and implicitly criticizes Kreacher's decade of neglect; Hermione defends Kreacher ("he's really old, he probably couldn't manage").
- Sirius enters carrying a bloodstained bag of dead rats — he's just fed Buckbeak the hippogriff, kept upstairs in his mother's old bedroom. He counters Hermione: "You'd be surprised what Kreacher can manage when he wants to."
- Sirius examines the shaking, locked writing desk/cabinet, suspects a boggart inside, suggests Mad-Eye Moody check it first given "knowing my mother" (implying something worse might be trapped — cut off mid-sentence at the chunk boundary).

**Named characters so far:** Harry Potter, Ron Weasley, Fred & George Weasley, Hermione Granger, Ginny Weasley, Molly Weasley, Sirius Black, Hagrid (dream only), Hedwig, Pigwidgeon, Kreacher (discussed, not yet seen on-page), Buckbeak the hippogriff (fed by Sirius), Crookshanks (implied present).

**Named places/objects/spells/creatures:**
- The drawing room of Grimmauld Place (long, high-ceilinged, olive-green walls, dirty tapestries, moss-green velvet curtains).
- Doxycide (anti-doxy spray, black liquid with nozzle).
- Doxies (small Dark fairy-like biting pests infesting the curtains).
- "Puskeins" — dead creatures found in a nest under the sofa (exact nature unclear/obscure, likely a Rowling-invented pest).
- A locked writing desk/cabinet suspected to contain a boggart.
- Buckbeak the hippogriff, kept in Mrs. Black's old bedroom upstairs, fed dead rats by Sirius.
- Owl Treats (fed to Hedwig and Pigwidgeon to keep them quiet at night).

**Specific numbers/quantities:**
- Kreacher hasn't cleaned "in ages"/for the full ten years the house has been empty.
- 3 a.m. — the time Kreacher wandered into Ron's room on his first night at Grimmauld Place.

**Small/obscure details:**
- Ron wears maroon pajamas.
- The blank portrait in Harry and Ron's room "breathes" as though someone invisible sleeps within it (never resolved who it is in this chapter).
- Harry's dream mixes Hagrid, many-legged weapon-like creatures with cannon heads, foreshadowing both Grawp and the "weapon" discussion.

**Chapter 6 continued — full plot events:**
- The doxy-spraying continues; Mrs. Weasley consults Gilderoy Lockhart's Guide to Household Pests (book) for doxy facts — warns the bites are poisonous and she has antidote ready.
- Fred secretly pockets a stunned doxy (and later more) to harvest venom for their joke-shop product, "Skiving Snackboxes" — explained to Harry: double-ended color-coded chews; e.g., Puking Pastilles (orange half makes you vomit, purple half cures you instantly) so students can fake illness to skip class. Testers are Fred and George themselves ("Mum thought we'd been dueling" after Nosebleed Nougat testing; George tested Fainting Fancies).
- Revealed: the twins are running their joke-shop idea as a mail-order business via Daily Prophet adverts, funded secretly by Harry, who gave them his 1,000-Galleon Triwizard Tournament winnings (from Goblet of Fire) to help start it — Mrs. Weasley is unaware and disapproves of a joke-shop career.
- De-doxying the curtains takes most of the morning; afterward Mrs. Weasley sits on the bag of dead rats by accident.
- They begin clearing glass-fronted cabinets by the mantelpiece: find rusty daggers, claws, a coiled snakeskin, tarnished silver boxes, and an ornate crystal bottle stoppered with an opal, containing what looks like blood.
- Doorbell rings; through the window they see Mundungus Fletcher arriving with a precarious stack of cauldrons (presumably stolen) — Mrs. Weasley is heard shouting at him downstairs: "WE ARE NOT RUNNING A HIDEOUT FOR STOLEN GOODS!"
- Kreacher the house-elf shuffles into the drawing room muttering insults under his breath about "Mudbloods," "blood traitors," and "half-breeds" polluting his Mistress's house, pretending not to see/be heard by the children; addresses Fred and George as "Young Master" while muttering insults simultaneously. Hermione introduces Harry by name; Kreacher recognizes "the boy who stopped the Dark Lord" by his scar. Ron and Ginny object to Kreacher calling Hermione a "Mudblood."
- Sirius returns and confronts Kreacher, accusing him of trying to smuggle house heirlooms into his own room to save them from being thrown out, including a seven-century-old family tapestry Kreacher is desperate to protect. Sirius bitterly notes his mother "didn't have a heart, she kept herself alive out of pure spite."
- Hermione suggests freeing Kreacher; Sirius refuses — Kreacher "knows too much about the Order" and the shock of freedom "would kill him."
- The family tapestry: "THE NOBLE AND MOST ANCIENT HOUSE OF BLACK," motto "Toujours Pur" ("Always Pure"), is examined. Sirius is burned off it (a small scorch mark) for running away from home at about age sixteen; he lived with the Potters (James's parents effectively adopted him as a second son, Sunday lunches included) and later got his own place at seventeen using gold left to him by his Uncle Alphard (also blasted off the tapestry).
- Sirius's reasons for leaving: hatred of his family's pure-blood mania; his younger brother Regulus Black believed the pure-blood propaganda, joined the Death Eaters, then tried to back out and was murdered on Voldemort's orders roughly fifteen years before the present (i.e., around the time of Voldemort's first fall) — "it's a lifetime of service or death."
- Other tapestry figures noted: Phineas Nigellus Black (Sirius's great-great-grandfather, "least popular headmaster Hogwarts ever had"); Araminta Meliflua (mother's cousin, tried to legalize Muggle-hunting via a Ministry Bill); Great-Aunt Elladora (started the family tradition of beheading house-elves when they grew too old — explaining the mounted elf heads on the staircase).
- Sirius reveals Tonks is his relative: her mother Andromeda (his favorite cousin) was burned off the tapestry for marrying a Muggle-born, Ted Tonks; Andromeda's sisters Bellatrix and Narcissa remain on the tree (married "respectably"). Narcissa Black is shown married to Lucius Malfoy, with a line to their son Draco Malfoy — confirming Sirius/Tonks/the Malfoys are all related. Bellatrix Black is married to Rodolphus Lestrange.
- Sirius confirms Bellatrix and Rodolphus Lestrange (and Rodolphus's brother Rabastan) are imprisoned in Azkaban — they were convicted alongside Barty Crouch Jr. Harry recalls seeing Bellatrix Lestrange in Dumbledore's Pensieve (from Goblet of Fire) at her trial, proudly proclaiming loyalty to Voldemort.
- Molly Weasley and Sirius are cousins by marriage; Arthur Weasley is "something like" Sirius's second cousin once removed — but the Weasleys, as "blood traitors," don't appear on the tapestry at all.
- Sirius explains the house's heavy security: it is Unplottable; his father added every security measure possible; Dumbledore (the Order's Secret-Keeper) added further protection — nobody can find headquarters unless Dumbledore personally tells them (as happened with the note Moody showed Harry).
- Sirius asks Dumbledore for permission to escort Harry to his hearing in his Animagus dog form ("Snuffles") for moral support.
- Time skip: Mrs. Weasley keeps everyone working for days; emptying cabinets, Sirius is bitten by a silver snuffbox containing "Wartcap powder" (hand grows a crusty brown covering, healed with a tap of his wand); George secretly pockets the box for the joke shop.
- They destroy a many-legged silver tweezer-like instrument that tries to puncture Harry's skin (Sirius smashes it with a book titled Nature's Nobility: A Wizarding Genealogy); a musical box that makes everyone drowsy until Ginny slams it shut; an unopenable heavy locket (note: this will become significant later in the series as a Horcrux, though not identified as such here — just found and set aside, presumably kept, not destroyed at this point); an Order of Merlin, First Class awarded to Sirius's grandfather "for Services to the Ministry" (Sirius says it really means "he gave them a load of gold") — thrown in the rubbish.
- Kreacher repeatedly tries to smuggle objects away, including a large gold ring with the Black crest (Sirius's father's); Kreacher bursts into tears when it's confiscated; Sirius mentions catching Kreacher "snogging a pair of my father's old trousers."
- The drawing room takes three days to fully clean; the family tree tapestry and the locked rattling writing desk remain (Moody hasn't yet examined the desk for the suspected boggart).
- They move to the ground-floor dining room: find saucer-sized spiders in the dresser (Ron flees for an hour and a half); china bearing the Black crest and old photographs (whose pictured occupants shriek when the glass smashes) are discarded.
- Visitors come and go (doorbell repeatedly triggers Mrs. Black's screeching): Snape visits several times (Harry and he never cross paths directly); Professor McGonagall is glimpsed in Muggle dress, too busy to stop.
- Tonks helps find a "murderous old ghoul" in an upstairs toilet; Lupin (living at the house with Sirius, but frequently away on "mysterious work" for the Order) helps repair a grandfather clock that shoots bolts at passersby; Mundungus redeems himself slightly by rescuing Ron from a strangling set of antique purple robes.
- Harry, despite continued bad dreams (corridors, locked doors, scar prickling), is happier/busier than all summer, but the dread of the approaching Ministry hearing resurfaces whenever idle.
- On Wednesday evening, Mrs. Weasley tells Harry she's ironed his best clothes and he should wash his hair for his"first impression" the next morning at the hearing. Arthur Weasley will escort him to the Ministry, where he'll wait in Arthur's office until the hearing. Dumbledore has decided Sirius should NOT accompany Harry (even in dog form) — Dumbledore visited the house the previous night while Harry was asleep, without asking to see Harry, which stings Harry deeply.

**Named characters (ch.6 additions):** Fred & George Weasley (Skiving Snackboxes, Puking Pastilles, Nosebleed Nougat, Fainting Fancies — all named products), Kreacher, Sirius Black, Hermione, Ron, Ginny, Phineas Nigellus Black, Araminta Meliflua, Elladora Black (great-aunt), Regulus Black (Sirius's late brother), Alphard Black (Sirius's uncle who left him gold), Andromeda Tonks (née Black, Sirius's favorite cousin, Tonks's mother), Ted Tonks (Muggle-born, Andromeda's husband), Bellatrix Lestrange (née Black), Rodolphus Lestrange, Rabastan Lestrange, Narcissa Malfoy (née Black), Lucius Malfoy, Draco Malfoy, Barty Crouch Jr. (referenced, imprisoned with the Lestranges), Molly Weasley, Arthur Weasley, Remus Lupin, Tonks, Mundungus Fletcher, Professor McGonagall (glimpsed), Severus Snape (glimpsed), Dumbledore (referenced, visited at night), James Potter and Lily Potter/the "Potters" (referenced — Sirius's adoptive family in youth; also "best man at the Potters' wedding" photo appears in ch.7).

**Named places/objects/spells/creatures (ch.6 additions):**
- Gilderoy Lockhart's Guide to Household Pests (book), Doxycide, doxies and doxy eggs.
- Skiving Snackboxes product line: Puking Pastilles, Nosebleed Nougat, Fainting Fancies.
- The Black family tapestry ("Toujours Pur" motto) in the drawing room.
- Wartcap powder (inside a biting silver snuffbox).
- Nature's Nobility: A Wizarding Genealogy (book used as a weapon against a cursed silver instrument).
- A heavy locket that cannot be opened (found among the Black family objects — notable for attentive readers/future books).
- Order of Merlin, First Class (medal, Sirius's grandfather's).
- Buckbeak the hippogriff (kept in Mrs. Black's old room).
- "Snuffles" — Sirius's Animagus dog-form alias.

**Specific numbers/quantities (ch.6 additions):**
- Regulus Black died about fifteen years before the present.
- The drawing room took three days to decontaminate.
- Ron hid from spiders for "an hour and a half."
- Harry gave Fred and George 1,000 Galleons (Triwizard winnings) to fund their joke shop (referenced from Goblet of Fire).
- Sirius left home at about age sixteen; got his own place at seventeen.

## Chapter 7: The Ministry of Magic

**Plot events (in order):**
- Harry wakes very early, terrified about the hearing; dresses in freshly laundered clothes Mrs. Weasley left out.
- Downstairs in the kitchen: Mr. and Mrs. Weasley, Sirius, Lupin, and Tonks are already up, as if waiting for him. Tonks (hair blonde and curly this morning) has been up all night on Order duty and mentions Rufus Scrimgeour (then Head of the Auror Office) has been asking her and Kingsley "funny questions" — they need to be careful.
- Mrs. Weasley offers a full breakfast menu (porridge, muffins, kippers, bacon and eggs, toast); Harry can only manage toast.
- Advice given to Harry: don't lose your temper, be polite, stick to the facts (Sirius); the law is on his side — underage magic is permitted in life-threatening situations (Lupin). Mrs. Weasley tries fruitlessly to flatten Harry's hair with a wet comb.
- Mr. Weasley reveals the hearing will be held in Amelia Bones's office — she is Head of the Department of Magical Law Enforcement and will question Harry; Tonks vouches that Bones is fair and will hear him out.
- Harry and Mr. Weasley leave on foot (Apparition impossible for Harry) and take the Muggle Underground into central London — Mr. Weasley delights naively in Muggle technology (ticket machines, the escalator, the ticket turnstile) and is hopeless with Muggle money, so Harry handles the ticket purchase.
- They reach a battered red telephone box (missing panes of glass) in a shabby side street — the public visitor's entrance to the Ministry of Magic. Mr. Weasley dials 62442 (spells "MAGIC" on a phone keypad) and a cool female recorded voice asks for name and business; issues Harry a silver visitor's badge reading "Harry Potter, Disciplinary Hearing."
- The box descends underground into the Ministry Atrium: a long hall with a peacock-blue ceiling of moving golden symbols, dark polished wood floor, fireplaces for Floo travel. A large fountain, the "Fountain of Magical Brethren," depicts a noble wizard, a beautiful witch, a centaur, a goblin, and a house-elf (the last three gazing adoringly up at the wizard and witch) — proceeds from coins thrown in go to St. Mungo's Hospital for Magical Maladies and Injuries.
- At the security desk, a security wizard named Eric scans Harry with a golden detector rod and registers his wand: eleven inches, phoenix-feather core, in use four years (confirms Harry's wand details). Eric notices Harry's scar; Mr. Weasley hastily moves Harry along.
- They pass through golden gates to the lifts (at least twenty lifts). A wizard named Bob holds a box containing a fire-breathing creature mistaken for a "bog-standard chicken" (a suspected illegal-breeding violation).
- The lift announces levels as they ascend: Level 7 — Department of Magical Games and Sports (incl. British and Irish Quidditch League HQ, Official Gobstones Club, Ludicrous Patents Office); Level 6 — Department of Magical Transport (Floo Network Authority, Broom Regulatory Control, Portkey Office, Apparation Test Center); Level 5 — Department of International Magical Cooperation (International Magical Trading Standards Body, International Magical Office of Law, International Confederation of Wizards British Seats); Level 4 — Department for the Regulation and Control of Magical Creatures (Beast/Being/Spirit Divisions, Goblin Liaison Office, Pest Advisory Bureau); Level 3 — Department of Magical Accidents and Catastrophes (Accidental Magic Reversal Squad, Obliviator Headquarters, Muggle-Worthy Excuse Committee); Level 2 — Department of Magical Law Enforcement (Improper Use of Magic Office, Auror Headquarters, Wizengamot Administration Services).
- Interdepartmental memos are enchanted pale-violet paper airplanes (replacing owls, which made too much mess).
- They get off at Level 2. Kingsley Shacklebolt greets Mr. Weasley coolly (pretending not to know Harry well, for secrecy) and hands him a fake assignment — compile info on flying Muggle vehicles sighted in the last 12 months (ostensibly tracking Sirius's old motorcycle) — actually a cover to pass Harry a smuggled copy of The Quibbler magazine. Kingsley's cubicle walls are covered with Sirius Black "Wanted" material, including a photo of Sirius as best man at the Potters' wedding, and a world map with glowing red pins (tracking sightings).
- Mr. Weasley's own office (Misuse of Muggle Artifacts Office) is tiny, windowless (fake weather via enchanted windows controlled by Magical Maintenance — once producing two months of fake hurricanes during a pay dispute), cluttered with Muggle-related posters/diagrams (car posters, postbox illustrations, a wiring diagram for a plug), a hiccuping old toaster, and a pair of self-animated leather gloves. A photo of the Weasley family on his desk conspicuously has Percy "walked out of it."
- Mr. Weasley's colleague is the elderly, timid Perkins. A new memo reports a third "regurgitating public toilet" incident in Bethnal Green (anti-Muggle prank; prior incidents in Wimbledon and Elephant and Castle) — this is Magical Law Enforcement Patrol's job, not Aurors'.
- Perkins rushes in: the hearing time and venue have changed — now 8 o'clock, moved down to "old Courtroom Ten." Mr. Weasley panics (they're supposedly five minutes late) and rushes Harry through the Ministry at a run, down past the Atrium, to the Department of Mysteries level, then further down an extra flight of stairs (below where the lift reaches) to an old dungeon-like corridor resembling the Hogwarts dungeons, finally reaching Courtroom Ten. Mr. Weasley is not permitted to accompany Harry inside and wishes him luck.

**Named characters:** Harry Potter, Arthur Weasley, Molly Weasley, Sirius Black, Remus Lupin, Nymphadora Tonks, Amelia Bones (Head of Dept. of Magical Law Enforcement, will conduct the hearing), Rufus Scrimgeour (referenced — then Head of the Auror Office, suspicious of Tonks/Kingsley), Kingsley Shacklebolt, Eric (security wizard), Bob (wizard with the fire-breathing "chicken"), Bode (sallow, mournful wizard who shares the lift down to the Department of Mysteries), Perkins (Arthur's elderly, timid office-mate), Percy Weasley (absent from the family photo), James and Lily Potter (referenced via wedding photo).

**Named places/objects/spells/creatures:**
- Grimmauld Place kitchen; the London Underground; the red telephone box visitor's entrance; the Ministry of Magic Atrium; the Fountain of Magical Brethren (statues: wizard, witch, centaur, goblin, house-elf); St. Mungo's Hospital for Magical Maladies and Injuries (fountain proceeds go here); Department levels 2–7 (full names listed above); Auror Headquarters; Misuse of Muggle Artifacts Office; Department of Mysteries (glimpsed, plain black door at corridor's end); Courtroom Ten (old, disused, dungeon-like).
- The Quibbler (magazine, smuggled to Sirius via Harry/Weasley — implies it published something about Sirius, likely Stan Shunpike's testimony or similar; exact content not detailed here).
- Interdepartmental memos (enchanted paper airplanes).
- Harry's wand details registered: eleven inches, phoenix-feather core, four years in use.
- Silver Sickles and bronze Knuts (coins in the fountain).
- Misuse of Muggle Artifacts posters: car posters (incl. a dismantled engine), postbox illustrations, a wiring diagram for a plug.

**Specific numbers/quantities:**
- Phone code to reach the Ministry: 6-2-4-4-2 (spells MAGIC).
- Harry's wand: eleven inches, phoenix feather, four years old.
- Hearing originally for 9 A.M., moved last-minute to 8 A.M., and moved to Courtroom Ten.
- Harry would pledge 10 Galleons to the fountain if not expelled.
- Two prior regurgitating-toilet incidents (Wimbledon, Elephant and Castle) before this (Bethnal Green) third one.
- Two months of fake hurricanes once produced by Magical Maintenance during a pay dispute.

**Small/obscure details:**
- Tonks's hair color changes session to session (blonde and curly this morning, vs. violet/pink earlier) — recurring Metamorphmagus motif.
- Mr. Weasley calls firearms "relegs" (mispronunciation joke) and insists the correct term is "firearms."
- Mr. Weasley's warning that Molly is making meatballs for dinner, said quietly to Kingsley as a friendly aside amid the cover performance of professional distance.
- The lift level list is a classic exhaustive trivia goldmine (exact department names/levels).
- Harry notices his own visitor's badge reads "Harry Potter, Disciplinary Hearing."
