## Chapter 8: The Hearing

**Plot events so far (in progress):**
- Harry enters Courtroom Ten, recognizing it as the same courtroom he saw in Dumbledore's Pensieve (Goblet of Fire) where the Lestranges were sentenced to life in Azkaban.
- The room: dark stone walls, torch-lit, empty benches, with many shadowy figures seated in the highest tier. A cold male voice (not yet named, presumably Fudge or Bones) reprimands Harry for being late, dismissing his explanation that he wasn't told of the time change, noting an owl had been sent that morning.
- Harry is directed to the chained chair in the center of the room — the same restraining chair he'd seen used on defendants in the Pensieve memory.

**Named characters so far:** Harry Potter, unidentified "cold male voice" (likely Fudge), the Lestranges (referenced, previously sentenced here).

**Named places/objects:** Courtroom Ten, the chained center chair, the Wizengamot (named as the body convening).

**Chapter 8 continued — full plot events:**
- The Wizengamot bench holds about fifty witches/wizards in plum-colored robes with a silver "W" on the chest. Cornelius Fudge (Minister of Magic, portly, usually wears a lime-green bowler hat — not today) sits center-front; to his left, a square-jawed witch with short gray hair and a monocle (Amelia Bones); to his right, a witch sitting in shadow (later revealed as Dolores Umbridge).
- Percy Weasley is the Court Scribe, sitting at the end of the front bench, pointedly not acknowledging Harry.
- Formal charge read: disciplinary hearing of 12 August into offenses under the Decree for the Reasonable Restriction of Underage Sorcery (1875) and the International Statute of Secrecy (Section 13) by "Harry James Potter," of 4 Privet Drive, Little Whinging, Surrey — specifically producing a Patronus Charm in front of a Muggle on 2 August at 9:23 p.m.
- Interrogators named formally: Cornelius Oswald Fudge (Minister of Magic), Amelia Susan Bones (Head of Dept. of Magical Law Enforcement), Dolores Jane Umbridge (Senior Undersecretary to the Minister); Court Scribe Percy Ignatius Weasley.
- Dumbledore arrives unannounced as "Witness for the defense, Albus Percival Wulfric Brian Dumbledore" (his full name given) wearing midnight-blue robes; Fudge is visibly flustered, stammering about the hearing's changed time; Dumbledore claims to have "missed" the message but arrived three hours early "due to a lucky mistake" (implying he suspected a trick) — conjures himself a squashy chintz armchair.
- Fudge reads the formal charges again; cross-examines Harry rapid-fire (yes/no), cutting off his explanations about the dementors.
- Madam Bones questions the Patronus's nature: confirms it's a fully corporeal Patronus (a stag), that Harry's been producing one for over a year (since age 13/third year, taught by Professor Lupin), calls it "very impressive indeed" — some Wizengamot members murmur approval, others disapprove.
- Harry blurts out "I did it because of the dementors!" — shocks the court. Fudge dismisses this as a rehearsed cover story, mockingly noting Muggles can't see dementors so there'd be no witnesses.
- Dumbledore reveals he has brought a witness: Mrs. Figg is called in (Percy fetches her). She testifies (nervously, over-rehearsed-sounding) that she was out buying cat food near Wisteria Walk around 9 p.m. on 2 August and saw two dementors "gliding" (corrected by Madam Bones from her initial "running") toward two boys in the alley; describes them vaguely as "big and wearing cloaks" (revealing she's likely only ever seen a picture, not real dementors) but accurately describes the sensory effects (cold, loss of happiness, bad memories) and accurately recounts Harry's two failed Patronus attempts followed by a successful one that drove off both dementors.
- Fudge dismisses Mrs. Figg as an unconvincing witness; Madam Bones defends her credibility, noting she described dementor effects accurately.
- Fudge argues it's implausible dementors would randomly wander into a Muggle suburb and just happen to find a wizard; Dumbledore states plainly he believes the dementors were deliberately ordered there — implying someone within the Ministry itself gave the order, since dementors (guarding Azkaban) are supposed to answer only to the Ministry.
- Dolores Umbridge is formally introduced/described in detail for the first time: squat, broad flabby face, no visible neck, wide bulging eyes, short curly hair with a black velvet bow, giving a toad-like appearance; speaks in an unexpectedly high, girlish, fluttery voice. She "innocently" reframes Dumbledore's implication as an absurd accusation that the Ministry ordered an attack on Harry, provoking false laughter from sycophantic Wizengamot members.
- Dumbledore logically reiterates his point (dementors answer only to the Ministry, two attacked Harry, ergo someone at the Ministry must have ordered it, OR the dementors are outside Ministry control) — Fudge angrily insists there are no dementors outside Ministry control, inadvertently committing to the idea that it must have been ordered internally; Dumbledore says he trusts a "full inquiry" will follow (sarcastically).
- Fudge tries to refocus the hearing strictly on the Underage Sorcery charge; Dumbledore cites "Clause Seven" of the Decree — permitting underage magic in life-threatening/exceptional circumstances. Fudge is cornered into acknowledging the clause.
- Fudge tries a new angle, bringing up Harry's history of "cock-and-bull stories," citing the Hover Charm incident from three years ago (actually performed by Dobby the house-elf, per Chamber of Secrets) and the time Harry "blew up his aunt" (Aunt Marge, Prisoner of Azkaban, no charges were pressed). Dumbledore calmly rebuts each, noting the Ministry lacks jurisdiction over Hogwarts students' school conduct, and pointedly reminds Fudge that the Ministry has no authority to expel students or confiscate wands before charges are proven — both things Fudge tried to do via letter in Chapter 2.
- Dumbledore notes pointedly that since his own recent removal from the Wizengamot, it has "already become practice" to hold a full criminal trial over simple underage-magic cases — implying Fudge arranged an unusually harsh/public hearing specifically to target Harry.
- Vote: Madam Bones calls for hands — more than half vote to clear Harry; a minority (including Fudge, Umbridge, a heavily mustached wizard, and a frizzy-haired witch) vote for conviction. Fudge, visibly furious, announces "cleared of all charges."
- Dumbledore immediately vanishes both conjured armchairs and leaves without looking at or speaking to Harry.

**Named characters (ch.8 additions):** Cornelius Fudge, Amelia Susan Bones, Dolores Jane Umbridge (fully introduced), Percy Weasley (Court Scribe), Albus Percival Wulfric Brian Dumbledore (full name given), Mrs. Arabella Doreen Figg (full name given), Dobby (referenced, the real Hover Charm culprit), Aunt Marge (referenced).

**Named places/objects/spells:**
- Courtroom Ten (same room seen in the Pensieve where the Lestranges were sentenced).
- The Decree for the Reasonable Restriction of Underage Sorcery (1875) — exact year given.
- International Confederation of Wizards' Statute of Secrecy, Section 13.
- Wizengamot Charter of Rights (grants the accused the right to call witnesses).
- Clause Seven of the Decree (permits underage magic in life-threatening situations).

**Specific numbers/quantities:**
- About fifty Wizengamot members present.
- Incident date/time restated precisely: 2 August, 9:23 p.m.
- Patronus taught to Harry by Lupin in third year; he's been producing it "for over a year."
- Harry is confirmed fifteen years old again.
- Vote split: "more than half" to clear, roughly six or so (half a dozen) hands for conviction.
- The Decree dates to 1875.

**Small/obscure details:**
- Fudge's lime-green bowler hat is noted as absent that day (a signature accessory).
- Percy shows a "sanctimonious little nod" agreeing with Fudge, deeply angering Harry.
- Mrs. Figg arrives still wearing her carpet slippers.
- Umbridge's voice is compared to a fly-catching toad with a sticky tongue — a recurring amphibian motif for her character.
- Dumbledore's wand-conjured chintz armchair is a small but specific visual detail.

---

## Chapter 9: The Woes of Mrs. Weasley

**Plot events (in order):**
- Harry, dazed with relief, finds the Wizengamot filing out; most avoid eye contact, though a few (including Madam Bones) greet Mr. Weasley. Fudge and Umbridge ignore Harry; Percy marches past his own father without acknowledgment, carrying parchment and quills.
- Mr. Weasley is overjoyed at the "cleared of all charges" news, astonished that Harry faced the "full court" (an unusually serious proceeding for such a minor charge).
- On the ninth-level corridor, they encounter Cornelius Fudge talking privately with Lucius Malfoy. Malfoy mockingly calls Harry "Patronus Potter," referencing Harry's "snakelike" ability to escape tight situations — a chilling echo, since Harry recognizes Malfoy's voice/eyes from the graveyard (Voldemort's return, Goblet of Fire) where Malfoy stood masked as a Death Eater.
- Malfoy taunts Arthur Weasley about his lowly Ministry job (Misuse of Muggle Artifacts) and implies he has "private business" with Fudge; the clink of gold in his pocket is noted. He calls Harry "Dumbledore's favorite boy."
- After Malfoy and Fudge leave together, Mr. Weasley speculates Malfoy was trying to sneak down and learn the hearing's outcome, and plans to warn Dumbledore that Malfoy met with Fudge again; he explains Malfoy curries Ministry favor through generous "donations" to delay unwanted laws.
- Harry worriedly asks if Fudge could be under the Imperius Curse from meeting Death Eaters privately; Mr. Weasley admits the Order has considered it, but Dumbledore believes Fudge is acting of his own free will "at the moment" — not a comforting thought.
- Passing the Fountain of Magical Brethren again, Harry empties his entire money bag (not just the planned 10 Galleons) into the fountain, reflecting that up close the statues (adoring goblin, centaur, house-elf gazing up at the wizard/witch) look rather absurd/servile — he imagines Hermione's reaction to the elf statue.
- Back at Grimmauld Place: Ron, Hermione, Mrs. Weasley, Fred, George, Ginny erupt in celebration ("He got off, he got off, he got off" chant/war dance). Harry's good mood is dented when he mentions the Malfoy encounter to Sirius and Mr. Weasley (who leaves again for his "vomiting toilet" job in Bethnal Green, covering for Tonks).
- Harry's scar burns painfully at the thought that Dumbledore saved him at the hearing but wouldn't speak to or even look at him — he hides the pain, saying only "Scar... but it's nothing, it happens all the time now."
- Over the following days, Harry notices Sirius is not fully happy about Harry's reprieve — he grows moodier, withdrawn, spends more time locked away with Buckbeak in his mother's old room. Hermione theorizes (while the three scrub a moldy cupboard) that Sirius subconsciously hoped Harry would be expelled so they could be "outcasts together," and that Sirius sometimes "gets confused" about whether Harry is Harry or James (echoing Mrs. Weasley's earlier accusation). Harry and Ron reject this theory; Hermione maintains Sirius has "been very lonely for a long time."
- Daily house-cleaning drudgery continues (moldy cupboards, bolted closets); Hermione floats the idea of a "sponsored scrub" of the Gryffindor common room to raise funds/awareness for S.P.E.W.; Ron is unenthusiastic.
- On the last day of the holidays, Hogwarts booklists arrive. New books required: The Standard Book of Spells, Grade 5 by Miranda Goshawk, and Defensive Magical Theory by Wilbert Slinkhard.
- Fred and George Apparate in, noting the new Defense Against the Dark Arts textbook author (Slinkhard) means Dumbledore has finally found a new DADA teacher for the year — remarking on the cursed job's history: Harry sums up the previous four teachers' fates: one sacked (Quirrell — actually died, per book 1, but referenced loosely), one dead (actually this refers potentially to differing accounts — list as book states: "one sacked, one dead, one's memory removed, and one locked in a trunk for nine months" — corresponding to Quirrell (died, book1), Lockhart (memory removed, book2), Lupin ("sacked," book3, resigned), and "Mad-Eye Moody" i.e., Barty Crouch Jr. impersonating Moody (real Moody locked in a trunk for nine months, book4).
- Ron receives his Hogwarts letter — he's been made a Gryffindor Prefect (badge: scarlet and gold, large "P" on the lion). Fred and George are stunned/incredulous, having assumed Harry would get it; they rib Ron mercilessly ("No one in their right mind would make Ron a prefect").
- Hermione bursts in, also made prefect, assumes Harry got the other badge too — awkward moment when it's clarified Ron got it instead of Harry.
- Harry privately struggles with jealousy/resentment, interrogating his own ego (recalling all the dangerous feats he's done that Ron/Hermione weren't present for: Quirrell confrontation, Chamber of Secrets/Riddle/basilisk, dementors the night Sirius escaped, the graveyard) before deciding not to spoil Ron's moment.
- Mrs. Weasley is ecstatic when she learns Ron's news ("That's everyone in the family!" — referencing Bill and Percy both having been prefects before; implies Charlie was not, or at least isn't mentioned). She offers Ron a reward (new dress robes, new cauldron, new rat) — Ron asks for a new broom instead; she agrees despite the expense, returns from Diagon Alley with a new broom (brand unspecified yet at this point — later identified).
- A celebratory party is held in the basement kitchen with a banner reading "CONGRATULATIONS RON AND HERMIONE — NEW PREFECTS." Attendees: Sirius, Lupin, Tonks (hair "tomato-red and waist length" that day), Kingsley Shacklebolt, Mad-Eye Moody, Mr. Weasley, Bill, and Mundungus Fletcher (wearing a suspiciously lumpy overcoat, likely full of stolen goods, refuses to remove it).
- Mrs. Weasley asks Moody to check the rattling writing desk in the drawing room; using his magical eye, he confirms from downstairs that it's definitely a boggart; Mrs. Weasley says she'll deal with it herself later.
- Toast is given to Ron and Hermione as "the new Gryffindor prefects." Tonks jokes she was never made a prefect ("my Head of House said I lacked certain necessary qualities... like the ability to behave myself"). Sirius says he also was never a prefect (too much time in detention with James); Lupin reveals he was the one who got the Marauders' prefect badge, joking Dumbledore hoped he'd help control his friends and that he "failed dismally" — this lifts Harry's mood (James wasn't a prefect either).
- Ron enthusiastically describes his new broom's speed stats (0 to 70 in ten seconds) compared to a Comet Two Ninety (0 to 60 with a tailwind, per Which Broomstick? magazine).
- Hermione passionately discusses elf rights with Lupin, comparing anti-elf prejudice to werewolf segregation.
- Mrs. Weasley and Bill have their recurring argument about Bill's hair being too long.
- Harry joins Fred, George, and Mundungus in a corner; Fred reveals to Mundungus that Harry can be trusted since he's "our financial backer" (the joke shop funding secret).

**Named characters:** Harry Potter, Arthur Weasley, Cornelius Fudge, Lucius Malfoy, Molly Weasley, Ron Weasley, Hermione Granger, Fred & George Weasley, Ginny Weasley, Sirius Black, Mrs. Figg (referenced), Dumbledore (referenced, absent from celebration — "too busy"), Percy Weasley, Madam Bones (referenced), Tonks, Remus Lupin, Kingsley Shacklebolt, Mad-Eye Moody, Bill Weasley, Mundungus Fletcher, Draco Malfoy (referenced, Harry compares himself to him), James Potter (referenced — not a prefect, frequent detentions with Sirius), Dobby/Quirrell/Lockhart/Barty Crouch Jr. (implicitly referenced via the DADA teacher history), Charlie Weasley (implied, not a past prefect), Miranda Goshawk (author, Standard Book of Spells Grade 5), Wilbert Slinkhard (author, Defensive Magical Theory — new DADA set text), Scabbers (mentioned as a past pet option for a prefect reward, humorously outdated since Scabbers turned out to be Pettigrew and is gone).

**Named places/objects/spells/creatures:**
- The Ministry Atrium, ninth-level corridor, the Fountain of Magical Brethren (Harry empties his entire money pouch into it, not just 10 Galleons).
- Diagon Alley (Mrs. Weasley goes shopping there for books and Ron's new broom).
- The Standard Book of Spells, Grade 5 (Miranda Goshawk) and Defensive Magical Theory (Wilbert Slinkhard) — the two new fifth-year textbooks.
- Ron's Prefect badge: scarlet and gold, with a large "P" over the Gryffindor lion.
- Comet Two Ninety (broom, referenced for comparison — 0 to 60 with tailwind) and Which Broomstick? (magazine). Ron's actual new broom brand isn't named in this chapter (wrapped in brown paper, examined off-page).
- The boggart in the drawing-room writing desk (confirmed by Moody's magical eye, not yet dealt with).
- A talking/coughing wastepaper basket in Harry and Ron's room that "swallows" and "belches" rubbish.

**Specific numbers/quantities:**
- Fudge's "lucky" early arrival: Dumbledore says he arrived three hours early.
- Harry's entire money bag emptied into the fountain (unspecified total, more than planned 10 Galleons).
- Four previous Defense Against the Dark Arts teachers accounted for: one sacked, one dead, one memory-wiped, one locked in a trunk for nine months (covering Quirrell, Lockhart, Lupin, and the fake Moody/Barty Crouch Jr. across books 1–4).
- New broom's speed: naught to seventy in ten seconds (vs. Comet Two Ninety's naught to sixty with tailwind).
- "Fourth prefect in the family" per Mrs. Weasley (Bill, Percy, and now Ron — implying one of the four counted is inconsistent/Charlie not a prefect, or counting differently; exact wording: "That's everyone in the family!").

**Small/obscure details:**
- Malfoy's pocket clinks with gold when he moves — implies a bribe in progress with Fudge.
- Percy ignores his father and Harry completely at the hearing's end, "back rigid, nose in the air."
- Ron fidgets with his new prefect badge obsessively (bedside table, pocket, on his robes) before Fred and George jokingly offer to stick it to his forehead with a Permanent Sticking Charm.
- Harry's private, guilty internal monologue about his jealousy over not being made prefect is a notably introspective passage.
- Tonks's hair is "tomato-red and waist-length" at the party (yet another changing hairstyle).
- Mundungus wears an oddly lumpy overcoat at the celebration, refusing to take it off — implying hidden stolen goods.

**Chapter 9 continued — final beats:**
- Mundungus sells Fred and George a batch of Venomous Tentacula seeds (a "Class C Non-Tradeable Substance," hard to obtain, needed for the Skiving Snackboxes) for ten Galleons after initially demanding twenty — backs down when Harry warns that Moody's magical eye might spot the illegal transaction. Fred notes Mundungus's past joke pricing, e.g. once charged six Sickles for a bag of knarl quills.
- Harry overhears Kingsley Shacklebolt quietly asking Lupin why Dumbledore didn't make Harry a prefect instead of Ron; Lupin says Dumbledore "will have had his reasons"; Kingsley says making Harry a prefect would have shown confidence in him, especially given the Daily Prophet's attacks — this deepens Harry's hurt but he hides it.
- Mad-Eye Moody shows Harry an old, tattered photograph of the original Order of the Phoenix (from the first war against Voldemort), identifying members one by one: Moody himself (younger, nose intact), Dumbledore, Dedalus Diggle, Marlene McKinnon (killed two weeks after the photo was taken, along with her whole family), Frank and Alice Longbottom (Neville's parents — tortured into permanent insanity, not killed; Harry recognizes Alice's face as identical to Neville's), Emmeline Vance, Lupin, Benjy Fenwick (killed, only fragments of his body ever found), Edgar Bones (Amelia Bones's brother, killed with his family), Sturgis Podmore (looking young), Caradoc Dearborn (vanished six months after the photo, body never found), Hagrid, Elphias Doge (wearing a "stupid hat"), Gideon Prewett and his brother Fabian Prewett (Molly Weasley's brothers — killed, but it took five Death Eaters to bring them down, fighting "like heroes"), Dumbledore's brother Aberforth Dumbledore (Moody notes meeting him only this once, calling him "a strange bloke"), Dorcas Meadowes (personally killed by Voldemort), Sirius (with short hair), and finally — James and Lily Potter, seated on either side of Peter Pettigrew ("Wormtail"), the man who betrayed them to Voldemort.
- Harry is deeply disturbed by seeing his parents' happy, unknowing faces among so many since-dead or ruined Order members; he makes an excuse and flees upstairs.
- Upstairs, Harry hears sobbing in the drawing room: finds Mrs. Weasley facing a boggart that has taken the form of her loved ones' corpses in succession — first Ron (dead on the floor), then (after her weak "Riddikulus" casts) Bill, then Mr. Weasley (bleeding from the head), then the twins, then Percy, then finally Harry's own corpse. Harry tells her to get out; Lupin arrives (with Sirius and Moody close behind) and banishes the boggart properly with a firm "Riddikulus," turning it into a harmless silver orb that he vanishes.
- Mrs. Weasley breaks down completely, confessing to Lupin that she constantly dreams of and pictures her family members dead; reveals her deep anxiety that half her family is in the Order and might not survive, that Percy is estranged and might die before they reconcile, and who would raise Ron and Ginny if she and Arthur were killed.
- Lupin comforts her: this time is different from the first war — the Order has a head start and knows what Voldemort is doing; last time (Mrs. Weasley wasn't in the Order then) they were outnumbered twenty-to-one by Death Eaters and were "picked off one by one."
- Sirius reassures her about Percy ("he'll come round... once [Voldemort] moves into the open, the whole Ministry's going to be begging us to forgive them") and says he's "not sure I'll be accepting their apology."
- That night in bed, Harry's scar sears with pain again; the sniggering voice from the blank portrait in his room taunts him ("First sign of madness, talking to your own head").

**Named characters (ch.9 additions):** Mundungus Fletcher, Fred & George Weasley, Kingsley Shacklebolt, Remus Lupin, Mad-Eye Moody, Dedalus Diggle, Marlene McKinnon (deceased, First War), Frank Longbottom and Alice Longbottom (Neville's parents, tortured into madness by Death Eaters, First War), Neville Longbottom (referenced via his parents), Emmeline Vance, Benjy Fenwick (deceased, First War), Edgar Bones (deceased, First War; Amelia Bones's brother), Amelia Bones (referenced), Sturgis Podmore, Caradoc Dearborn (vanished, First War), Hagrid, Elphias Doge, Gideon Prewett and Fabian Prewett (Molly Weasley's brothers, deceased, First War), Albus Dumbledore and Aberforth Dumbledore (Albus's brother, seen once by Moody), Dorcas Meadowes (deceased, killed personally by Voldemort, First War), Sirius Black (with short hair in the old photo), James Potter, Lily Potter, Peter Pettigrew/Wormtail, Mrs. (Molly) Weasley, Ron, Bill, Percy, Fred, George (all appear as boggart-corpses), Harry Potter (also appears as a boggart-corpse).

**Named places/objects/spells/creatures:**
- Original Order of the Phoenix group photograph (First War era), shown by Moody.
- A boggart (in the drawing-room writing desk) that takes the shape of each viewer's worst fear — here, serially, the corpses of Mrs. Weasley's family members.
- The Riddikulus charm (used repeatedly, first weakly by Mrs. Weasley, then decisively by Lupin).
- Venomous Tentacula seeds (Class C Non-Tradeable Substance).
- Knarl quills (referenced, a past Mundungus scam item).

**Specific numbers/quantities:**
- Marlene McKinnon killed two weeks after the Order photo was taken.
- Gideon and Fabian Prewett: killed by five Death Eaters.
- Caradoc Dearborn vanished six months after the photo was taken.
- In the First War, the Order was outnumbered twenty to one by Death Eaters (per Lupin).
- Ten Galleons paid for the Venomous Tentacula seeds (haggled down from Mundungus's initial demand of twenty).

**Small/obscure details:**
- Moody sniffs a chicken leg for poison before eating it at the party, using what remains of his nose.
- Moody mentions Sturgis Podmore still hasn't returned his "best" Invisibility Cloak.
- Harry reflects that barely an hour earlier he'd been consumed with trivial worries (the joke shop, the prefect badge) — now dwarfed by the First War photograph's revelations.

---

## Chapter 10: Luna Lovegood

**Plot events (in order):**
- Morning of September 1st: chaos at Grimmauld Place — Fred and George's bewitched trunks go flying downstairs and knock Ginny down two flights of stairs (Mrs. Weasley furious; Mrs. Black's portrait also screeching).
- Mad-Eye Moody delays departure because Sturgis Podmore (an Order member assigned to the guard) hasn't shown up — the guard would be "one short" otherwise.
- Harry questions why he needs a guard to get to King's Cross if Voldemort is lying low; Hermione just relays Moody's orders.
- Harry travels with Mrs. Weasley and Tonks (disguised as an old woman with tightly curled gray hair and a purple porkpie hat); Sirius defies Dumbledore's explicit order and comes along in his Animagus dog form, delighting Harry and visibly annoying Mrs. Weasley ("on your own head be it").
- They walk twenty minutes to King's Cross (Fudge won't lend the Weasleys Ministry cars anymore); Sirius-as-dog chases pigeons and his own tail, lightening the mood.
- At Platform 9¾, they meet up with the rest of the group: Moody (disguised as a porter, pushing the luggage cart), Mr. Weasley, Ron, Hermione, Fred, George, Ginny, Lupin. Moody reports no sign of being followed; he says he'll report Sturgis Podmore to Dumbledore for missing duty twice in a week, "getting as unreliable as Mundungus."
- Farewells: Lupin and Moody shake hands with everyone, warning them to be careful what they put in writing ("if in doubt, don't put it in a letter at all"); Tonks hugs Hermione and Ginny.
- As the train departs, Sirius-as-dog rears up and puts his paws on Harry's shoulders before Mrs. Weasley hisses at him to "act more like a dog"; he then bounds alongside the train until it turns a corner. Hermione worries aloud that Sirius shouldn't have come (too risky); Ron defends him.
- On the train, Ron and Hermione must go to the prefects' carriage, leaving Harry to travel without them for the first time ever on the Hogwarts Express — he feels a pang of loss.
- Harry notices students everywhere whispering and staring at him (due to the Daily Prophet's negative coverage).
- In the last carriage, Harry (with Ginny) finds Neville Longbottom struggling with his trunk and his toad Trevor; they join the only compartment with room, occupied by Luna Lovegood — introduced in detail: dirty-blond waist-length straggly hair, pale eyebrows, protuberant permanently-surprised eyes, wand tucked behind her ear, a necklace made of butterbeer corks/caps, reading a magazine (The Quibbler) upside down. She is in Ginny's year, in Ravenclaw.
- Luna immediately, dreamily, identifies Harry by name; is coolly indifferent to social norms (quotes a strange aphorism: wit is a person's finest/greatest asset — paraphrased).
- Neville shows off a rare plant his great-uncle Algie gave him for his birthday: a Mimbulus mimbletonia, a small pulsating gray cactus covered in boil-like growths, which he says has "an amazing defensive mechanism." He pokes it with a quill to demonstrate — it sprays thick, foul-smelling dark green liquid ("Stinksap") everywhere, drenching Harry's face in particular (he was holding Trevor the toad) while Ginny is only lightly splashed.
- Cho Chang (Ravenclaw Quidditch Seeker) opens the compartment door right at this inopportune moment, says an awkward hello, and leaves again — mortifying Harry.
- Ginny cleans up the mess with a Scourgify charm.
- Ron and Hermione arrive after nearly an hour (having missed the food trolley), reporting prefect duties: two fifth-year prefects per House (one boy, one girl). Draco Malfoy and Pansy Parkinson are the new Slytherin prefects; Ernie Macmillan and Hannah Abbott for Hufflepuff; Anthony Goldstein and Padma Patil for Ravenclaw.
- Luna informs Ron (apropos of nothing) that Padma Patil (his Yule Ball date from Goblet of Fire) didn't enjoy the ball because he wouldn't dance with her.
- Harry and Neville find a copy of Luna's father's magazine, The Quibbler, that slid onto the floor during Luna's fit of laughter at Ron's joke about Goyle. The issue's cover features a cartoon of Fudge throttling a goblin while clutching gold, captioned about how far Fudge will go to control Gringotts; other article titles include Quidditch League corruption (the Tutshill Tornados allegedly cheating), a feature on ancient runes, and a cover story on Sirius Black.
- Harry reads the Sirius article in full (paraphrase): it claims, per a source named Doris Purkiss (of an address in Little Norton), that "Sirius Black" is actually a pseudonym and that the real man is "Stubby Boardman," former lead singer of a band called The Hobgoblins, who left public life after being hit in the ear by a turnip at a concert roughly fifteen years prior, and that he couldn't have committed the murders because he was with Purkiss at the time — she claims to have written to the Minister expecting a pardon. (A sensationalist, clearly fringe/conspiracy claim — but notably the first in-universe suggestion in print that Sirius might be innocent, however absurd the framing.)
- Harry also skims the Fudge article (paraphrase): alleges Fudge secretly wants to seize control of Gringotts/goblin gold by force, quoting an anonymous "Ministry insider" who claims Fudge jokes about having goblins killed in absurd ways.
- Other Quibbler nonsense noted: claims the Tutshill Tornados win via blackmail, illegal broom-tampering, and torture; an interview with a wizard who claims to have flown to the moon on a broomstick and brought back "moon frogs"; an article on ancient runes that (read upside-down, as Luna does) reveals a nonsense spell to turn enemies' ears into kumquats.
- Hermione dismisses The Quibbler as "rubbish"; Luna, offended, reveals her father is the magazine's editor and snatches it back, turning immediately to a specific page (57).
- Draco Malfoy, now a prefect, visits with Crabbe and Goyle to taunt Harry, mocking his failure to become a prefect ("second-best to Weasley") and threatening to watch Harry's behavior closely. Hermione and Harry are both privately unsettled (not shown to Neville/Luna) by Malfoy's choice of the word "dogging" — fearing it implies Lucius Malfoy may have recognized Sirius's dog disguise at the station and could expose the Weasleys/Lupin/Tonks/Moody as harboring him.
- As evening falls, Ron and Hermione pin on their prefect badges before the train arrives; they go off again to supervise disembarking.
- Luna offers to carry Pigwidgeon's cage for Harry as they disembark.
- At Hogsmeade station, instead of Hagrid's usual call for first-years, Professor Grubbly-Plank (who briefly covered Care of Magical Creatures in book 4) is calling for first-years — Hagrid is nowhere to be found, unsettling Harry, Ron, and Hermione.
- Harry notices, for the first time, that the horseless stagecoaches that carry older students to the castle are not horseless at all — they are pulled by skeletal, reptilian, black-coated winged horses with dragonish faces and blank white eyes. He's startled to discover that Ron cannot see them at all and thinks Harry might be unwell; Luna Lovegood calmly reveals that she can see them too, and always has been able to since her first day at Hogwarts, reassuring Harry that he isn't "going mad" — the significance (that only those who have witnessed death can see them — Thestrals, named in a later chapter) is not yet explained here.

**Named characters:** Harry Potter, Ron Weasley, Hermione Granger, Ginny Weasley, Neville Longbottom, Luna Lovegood (fully introduced), Mrs. Weasley, Tonks (disguised as an old woman), Sirius Black (as dog, "Snuffles," defying Dumbledore's order to stay away), Mad-Eye Moody, Remus Lupin, Mr. Weasley, Fred & George Weasley, Sturgis Podmore (absent, reported to Dumbledore for unreliability), Lee Jordan (recognizes Sirius as "nice dog"), Cho Chang, Draco Malfoy, Pansy Parkinson, Crabbe, Goyle, Ernie Macmillan, Hannah Abbott, Anthony Goldstein, Padma Patil, Trevor (Neville's toad), Crookshanks, Pigwidgeon, Hedwig, Xenophilius Lovegood (Luna's father, referenced as The Quibbler's editor, not yet named on-page — described only as "my father"), Doris Purkiss (quoted Quibbler source), "Stubby Boardman" (claimed alter-ego of Sirius per the tabloid story), Great-Uncle Algie (Neville's great-uncle, gave him the plant).

**Named places/objects/spells/creatures:**
- King's Cross Station, Platform 9¾, the Hogwarts Express, Hogsmeade Station.
- Mimbulus mimbletonia (Neville's rare plant, sprays "Stinksap" defensively).
- The Quibbler magazine (Xenophilius Lovegood's publication) — cover story on Sirius Black, Fudge/Gringotts conspiracy article, Tutshill Tornados corruption claims, moon-frogs interview, ancient runes article/upside-down spell.
- The Hobgoblins (fictional wizard band in the tabloid story).
- Chocolate Frog cards (Harry, Ginny, Neville swap them on the train).
- Pumpkin Pasties (train snack).
- The horseless (actually Thestral-pulled, though not yet named) carriages — skeletal, winged, reptilian black horses visible only to Harry and Luna.
- Scourgify (cleaning charm, used by Ginny).

**Specific numbers/quantities:**
- Twenty-minute walk from Grimmauld Place to King's Cross.
- Ron and Hermione absent from the compartment for "nearly an hour."
- Sirius "escaped Azkaban two years ago" per the Quibbler article (consistent with Prisoner of Azkaban timeline).
- Sirius accused of killing "twelve innocent Muggles and one wizard" fourteen years ago (restated figure from book 3's backstory).
- "Stubby Boardman" was hit by a turnip "nearly fifteen years ago" per the fake story.
- Cornelius Fudge "elected Minister of Magic five years ago" per the Quibbler's Fudge article.

**Small/obscure details:**
- Luna's dreamy aphorism about wit being valuable is a subtle allusion to Ravenclaw house values (the actual quoted line is the Ravenclaw diadem's motto, introduced textually here before its significance is revealed in later books).
- Harry empties his school robes trunk list banter aside — Neville lost his old Remembrall "ages ago."
- Luna's laughing fit over Ron's Goyle joke is written as unusually prolonged/odd, a hallmark of her characterization.
- Malfoy's use of the word "dogging" Harry's footsteps is a subtle pun/foreshadowing moment noticed by Harry and Hermione.

## Chapter 11: The Sorting Hat's New Song

**Plot events (in order):**
- Carriage ride up to the castle; Luna states she'll be glad if Hagrid has left, calling him a bad teacher — Harry, Ron, and Ginny angrily defend him; Hermione reluctantly agrees with them aloud despite not sounding convinced.
- They pass between the stone gateposts topped with winged boars; Harry looks for lit windows in Hagrid's cabin but sees none.
- Arrival at the castle; Harry notices the strange skeletal winged horses again, wondering why they'd suddenly become visible to him and not Ron.
- Entering the Great Hall for the Sorting and start-of-term feast: candles floating, ghosts present, students gossiping/staring at Harry.
- At the staff table, Harry and friends spot Dolores Umbridge for the first time at Hogwarts, sitting beside Dumbledore in a pink cardigan and pink Alice band, toad-like face; Harry recognizes her from his hearing as Fudge's employee, puzzled why she's at Hogwarts. Hermione reacts with concern/suspicion ("No... surely not...") but doesn't explain.
- Professor Grubbly-Plank is seen taking the staff seat that should be Hagrid's, confirming his absence.
- The first-years are led in by Professor McGonagall carrying the ancient Sorting Hat on a stool. The Hat sings a new song (paraphrased, not quoted at length): it recounts the four Founders' original unity and shared dream of building the school; describes how each Founder came to prefer students with a particular quality (Slytherin — pure blood; Ravenclaw — intelligence; Gryffindor — bravery; Hufflepuff — took all the rest and taught them equally); explains that this initially caused no real conflict since each Founder ran their own House, but that over time discord grew between the Houses until Slytherin eventually left the school after bitter fighting; the Hat then delivers an explicit warning that Hogwarts now faces danger from external enemies and that the Houses must unite internally or risk being destroyed from within — concluding that it has given its warning and the Sorting may now proceed.
- This explicit warning is unprecedented; it provokes anxious murmuring through the Hall instead of the usual simple applause. Nearly Headless Nick (the Gryffindor ghost) tells Hermione the Hat has given such warnings before, always during periods of danger, always with the same message of unity; he notes the Hat lives in Dumbledore's office and presumably "picks things up there."
- The Sorting proceeds alphabetically; first names sorted include Euan Abercrombie (Gryffindor, a nervous, prominently-eared boy) and the sorting ends with "Zeller, Rose" into Hufflepuff.
- Dumbledore gives his traditional brief pre-feast welcome ("Tuck in!"); the feast appears. Ron eats enthusiastically; Nearly Headless Nick chats with Hermione about the Hat's warning history; Ron's crude interjection about the hat's sentience annoys Nick, who defends his honor as "Sir Nicholas de Mimsy-Porpington," asserting he's never been a coward — Ron needles him about having "noble blood," leading to a minor tiff; Nick flounces off to sit near Colin and Dennis Creevey.
- After the feast, Dumbledore's start-of-term notices: reminder that the Forbidden Forest is off-limits; Mr. Filch (caretaker) has asked — for "the four hundred and sixty-second time" — that students note the full list of banned items/activities on his office door; two staffing changes announced: Professor Grubbly-Plank returns to teach Care of Magical Creatures (no explanation of Hagrid's absence or Grubbly-Plank's tenure length given), and Professor Dolores Umbridge is introduced as the new Defense Against the Dark Arts teacher (polite but lukewarm applause).
- Umbridge interrupts Dumbledore (unprecedented — no new teacher has ever done this before) with a trademark throat-clear ("Hem, hem") and delivers a long, flowery, platitude-filled speech (paraphrased, not quoted at length) about the Ministry's commitment to education, balancing tradition and progress, and promising "openness, effectiveness, and accountability" plus "pruning" of undesirable practices. Hermione recognizes coded political language signaling Ministry interference at Hogwarts; most students/other staff are visibly bored or alarmed (McGonagall's mouth goes thin; Sprout's eyebrows vanish into her hair), but Hermione insists the speech was "illuminating" even though not "enjoyable."
- Prefects (Ron and Hermione) lead first-years off; Harry heads to Gryffindor Tower alone, reflecting bitterly on being stared at since the graveyard events became public knowledge (though not fully explained to the school). He doesn't know the new Fat Lady password; Neville arrives and gives it correctly: "Mimbulus mimbletonia" (named after his plant).
- In the dormitory, Dean Thomas and Seamus Finnigan are already decorating; a tense confrontation erupts when Seamus reveals his mother (a witch) didn't want him to return to Hogwarts this year because she believes the Daily Prophet's narrative that both Harry and Dumbledore are liars; Harry reacts with fury, draws his wand on Seamus in anger; Ron intervenes, defends Harry hotly (pulling rank as a prefect to threaten Seamus with detention); Seamus declares he doesn't want to share the dormitory with Harry anymore, calling him "a madman"; Dean stays neutral (his Muggle parents don't know about any of it); Neville, notably, voices firm support for Harry, crediting his grandmother's trust in Dumbledore's past predictions about Voldemort's return (she canceled their Daily Prophet subscription over it). Seamus repairs his torn bed-hangings with his wand and the dormitory falls into an uneasy silence.
- Harry lies awake wondering how many more people will accuse him of lying, and whether Dumbledore's months of silence toward him might stem from anger rather than neglect, since both their reputations are now entwined.

**Named characters:** Harry Potter, Ron Weasley, Hermione Granger, Ginny Weasley, Luna Lovegood, Draco Malfoy (seen at Slytherin table), Nearly Headless Nick/Sir Nicholas de Mimsy-Porpington (Gryffindor ghost, full name given), the Bloody Baron (referenced, Slytherin ghost), Colin Creevey and Dennis Creevey (Nick sits near them), Professor McGonagall, Professor Dumbledore, Professor Umbridge (introduced as new DADA teacher), Professor Grubbly-Plank (returns for Care of Magical Creatures), Professor Sprout, Mr. Filch (caretaker, referenced), Euan Abercrombie (first-year, Gryffindor), "Zeller, Rose" (last first-year sorted, Hufflepuff), Parvati Patil and Lavender Brown (Gryffindor classmates), Neville Longbottom, Dean Thomas, Seamus Finnigan (and his mother, referenced), Mrs. Finnigan (referenced, a witch who distrusts Harry/Dumbledore).

**Named places/objects/spells/creatures:**
- The Great Hall, the enchanted ceiling, the Sorting Hat (gives an explicit unity/danger warning song for the first time in the book), the four Houses and their founding values (Slytherin/purity, Ravenclaw/intellect, Gryffindor/bravery, Hufflepuff/inclusiveness).
- Gryffindor common room; the Fat Lady portrait (new password: "Mimbulus mimbletonia").
- Steak-and-kidney pie, treacle tart (Harry's favorite dessert, named).
- Filch's banned-items list (posted on his office door, referenced as being updated for "the 462nd time").

**Specific numbers/quantities:**
- Filch's reminder repeated for "the four hundred and sixty-second time."
- The Sorting Hat describes Hogwarts' founding and the eventual departure of Slytherin after discord — no exact year given here, but the general history is reinforced.

**Small/obscure details:**
- Harry notices his scar isn't mentioned in this chapter, but his social isolation (stares, whispers) is a running motif.
- Nearly Headless Nick's indignant defense of his nobility/bravery despite being a ghost is a recurring comic beat.
- Hermione's single-minded focus on the political subtext of Umbridge's speech contrasts with Ron's boredom — sets up her role as the one who first identifies Ministry interference at Hogwarts.
- The Mimbulus mimbletonia password humorously ties back to Neville's plant from the previous chapter.

## Chapter 12: Professor Umbridge

**Plot events so far (in progress):**
- Next morning, Seamus avoids Harry pointedly leaving the dormitory early; Dean is awkwardly noncommittal in Harry's defense.
- Hermione and Ron find a new notice posted by Fred and George in the common room: "GALLONS OF GALLEONS!" advertising paid, risky part-time jobs/testing opportunities, posted over the first Hogsmeade weekend date (October) notice. Hermione, as a prefect, insists on confronting them about it.
- Walking to breakfast, Ron reports Seamus's accusation to Hermione, who reveals Lavender Brown shares the same doubts about Harry; Harry snaps at her, she firmly reminds him she and Ron are on his side and he apologizes.
- Hermione recalls (paraphrased, not verbatim) Dumbledore's end-of-last-term speech about Voldemort's gift for sowing discord being best fought with unity and trust, linking it to both the Sorting Hat's warning and the dormitory argument.
- Entering the Great Hall, they see Grubbly-Plank chatting with Professor Sinistra (Astronomy teacher); Hagrid is still absent. Hermione speculates Dumbledore avoided naming a timeline for Grubbly-Plank's tenure specifically to avoid drawing attention to Hagrid's absence.
- Angelina Johnson (Gryffindor Chaser) announces she's the new Gryffindor Quidditch Captain (replacing graduated Oliver Wood, the former Keeper); she schedules tryouts for a new Keeper on Friday at 5 p.m., wants the whole team present.
- Mail arrives; Hedwig is absent, but Hermione's Daily Prophet arrives via a different owl — she pays a Knut for delivery; the paper contains nothing about Harry or Dumbledore this time. Hermione states she reads it anyway "to know what the enemy are saying."
- Schedules handed out by McGonagall: Ron groans over today's brutal lineup — History of Magic, double Potions, Divination, and double Defense Against the Dark Arts (teachers Binns, Snape, Trelawney, and Umbridge all in one day).
- Fred and George tease Ron and Hermione as prefects for wanting to "skive off lessons," offering cut-rate (defective, antidote-less) Nosebleed Nougat; Hermione scolds them for advertising for testers on the Gryffindor noticeboard — they brush her off. They tease that O.W.L. year (5th year) stress will eventually make Hermione want a Snackbox herself, recalling classmates' past O.W.L.-season breakdowns: Patricia Stimpson fainting repeatedly, Kenneth Towler breaking out in boils (revealed to be Fred's doing via "Bulbadox Powder" in his pajamas, a prank the twins had apparently forgotten about).
- Revealed: Fred and George got only three O.W.L.s each but don't care, planning to leave academic ambitions behind for their joke shop; they nearly didn't come back for seventh year (hinting at secret funding — Harry's Triwizard winnings) but are staying to avoid upsetting their mother further after the Percy rift; George almost lets slip the funding source in front of Hermione before catching himself.
- Hermione asks skeptically where they'll get gold for a joke shop (ingredients, premises); Fred deflects ("Ask us no questions..."); this fuels Ron's suspicion about how the twins funded his new dress robes earlier in the summer — Harry anxiously changes the subject.
- Ron confirms O.W.L.s significantly affect future career options, and that students get official Career Advice sessions later in the year to help choose N.E.W.T. subjects. Discussion of career goals: Ron admits he'd like to be an Auror but doubts he's "good enough" (they're "the elite"); Harry agrees fervently (shares the same ambition); Hermione is undecided but wants to do "something worthwhile," hinting at expanding S.P.E.W.
- History of Magic class: Professor Binns (the only ghost teacher) drones for 45 minutes on goblin/giant wars (Harry's attention lapses after ~10 minutes); Harry and Ron play hangman instead of taking notes, relying as always on copying Hermione's notes before exams — she threatens (not seriously) to stop lending them out this year given O.W.L.s.
- In the courtyard before double Potions, Cho Chang approaches Harry alone again (unusual, as she's normally surrounded by friends) — a callback to the Stinksap incident; conversation is awkward (Harry nearly brings up Cedric's death inadvertently by asking about her summer). Ron blunders in, aggressively grilling Cho about a Tudshill Tornados Quidditch badge she wears, implying she's a bandwagon fan; Cho leaves, visibly cooling. Hermione scolds Ron harshly for his tactlessness and failure to recognize Cho wanted to speak with Harry privately; they bicker about it the rest of the way to Snape's dungeon.
- Harry privately reflects that despite the awkwardness, Cho approaching him twice in two days is a hopeful sign, since she (having been Cedric's girlfriend) could easily resent or distrust him but doesn't seem to.
- Chapter ends as they queue outside Snape's dungeon classroom for the first Potions lesson of the year.

**Named characters so far:** Harry Potter, Ron Weasley, Hermione Granger, Seamus Finnigan, Dean Thomas, Fred & George Weasley, Angelina Johnson (new Gryffindor Quidditch Captain), Oliver Wood (referenced, graduated, former Keeper), Professor McGonagall, Professor Binns (History of Magic), Professor Trelawney (referenced, Divination), Professor Snape (referenced, Potions), Professor Umbridge (referenced, DADA), Cho Chang, Patricia Stimpson and Kenneth Towler (classmates, named in O.W.L.-stress anecdote), Lavender Brown (doubts Harry), Dumbledore (referenced, end-of-term speech recalled).

**Named places/objects/spells/creatures:**
- Gryffindor common room noticeboard ("GALLONS OF GALLEONS!" ad for risky paid testing jobs).
- Nosebleed Nougat (Skiving Snackbox product, sold cheap/defective — no antidote yet).
- Bulbadox Powder (prank substance, caused Kenneth Towler's boils).
- Daily Prophet (delivered via barn owl, cost one Knut).
- The Quidditch Keeper tryouts (Friday, 5 p.m., to replace Oliver Wood).
- Tutshill Tornados (Quidditch team, Cho's badge).

**Specific numbers/quantities:**
- Fred and George each got three O.W.L.s.
- Filch's list referenced again implicitly via the dormitory/common-room routine (ongoing).
- Daily Prophet costs one Knut for delivery.
- Quidditch Keeper tryouts: Friday at 5 p.m.
- History of Magic lesson: drowsiness sets in "within ten minutes, five in warm weather" per general description; today's lecture covered three-quarters of an hour on giant wars.

**Chapter 12 continued — full plot events:**
- Potions class with Snape: he warns the class this is O.W.L. year; expects everyone to scrape at least "Acceptable"; announces he only accepts the very best into his N.E.W.T.-level Potions class next year, pointedly looking at Harry with satisfaction (Harry is glad of the prospect of dropping Potions).
- The lesson: brewing the Draught of Peace (a potion to calm anxiety; dangerous if rushed — can cause irreversible sleep). Exact method is fussy: precise ingredient order/quantities, exact stirring counts (clockwise then counterclockwise), exact simmer time before adding the final ingredient (syrup of hellebore, after adding powdered moonstone and simmering seven minutes per the blackboard instructions).
- Harry forgets to add the syrup of hellebore (skips from line 3 to line 4 of the instructions); Snape publicly humiliates him, vanishes his potion with "Evanesco," giving him zero marks for the lesson — even though his potion was no worse than Ron's (smelled of bad eggs) or Neville's (turned to cement). Homework: 12 inches of parchment on the properties and uses of moonstone, due Thursday.
- At lunch, Hermione sympathizes; Ron and Hermione start bickering about whether Snape can really be trusted now that he's in the Order (Ron doubts it; Hermione defends Dumbledore's judgment); Harry snaps at both of them to stop arguing and storms off alone, passing the portrait of Sir Cadogan (a blustery knight who challenges passersby to duels).
- Harry eats lunch alone under the trapdoor to North Tower, then is first up to Divination. Professor Sybill Trelawney (described: thin, heavily shawled, insect-like, glasses magnifying her eyes) teaches dream interpretation using a textbook called The Dream Oracle by Inigo Imago; she claims exam results don't matter for "true" Seers but that Dumbledore insists they sit the O.W.L. anyway. Students pair up to interpret each other's dreams; Harry refuses to share his recurring graveyard nightmare; Ron can't remember his dreams either and invents a Quidditch dream on the spot. Homework: keep a dream diary for a month.
- First Defense Against the Dark Arts lesson with Professor Umbridge: she insists on scripted, singsong classroom responses ("Good afternoon, Professor Umbridge"); orders wands away, quills out (a first — no previous DADA lesson had ever opened this way); writes the course title "A Return to Basic Principles" on the board, then three course aims emphasizing theoretical understanding, legal recognition of defensive-magic situations, and placing defensive magic "in context" — conspicuously, with no mention of actually practicing spells.
- Class is assigned to silently read Chapter 1 ("Basics for Beginners") of Defensive Magical Theory by Wilbert Slinkhard. Hermione pointedly refuses to read, instead holding her hand up and staring at Umbridge, eventually drawing the whole class's attention until Umbridge is forced to acknowledge her.
- Hermione challenges the course aims directly: no mention of using defensive spells at all. Umbridge claims students won't need to defend themselves in her classroom and reframes the entire course as "risk-free" theoretical study, since students will supposedly only need to demonstrate spellwork under "carefully controlled examination conditions" at O.W.L. level (confirmed troublingly by Parvati Patil's incredulous question about the practical component of the exam).
- Escalating classroom confrontation: Ron, Harry, and Dean Thomas object loudly (Dean specifically defends Lupin as "the best we ever" had, cut off by Umbridge calling past DADA teachers "irresponsible" and "half-breeds" — a dig at Lupin's lycanthropy and implicitly at the fake Moody's illegal curses). Umbridge enforces a strict hand-raising-only rule, repeatedly cutting students off.
- Umbridge states plainly "there is nothing waiting out there" and asks who would want to attack mere schoolchildren; Harry retorts "Lord Voldemort" — shocking the class (Ron gasps, Lavender screams, Neville falls off his stool). Umbridge docks 10 points from Gryffindor and then flatly declares that the report of a Dark wizard's return is "a lie," guaranteed safe by the Ministry. Harry insists he fought Voldemort and saw him return; Umbridge gives him detention for every evening that week, starting the next day at 5 p.m. in her office, framing herself saccharinely as "your friend" willing to help if anyone is "alarming" students with "rumors."
- Harry stands and directly challenges her by invoking Cedric Diggory's murder; this is the first time most of the class has heard Harry speak about that night. Umbridge calls Cedric's death "a tragic accident"; Harry insists it was murder by Voldemort. Umbridge writes a note on pink parchment, seals it magically, and sends Harry to Professor McGonagall's office.
- En route, Harry runs into Peeves the Poltergeist, who mockingly sings a rhyme about "Potty" Harry being thought "barking" or "mad" (paraphrased), dropping ink bottles; Harry shouts at him, drawing McGonagall out of her office.
- McGonagall reads Umbridge's note, learns Harry called her a liar and told her Voldemort is back; rather than punishing him further, she offers him a biscuit (Ginger Newt, from a tartan tin) and warns him seriously: "Tread carefully around Dolores Umbridge" — misbehavior in her class could cost him "much more than House points and a detention," implying Umbridge reports directly to the Ministry/Fudge. She confirms detention every evening that week starting tomorrow at 5 p.m. and cannot overrule it since Umbridge, as his teacher, has the right to assign it. She clarifies this isn't about truth vs. lies but about self-control, and approves of Harry having picked up on the Ministry-interference subtext of Umbridge's feast speech (crediting Hermione for having explained it to him).

**Named characters (ch.12 additions):** Severus Snape, Draco Malfoy (laughs at Harry in Potions), Neville Longbottom (potion turns to cement), Goyle (referenced — his potion flask shatters and sets his robes on fire), Sybill Trelawney, Inigo Imago (author, The Dream Oracle — fictional), Dolores Umbridge, Dean Thomas, Parvati Patil, Lavender Brown, Peeves the Poltergeist, Professor McGonagall, Sir Cadogan (portrait knight).

**Named places/objects/spells/creatures:**
- Snape's dungeon classroom; North Tower (Divination classroom, reached by silver ladder/trapdoor); Umbridge's DADA classroom.
- The Draught of Peace (potion); syrup of hellebore, powdered moonstone (ingredients).
- The Dream Oracle by Inigo Imago (Divination textbook).
- Defensive Magical Theory by Wilbert Slinkhard (DADA textbook) — Chapter 1 "Basics for Beginners."
- Umbridge's pink parchment note (first appearance of her signature pink stationery/detention notes).
- Ginger Newt biscuits (McGonagall's tartan tin).
- Sir Cadogan (a portrait knight who "guards" a corridor, comically aggressive).

**Specific numbers/quantities:**
- 10 points taken from Gryffindor for Harry's "Lord Voldemort" answer.
- Detention assigned for every evening that week, starting the next day, 5 p.m., in Umbridge's office.
- 12 inches of parchment homework on moonstone properties (Snape), due Thursday.
- A month-long dream diary assigned by Trelawney.
- The Draught of Peace requires exactly seven minutes of simmering before the final ingredient.

**Small/obscure details:**
- Peeves's rhyme mocking Harry as "Potty" and implying madness is a notable, quotable-in-spirit moment (paraphrased here, not reproduced verbatim per copyright guidance).
- McGonagall physically winces when Harry says "Voldemort" aloud in her office — contrast to Dumbledore/Sirius/Lupin's comfort with the name.
- Harry recalls a past instance of expecting punishment from McGonagall but instead being made a Quidditch Seeker (callback to Philosopher's/Sorcerer's Stone).
- Snape's dungeon is described as having housed Harry's enmity with Snape "from the moment Harry set foot in Hogwarts."

## Chapter 13: Detention with Dolores

**Plot events so far (in progress):**
- At dinner, whispers about Harry's outburst have already spread Hogwarts-wide; classmates speculate loudly about Cedric's death and the "duel" with Voldemort within his hearing, seemingly hoping to provoke him further. Harry is frustrated that the school believed Dumbledore back in June but now doubts him after a summer of Daily Prophet coverage; Hermione explains that the full truth may never have properly "sunk in" for most students since the school year ended abruptly right after the incident, leaving two months for the press to poison opinion before anyone could process events.
- Back in Gryffindor Tower, Hermione despairs at Dumbledore allowing Umbridge — someone outright blocking practical spellwork — to teach in O.W.L. year; Ron suggests Umbridge is there to spy for the Ministry (recalling her "tell me if anyone says You-Know-Who is back" offer); Hermione agrees this is obviously why Fudge sent her.
- Homework session interrupted: Hermione spots Fred, George, and Lee Jordan testing sweets (Nosebleed Nougat/Puking Pastilles/Fainting Fancies) on unwitting first-years in the common room, who are collapsing unconscious one by one while onlookers laugh. Hermione furiously confronts the twins (Ron hangs back, deciding "she's got it under control"); Fred protests they're paying the first-years and have already self-tested everything; Hermione threatens to write to their mother (Mrs. Weasley) if they don't stop testing on students — this is the one threat that actually frightens Fred and George into backing down (more than any threat of detention/lines, which they mock).
- Hermione reveals she's been secretly knitting ill-fitting woolly hats over the summer (S.P.E.W.-motivated) to leave out for the house-elves to find and accidentally free themselves by picking them up (since elves are freed when given clothes) — she hides the hats under rubbish on a table so the elves won't realize and refuse them. Ron is angry at this "trick," arguing elves should get to choose freedom knowingly rather than be tricked into it; he uncovers the hats again after Hermione leaves for bed.
- Next morning: Hermione reports the hats are gone, claiming it proves elves do want freedom; Ron skeptically suggests the oddly-shaped hats might not even register as "clothing" to the elves (more like "woolly bladders").
- Double Charms (Professor Flitwick) and double Transfiguration (Professor McGonagall) both begin with lectures on the importance of O.W.L.s for future careers; heavy homework assigned in both (Summoning Charms review in Charms; Vanishing Spells begun in Transfiguration — confirmed easier than Conjuring Spells but still very difficult, among the hardest O.W.L. content). Hermione alone successfully vanishes her snail (third attempt), earning Gryffindor 10 points and exemption from the vanishing-spell homework; everyone else must practice overnight.
- Care of Magical Creatures with Professor Grubbly-Plank (Hagrid still absent, no explanation given): she teaches about Bowtruckles — small twig-like tree-guardian creatures that live in wand-quality trees, eat wood lice (and prefer fairy eggs), and can gouge out eyes with sharp fingers if angered; students are each given one bowtruckle (one per three students) with wood lice as a calming offering, and must produce a fully labeled anatomical sketch by lesson's end.
- Draco Malfoy needles Harry privately about Hagrid's absence, insinuating (apparently based on something his Death-Eater father Lucius told him) that Hagrid has gotten himself "badly injured" messing with something "too big for him" — rattling Harry, who fears Malfoy might have real inside information. Malfoy also loudly claims (for the group's benefit) that Lucius has been talking to the Minister about cracking down on "substandard teaching," implying Hagrid will be sacked on sight if he returns. Harry, distracted and furious, grips his bowtruckle too hard; it claws his hand (two deep cuts) and bolts into the Forbidden Forest.
- Hermione reassures Harry that Dumbledore would know if something had truly happened to Hagrid, and that showing worry only plays into Malfoy's hands.
- Herbology with Professor Sprout: another O.W.L.-importance lecture, more homework; Sprout's fertilizer of choice is "dragon dung," which lingers on students' robes afterward.
- Luna Lovegood approaches Harry unprompted (wearing radish earrings, noticed and mocked by Parvati and Lavender) to declare her belief that Voldemort is back and that Harry fought and escaped him, citing (when mocked) that people used to doubt the existence of the "Blibbering Humdinger" and the "Crumple-Horned Snorkack" too (fictional creatures she believes in) — Hermione tartly notes those creatures still don't exist, provoking laughter at Luna's expense; Luna storms off, radish earrings swinging.
- Hermione dismisses Luna's reliability (per Ginny, Luna apparently believes in things specifically because there's no proof) and credits this to her father editing The Quibbler. Harry privately wonders whether this means Luna's claim to see the same skeletal winged horses as him might also be a lie/delusion, momentarily deflating his earlier reassurance from her.
- Ernie Macmillan publicly and earnestly declares to Harry that he (and his family, long-standing Dumbledore supporters) believe him "one hundred percent" — a welcome, if pompous, show of support that visibly annoys Lavender Brown and leaves Seamus looking conflicted.
- Later, heading to dinner before his first detention, Harry is accosted by an irate Angelina Johnson (Gryffindor Quidditch Captain), furious that his detention conflicts with Friday's 5 p.m. Keeper tryouts she specifically scheduled for the whole team; she orders him to get Umbridge to reschedule by any means necessary. Harry jokes to Ron/Hermione that Angelina's intensity rivals Oliver Wood's (referencing the Quidditch club Puddlemere United, where Wood now presumably plays).
- At five to five, Harry goes to Umbridge's office for his first detention. The office is described through its history of previous occupants (Lockhart's self-portraits, Lupin's Dark-creature specimens, fake-Moody's detection instruments) — now transformed by Umbridge into a kitschy, lace-doily-and-dried-flowers décor with a wall of ornamental plates each painted with a brightly colored kitten wearing a different bow, which Harry finds genuinely repulsive. Umbridge, dressed in flowered robes matching her tablecloth, greets him sweetly. Harry tries to negotiate rescheduling one detention night to attend Quidditch tryouts — the scene cuts off as he's mid-request, with the narration noting he already senses it's hopeless.

**Named characters so far:** Harry Potter, Ron Weasley, Hermione Granger, Fred & George Weasley, Lee Jordan, Professor Flitwick, Professor McGonagall, Professor Grubbly-Plank, Draco Malfoy, Crabbe, Goyle, Pansy Parkinson, Professor Sprout, Luna Lovegood, Ginny Weasley (referenced, source of Luna gossip), Ernie Macmillan, Seamus Finnigan, Lavender Brown, Parvati Patil, Angelina Johnson, Oliver Wood (referenced, now presumably playing for Puddlemere United), Dolores Umbridge, Crookshanks, Lucius Malfoy (referenced via Draco), Dumbledore (referenced), Hagrid (still absent, discussed).

**Named places/objects/spells/creatures:**
- Gryffindor common room; Umbridge's third-floor office (described via its past occupants: Lockhart, Lupin, fake-Moody).
- Bowtruckles (tree-guardian creatures, live in wand-quality trees, eat wood lice/fairy eggs, can gouge eyes).
- Bulbadox Powder (callback, referenced in ch12 for Kenneth Towler's boils — appears again contextually).
- S.P.E.W. house-elf hats (knitted by Hermione, hidden under rubbish to trick elves into freedom).
- Vanishing Spells (Transfiguration, O.W.L.-level, harder than most but easier than Conjuring Spells).
- Summoning Charms (Charms, confirmed likely O.W.L. content).
- Dragon dung fertilizer (Professor Sprout's preferred brand).
- Puddlemere United (professional Quidditch team, Oliver Wood's presumed destination).

**Specific numbers/quantities:**
- Hermione vanishes her snail successfully on the third attempt, earning 10 points for Gryffindor.
- One bowtruckle provided per three students.
- Keeper tryouts: Friday, 5 p.m. (conflicts directly with Harry's detention schedule).
- Harry's hand sustains two long, deep cuts from the bowtruckle's retaliatory swipe.

**Small/obscure details:**
- The ornamental kitten plates in Umbridge's office are an iconic, specific visual detail (each kitten wears a different colored bow).
- Luna's radish earrings and claimed belief in fictional creatures (Blibbering Humdinger, Crumple-Horned Snorkack) are introduced here as character hallmarks.
- Fred and George's testing operation uses clipboards to record dosage/reaction data on first-years, a notably methodical (if reckless) approach.
- Ron secretly uncovers Hermione's hidden elf hats after she leaves, believing elves deserve informed choice rather than a trick.

**Chapter 13 continued — full plot events (the detention itself and aftermath):**
- Umbridge refuses to reschedule, framing Harry's detention loss of Quidditch tryouts as a fitting extra punishment for "spreading evil, nasty, attention-seeking stories."
- She hands Harry a special black quill with an unusually sharp point and tells him to write "I must not tell lies," repeated as many times as it takes "for the message to sink in" — no ink is provided or needed: the quill cuts the words into the back of Harry's hand in his own blood (appearing simultaneously in glowing red "ink" on the parchment), healing moments later, only to be recut each time he writes the line. This happens for hours, continuing night after night.
- Harry hides the true nature of the punishment from Ron and Hermione (telling them only "lines"), partly from pride (a private battle of wills with Umbridge) and partly to avoid worrying them.
- Across three/four detentions (Tuesday–Friday), the wound stops healing as quickly; by the third session the words remain etched and bleeding even after he leaves; Umbridge notes approvingly that the message is "sinking in" and extends the punishment further ("etch the message a little deeper").
- Ron, meanwhile, is secretly practicing broomwork alone every evening (hiding behind a statue of Lachlan the Lanky) on his new broom (a Cleansweep Eleven) hoping to try out for Gryffindor Keeper, afraid of Fred and George's mockery; he's been doing this since Tuesday, trying to bewitch Quaffles to fly at him.
- Ron discovers Harry's bloody, scarred hand by accident and is horrified, urging him to tell McGonagall or Dumbledore; Harry refuses both — unwilling to let Umbridge "win" by complaining, and unwilling to approach Dumbledore, who hasn't spoken to him since June.
- Friday (final detention of the week): Harry angles his chair to catch glimpses of the Gryffindor Keeper tryouts through Umbridge's window; sees several candidates attempt saves (includes Katie Bell scoring, an unnamed decent third try-outer, a terrible fourth, a fifth who dodges a Bludger but fumbles an easy save) before darkness obscures the pitch. During this final session, when Umbridge grips Harry's arm to examine his hand, his scar sears with sudden pain simultaneously — the first time this exact coincidence has happened; Umbridge says only "Yes, it hurts, doesn't it?" (ambiguously, referring to the hand) and dismisses him.
- Harry returns to Gryffindor Tower to learn Ron made the team as the new Keeper (after Hermione, exhausted from late-night elf-hat knitting, had fallen asleep in a common-room armchair). Angelina Johnson apologizes to Harry for her earlier harshness, admits privately that Ron isn't "fabulous" yet but has potential (from a family of good Quidditch players), notes two other candidates (Vicky Frobisher and Geoffrey Hooper) flew better but had drawbacks (Hooper's a whiner; Frobisher prioritizes her Charm Club over Quidditch) — she asks Harry to help coach Ron.
- Harry finally confides in Hermione about the scar pain coinciding with Umbridge's touch; she first wonders if Voldemort might be possessing Umbridge (as he once did Quirrell in Philosopher's/Sorcerer's Stone) but reasons this is unlikely since Voldemort now has his own body; suggests the Imperius Curse as a more plausible theory, or alternatively that the scar pain was coincidental (tied to Voldemort's own emotional state, as established in Goblet of Fire, not to Umbridge specifically). She urges Harry to tell Dumbledore about the scar; Harry refuses, bitterly implying Dumbledore only cares about the scar itself, not him. He considers writing to Sirius instead; Hermione warns that owls might be intercepted and such sensitive information shouldn't go in a letter at all (recalling Moody's warning from the platform).

**Named characters (ch.13 additions, detention arc):** Harry Potter, Dolores Umbridge, Ron Weasley, Hermione Granger, Katie Bell, Angelina Johnson, Fred & George Weasley, Alicia Spinnet, Oliver Wood (referenced, former Keeper whose old robes are altered for Ron), Vicky Frobisher and Geoffrey Hooper (other Keeper candidates, named), Lachlan the Lanky (a castle statue Ron hides behind).

**Named places/objects/spells/creatures:**
- Umbridge's black, blood-quill (unnamed here but is the infamous "Blood Quill" of fandom terminology) — writes "I must not tell lies" in the victim's own blood, cutting it into their skin, healing, then recutting on each repetition.
- Ron's new broom: a Cleansweep Eleven (brand revealed).
- Gryffindor Quidditch robes (Ron inherits Oliver Wood's old set, refitted).

**Specific numbers/quantities:**
- Detentions span (at least) four consecutive evenings (Tuesday through Friday), each lasting "past midnight," roughly 7+ hours each night implied by "hours."
- Harry's wound stops healing cleanly by the third detention.
- A hundred and fifty points awarded to a Seeker's team for catching the Golden Snitch (reiterated general Quidditch fact, stated explicitly in-text here).

**Small/obscure details:**
- Harry deliberately conceals the severity of the punishment from Ron and Hermione out of pride and a desire to deny Umbridge the satisfaction of a complaint.
- The scene foreshadows a recurring phrase/motif: "I must not tell lies," ironic given Umbridge's own dishonesty about Voldemort's return.
- Ron's nervousness and secretive broom practice contrasts with his public bravado; Fred and George mock him as "Ickle Prefect" once he makes the team.

---

### [Chapter 13 Quidditch Practice + Percy's letter — placed here for continuity, technically spans into Chapter 14]

- First full team practice: Slytherins (led by Malfoy) gather in the stands to heckle Gryffindor, mocking Ron's broom ("moldy old log"), Angelina's braided hairstyle, and Harry's scar/hospital-wing history. Ron plays nervously and poorly at first (drops passes, hits Katie Bell in the face with a badly aimed throw, causing a nosebleed that Fred's joke-shop remedy — a "Blood Blisterpod," mistakenly implied — actually worsens rather than helps, since it's secretly a Skiving Snackbox prototype). Angelina ends practice early once Katie's bleeding becomes severe, sending her to the hospital wing escorted by Fred and George.
- Harry privately reassures Ron and defends him to Hermione afterward despite Ron's poor showing; Ron is embarrassed and short-tempered about it.
