## Chapter 14: Percy and Padfoot

**Plot events (in order):**
- Saturday morning: Harry wakes early, tries to write a carefully coded letter to Sirius (addressed to "Snuffles") about the recurrence of his scar pain during detention, Umbridge's new DADA role, and a veiled hint about missing Hagrid ("our biggest friend") — deliberately vague to protect against intercepted mail.
- On the way to the Owlery, Nearly Headless Nick warns him to avoid a corridor where Peeves is rigging a bust of Paracelsus to fall on the next passerby.
- Harry encounters Mrs. Norris (Filch's cat) acting suspiciously, as though reporting on him.
- At the Owlery, Harry glimpses — for a second time — a reptilian, winged, pterodactyl-like black horse-creature bursting out of the Forbidden Forest canopy and diving back in; he's increasingly unsettled.
- Cho Chang arrives to send a birthday gift to her mother; they have an easier, longer conversation than before (discussing Quidditch, Ron's new Keeper position — Cho calls Ron "the Tornado-hater," referencing his earlier rudeness about her fan badge). Cho praises Harry directly for standing up to Umbridge about Cedric's death ("You were really brave"), which visibly delights him.
- Filch bursts in accusing Harry of ordering Dungbombs via owl post (from an anonymous "tip-off"); Harry has already sent his letter so has nothing incriminating; Cho falsely but firmly backs Harry up, claiming she personally saw him send the letter, chasing Filch off. Harry and Cho part amicably, each pleased with the exchange.
- At breakfast, Ron asks Harry for early solo Quidditch practice before the full team session; Hermione disapproves given their homework backlog but they go anyway.
- The Daily Prophet (delivered to Hermione) reports a Ministry "tip-off" claiming Sirius Black is hiding in London — Harry suspects Lucius Malfoy is the source, having recognized Sirius's dog form at King's Cross; the article repeats standard Sirius manhunt rhetoric (dangerous, killed thirteen people — note: a slightly different figure than the "twelve Muggles and one wizard" cited in The Quibbler, though both equal thirteen total victims attributed to him from the Pettigrew frame-up).
- A tiny, easily overlooked Prophet item (headlined "Trespass at Ministry") reports that Sturgis Podmore (38, of Clapham) was caught breaking into a top-security Ministry door at 1 a.m. on 31 August by a Ministry watchwizard named Eric Munch, refused to defend himself at trial, and was sentenced to six months in Azkaban for trespass and attempted robbery. Harry recalls Podmore was the Order member who mysteriously failed to show up for the King's Cross guard duty — the trio speculates (half-seriously) that this was either a genuine unauthorized solo mission for the Order, or possibly a Ministry frame-up targeting known Dumbledore associates.
- Morning Quidditch practice goes reasonably well (Harry privately coaching Ron as Chaser-practice partner); the official afternoon team practice is badly undermined by Slytherin hecklers in the stands (Malfoy, Crabbe, Goyle, Pansy Parkinson) mocking Ron's broom, Angelina's hair, and Harry's scar/hospital history; Ron performs poorly under the pressure (drops a pass, nearly dives into the ground, eventually accidentally beans Katie Bell in the face with the Quaffle, giving her a bad nosebleed); Fred's attempt to help her with a sweet from his pocket (intended to be something else, revealed to be a "Blood Blisterpod" by mistake, implying it worsens things) backfires, forcing Angelina to end practice early and send Katie to the hospital wing with Fred and George.
- That evening, Ron receives a letter via Percy's owl Hermes — a long, sanctimonious letter from Percy (quoted in detail in the text; paraphrase here): Percy has heard via Fudge himself (who heard from Umbridge) that Ron is now a prefect; offers congratulations but pivots quickly into a lecture urging Ron to cut ties with Harry, since Percy believes Dumbledore's "regime" at Hogwarts is ending and that proximity to Harry will damage Ron's prospects (citing Harry's disciplinary hearing, which Percy claims Harry only escaped "on a technicality"); Percy suggests Ron report any concerning behavior from Harry directly to "a really delightful woman," Professor Umbridge; Percy hints at major Daily Prophet news coming "tomorrow" involving Umbridge's expanding role, implies a student who cozies up to Umbridge now could be well-placed for a future Head Boy appointment; Percy also criticizes their parents for associating with "the dangerous crowd around Dumbledore," cites Sturgis Podmore's Azkaban sentence as supposed proof of the danger of that crowd, and states he's waiting for an eventual "apology" from his parents once they realize he was right.
- Ron is furious, calls Percy "the world's biggest git," and burns the letter.
- Late that night, while Hermione (uncharacteristically) offers to proofread and fix Ron's and Harry's overdue essays (Transfiguration's Inanimatus Conjurus spell essay, Astronomy's Jupiter's moons essay) so they can finally finish their backlogged homework, Harry spots Sirius's head appearing in the common-room fire (as in Goblet of Fire's Triwizard-era fire-call) — Sirius has been checking the fireplace hourly, risking detection (he mentions a first-year girl may have glimpsed him briefly but assumes she took him for an oddly shaped log) specifically to respond to Harry's letter without using written code, since "codes are breakable."
- Sirius downplays the scar pain, noting it ached on and off all last year too, attributing it to Voldemort's emotional state (as Dumbledore explained after Goblet of Fire) rather than anything to do with Umbridge specifically; he doubts Umbridge is a Death Eater but confirms she's a "nasty piece of work" by reputation — Lupin has firsthand experience, since Umbridge drafted anti-werewolf legislation two years prior that makes it nearly impossible for werewolves to find employment, explaining Lupin's shabby state. Sirius adds Umbridge previously campaigned to have merpeople "rounded up and tagged," revealing a pattern of prejudice against "part-humans"/"half-breeds." Hermione is upset at Sirius's dismissive "little toerags like Kreacher" comment, defending the elf and urging Sirius to treat him better per Dumbledore's advice — Sirius deflects by changing the subject.
- Sirius explains, per Order intelligence, that Fudge is deliberately preventing students from learning practical defensive magic because he fears Dumbledore is secretly building a private student "army" to overthrow the Ministry — confirming the trio's suspicion that Umbridge's teaching style is politically motivated, not pedagogically. Sirius warns that Fudge is growing increasingly paranoid about Dumbledore and may eventually have him arrested on a "trumped-up charge."
- Harry mentions Percy's letter hinted at a major Daily Prophet story coming the next day about Umbridge; Sirius hasn't heard anything specific (no Order contact over the weekend, feeling isolated and bitter, alone in the house except for Kreacher).
- Sirius confirms Hagrid still hasn't returned and that "no one's sure what's happened to him," though Dumbledore isn't worried; reveals Hagrid was traveling with Madame Maxime (Beauxbatons headmistress, referenced) but they got separated on the journey home; cautions the trio not to draw attention by asking too many questions about Hagrid's absence.
- Sirius proposes risking another dog-disguise visit to Hogsmeade on the next Hogsmeade weekend; Harry and Hermione both vehemently refuse, citing their fear that Lucius Malfoy recognized him at King's Cross and the new "Sirius hiding in London" Prophet story. Sirius is visibly hurt/annoyed, remarking coldly that Harry is "less like your father than I thought" — James would have found the risk part of the fun — before making an excuse (Kreacher coming) and vanishing from the fire.

**Named characters:** Harry Potter, Ron Weasley, Hermione Granger, Cho Chang, Argus Filch, Mrs. Norris, Nearly Headless Nick, Percy Weasley (letter), Cornelius Fudge (referenced), Dolores Umbridge, Hermes (Percy's owl), Oliver Wood (referenced), Katie Bell, Angelina Johnson, Fred & George Weasley, Alicia Spinnet, Draco Malfoy, Crabbe, Goyle, Pansy Parkinson, Sirius Black (fire-call), Remus Lupin (referenced, affected by Umbridge's anti-werewolf legislation), Kreacher (referenced), Hagrid (still missing), Madame Maxime (referenced, Beauxbatons headmistress, was traveling with Hagrid), Sturgis Podmore (convicted, sentenced to Azkaban), Eric Munch (Ministry watchwizard who arrested Podmore), Lucius Malfoy (suspected Prophet source on Sirius), Dumbledore (referenced), Voldemort (referenced), James Potter (referenced by Sirius).

**Named places/objects/spells/creatures:**
- The Owlery; the Forbidden Forest (source of the winged horse sighting); Hogsmeade (mentioned, next weekend trip).
- Madame Malkin's Robes for All Occasions (referenced, Prophet advertisement).
- The Weird Sisters (wizarding band, referenced via gossip item about their bassist's marriage).
- Inanimatus Conjurus spell (Transfiguration essay topic); Jupiter's moons essay (Astronomy, Professor Sinistra) — correct facts given: Ganymede is Jupiter's biggest moon (not Callisto, as Ron wrote), and Io has the volcanoes (not Europa, which is ice-covered, as Harry wrote).
- Self-Fertilizing Shrubs (Herbology essay topic, Professor Sprout).
- Hermione's house-elf items: now knitting "elf socks" in addition to hats.
- "Blood Blisterpod" (joke-shop item/ingredient, implicated in Katie Bell's worsened nosebleed).

**Specific numbers/quantities:**
- Sturgis Podmore: age 38, of 2 Laburnum Gardens, Clapham; arrested 31 August at 1 a.m.; sentenced to six months in Azkaban.
- Sirius accused (per this Prophet article) of killing "thirteen" people — slightly varies in phrasing from the Quibbler's "twelve Muggles and one wizard" (same total, different emphasis).
- Percy's letter references Educational Decree Twenty-two (passed 30 August, allows the Ministry to appoint a teacher if Dumbledore can't find one) — foreshadowing the next chapter's Decree Twenty-three.
- Lupin's anti-werewolf legislation from Umbridge: drafted "two years ago."
- A hundred and fifty points: value of catching the Golden Snitch (restated).

**Small/obscure details:**
- Percy's letter is a long, fully quoted (in the original text) piece of dramatic irony/characterization — paraphrased above in detail per copyright guidance.
- Hermione's elf-hat campaign causes the Gryffindor common room to be increasingly littered with hidden woolly hats as the "robbing" by self-liberating elves apparently continues offscreen.
- Harry reflects that Percy once awarded him full marks in the Triwizard Tournament's second task (Goblet of Fire callback) — now Percy considers him "unbalanced" and "possibly violent."
- Educational Decree Twenty-two is explicitly dated to 30 August 1995 in-universe (per the chapter 15 Prophet article that follows), establishing a precise timeline for Ministry encroachment.

## Chapter 15: The Hogwarts High Inquisitor

**Plot events so far (in progress):**
- Sunday morning: the Daily Prophet headline announces Dolores Umbridge has been appointed Hogwarts's first-ever "High Inquisitor" under a new Ministry law (paraphrased article): Fudge, responding to "anxious parents' concerns" (per Percy Weasley, quoted as Junior Assistant to the Minister), has used Educational Decree Twenty-two (passed 30 August, allowing the Ministry to appoint a teacher when the headmaster can't find one — explaining Umbridge's original hiring) as a stepping stone to Educational Decree Twenty-three, formally creating the "Hogwarts High Inquisitor" position to give Umbridge (and by extension the Ministry) ongoing oversight/"on-the-ground feedback" about the school.
- Harry's reaction is immediate alarm/outrage, reading aloud with Hermione as the article continues (chapter cuts off mid-article).

**Named characters so far:** Harry Potter, Hermione Granger, Dolores Umbridge, Percy Weasley (quoted in the Prophet), Cornelius Fudge (referenced), Dumbledore (referenced).

**Named places/objects/spells:**
- Educational Decree Twenty-two (30 August) and Educational Decree Twenty-three (creating the High Inquisitor post) — both named explicitly.
- The Daily Prophet headline: "MINISTRY SEEKS EDUCATIONAL REFORM — DOLORES UMBRIDGE APPOINTED FIRST-EVER 'HIGH INQUISITOR.'"

**Chapter 15 continued — full plot events:**
- Article continues (paraphrased): Umbridge's new Inquisitorial powers let her formally inspect fellow teachers. Lucius Malfoy (age 41, quoted speaking from his Wiltshire mansion) publicly praises the move, citing "concerns" about Dumbledore's "eccentric" staffing decisions — the Prophet lists Remus Lupin (werewolf), Hagrid (half-giant), and the real Mad-Eye Moody ("delusional ex-Auror") as examples of such hires. The article notes rumors that Dumbledore (described with full former titles: Supreme Mugwump of the International Confederation of Wizards, Chief Warlock of the Wizengamot) may be unfit to continue as headmaster, with an anonymous "Ministry insider" framing the Inquisitor post as a first step toward replacing him.
- Two named Wizengamot elders, Griselda Marchbanks and Tiberius Ogden, resign in protest over the Inquisitor post; Marchbanks is quoted condemning it as an attempt to discredit Dumbledore — undercut by a snide editorial note referring readers to an unrelated page-17 smear piece about her "alleged links to subversive goblin groups."
- Hermione is incensed; Ron is darkly amused at the thought of McGonagall being "inspected." Umbridge doesn't appear at History of Magic or Potions that day. In Potions, Snape returns graded moonstone essays using strict O.W.L.-equivalent marks — Harry gets a D ("Dreadful"); Ron gets a P ("Poor"). The class discusses the O.W.L. grading scale in full: Outstanding (O), Exceeds Expectations (E), Acceptable (A), Poor (P), Dreadful (D), and Troll (T) — the lowest. Harry manages a passable Strengthening Solution this lesson (blue, not perfect turquoise like Hermione's, but better than Neville's pink).
- Fred and George report their first "inspected" class (Charms, with Flitwick) went smoothly — Umbridge mostly observed quietly and asked Alicia Spinnet a few questions.
- Divination inspection: Umbridge interrogates Trelawney condescendingly about her tenure (almost sixteen years), her lineage (great-great-granddaughter of the famous Seer Cassandra Trelawney), and pointedly notes she's the first in the family since Cassandra with "the Sight" (implying the gift skipped generations suspiciously conveniently). Umbridge demands Trelawney make an on-demand prediction; Trelawney, flustered, declares the Inner Eye doesn't work on command, then hastily invents a prediction that Umbridge herself is in "grave danger" — Umbridge is unmoved and unimpressed, noting the comment on her clipboard. Harry and Ron, despite finding Trelawney a fraud, feel sympathy for her against Umbridge — until she turns her sharp tongue on their faked dream-diary entries moments later, predicting gruesome deaths for all of them (even from mundane details like eating porridge).
- DADA class: Umbridge assigns Chapter 2 silently; Hermione reveals she's already read the entire textbook, correctly summarizes (and then explicitly disagrees with) Slinkhard's claim that "counterjinxes" are just relabeled jinxes, arguing jinxes can be legitimately useful defensively. Umbridge docks 5 points from Gryffindor for "disrupting" class with opinions, dismissing Hermione's view as irrelevant since only Slinkhard's (Ministry-approved) opinion matters. She further claims no previous DADA teacher except arguably Quirrell would have passed a Ministry inspection; Harry retorts sarcastically that Quirrell's only flaw was having "Lord Voldemort sticking out of the back of his head" (referencing Philosopher's/Sorcerer's Stone's twist) — stunned silence, then Umbridge assigns him another full week of detention.
- Second week of detentions continues (quill still cutting his hand); the next morning Angelina Johnson confronts Harry loudly about missing more practices, drawing Professor McGonagall over — McGonagall docks 5 points from Gryffindor for Angelina's shouting, then another 5 points from Harry upon learning he lost his temper with Umbridge again, scolding him sharply ("detentions do not appear to have any effect on you whatsoever") and warning Angelina to confine arguments to the Quidditch pitch or risk her captaincy.
- Harry is furious at the unfairness (being punished further for being punished); Ron sympathizes but Hermione is more measured, agreeing with McGonagall's underlying warning even while disliking the lost points — leads to a cold silence between Harry and Hermione through Charms.
- Transfiguration inspection: McGonagall pointedly ignores Umbridge's "Hem, hem" interruptions entirely, rebuffing her attempts to ask questions mid-lesson with icy precision, delighting the students; class is vanishing mice this lesson (harder than snails, since mammals are more complex than invertebrates); Harry gets an A on his returned moonstone-style essay (actually this is the Vanishing homework context — clarify: it's implied as a separate assignment). Umbridge interviews McGonagall afterward: confirms thirty-nine years of teaching at Hogwarts; tells her she'll receive inspection results in ten days.
- Care of Magical Creatures: Umbridge interrogates Professor Grubbly-Plank about Hagrid's prolonged absence (which "the headmaster seems strangely reluctant" to explain) — Grubbly-Plank genuinely doesn't know more than anyone else, just got an owl from Dumbledore asking her to substitute teach for a couple of weeks. Malfoy, eager to badmouth Hagrid to a Ministry official, boasts about being "slashed by a hippogriff" (Buckbeak, from Prisoner of Azkaban, though not named explicitly here) — implying Hagrid's incompetence; Harry snaps that it was Malfoy's own fault for disobeying instructions, earning him yet another detention. Grubbly-Plank confirms she plans to cover O.W.L.-relevant creatures: porlocks, kneazles, crups, and knarls (already covered unicorns and nifflers). Umbridge also appears generally impressed that Grubbly-Plank runs a competent, Ministry-friendly class.
- That night, Hermione gives Harry a soothing solution of strained and pickled murtlap tentacles to treat his bleeding hand; Ron again urges him to report Umbridge, but Harry refuses, reasoning that Umbridge could simply pass a new Decree criminalizing complaints against the High Inquisitor. Hermione then pivots to proposing they "do something" about Umbridge's inadequate teaching — specifically, that the group (eventually revealed to mean a wider group of students) should learn Defense Against the Dark Arts on their own, with Harry as the instructor, since the situation has gone beyond what books alone can fix and Lupin is too busy with Order work to help regularly.
- Harry reflexively resists, insisting his past survivals (the Stone, the Basilisk, the Dementors, the graveyard) were luck/help from others, not skill — but his temper flares when Ron and Hermione list his accomplishments admiringly; he furiously insists they don't understand what it's actually like to face mortal danger and feels they're trivializing Cedric Diggory's death. Hermione, visibly shaken, says — using Voldemort's name for the first time in the book herself — that this raw, lived experience is exactly why they need him to teach them. Harry, calmed by her use of the name, breaks the bowl of murtlap essence in his agitation, repairs it with "Reparo," and agrees to "think about it."

**Named characters (ch.15 additions):** Lucius Malfoy (quoted, age 41), Griselda Marchbanks and Tiberius Ogden (Wizengamot elders who resign in protest), Cassandra Trelawney (Sybill's famous ancestor, referenced), Sybill Trelawney, Fred & George Weasley, Lee Jordan, Alicia Spinnet, Professor Flitwick, Professor McGonagall, Dean Thomas, Lavender Brown, Draco Malfoy, Crabbe, Goyle, Professor Grubbly-Plank, Zacharias Smith (not yet named — appears next chapter), Crookshanks, Viktor Krum (referenced indirectly via Hermione's pen pal mention — appears more explicitly in ch16).

**Named places/objects/spells/creatures:**
- Educational Decree Twenty-three (creates the High Inquisitor post).
- O.W.L. grading scale, fully spelled out: Outstanding, Exceeds Expectations, Acceptable, Poor, Dreadful, Troll.
- Murtlap tentacles (strained/pickled, used medicinally to soothe Harry's wound) — introduces murtlap essence as a recurring remedy.
- Porlocks, kneazles, crups, knarls, unicorns, nifflers (creatures referenced as Care of Magical Creatures O.W.L. curriculum).
- Reparo (mending charm, used on the broken bowl).

**Specific numbers/quantities:**
- Trelawney has taught Divination at Hogwarts for "nearly sixteen years."
- McGonagall has taught at Hogwarts for "thirty-nine years this December."
- Inspection results to be delivered "within ten days" (stated multiple times, for Trelawney, McGonagall, and Grubbly-Plank's classes).
- Harry's and Ron's O.W.L.-equivalent essay grades: Harry gets a D, Ron gets a P.
- 5 points taken from Gryffindor (Hermione's DADA "disruption"); 5 points taken for Angelina's shouting in the Great Hall; 5 more points taken from Harry directly by McGonagall.
- A fortnight (two weeks) of Harry mulling over Hermione's teach-the-students proposal before discussing it again.

**Small/obscure details:**
- Lucius Malfoy's exact age (41) and location (a Wiltshire mansion) given.
- The Prophet snidely implies Marchbanks has sinister goblin ties in an unrelated page-17 piece — a glimpse of the paper's propaganda tactics.
- Fred jokes he and George "exceeded expectations" merely by showing up for their O.W.L.s.
- Hermione has already vanished a kitten in Transfiguration (harder than a mouse), ahead of the rest of the class.
- Hermione reveals she still corresponds with Viktor Krum (Durmstrang Triwizard champion, Goblet of Fire) as a pen pal, irritating Ron.

## Chapter 16: In the Hog's Head

**Plot events (in order):**
- Time skip to late September: Harry's detentions with Umbridge finally end (the scar on his hand from "I must not tell lies" never fully fades); Ron has had several more Quidditch practices without incident; all three have advanced to vanishing mice (Hermione's ahead of the curve, vanishing kittens).
- In the library, Hermione revives the Defense-teaching proposal; reveals she and Ron have already been quietly recruiting interested students. Harry admits he's been subconsciously thinking through lesson plans despite his reservations.
- Hermione cites Harry's unique qualifications: he's the only person who fully threw off the Imperius Curse (Goblet of Fire, Barty Crouch Jr.'s class demonstration), can produce a corporeal Patronus, and per Viktor Krum (confirmed to still be Hermione's pen pal, to Ron's visible jealousy), could do things even a final-year Durmstrang student couldn't.
- Hermione insists the group should be open to anyone interested, not just Ron and herself, "because we're talking about defending ourselves against Voldemort" — proposes using the upcoming first Hogsmeade weekend (early October) to gather interested students off-campus, away from Umbridge's notice, at the Hog's Head rather than the busier/noisier Three Broomsticks.
- Harry worries privately that Sirius (still sulking/silent since his dog-visit offer was refused) might recklessly show up in Hogsmeade in his dog disguise; Ron and Hermione reassure him Sirius wouldn't risk Dumbledore's anger, and that Sirius must stay hidden until Death Eaters are publicly caught again (proving Sirius lacks the Dark Mark and isn't one of them).
- Morning of the Hogsmeade trip: Filch checks names against a parental-permission list at the entrance (sniffing Harry for contraband, presumably Dungbombs, per the earlier false tip-off); the trio discusses who might have tipped off Filch about Dungbombs — Harry suspects Malfoy; Hermione is skeptical but thoughtful.
- They walk into Hogsmeade, pass Zonko's Joke Shop (where Fred, George, and Lee Jordan are shopping) and the post office, and arrive at the Hog's Head — a shabby, dirty, goat-smelling pub Harry recalls Hagrid mentioning in his first year (site of the dragon-egg wager with a hooded stranger, later revealed in this series to have been Quirrell/Voldemort). Patrons include a bandage-headed man drinking through a mouth-slit, two hooded figures with Yorkshire accents, and a thickly veiled witch Harry briefly suspects might be Umbridge in disguise (Hermione judges her too tall to be Umbridge). The grumpy, long-haired, bearded barman looks vaguely familiar to Harry (later revealed to be Aberforth Dumbledore, Albus's brother — not identified by name in this chapter, but primed by the earlier Order-photo mention in Chapter 9).
- Hermione confirms via Professor Flitwick that students are permitted in the Hog's Head (advised to bring their own glasses) and that study/homework groups are explicitly allowed under school rules — her legal groundwork for the meeting.
- A much larger crowd than expected arrives to meet them: named attendees include Neville Longbottom, Dean Thomas, Lavender Brown, Parvati and Padma Patil, Cho Chang and a mistrustful reddish-blonde-haired friend (Marietta Edgecombe, not yet named), Luna Lovegood, Katie Bell, Alicia Spinnet, Angelina Johnson, Colin and Dennis Creevey, Ernie Macmillan, Justin Finch-Fletchley, Hannah Abbott, an unnamed Hufflepuff girl with a long plait, Ravenclaws Anthony Goldstein, Michael Corner, and Terry Boot, Ginny Weasley, an unnamed tall blond Hufflepuff Quidditch player, and Fred, George, and Lee Jordan (carrying Zonko's shopping bags). Fred orders 25 butterbeers for the group.
- Hermione opens the meeting nervously, explaining the goal: real practical Defense instruction (not Umbridge's theory-only approach) with Harry as instructor, explicitly stating the reason is that "Lord Voldemort's back" — causing a ripple of flinches/shock (Cho's friend shrieks and spills her drink; Terry Boot twitches; Padma shudders; Neville yelps).
- Pushback begins: Zacharias Smith (named here, a Hufflepuff, described as blond with an aggressive tone) publicly demands proof that Voldemort is really back, pointedly implying Dumbledore merely "believes him" (Harry) rather than having independent evidence — Ron responds rudely; Hermione tries to redirect the meeting before it derails into a credibility debate (scene cuts off here).

**Named characters so far:** Harry Potter, Ron Weasley, Hermione Granger, Viktor Krum (referenced, Hermione's pen pal), Sirius Black (referenced, worry over his silence/potential recklessness), Filch, Fred & George Weasley, Lee Jordan, the Hog's Head barman (unnamed, later revealed as Aberforth Dumbledore), Neville Longbottom, Dean Thomas, Lavender Brown, Parvati Patil, Padma Patil, Cho Chang, Cho's unnamed mistrustful friend (Marietta Edgecombe), Luna Lovegood, Katie Bell, Alicia Spinnet, Angelina Johnson, Colin Creevey, Dennis Creevey, Ernie Macmillan, Justin Finch-Fletchley, Hannah Abbott, Anthony Goldstein, Michael Corner, Terry Boot, Ginny Weasley, Zacharias Smith (Hufflepuff, demands proof of Voldemort's return), Draco Malfoy (referenced, suspected Filch informant), Hagrid (referenced, Hog's Head dragon-egg story from book 1), Quirrell (referenced indirectly via the hooded-stranger story), Professor Flitwick (confirms Hog's Head/study-group rules).

**Named places/objects/spells/creatures:**
- The Hog's Head Inn, Hogsmeade (sign depicts a severed boar's head); contrasted with The Three Broomsticks.
- Zonko's Joke Shop, the Hogsmeade post office.
- Butterbeer (25 ordered for the group); Firewhisky (Ron expresses curiosity, scolded by Hermione for being a prefect).
- Murtlap essence (callback, soothing remedy).

**Specific numbers/quantities:**
- Fred orders exactly 25 butterbeers for the assembled group.
- Six Sickles charged for the trio's first three butterbeers.
- The meeting takes place on the first Hogsmeade weekend of the year, early October.

**Small/obscure details:**
- The Hog's Head's clientele (bandaged man, hooded "dementor-like" Yorkshire-accented men, veiled witch) is a classic atmospheric/trivia-rich detail.
- The barman's unnamed familiarity to Harry is a subtle setup for his identity reveal later in the book/series (Aberforth Dumbledore).
- Hermione's meticulous rules-lawyering (confirming the legality of the meeting via Flitwick) foreshadows her strategic mind in forming the group that will become Dumbledore's Army.

**Chapter 16 continued — full plot events:**
- Harry refuses to elaborate on Cedric's death when pressed by Zacharias Smith, stating flatly that Dumbledore already told the school the facts last year and he won't rehash it for entertainment; the group stays despite this rebuff.
- Susan Bones (niece of Amelia Bones, confirmed) asks if Harry can really cast a corporeal Patronus (a stag); Lee Jordan is amazed he never knew; Fred reveals Mrs. Weasley told Ron not to spread it around since Harry "got enough attention as it was."
- Terry Boot asks if Harry really killed a basilisk with a sword in Dumbledore's office (per a portrait's gossip) — confirmed. Neville mentions Harry saved the Sorcerer's/Philosopher's Stone in first year (Hermione corrects "Sorcerer's" vs. Neville's "Sorcerous"). Cho praises his Triwizard Tournament feats (dragons, merpeople, acromantulas). The group's growing admiration embarrasses Harry, who tries to insist most of his survival was luck/help from others — Michael Corner and Susan Bones push back, noting no one helped him with the first dragon task or the dementors over the summer.
- Zacharias Smith needles Harry about "weaseling out" of demonstrating anything; Ron snaps at him ("why don't you shut your mouth") — Fred and George menace Smith with a sinister ear-cleaning instrument from Zonko's, prompting Hermione to redirect the meeting.
- Group agrees (Zacharias abstaining/silent) to be taught by Harry. Scheduling debate: once a week proposed; must avoid clashing with Quidditch practices (Angelina, Cho, and Zacharias all have teams) and other commitments. Ernie Macmillan delivers a pompous speech about the importance of defense training given Umbridge's uselessness and the Ministry's denial.
- Hermione explains the group's working theory for why Umbridge blocks practical defense training: she believes Dumbledore might be building a private student "army" to use against the Ministry. Luna Lovegood unexpectedly "confirms" this logic by claiming Cornelius Fudge has his own private army of "heliopaths" (fire spirits) — Hermione flatly denies these exist; Luna insists they do, based on "eyewitness accounts." Ginny defuses the tangent with a spot-on Umbridge impression ("Hem, hem").
- Location debate: library (ruled out, Madam Pince won't allow jinxing), an unused classroom suggested; Ron suggests asking McGonagall for her room (as during Harry's Triwizard prep) — Harry doubts she'd risk it with this particular group's purpose. Hermione says they'll notify everyone once a time/place is set.
- Hermione has everyone sign a parchment list, explicitly as a pledge not to reveal the group's existence to Umbridge or anyone else — important for later: she has secretly jinxed this list (revealed in ch17) as insurance against informants. Several attendees hesitate (Zacharias refuses outright, deferring to Ernie; Ernie himself wavers, citing his prefect status and the risk of the list being found, but signs after Hermione reassures him); Zacharias signs last.
- Group disperses; Cho is shepherded out by her disapproving friend (not yet named — later revealed as Marietta Edgecombe) despite looking back to wave at Harry.
- Walking back, Hermione reveals Ginny is dating Ravenclaw Michael Corner (they got together at the end of last year, having met at the Yule Ball in Goblet of Fire) — shocking/outraging Ron (protective-brother reaction). Hermione also reveals, gently, that Ginny "used to fancy" Harry but gave up on him "months ago" — which is why Ginny now talks normally around Harry (previously too shy). They stop at Scrivenshaft's Quill Shop; Hermione buys a new quill (15 Sickles, 2 Knuts) while Ron grumbles about Michael Corner. Hermione then teases Harry about Cho's visible attentiveness to him throughout the meeting.

**Named characters (ch.16 additions):** Susan Bones (Amelia Bones's niece, confirmed), Terry Boot, Justin Finch-Fletchley, Hannah Abbott, Michael Corner (Ginny's boyfriend, Ravenclaw), Lavender Brown, Ernie Macmillan, Luna Lovegood, Ginny Weasley, Zacharias Smith, Fred & George Weasley, Lee Jordan, Cho Chang, Madam Pince (referenced, librarian), Professor McGonagall (referenced).

**Named places/objects/spells/creatures:**
- Scrivenshaft's Quill Shop, Hogsmeade (pheasant-feather quills).
- Heliopaths (Luna's claimed fire-spirit creatures, fictional/unconfirmed — presented as another of her "Quibbler" beliefs).
- The secretly-jinxed sign-up parchment (established here, consequences shown in ch.17).

**Specific numbers/quantities:**
- Hermione pays 15 Sickles and 2 Knuts for a new quill.
- Twenty-eight total members are later confirmed to have signed up (revealed explicitly in Chapter 17 via Hermione's count during the Sirius conversation).

**Small/obscure details:**
- Terry Boot's claim about overhearing basilisk gossip "from a portrait" in Dumbledore's office is a nice worldbuilding detail (paintings as gossip conduits).
- Cho's friend's cold, mistrustful demeanor throughout foreshadows her later role as an informant (Marietta Edgecombe, named in a later chapter).
- Ron's visceral reaction to "weasel" as an insult (given his surname) is a small character beat.

## Chapter 17: Educational Decree Number Twenty-Four

**Plot events (in order):**
- Monday morning: Harry is still elated from the Hog's Head meeting. He and Ron discover a new large official notice has been posted on the Gryffindor board, covering over all previous postings (secondhand spellbook ads, Filch's rules, Quidditch schedule, Weasleys' tester ad, Hogsmeade dates, lost-and-found). The notice (quoted in full in the original text; paraphrased here): issued "By Order of the High Inquisitor of Hogwarts," it disbands all student organizations, societies, teams, groups, and clubs (defined as any regular gathering of three or more students) with immediate effect; such groups may only re-form with Umbridge's explicit personal approval; any student found participating in an unapproved group will be expelled. This is designated "Educational Decree Number Twenty-four."
- Harry immediately suspects betrayal from within their new group, given the size of the Hog's Head crowd and uncertainty over who could be trusted (floats Zacharias Smith and Michael Corner as suspects).
- Ron tries to reach Hermione in the girls' dormitory to show her; a magical alarm (described as a "wailing, klaxonlike sound") triggers, turning the staircase into a slide that dumps him back down at Harry's feet — revealed (by Hermione, who appears having used the slide normally) to be a very old protective charm from the school's founding, noted in Hogwarts: A History, reflecting the founders' belief that boys were less trustworthy than girls (hence boys can't climb to the girls' dorms, but girls can access the boys').
- Hermione reassures Ron that no one could have betrayed them, since she secretly jinxed the sign-up parchment: anyone who later reveals the group's existence to an outsider will suffer visible consequences worse than classmate Eloise Midgen's famous acne (implying some kind of hex/curse tied to loose lips) — she doesn't specify the exact effect yet.
- The decree notice turns out to have been posted in every House common room. At breakfast, Neville, Dean, Fred, George, and Ginny converge on the trio anxiously; Harry quietly confirms "we're going to do it anyway." Fred confirms prefects (Ron and Hermione) are still in; Ernie and Hannah Abbott, plus Ravenclaw boys and Zacharias Smith approach but are shooed back to their own tables by Hermione to avoid suspicion; Ginny goes to warn Michael Corner's group.
- Angelina Johnson intercepts Harry and Ron in a panic: the new decree explicitly covers sports teams too, meaning even the Gryffindor Quidditch team must seek Umbridge's re-approval to continue existing; she begs Harry (again) not to lose his temper with Umbridge, fearing he'll jeopardize the whole team's survival.
- In History of Magic (Binns, droning on about giant wars as always), Hedwig arrives at the classroom window mid-lesson (highly unusual — she should have delivered mail at breakfast) with visibly ruffled, damaged feathers and an oddly-angled wing; she's clearly been attacked/injured rather than just tired. Harry fakes feeling ill to leave class (Binns, oblivious as ever, barely registers this) and carries Hedwig in search of help.
- Unable to find Hagrid (still missing) or locate Grubbly-Plank near Hagrid's cabin, Harry finds her in the staffroom (guarded by two snarky talking gargoyle statues) with McGonagall. Grubbly-Plank examines Hedwig, suspects something attacked her — speculates thestrals (introduced by name here for the first time in the book, Hagrid's creatures, normally trained not to go for owls) might be responsible, though she can't say for certain. She agrees to treat Hedwig, keeping her grounded for a few days.
- McGonagall pointedly asks how far Hedwig had traveled; Harry says "from London" — the unspoken implication (Grimmauld Place) passes between them silently. McGonagall retrieves the attached letter for Harry and, crucially, warns him quietly that "channels of communication in and out of Hogwarts may be being watched" — a clear, serious hint that the school's mail (and likely Floo) is under surveillance.
- The letter, from Sirius (signed implicitly, in his hand), reads simply: "Today, same time, same place" — understood by the trio to mean the common-room fire, at the same hour as his first visit. Hermione is immediately anxious about security, noting Hedwig's unprecedented injury suggests someone tried to intercept the letter en route, and that even a "sealed" scroll could be magically resealed after tampering.
- Outside Snape's dungeon, Draco Malfoy loudly brags that Umbridge pre-approved the Slytherin Quidditch team's continuation instantly (due to his father's Ministry connections), speculating Gryffindor won't be so lucky; he needles further, claiming Lucius says the Ministry has long wanted an excuse to sack Arthur Weasley, and that Harry himself is likely to be committed to St. Mungo's Hospital's special ward for magically-addled brains "any day now," mimicking a deranged expression for laughs (Crabbe, Goyle, Pansy Parkinson laugh along).
- Neville, provoked specifically by the St. Mungo's/brain-damage jab (not the Weasley or Potter insults), charges at Malfoy in a sudden rage; Harry and Ron physically restrain him before a fight breaks out with Crabbe and Goyle. Snape arrives, docks 10 points from Gryffindor for the scuffle, threatens detention, and orders everyone inside. Harry reflects that he understands exactly why this particular insult hit Neville so hard (a secret involving his parents that Harry is sworn to Dumbledore not to reveal — tying back to Frank and Alice Longbottom's fate as shown in Moody's old Order photograph in Chapter 9, tortured into insanity and institutionalized at St. Mungo's, though this is only hinted at, not stated outright, since Harry doesn't even know if Neville knows that Harry knows).
- Potions class: Umbridge observes and interrogates Snape directly in front of the class — confirms he's taught at Hogwarts fourteen years, that he originally applied for the Defense Against the Dark Arts post and was turned down, that he has reapplied "regularly" every year since, and pointedly asks if he knows why Dumbledore has "consistently refused" to give him the position — Snape curtly tells her to ask Dumbledore himself; Umbridge says she will. She also suggests the Ministry might want the Strengthening Solution potion removed from the O.W.L. syllabus as too advanced. Harry, distracted watching this exchange, ruins his own potion yet again (adds the wrong ingredient, it turns to a foul orange/burned-rubber-smelling mess); Snape vanishes it and assigns a punitive essay on correct potion composition, due next lesson.
- Harry considers skipping Divination to do the Potions essay instead; Hermione talks him out of it, not wanting him to miss more class after already skipping History of Magic that morning (for Hedwig).
- In Divination, Professor Trelawney is clearly distraught and short-tempered, revealed to have received the results of her own Umbridge inspection; she rants emotionally about being "insulted" after "sixteen years' devoted service," framing herself as a persecuted Seer misunderstood by the "Mundane" world, refuses to discuss specifics, and spends the lesson muttering ominous half-threats ("may well choose to leave... on probation... we shall see... how she dares..."), confirming to Harry/Ron that she's effectively been placed on probation by Umbridge.
- In the following DADA lesson, nobody bothers taking out wands anymore (a telling, grim bit of routine); Umbridge assigns Chapter 3 of Defensive Magical Theory ("The Case for Non-Offensive Responses to Magical Attack"), and the trio mutters her "no need to talk" line in unison, mocking her predictability.
- That evening: Angelina reports Umbridge has not yet decided whether to reinstate the Gryffindor Quidditch team (unlike Slytherin's instant approval), stringing them along deliberately. Fred and George publicly demonstrate a now-perfected Skiving Snackbox (the Puking Pastilles) to a cheering crowd in the common room — repeatedly vomiting into a bucket via the orange half, then curing instantly via the purple half, with Lee Jordan vanishing the mess between demonstrations; they rake in roughly 26 Galleons from eager buyers. Hermione dismisses their cleverness as "flashy" and "no real use," irritating Ron, who points out the profits speak for themselves.
- Late that night, once the common room empties, Sirius's head reappears in the fire as promised. He reveals he already knows about both new developments — the Quidditch ban and the secret Defense group — because Mundungus Fletcher (disguised as the heavily veiled witch at the Hog's Head) was there eavesdropping the whole time, acting as Harry's covert watcher once again. Mundungus was banned from the Hog's Head 20 years ago (the barman has a long memory) and has taken to dressing as a witch since Moody's spare Invisibility Cloak was lost when Sturgis Podmore was arrested.
- Sirius delivers a message from Mrs. Weasley (passed via him since a letter risked interception and she's "on duty" that night): she vehemently opposes the secret Defense group for Ron (certain expulsion, ruined future, "too young to worry about it"), and separately urges Harry and Hermione not to proceed either, while acknowledging she has no actual authority over them.
- Sirius, by contrast, enthusiastically endorses the plan — reveals that he and James would never have meekly obeyed a figure like Umbridge either, and argues that since there's now a confirmed external threat (Voldemort/Death Eaters), learning real defensive magic is clearly wise regardless of expulsion risk ("better expelled and able to defend yourselves than sitting safely in school without a clue"). This contrasts with his earlier (book 4-referenced) caution, justified by the shift from an internal Hogwarts threat (Barty Crouch Jr.) to an external one.
- They discuss meeting locations: Sirius suggests the Shrieking Shack (where the Marauders used to meet) or a secret passage behind a fourth-floor mirror — Hermione notes the Marauders were only four Animagi/Invisibility-Cloak users, while their group now numbers 28 members, far too many to hide the same way; the fourth-floor passage is confirmed (by Fred and George, per Harry) to have caved in and become impassable.
- Sirius abruptly cuts off mid-sentence, alarmed, and vanishes from the fire; a stubby, be-ringed hand (unmistakably Umbridge's) reaches groping into the flames a moment later, having nearly caught him. The trio flees to the dormitory stairs in shock.

**Named characters:** Harry Potter, Ron Weasley, Hermione Granger, Dolores Umbridge, Argus Filch (referenced via the decree enforcement), Neville Longbottom, Draco Malfoy, Crabbe, Goyle, Pansy Parkinson, Severus Snape, Professor McGonagall, Professor Grubbly-Plank (named "Wilhelmina" by McGonagall), Professor Binns, Hedwig, Sirius Black (fire-call), Mundungus Fletcher (revealed as the veiled "witch" at the Hog's Head), Molly Weasley (message relayed via Sirius), Arthur Weasley (referenced, rumored Ministry target), James Potter (referenced by Sirius), Angelina Johnson, Fred & George Weasley, Lee Jordan, Eloise Midgen (referenced, famous for bad acne), Frank and Alice Longbottom (implied, not named, re: Neville's secret), Lavender Brown, Parvati Patil, Seamus Finnigan, Dean Thomas, Crookshanks, Sturgis Podmore (referenced).

**Named places/objects/spells/creatures:**
- Hogwarts: A History (book, referenced for the girls'-dormitory protective charm/slide trap).
- Educational Decree Number Twenty-four (the full text of the club-ban notice, paraphrased above).
- Thestrals (named explicitly for the first time — Hagrid's creatures, normally trained not to attack owls).
- St. Mungo's Hospital for Magical Maladies and Injuries (referenced again, re: Harry's rumored commitment and — implicitly — Neville's parents).
- The Shrieking Shack (suggested meeting place, Marauders' old haunt).
- The fourth-floor secret passage behind a mirror (suggested, confirmed caved-in).
- Skiving Snackboxes: Puking Pastilles (fully perfected and sold this chapter).
- Scrivenshaft's (callback). Salamander blood, pomegranate juice, powdered griffin claw (potion ingredients named in the Strengthening Solution lesson).
- Accio (Summoning Charm, used on Harry's escaping bullfrog — actually chapter 18, included here for proximity) — note, see ch18 section below.

**Specific numbers/quantities:**
- Twenty-eight total members in the secret Defense group (Hermione's count, mentioned explicitly in dialogue with Sirius).
- Snape has taught at Hogwarts for fourteen years and has applied for the DADA post every year since, always rejected.
- Mundungus was banned from the Hog's Head twenty years ago.
- Fred, George, and Lee collect roughly 26 Galleons from Puking Pastille sales in one night.
- 10 points taken from Gryffindor by Snape for the Malfoy/Neville near-fight.

**Small/obscure details:**
- The gargoyle statues guarding the staffroom door have snarky, talking personalities — a fun, easily-missed worldbuilding detail.
- Grubbly-Plank is given a first name, "Wilhelmina," by McGonagall here.
- Harry's internal realization about why the St. Mungo's jibe devastated Neville specifically (tied to his parents) is a key piece of foreshadowing for a much later chapter (Christmas at St. Mungo's).
- The decree's precise legal definition of a "club" (three or more students meeting regularly) is a notably exact, quotable detail.

## Chapter 18: Dumbledore's Army

**Plot events so far (in progress):**
- Hermione is now certain Umbridge has been intercepting/reading Harry's mail, reasoning backward from the earlier Dungbomb accusation: it was always a flimsy pretext (the letter's innocence would have been obvious once read) unless its real purpose was simply to create an excuse for Filch to seize and read Harry's post on Umbridge's behalf. She deduces that Hedwig's injury means someone (presumably Umbridge, or an agent) physically intercepted her in transit for the "same time, same place" note, confirming just how close a call the near-catastrophe with Sirius in the fire actually was.
- This conversation happens during Charms class (practicing Summoning and Silencing Charms on bullfrogs and ravens), chosen deliberately for its noisy cover. Harry Summons his escaping bullfrog back with "Accio"; Hermione practices a Silencing Charm ("Silencio") on hers mid-conversation.

**Named characters so far:** Harry Potter, Hermione Granger, Ron Weasley (implied present), Dolores Umbridge (discussed), Argus Filch (discussed), Sirius Black (discussed, referred to by his alias "Snuffles").

**Named places/objects/spells:**
- Charms classroom; Accio (Summoning Charm); Silencio (Silencing Charm).

**Chapter 18 continued — full plot events:**
- Hermione worries the group can't warn Sirius not to risk the fire-call again since owls aren't safe; Ron thinks Sirius won't risk it twice, knowing how close Umbridge came. They continue practicing Silencing Charms on frogs/ravens in Charms (Flitwick praises Hermione's technique; Ron accidentally pokes his frog in the eye instead).
- During break, Angelina announces Umbridge has approved reinstating the Gryffindor Quidditch team (likely after McGonagall appealed to Dumbledore) — practice is set for 7 p.m. that night, with the first match only three weeks away.
- Hermione unexpectedly voices doubt about continuing the secret Defense group — not because of risk, but because Sirius's enthusiastic endorsement troubles her: she suspects Sirius, bored and frustrated by his confinement at Grimmauld Place, is "living through" them, egging them on recklessly because he can't act himself. Ron dismisses this as sounding just like his mother; Harry defends Sirius's judgment instinctively.
- Evening Quidditch practice is washed out by torrential rain and wind; Fred and George debate testing untested Skiving Snackbox prototypes (a "Fever Fudge" that raises temperature but causes undisclosed, unremovable boils in private areas, uncomfortable for broom-sitting) to skip practice, but don't in the end. Angelina has the team practice flying in storm conditions (relevant since their opening match vs. Slytherin may be in similar weather); Harry teaches everyone the Impervius Charm (originally Hermione's idea) to keep rain off their glasses/faces. Practice is grueling and largely pointless due to poor visibility; it's called off after nearly an hour.
- In the changing room afterward, Harry's scar sears painfully — the worst in months — while Fred and George grouse about self-inflicted "ruptured" boils from (implicitly) having tried the Fever Fudge earlier despite the stated plan not to. Harry conceals the real cause from teammates, only confiding in Ron: he intuits (new development) that he's sensing Voldemort's emotional state directly — this time, fury, because "he wants something done and it's not happening fast enough." Harry explains that the previous scar pain (during Umbridge's detention touch) was actually Voldemort feeling intense pleasure/anticipation, which Harry misread (at the time) as a feeling in his own stomach; he connects this to Dumbledore's past explanation (from Goblet of Fire) that the scar reacts to Voldemort's proximity or strong emotion. Ron is both awed and frightened, suggesting Harry is essentially "reading You-Know-Who's mind" (Harry corrects: more like sensing his mood, not his thoughts). Ron urges Harry to tell someone (Dumbledore); Harry refuses, noting Dumbledore already knows about the scar-pain phenomenon in general and that communications are being watched anyway (so he can't easily update Sirius either).
- Walking back, Harry privately recalls Sirius's earlier remark (Chapter 5) about Voldemort seeking "a weapon... something he didn't have last time... stuff he can only get by stealth" — wondering if Voldemort's anger stems from being thwarted in acquiring it, and who might currently hold it.
- That night, while pretending to work on his Potions essay (via a textbook passage about scurvy-grass, lovage, and sneezewort causing "hot-headedness and recklessness" in potions — which Harry's drowsy mind garbles together with Hermione's "Sirius is being reckless" theory), Harry falls asleep in his armchair and dreams again of the windowless corridor and the locked door, reaching for the handle just as he's woken by Dobby the house-elf.
- Dobby has personally returned a now-healed Hedwig (treated successfully by Professor Grubbly-Plank), wearing an absurd towering stack of all of Hermione's knitted elf hats (plus numerous scarves and socks) as a show of appreciation/thrift — revealing he distributes the clothes to Winky too, though she remains inconsolable, still drinking heavily, and still refuses to wear clothes; the other Hogwarts house-elves are now refusing to clean Gryffindor Tower at all out of sheer insult at the hidden hats/socks everywhere, leaving Dobby to do all the cleaning himself without complaint, delighted simply to have randomly gotten the chance to see Harry.
- Dobby, devoted to Harry for freeing him (Chamber of Secrets), offers to help with anything; Harry, prompted by the sight of his own scarred hand in the firelight, asks Dobby to find a secret, undetectable place where 28 people can practice Defense without being discovered by any teacher, especially Umbridge.
- Dobby enthusiastically reveals the "Room of Requirement" (also called the "Come and Go Room" by house-elves): a magical room that only appears to those with genuine need of it, always equipped for the seeker's specific purpose; Dobby has personally used it to hide and sober up a very drunk Winky (complete with an elf-sized bed and hangover cures), and knows Filch has found extra cleaning supplies there. Confirms (per a half-remembered comment from Dumbledore at the Yule Ball, Goblet of Fire) that it would even produce chamber pots if someone genuinely needed a bathroom. Very few people know of it; most who stumble upon it never find it again since they don't realize it's always there, waiting.
- Harry, exhausted, declines Dobby's offer to show him immediately (despite temptation, invoking an internal "Hermione voice" warning against recklessness) but gets full directions for finding it himself later.

**Named characters (ch.18 additions):** Harry Potter, Ron Weasley, Hermione Granger, Angelina Johnson, Fred & George Weasley, Dobby, Winky (referenced, still grieving/drinking, Goblet of Fire holdover), Hedwig, Professor Grubbly-Plank (healed Hedwig), Professor Flitwick, Katie Bell (referenced, hit by Peeves's ink pellet), Crookshanks, Dumbledore (referenced, mentioned the Room of Requirement to Harry "in passing" at the Yule Ball), Sirius Black (discussed, not present), Voldemort (sensed emotionally by Harry).

**Named places/objects/spells/creatures:**
- The Room of Requirement / "Come and Go Room" (fully introduced and explained by Dobby).
- Impervius Charm (water-repelling charm for glasses/faces, Hermione's idea, taught to the whole Quidditch team).
- Fever Fudge (an unfinished, flawed Skiving Snackbox prototype causing boils).
- Scurvy-grass, lovage, sneezewort (potion ingredients from Harry's textbook, used for "Confusing and Befuddlement Draughts").
- Sloth Grip Roll (Quidditch maneuver, used by Harry to dodge a Bludger).
- The Marauder's Map (referenced implicitly as what Harry later uses — not yet explicit here, but set up for next scene).

**Specific numbers/quantities:**
- Twenty-eight people are confirmed as the number needing a practice space (Harry's explicit request to Dobby).
- The Gryffindor Quidditch team's first match is three weeks away at this point.
- Practice lasts "nearly an hour" before being called off due to weather.

**Small/obscure details:**
- Fred and George's Fever Fudge side-effect (boils in an undisclosed private location) is a recurring comedic beat about their risky self-testing.
- Dobby's elongated-head hat-stack visual (wearing every hat Hermione ever knitted, one atop another) is a vivid, specific, quotable image.
- The chamber-pot detail about the Room of Requirement is a direct callback to something Dumbledore said at the Yule Ball in Goblet of Fire.

### Chapter 18 (continued) — The D.A.'s First Meeting

- Next day: rain continues; Care of Magical Creatures is moved indoors; Quidditch practice is cancelled due to weather, which pleases Harry since the group's first Defense meeting is scheduled.
- Harry announces to Angelina the meeting location (seventh floor, opposite the tapestry of "Barnabas the Barmy" being clubbed by trolls while trying to teach them ballet) for 8 p.m. that night; asks her to pass the word to Katie and Alicia.
- Hermione briefly worries about trusting a "mad idea of Dobby's" (recalling a past incident — referenced but not detailed here — where Dobby's meddling cost Harry all the bones in his arm, a Chamber of Secrets callback), but is reassured once told Dumbledore himself is aware of the Room of Requirement's existence.
- Harry, Ron, and Hermione spend the day notifying all confirmed Hog's Head signatories (25 of the original list show up/are confirmed, per the text) of the meeting details; Ginny is the one who successfully informs Cho and her friend.
- At 8 p.m., the trio uses the Marauder's Map (first explicit reference by name in this chapter to "a certain piece of aged parchment," activated with "I solemnly swear that I am up to no good") to confirm Filch's and Mrs. Norris's locations (2nd and 4th floors) and Umbridge's (in her office) before heading to the seventh floor.
- They find the correct stretch of wall opposite the Barnabas the Barmy tapestry, walk past it three times while concentrating on their need ("somewhere to learn to fight... somewhere they can't find us"), and the Room of Requirement manifests as a polished door.
- Inside: a spacious torch-lit room (echoing the dungeons eight floors below) with silk cushions instead of chairs, bookshelves lining the walls, and a shelf of Dark Detectors — Sneakoscopes, Secrecy Sensors, and a large cracked Foe-Glass Harry recognizes from the fake Moody's office the previous year (Goblet of Fire). Hermione is delighted by an extensive library of practical defense books, including titles like A Compendium of Common Curses and Their Counter-Actions, The Dark Arts Outsmarted, and Self-Defensive Spellwork; she immediately begins reading Jinxes for the Jinxed.
- Members arrive gradually (listed again by name across the scene): Ginny, Neville, Lavender, Parvati, Dean first; eventually all 25+ fill every cushion by 8 p.m. Fred notes the room was once just a broom cupboard when he and George hid from Filch in it before — confirming the room's need-based, shape-shifting nature.
- Harry locks the door; explains the space; Hermione proposes (and the group votes unanimously, including a reluctant Zacharias Smith) to formally elect Harry as leader.
- Hermione proposes the group needs a name for unity/safety (so they can refer to it outside meetings without revealing its purpose); joke suggestions include "Anti-Umbridge League" (Angelina) and "Ministry of Magic Are Morons Group" (Fred). Cho suggests "Defense Association" (D.A. for short); Ginny proposes the D.A. should secretly stand for "Dumbledore's Army" instead, specifically because that is the Ministry's worst fear — the group votes this in by majority, and Hermione pins the signed member list to the wall, titling it "DUMBLEDORE'S ARMY" in large letters.
- First lesson: Harry teaches the Disarming Charm, Expelliarmus, over Zacharias Smith's scornful objection that it's too basic to be useful against Voldemort — Harry reveals quietly that he used Expelliarmus against Voldemort himself, and it saved his life "last June" (the graveyard duel, Goblet of Fire) — silencing Smith.
- Pairs practice: Neville (partnered with Harry, since left without a partner) successfully disarms Harry on his first-ever attempt, thrilled; general spellwork is sloppy across the room. Fred and George are caught secretly jinxing Zacharias Smith from behind to make it look like Anthony Goldstein is repeatedly disarming him (petty payback for his attitude). Ginny performs very well against Michael Corner (who seems reluctant to jinx his own girlfriend); the Creevey brothers are enthusiastic but clumsy, knocking books everywhere; Luna Lovegood is inconsistent, sometimes disarming Justin Finch-Fletchley, sometimes just making his hair stand on end.
- Harry circulates, coaching; eventually approaches Cho and her friend (named here: Marietta, confirmed to be Marietta Edgecombe in later chapters but just "Marietta" here) — Cho fumbles the incantation nervously in front of Harry ("Expelliarmious! Expellimellius!"), accidentally sets Marietta's sleeve on fire; Marietta is visibly displeased. Cho blames her nervousness on Harry's presence, then they share a light, flirtatious exchange.
- Cho privately explains Marietta only came because Cho insisted; Marietta's Ministry-employed mother has forbidden her from doing anything that might upset Umbridge. Cho's own parents have similarly warned her not to antagonize Umbridge, but she states firmly she intends to fight Voldemort regardless, given what happened to Cedric — she trails off, visibly emotional, before an awkward silence is broken by a stray spell.
- Luna interjects (having eavesdropped) with a wild tangent about her father's firm anti-Ministry/anti-Fudge stance, including wild claims about assassinated goblins, secret Department of Mysteries poisons, and a mysterious "Umgubular Slashkilter" device — comic relief, with Harry deflecting Cho's confused questions.
- The meeting runs over time (discovered to be 9:10 p.m., past the curfew risk); Harry dismisses everyone, checking the Marauder's Map again to ensure safe dispersal in staggered small groups back to their respective common rooms/dormitories (Hufflepuffs toward the basement/kitchens corridor, Ravenclaws to their west-side tower, Gryffindors back via the Fat Lady). A second meeting is set for the following Wednesday, with Dean eagerly requesting it be sooner and Angelina reminding everyone Quidditch season is starting.
- Walking back, Hermione and Ron bicker good-naturedly about who disarmed whom more often during practice; Harry is privately replaying Cho's comment about him making her nervous.

**Named characters (ch.18 continued — meeting roster, consolidated):** Harry Potter, Ron Weasley, Hermione Granger, Ginny Weasley, Neville Longbottom, Lavender Brown, Parvati Patil, Dean Thomas, Fred & George Weasley, Angelina Johnson, Katie Bell, Alicia Spinnet, Zacharias Smith, Cho Chang, Marietta (Edgecombe, surname not yet given), Terry Boot, Anthony Goldstein, Michael Corner, Colin Creevey, Dennis Creevey, Ernie Macmillan, Justin Finch-Fletchley, Luna Lovegood, Dumbledore (referenced, Yule Ball comment), Severus Snape (referenced, cracked Foe-Glass previously in fake-Moody's office), Barty Crouch Jr./fake Moody (referenced indirectly via the Foe-Glass).

**Named places/objects/spells/creatures:**
- The Room of Requirement (fully realized this chapter): silk cushions, Dark Detector shelf (Sneakoscopes, Secrecy Sensors, a cracked Foe-Glass), extensive defense-book library (named titles: A Compendium of Common Curses and Their Counter-Actions, The Dark Arts Outsmarted, Self-Defensive Spellwork, Jinxes for the Jinxed).
- The tapestry of Barnabas the Barmy (depicting his failed attempt to train trolls in ballet — comic worldbuilding detail, trolls clubbing him).
- Expelliarmus (the Disarming Charm) — the D.A.'s first-taught spell, explicitly tied back to Harry's graveyard duel with Voldemort.
- The Marauder's Map (named/used explicitly, activated via its password phrase).
- "Dumbledore's Army" (the group's chosen name, suggested by Ginny).

**Specific numbers/quantities:**
- Meeting runs from 8:00 p.m. to 9:10 p.m. (an overrun past the planned time).
- 25 of the original Hog's Head attendees are confirmed present/notified (text states this explicitly).
- Next meeting set for the following Wednesday.

**Small/obscure details:**
- Fred recalls hiding from Filch in the same room before, when it was "just a broom cupboard" — nice continuity/foreshadowing of the room's adaptive nature.
- Luna's rambling claims about Fudge's "Umgubular Slashkilter" and goblin assassinations are comic, Quibbler-flavored nonsense, deliberately left unexplained ("Don't ask," Harry tells Cho).
- Cho's near-miss setting Marietta's sleeve on fire is a small but vivid, specific classroom-mishap detail.

## Chapter 19: The Lion and the Serpent

**Plot events (in order):**
- Time skip covering several weeks: Harry draws quiet strength from the D.A.'s secret existence and progress, replaying memories of members' successes during Umbridge's dull lessons — Neville successfully disarming Hermione, Colin Creevey mastering the Impediment Jinx after three meetings, Parvati Patil's Reductor Curse accidentally destroying a table of Sneakoscopes.
- Scheduling D.A. meetings around three separate House Quidditch teams' practices proves difficult but Harry considers the resulting unpredictability a security benefit (harder for anyone to detect a pattern).
- Hermione invents a sophisticated, covert signaling system: fake Galleon coins (modeled consciously, she admits, on the way Voldemort magically brands his Death Eaters' Dark Marks to summon them — but using enchanted metal instead of skin) bearing serial numbers that change via a Protean Charm to display the date/time of the next meeting, growing warm when updated so members can feel the change even in a pocket. This impresses Terry Boot enormously (Protean Charms are N.E.W.T.-level magic), who is astonished Hermione isn't in Ravenclaw — she reveals the Sorting Hat seriously considered Ravenclaw for her before choosing Gryffindor.
- D.A. meetings are paused as the first Quidditch match of the season (Gryffindor vs. Slytherin) approaches, with Angelina scheduling near-daily team practices. Professor McGonagall, visibly invested in beating Slytherin, excuses Gryffindor students from homework the week of the match (startling given her usual strictness) — an unusually partisan move for her. Snape is equally partisan in Slytherin's favor: monopolizing the Quidditch pitch for their practices and ignoring multiple eyewitness reports (fourteen witnesses, specifically) that Slytherin Keeper Miles Bletchley hexed Alicia Spinnet from behind in the library (he instead blames her overgrown eyebrows on a self-inflicted Hair-Thickening Charm gone wrong; she's treated by Madam Pomfrey).
- Ron, as new Keeper, struggles with confidence (prone to spiraling after conceding one goal) but shows flashes of brilliance — notably a one-handed, broom-dangling kick-save compared favorably by teammates to a real save by Irish International Keeper Barry Ryan against Polish Chaser Ladislaw Zamojski. Fred half-jokingly concedes Ron "might make us proud" and jokes about finally admitting family relation to him (having denied it for four years).
- Slytherin players (including older, larger seventh-years) wage a psychological campaign against Ron specifically, taunting him in corridors ("Got your bed booked in the hospital wing, Weasley?") and Malfoy repeatedly, mockingly mimes Ron dropping the Quaffle whenever he's in view — causing Ron visible anxiety (turning green, shaking hands) in contrast to Harry's thick-skinned amusement at similar jabs aimed at himself.
- Season turns to November: harsh cold, frost, snow-capped mountains, dragon-skin gloves worn in corridors.
- Morning of the match: Ron is sick with nerves, barely eating; confesses privately to Harry that his recent celebrated "brilliant" save was actually an accident (he'd slipped off his broom and the kick was pure luck recovering his balance) — Harry, undeterred, jokes that more "accidents" like that would win them the game.
- At breakfast, the Slytherin table is conspicuously wearing new silver badges shaped like crowns, waving and laughing at Ron specifically — Hermione urgently warns Harry not to let Ron see what's written on them (the badges bear a mocking rhyme, later revealed during the match: a chant calling Ron "Weasley, Our King" while mocking his goalkeeping — paraphrased here per copyright guidance, the chant mocks that "Weasley" lets every Quaffle in and was "born in a bin").
- Luna Lovegood shows up wearing a giant, charmed lion's-head hat that roars realistically when tapped with her wand, in support of Gryffindor (she'd wanted it to also chew up a toy serpent for Slytherin but ran out of time); wishes Ron good luck.
- Hermione kisses Ron on the cheek for luck before the match (also wishing Harry well) — Ron is visibly stunned/confused by this.
- The match: Slytherin's new team includes Captain Montague (physically likened to Dudley Dursley) and new Beaters Crabbe and Goyle (replacing graduated Derrick and Bole) — both large but unskilled flyers, per Angelina's scouting report. Madam Hooch referees. Lee Jordan provides commentary (repeatedly reprimanded by McGonagall for editorializing, including an aside about fancying Angelina Johnson).
- The Slytherin crowd sings the mocking "Weasley Is Our King" chant loudly and repeatedly throughout the match, specifically timed to rattle Ron whenever Slytherin presses an attack on the Gryffindor goal; this psychological pressure visibly affects him. Early in the match, under the weight of the song, Ron dives the wrong way on a straightforward shot from Warrington, and Slytherin takes a 10-0 lead.

**Named characters:** Harry Potter, Ron Weasley, Hermione Granger, Ginny Weasley, Luna Lovegood, Angelina Johnson, Katie Bell, Alicia Spinnet, Fred & George Weasley, Lee Jordan, Professor McGonagall, Professor Snape, Madam Pomfrey, Madam Hooch, Draco Malfoy, Miles Bletchley (Slytherin Keeper), Montague (new Slytherin Captain), Crabbe and Goyle (new Slytherin Beaters), Warrington (Slytherin Chaser), Barry Ryan (Irish International Keeper, referenced), Ladislaw Zamojski (Polish Chaser, referenced), Derrick and Bole (former Slytherin Beaters, graduated/left), Colin Creevey, Parvati Patil, Terry Boot, Pansy Parkinson.

**Named places/objects/spells/creatures:**
- The fake enchanted Galleons (D.A. communication device) — Protean Charm.
- The Impediment Jinx, the Reductor Curse (spells mastered/misused by D.A. members).
- The Quidditch pitch, the crown-shaped silver Slytherin badges (mocking Ron).
- Luna's roaring lion hat.
- Barnabas the Barmy tapestry (callback).
- Dragon-skin gloves (winter wear).

**Specific numbers/quantities:**
- Colin Creevey masters the Impediment Jinx after three meetings.
- Fourteen eyewitnesses reported seeing Miles Bletchley hex Alicia Spinnet (ignored by Snape).
- Slytherin leads 10-0 early in the match after Ron lets in a goal from Warrington.
- Crabbe and Goyle replace Derrick and Bole as Slytherin Beaters this year.

**Small/obscure details:**
- Hermione explicitly compares her fake-Galleon system to the Death Eaters' Dark Mark branding — a notably dark, self-aware comparison from her.
- Terry Boot's astonishment that Hermione isn't a Ravenclaw, and her reveal that the Sorting Hat considered it, is a nice piece of character trivia.
- Ron's admission that his "brilliant" save was an accident (he'd fallen off his broom) undercuts the team's growing confidence in him and is a key piece of dramatic irony for the match.
- Lee Jordan's commentary bias and flirtatious aside about Angelina Johnson recur as comic relief.

**Chapter 19 continued — full plot events:**
- The Slytherin crowd's "Weasley Is Our King" chant continues relentlessly throughout, badly distracting Harry (he loses track of the Snitch for over a minute, drawing Angelina's fury) and demoralizing Ron, who lets in goal after goal; by the time Harry finally spots the Snitch, Slytherin leads 50-10 (progression: 10-0, then more goals making it effectively a blowout before Harry's catch — exact score at catch is 60-10 per standard canon, but text shows incremental 10-0, 20-0, 40-10 progression).
- Harry and Malfoy race for the Snitch low over the ground; Harry catches it a split second before Malfoy's clawing fingers can intervene — Gryffindor "wins" on the Snitch catch (150 points) despite being badly outscored on goals, since Slytherin's lead was only 10-0 at the moment of the catch in this telling (narrative math: final score difference means catching the Snitch turns a loss into a win) — Gryffindor wins the match.
- Immediately after the catch, Crabbe illegally hits Harry with a Bludger after the whistle (post-catch), knocking him off his broom (he falls only 5-6 feet and is winded but unhurt). Madam Hooch confronts Crabbe over the illegal hit.
- Draco Malfoy, humiliated by the loss, needles Harry and the Weasleys viciously: mocks Ron as "born in a bin," reveals the crowd chant was his own lyrical composition, then escalates into crude personal insults about Fred and George's parents (implying poverty/failure — "useless loser" for Arthur) and about Harry's late mother, vilely suggesting the Weasleys' home smells like the house Harry's mother died in. This finally provokes Harry past his breaking point — he and George (not Fred) throw themselves at Malfoy, leading to a brawl; Harry punches Malfoy hard in the stomach with the fist still clutching the just-caught Snitch, giving Malfoy a bloody nose; George gets a swollen lip in the scuffle.
- Madam Hooch breaks up the fight with an Impediment Jinx (hitting Harry), furious, and sends both Harry and George straight to their Head of House's office — unprecedented disciplinary action after a match.
- In McGonagall's office, she is initially harshly critical of their "two-on-one" assault (unaware yet of the full provocation) and about to hand down a week's detention each (dismissing Malfoy's insults, even about their families, as no excuse for "Muggle dueling" via fists) — when Dolores Umbridge interrupts ("Hem, hem"), having apparently been summoned/arrived to claim jurisdiction. Umbridge reveals she has just received a new Ministry decree — Educational Decree Number Twenty-five — explicitly granting the High Inquisitor "supreme authority over all punishments, sanctions, and removal of privileges," including the power to override any other staff member's disciplinary decisions. She explains she pushed Fudge for this new decree specifically because McGonagall had successfully overridden her earlier Quidditch-team ban by appealing to Dumbledore.
- Using this new unilateral power, Umbridge announces a lifetime ban from Quidditch for Harry and George — and, "to be safe," for Fred too (despite having done nothing but being restrained by teammates) — confiscating all three of their broomsticks, to be locked in her office to prevent any violation of the ban. She explicitly exempts the rest of the team from punishment, having "seen no signs of violence" from them, and departs with visible, gloating satisfaction, leaving McGonagall standing rigid with fury but powerless to override the new decree.
- That night in the common room, the mood is funereal rather than victorious: Angelina despairs over having lost both her Seeker and both Beaters in one stroke; Alicia is outraged that Crabbe's illegal post-whistle Bludger attack went entirely unpunished (he received only lines, which Montague was overheard laughing about at dinner) while Fred — who never actually struck anyone, having been physically restrained — received a full lifetime ban anyway. The stolen Golden Snitch (which Harry apparently kept, despite the ban) flies loose around the common room, chased by Crookshanks, as a bitter visual irony.
- Ron, who vanished alone after the match (returning late, snow-covered, having gone "walking" in his kit), tearfully blames himself for the entire disaster and announces he intends to quit the Quidditch team the next morning; Harry rebuts this (pointing out that would leave Gryffindor with only three players after the bans) and reveals the full scope of the punishment (his and the twins' lifetime bans) for the first time to Ron, via Hermione's retelling.
- Harry and Ron briefly squabble over whose fault the whole mess is before Hermione — trying to shift the mood — announces the one piece of good news: Hagrid is back.

**Named characters (ch.19 additions):** Harry Potter, Ron Weasley, Hermione Granger, Draco Malfoy, Crabbe, Goyle, Madam Hooch, Professor McGonagall, Dolores Umbridge, Angelina Johnson, Alicia Spinnet, Katie Bell, Fred & George Weasley, Montague (Slytherin Captain), Cornelius Fudge (referenced, signs the new Decree), Lee Jordan, Ginny Weasley, Luna Lovegood (referenced, lion hat), Crookshanks.

**Named places/objects/spells/creatures:**
- Educational Decree Number Twenty-five — grants the High Inquisitor supreme override authority over all student punishments/privileges Hogwarts-wide; explicitly quoted/paraphrased in-text, signed by "Cornelius Fudge, Minister of Magic, Order of Merlin First Class, etc., etc."
- The Impediment Jinx ("Impedimenta") — used by Madam Hooch to stop Harry's attack on Malfoy.
- Ginger Newts (McGonagall's biscuit tin, spilled in her anger).
- The Golden Snitch Harry caught (confiscated broomsticks aside, he appears to keep the Snitch itself, which flies loose in the common room).

**Specific numbers/quantities:**
- Final Quidditch score at the moment of Harry's catch: Slytherin leading, Gryffindor wins overall via the 150-point Snitch catch (exact running score shown: 10-0, then 20-0/40-10 progression — precise final numbers given in text as Slytherin 10, Gryffindor 0 shortly before the catch, meaning Gryffindor's win margin comes entirely from the catch).
- Harry and George (and Fred, by association) receive lifetime Quidditch bans — the first such punishment depicted in the series.
- A full week's detention was McGonagall's original, overridden sentence for Harry and George.

**Small/obscure details:**
- Lee Jordan's commentary bias (flirting with Angelina on-air) recurs as a running joke, repeatedly reprimanded by McGonagall.
- The specific, personal nature of Malfoy's insults (targeting Fred/George's parents and Harry's dead mother) is what finally breaks Harry's control — an important characterization beat distinguishing genuine provocation from routine schoolyard taunting.
- Umbridge's petty motivation for Decree 25 — pure spite over being overruled on the earlier Quidditch-team ban — is explicitly stated by her own words, a clear characterization of her vindictiveness.
