## Chapter 20: Hagrid's Tale

**Plot events (in order):**
- That same snowy night, Harry, Ron, and Hermione sneak out under the Invisibility Cloak (checking the Marauder's Map for Filch/Mrs. Norris) to visit Hagrid's cabin, having learned he's back. Ron (grown tall) must crouch under the Cloak to avoid exposing his feet. They pass only Nearly Headless Nick along the way, humming the "Weasley Is Our King" tune obliviously.
- At Hagrid's cabin: Fang barks; Hagrid, initially pleased ("Shoulda known!"), opens the door and reveals severe injuries — matted blood in his hair, one eye swollen into a purple-black slit, numerous still-bleeding cuts, and a limping gait suggesting broken ribs. A large traveling cloak and an oversized haversack indicate he's only just returned. He initially deflects all questions ("Told yeh, nuthin'"), insists he's fine, and applies a raw, greenish slab of dragon meat to his swollen eye as a folk remedy (clarifying it's "s'posed ter look like that" and wasn't meant to be eaten).
- Hermione guesses outright that giants did this to him; Hagrid is visibly startled (drops the steak) and grumpily concedes the trio always "know more'n yeh oughta," confirming he was on a secret mission to contact the giants.
- Hagrid recounts his summer in detail (quoted at length in the original; paraphrased here): He and Madame Maxime (Olympe), who accompanied him, set off right after the previous term ended on Dumbledore's orders to find Britain's expatriate giant communities and deliver a diplomatic message, specifically to try to win them away from Voldemort before Death Eaters could recruit them. Dumbledore provided directions, since the giants' remote mountain location (not literally secret, just logistically difficult and avoided by wizards) made the journey hard. The trip took about a month, partly because — due to Ministry surveillance of anyone connected to Dumbledore — they had to travel mostly like Muggles (posing as tourists heading toward Beauxbatons, shaking off a Ministry tail "round about Dijon," France, then using magic more freely afterward) to avoid detection, encountering "a couple of mad trolls" on the Polish border and "a slight disagreement with a vampire" in a pub in Minsk en route.
- On reaching the giants' mountain territory, they had to approach cautiously and without magic initially (per Dumbledore's specific warning that Voldemort had likely already sent his own messengers to the giants, and that Death Eaters might be in the vicinity).
- They found the giant camp: roughly 70-80 giants remaining in total (down from perhaps a hundred different tribes worldwide historically), standing 20-25 feet tall; they are dying out partly from wizard persecution historically but mostly from their own near-constant internal violence, worsened by having been forced by wizards long ago to live crowded together for mutual protection rather than spread out as nature intended (a fact Dumbledore attributes to wizard culpability).
- Protocol for approaching giants (per Dumbledore's instructions): identify and present gifts of magic (which giants love, though they resent having it used against them) to the "Gurg" (chief) — identifiable as the biggest, ugliest, and laziest giant, lounging while others serve him. The then-Gurg, Karkus (estimated 22-23 feet tall, built like "a couple of bull elephants," rhino-like hide), received a branch of Gubraithian Fire (a type of everlasting magical flame, a rare feat of magic even for skilled wizards — Hermione notes Flitwick has mentioned it in class) as a first-day gift, delivered with a formal greeting invoking Dumbledore's name; Karkus didn't speak English but had translators among his tribe. The gift was well received.
- Protocol called for a slow, trust-building approach: visiting daily with escalating gifts (next: an indestructible, goblin-made battle helmet) rather than overwhelming the giants with information at once (Hagrid notes giants like Karkus might kill messengers simply to "simplify things" if overloaded). On the second visit, Karkus listened attentively to Dumbledore's message (relayed via bilingual giants), showing some receptiveness, having already heard of Dumbledore's past advocacy against the killing of Britain's last native giants.
- That same night, a massive, hours-long giant civil war erupted in the camp (giants constantly fight each other over territory/food/mates despite their dwindling numbers); by morning Karkus had been beheaded, his head found in the valley lake. A new Gurg, Golgomath, took power — massive, black-haired, wearing a necklace that includes human bones, and clearly more hostile.
- Hagrid and Maxime, having come too far to quit, approached Golgomath with their next planned gift (a dragon-skin roll) anyway; Golgomath immediately had Hagrid seized and hung upside down by two of his men. Madame Maxime saved him by rapidly casting Conjunctivitus Curses on both captors' eyes, forcing them to release Hagrid — but using magic against the giants fatally compromised any further diplomatic approach to Golgomath's main camp (a line giants will not forgive).
- Subsequently, Hagrid and Maxime observed from hiding for a few more days rather than giving up outright (contrary to Hermione's assumption of a quick 3-day trip) — discovering Golgomath was entertaining visiting Death Eaters, bringing him gifts and receiving friendly treatment (unlike Hagrid). Hagrid specifically recognizes one visitor: Walden Macnair, the Ministry-sanctioned executioner originally sent to kill Buckbeak the hippogriff (Prisoner of Azkaban callback) — a man who, per Hagrid, enjoys killing as much as Golgomath does, explaining their apparent rapport.
- Rather than abandon the mission given Golgomath's apparent lean toward Voldemort, Hagrid and Maxime sought out dissenting giants — specifically the ones being brutally beaten by Golgomath's faction, found hiding in mountain caves (avoiding Death Eater patrols who were also actively searching for Hagrid and Maxime by night, likely tipped off by Golgomath). On the third night after Karkus's death, in roughly the sixth cave searched, they found three badly beaten giants sheltering, one with some English — this group was more receptive to the overture (unable to retaliate in their injured state, but genuinely willing to listen); through repeated visits, Hagrid and Maxime got about six or seven giants sympathetic to Dumbledore's message at one point.
- However, Golgomath's forces subsequently raided these same caves, and the giants who survived wanted nothing further to do with Hagrid and Maxime afterward — ending any hope of actively recruiting giant allies this trip. Still, Hagrid takes grim comfort that the message was delivered and heard by some, hoping word may spread and that some giants dissatisfied with Golgomath might eventually recall Dumbledore's goodwill and leave the mountains to join his side later.
- Hermione gently asks if Hagrid learned anything about his mother (the giantess Fridwulfa, implied from earlier books though not named here) during the trip; Hagrid flatly reports she died years ago, expresses no real grief ("Wasn't a great mother," he barely remembers her), shutting down further discussion.
- The trio presses again about Hagrid's late return and injuries (noting Madame Maxime got back from the same trip much earlier, per Sirius's intel) — but they're interrupted by a knock at the door: Dolores Umbridge has arrived to inspect/investigate Hagrid in person. Harry, Ron, and Hermione scramble under the Invisibility Cloak; Hagrid hastily hides their mugs in Fang's dog-basket.
- Umbridge (wearing a green tweed cloak and matching ear-flapped hat) interrogates Hagrid pointedly: confirms her own titles (former Senior Undersecretary to the Minister, now DADA teacher and Hogwarts High Inquisitor); notes a broken mug on the floor (Hagrid claims Fang broke it); claims to have heard "voices" (Hagrid claims he was talking to Fang); and, most alarmingly, reveals she has already noticed three sets of footprints in the snow leading to the cabin with none leading away — directly implying she knows others are present and hidden. She methodically searches the cabin (under the bed, in cupboards, in his cauldron), passing within inches of the hidden trio without detecting them.
- She grills Hagrid about his injuries (he claims he "tripped... over a friend's broomstick," then, pressed, claims he fell from riding a friend's winged Abraxan horse) and his two-month unexplained absence (he claims he was away "for his health," then claims he was sunning himself in the South of France) — Umbridge dismantles each excuse coolly (noting his total lack of a tan, pointedly asking "Mountain scenery?" which visibly rattles both Hagrid and the hidden Harry). She states she'll report his late return to the Minister and that, as High Inquisitor, she intends to formally inspect him ("we shall meet again soon enough") as part of the Ministry's effort to "weed out unsatisfactory teachers" — then leaves.
- Once safely confirmed gone (Hagrid peers out to watch her cross back to the castle), the trio emerges. Hermione learns, with dismay, that Hagrid plans wildly ambitious, dangerous lesson content for his O.W.L.-year classes — refusing to disclose details ("I don't want ter spoil the surprise"), mentioning he keeps "the only domestic herd in Britain" of something impressive and has been trying (apparently unsuccessfully, implied) to obtain chimaera eggs. Hermione begs him to teach dull, safe, O.W.L.-relevant material instead (porlocks, knarls vs. hedgehogs) to avoid giving Umbridge any pretext to fire him, explicitly naming Umbridge's strategy of targeting Dumbledore-loyal staff — Hagrid is sleepily dismissive, confident in his planned lessons, and ushers them out for the night (reminding them to erase their footprints).
- Walking back (Hermione casting an Obliteration Charm on their tracks), Ron doubts the message got through; Hermione vows to go back the next day to plan Hagrid's lessons for him herself if necessary, declaring she won't let Umbridge "take Hagrid" the way she's taken Trelawney's dignity.

**Named characters:** Harry Potter, Ron Weasley, Hermione Granger, Hagrid, Fang, Madame Olympe Maxime (Beauxbatons headmistress, Hagrid's diplomatic partner, described warmly/fondly by Hagrid, "fiery... 'spect it's the French in her"), Karkus (deceased giant Gurg, beheaded), Golgomath (new, hostile giant Gurg, wears a necklace including human bones), Walden Macnair (Death Eater, Ministry executioner, recognized by Hagrid from the Buckbeak case, Prisoner of Azkaban callback), Buckbeak (referenced), Dumbledore (gave instructions/mission), Dolores Umbridge (inspects Hagrid's cabin), Sirius Black (referenced, re: Madame Maxime's earlier return), Hagrid's unnamed mother (giantess, confirmed deceased "years ago").

**Named places/objects/spells/creatures:**
- The giants' mountain territory (unspecified exact location, reached via France, Dijon, Polish border, Minsk).
- Gubraithian Fire (everlasting magical flame, rare/advanced magic), an indestructible goblin-made battle helmet (diplomatic gifts to the Gurg).
- Conjunctivitus Curse (used by Madame Maxime to blind Hagrid's giant captors).
- Abraxan horses (giant winged horse breed, referenced as Hagrid's cover story — also the Beauxbatons carriage-horses from Goblet of Fire).
- Dragon meat/steak (folk remedy for Hagrid's bruising).
- The Forbidden Forest (Hagrid's new class venue, teased as containing something "they prefer the dark").

**Specific numbers/quantities:**
- About 70-80 giants remain worldwide-descended tribes (down from "about a hundred different tribes").
- Giants stand roughly 20-25 feet tall; Karkus specifically estimated at 22-23 feet, "weight of a couple of bull elephants."
- The journey to the giants took about a month.
- Karkus was killed/replaced roughly two days after first contact.
- Six or seven giants were "convinced" (sympathetic) at the trip's peak, before Golgomath's raid ended that goodwill.
- Three giants found hurt in roughly the sixth cave searched, on the third night after Karkus's death.

**Small/obscure details:**
- Hagrid's budding, warmly described affection for Madame Maxime ("fiery... 'spect it's the French in her") is a notable, specific character beat.
- Hagrid's claim to have "the only domestic herd in Britain" of an unnamed creature is a specific, quotable tease (payoff comes with Grawp later, though this particular line concerns a different creature — likely foreshadowing the Thestral herd or another beast, left deliberately vague).
- Umbridge's inspection of Hagrid's cabin — searching under the bed, in the cupboard, in the cauldron, and passing within two inches of the hidden trio — is a tense, specific set-piece moment.
- Hagrid mentions two of his teeth were knocked out during his ordeal (visible when he smiles at Umbridge).

---

## Chapter 21: The Eye of the Snake

**Plot events so far (in progress):**
- The weekend after Hagrid's return: heavy snow blankets the grounds; students enjoy skating, tobogganing, and (per Fred and George) bewitching snowballs to rap on the Gryffindor Tower windows — Ron, now a prefect, futilely shouts down a threat and gets hit in the face with snow for his trouble, identifying Fred and George as the culprits.
- Hermione visits Hagrid again to try to help plan safer lesson content; she reports he wasn't home when she arrived (out in the Forbidden Forest), and when he did return, he dismissed her advice entirely, fixated on his undisclosed "impressive" creatures (apparently failing to acquire chimaera eggs despite trying) and uninterested in the safer Grubbly-Plank-style curriculum. His mood is described as "funny," and he still won't explain his injuries.
- Hagrid's return to the staff table is met with mixed reactions from students: Fred, George, and Lee are thrilled and greet him enthusiastically; others (Parvati, Lavender) are visibly unhappy, since many students have come to prefer Professor Grubbly-Plank's safer, more predictable teaching style — Harry privately admits there's some truth to the preference (decapitation risk vs. Grubbly-Plank's tamer lessons).
- Tuesday's Care of Magical Creatures class: Hagrid's injuries (now green-yellow with age rather than fresh purple) are still visibly unhealed/slow to heal, puzzling Harry (he wonders if some venomous creature caused wounds that resist normal healing). Hagrid arrives carrying half a dead cow over his shoulder and announces the class will meet inside the Forbidden Forest itself ("more sheltered... they prefer the dark"), alarming Malfoy, who (per Harry's amused recollection of his past cowardice entering the forest before) reacts with visible nervous panic, asking Crabbe and Goyle what Hagrid meant by creatures that "prefer the dark." Hagrid announces the lesson's subject as something "pretty rare," which he's been saving specifically for their O.W.L. year.

**Named characters so far:** Harry Potter, Ron Weasley, Hermione Granger, Hagrid, Fred & George Weasley, Lee Jordan, Parvati Patil, Lavender Brown, Draco Malfoy, Crabbe, Goyle, Professor Grubbly-Plank (referenced, comparison).

**Named places/objects/creatures:**
- The Forbidden Forest (class relocated here); the frozen lake (skating).
- Half a dead cow (bait/food Hagrid brings for the mystery creatures).
- Chimaera eggs (referenced, Hagrid reportedly failed to obtain any).

**Chapter 21 continued — full plot events:**
- Malfoy, visibly scared, needles Hagrid about whether his new creatures are really tame, implying Hagrid has a history of bringing dangerous/untrained creatures to class (true, per past books); Hagrid curtly refuses to explain his injuries and leads the class deeper into the Forbidden Forest to a darker clearing with no snow on the ground.
- Hagrid gives an eerie shrieking call to summon the creatures; a thestral emerges — the same type of skeletal, black, winged, dragonish horse Harry has been seeing since Chapter 10. It begins eating the dead cow carcass. Harry feels enormous relief at proof he isn't imagining them.
- Only three students besides Harry can see the thestrals: Neville, and an unnamed "stringy" Slytherin boy. Most of the class (seeing meat vanish into thin air with no visible creature) finds this unsettling; Parvati Patil is frightened and repeats a superstition from Professor Trelawney that thestrals bring terrible misfortune on those who can see them.
- Hagrid formally names the creatures: Thestrals, confirms Hogwarts keeps a whole herd in the forest, mainly used to pull the school carriages (or for Dumbledore's long-distance travel when he doesn't want to Apparate). He dismisses the "unlucky" superstition and calls them "dead clever and useful." He introduces the first-born thestral by name: Tenebrus, his "special favorite," born in the forest, from an original founding herd of one male and five females.
- Hermione correctly answers (earning Gryffindor 10 points) that only people who have witnessed death can see thestrals — confirming the significance of Harry's ability to see them (tied to witnessing Cedric Diggory's murder) and Neville's (his grandfather's death, revealed here for the first time on-page).
- Umbridge arrives to formally inspect the lesson, speaking to Hagrid in an exaggeratedly loud, slow voice as though he's foreign or dim-witted; Hagrid, unfamiliar with her trademark "Hem, hem" fake cough, initially mistakes the sound for a thestral noise. Umbridge narrates Hagrid's confusion onto her clipboard in real time with cutting commentary (paraphrased: implying he resorts to "crude sign language" and has "poor short-term memory"), loudly enough for the class to hear and for Malfoy to visibly relish it while Hermione seethes.
- Umbridge challenges Hagrid on the Ministry's official classification of thestrals as "dangerous"; Hagrid dismisses this (they might bite if baited, like any animal, but aren't inherently dangerous) — Umbridge notes on her clipboard that he "shows signs of pleasure at the idea of violence." She then theatrically mimes simple instructions at Hagrid (pointing at her mouth, miming walking) as if he can't understand spoken English, drawing laughter from Malfoy and Pansy Parkinson.
- Umbridge interrogates students directly: Pansy Parkinson (through poorly concealed giggles) claims she can barely understand Hagrid's speech ("sounds like grunting"); Neville is asked whom he saw die (his grandfather, confirmed here) and whether he's frightened of the thestrals — when he denies fear, Umbridge notes on her clipboard that "students are too intimidated to admit they are frightened," patronizingly patting his shoulder. She concludes the inspection, promising results in ten days (as with all previous inspections), and leaves, having thoroughly humiliated Hagrid in front of the class.
- Walking back to the castle, Hermione furiously condemns Umbridge's tactics as another iteration of her prejudice against "half-breeds" (Hagrid being half-giant), arguing the thestral lesson was actually a genuinely good one for Hagrid (certainly better than past disasters like the Blast-Ended Skrewts). She expresses wistful curiosity about thestrals' visibility criterion, briefly wishing she could see them too, before immediately realizing/apologizing for the insensitivity of that wish (since it would require having witnessed death) — Harry reassures her it's fine.
- Malfoy, Crabbe, and Goyle catch up and cruelly needle Ron, suggesting that since three people in the class (implying death-witnesses) can apparently judge the Quaffle better, maybe Ron needs to watch someone die to improve his Keeping — then break into the "Weasley Is Our King" chant again as they pass. Hermione pointedly ignores them, clearing a path through the snow with a wand-cast warming charm.
- Time skip into December: heavy snow, mounting O.W.L.-year homework, and increasing prefect duties for Ron and Hermione (decorating the castle despite Peeves's interference, supervising younger students confined indoors by cold, patrolling with Filch, who anticipates a surge in dueling around the holidays). Hermione, swamped with work, has nearly run out of hand-knitted elf hats/socks (down to her last three) and worries about elves being "stuck" at Hogwarts over Christmas for lack of hats — unaware (though Harry knows and doesn't tell her) that Dobby is personally taking and wearing all her knitted items himself rather than other elves accepting them.
- Harry, uncharacteristically for the first time in his school career, actively wants to leave Hogwarts for the holidays — resentful over his lifetime Quidditch ban and continued uncertainty about Hagrid's job security; the D.A. is now his only remaining source of satisfaction, but meetings must pause over the three-week break since most members are going home. Hermione reveals she's going skiing with her Muggle parents (amusing Ron, unfamiliar with the concept); Ron is going home to the Burrow — and, after several days of Harry quietly fretting over his own holiday plans, casually reveals Mrs. Weasley invited Harry weeks ago (Ron had simply forgotten to mention it). Harry is overjoyed, though guiltily aware this means missing Christmas with Sirius, who remains isolated at Grimmauld Place with only Kreacher for company and hasn't fire-called since the near-miss with Umbridge; Harry doesn't attempt to contact him, mindful of ongoing surveillance.
- At the last D.A. meeting before the holidays, Harry arrives early to the Room of Requirement and discovers (and mostly removes) a hundred golden Christmas baubles that Dobby has hung from the ceiling, each bearing Harry's face and a "HAVE A VERY HARRY CHRISTMAS!" message — an embarrassing, over-the-top gesture of devotion. Luna Lovegood arrives early too, commenting (with her usual non sequitur logic) on a sprig of mistletoe positioned directly over Harry's head, warning it may be infested with "nargles" (an unexplained, presumably fictional pest).
- Angelina, Katie, and Alicia arrive and coldly inform Harry that Gryffindor has replaced its banned Seeker and Beaters: Ginny Weasley is the new Seeker (confirmed "pretty good," though "nothing on" Harry per Angelina); Andrew Kirke and Jack Sloper are the new Beaters (unenthusiastically rated as merely adequate compared to other candidates). Harry suppresses his bitterness at this news.
- The meeting itself is a review/consolidation session (no new content, given the upcoming three-week break) — Zacharias Smith complains loudly about this, drawing a retort from Fred. The group practices the Impediment Jinx in pairs for ten minutes, then moves to Stunning Spells on cushions (too cramped for everyone to practice simultaneously, so they alternate). Neville has improved dramatically (manages to disarm/jinx Harry three times in a row at one point); general progress across the group is strong, though space constraints cause some comic mishaps (Neville Stuns Padma Patil instead of his intended target, Dean). Harry announces that after the holidays, they'll begin working on producing Patronuses — generating excited murmurs.
- After the meeting, Harry lingers hoping for a word from Cho, who is seen dismissing her friend Marietta so they can speak privately. Instead of a casual goodbye, Cho breaks down crying, explaining that learning practical defense makes her wonder whether Cedric might have survived "if he'd known it all" — Harry gently points out Cedric was already skilled (he made it to the center of the Triwizard maze) but that nothing could have saved him against Voldemort's direct intent to kill. Cho references Harry's own survival as a baby; Harry deflects, saying he doesn't know why he survived and takes no pride in it.
- The emotional moment culminates in Cho noting the mistletoe overhead (a callback to Luna's earlier "nargles" remark, which Harry repeats awkwardly) and declaring "I really like you, Harry" — the scene ends on the verge of their first kiss (confirmed to have happened in the next scene back in the common room).
- Back in Gryffindor Tower, Ron and Hermione interrogate Harry about what kept him; Hermione guesses immediately that Cho "cornered" him, and directly asks if they kissed — Harry confirms. Describing the kiss as "wet" (because Cho was crying throughout), he admits he only "patted her on the back" in response, to Hermione's visible exasperation. Hermione delivers an extended, empathetic analysis of Cho's complicated emotional state (grief over Cedric, guilt about liking Harry, confusion over conflicting feelings, social anxiety about being judged, and worry about her flying form threatening her spot on the Ravenclaw Quidditch team) — Ron dismisses this as implausible ("one person can't feel all that at once, they'd explode"), earning Hermione's scorn ("the emotional range of a teaspoon"). Hermione is revealed to be writing a long letter to Viktor Krum (confirmed still corresponding, to Ron's visible irritation) as this conversation happens.
- Harry goes to bed troubled by the new social complications of possibly needing to ask Cho out and spend time alone with her, and dreams first a comic, anxiety-themed dream involving Cho, stolen Chocolate Frog cards, Hermione, and his confiscated Firebolt — before the dream shifts abruptly into a vivid, first-person sensory experience: Harry finds himself inhabiting the perspective of a huge snake gliding through a dark stone corridor, tasting a drowsing man's scent on the air, fighting the urge to bite him "for more important work" — until the man wakes and draws his wand, prompting the snake (Harry) to strike repeatedly, fangs sinking into the man's side amid a "warm gush of blood," before Harry wakes screaming in agony from his scar, vomiting from the pain, and gasping out to a horrified Ron that "your dad's been attacked" by a giant snake, insisting (over everyone's assumption it was just a nightmare) that he witnessed it as if he were the snake itself.
- Neville runs for help; Professor McGonagall arrives in her nightclothes, initially skeptical ("You mean you dreamed this?") but ultimately believes Harry's insistence that it was real and not an ordinary dream, and takes him and Ron immediately to see Dumbledore.

**Named characters (ch.21 additions):** Harry Potter, Ron Weasley, Hermione Granger, Hagrid, Draco Malfoy, Crabbe, Goyle, Parvati Patil, Neville Longbottom (reveals his grandfather's death), Dolores Umbridge, Pansy Parkinson, Angelina Johnson, Katie Bell, Alicia Spinnet, Ginny Weasley (new Seeker), Andrew Kirke and Jack Sloper (new Beaters), Fred & George Weasley, Zacharias Smith, Dobby, Luna Lovegood, Cho Chang, Marietta (Edgecombe), Viktor Krum (Hermione's correspondent), Dean Thomas, Seamus Finnigan, Professor Trelawney (referenced, thestral superstition), Professor McGonagall, Arthur Weasley (victim of the vision), Cedric Diggory (referenced in Cho's grief).

**Named places/objects/spells/creatures:**
- Thestrals (fully explained): skeletal winged horses visible only to those who've witnessed death; Hogwarts's herd founded from one male and five females; the first-born named Tenebrus (Hagrid's favorite); used to pull school carriages and for Dumbledore's unseen long-distance travel.
- Mistletoe and "nargles" (Luna's invented pest, left unexplained).
- The Room of Requirement's Christmas decorations (Dobby's hundred Harry-face baubles).
- The Impediment Jinx, Stunning Spells (D.A. practice material).
- Fizzing Whizbees (revealed later as Dumbledore's office password — not yet shown in this excerpt but relevant).

**Specific numbers/quantities:**
- Hogwarts's thestral herd originally founded from 1 male + 5 females.
- Hermione is down to her last three knitted elf hats by December.
- A hundred golden baubles hung by Dobby in the Room of Requirement.
- Neville disarms/Stuns Harry three times in a row during D.A. practice.
- Inspection results promised "in ten days" (recurring Umbridge phrase, restated for Hagrid's inspection).

**Small/obscure details:**
- Neville's grandfather's death (not his parents, who are alive but hospitalized — a careful distinction, since Neville doesn't know that Harry knows about his parents specifically) is the explicit reason he can see thestrals — an important, precise detail for trivia purposes.
- Malfoy's cruel jab linking death-sight to better Quidditch "vision" for Ron is a nasty, specific insult worth noting.
- The dream sequence's snake-POV sensory details (tasting scent on the air, feeling ribs splinter under fangs) are vivid and specific — important for the "Harry is possibly a Horcrux/connected to Voldemort" plot thread (not resolved in this book but central to series).

## Chapter 22: St. Mungo's Hospital for Magical Maladies and Injuries

**Plot events so far (in progress):**
- McGonagall takes Harry and Ron to Dumbledore's office (password: "Fizzing Whizbee" — a Honeydukes sweet brand) via the spiral staircase behind the gargoyle statue. Despite the late hour, muffled voices (overheard portraits, it's later revealed) had been coming from inside.
- Dumbledore, awake and dressed in nightclothes, receives the report calmly. Crucially, he asks Harry a precise question: from what vantage point did he witness the attack — beside the victim, or looking down from above? Harry realizes, startled, that he was seeing through the snake's own eyes/perspective, not merely dreaming about the event as an observer — a deeply unsettling revelation about Harry's psychic connection to Voldemort (implied, though not yet named outright, that the snake may be under Voldemort's control or that Harry is tapping into Voldemort's own senses via the snake).
- Dumbledore springs into action: wakes two portraits of former Hogwarts Heads with portraits elsewhere in the wizarding world — Everard and Dilys — instructing them to find Arthur Weasley (described: red hair, glasses) and ensure he's found and helped; he also activates a mysterious silver instrument that produces smoke in the shape of a serpent, which splits into two when Dumbledore asks (cryptically) "but in essence divided?" — seemingly confirming some detail of Harry's account that isn't explained to the reader yet.
- Everard reports back: he raised an alarm, Arthur was found collapsed and bleeding and carried away; Dilys confirms Arthur has been taken to St. Mungo's Hospital and "looks bad."
- Dumbledore sends McGonagall to wake Fred, George, and Ginny; sends Fawkes the phoenix off as an early-warning sentry against anyone approaching the office; prepares a Portkey by charming an old blackened kettle with "Portus" (since the Floo Network is being watched and thus unsafe).
- Dumbledore also wakes a reluctant portrait of Phineas Nigellus (a former Hogwarts headmaster with a pointed beard and Slytherin colors) — revealed to be the same portrait that hangs (and normally feigns being "empty"/asleep) in Sirius's bedroom at Grimmauld Place, explaining the "sniggering" blank portrait running gag from earlier chapters. Other portraits shame Phineas (who initially feigns exhaustion/reluctance) into cooperating, including the indignant Armando Dippet (identified as Dumbledore's own predecessor as headmaster) and a witch who threatens him with a wand "not unlike a birch rod." Phineas is sent to deliver a message to Sirius: Arthur Weasley has been gravely injured, and his wife, children, and Harry will be arriving at Grimmauld Place shortly.
- Fred, George, and Ginny arrive, frightened and still in nightclothes; Dumbledore explains Arthur was injured "in the course of his work for the Order of the Phoenix" and has been taken to St. Mungo's; he's sending them all to Sirius's house (more convenient to the hospital than the Burrow) via Portkey (the old kettle) rather than Floo powder, since the Floo Network is being watched/unsafe. He insists on waiting for Phineas's confirmation that the coast at Grimmauld Place is clear before sending them.
- Fawkes flashes into the room briefly and leaves behind a single golden feather — a pre-arranged signal that Mrs. Weasley (who has her own means of knowing when something's wrong, implied to be the family clock from the Weasleys' kitchen, as referenced by Dumbledore to McGonagall) is stirring/aware something's happened; McGonagall rushes off to intercept her with a cover story.
- Phineas Nigellus confirms Sirius has received the message and "will be delighted" to host them (a dry, backhanded remark about his "great-great-grandson" always having "odd taste in houseguests" — confirming Sirius is Phineas's great-great-grandson, i.e., part of the Black family lineage, tying Phineas's portrait explicitly into the Black family tapestry/portrait gallery).
- As the group touches the Portkey and Dumbledore counts down, Harry's scar sears white-hot right as Dumbledore's eyes meet his — accompanied by a terrifying, alien surge of pure hatred and an urge to "bite" Dumbledore, which Harry does not understand or control — a brief, unsettling, unexplained moment of Voldemort's consciousness/influence bleeding through Harry's own mind at the worst possible instant.
- The Portkey activates; they land hard in the dim basement kitchen of Grimmauld Place. Kreacher, present and muttering insults ("the blood traitor brats... is it true their father's dying") is chased off by Sirius, who is unshaven, in day clothes, smelling faintly of alcohol, clearly having been drinking alone. Sirius had already been informed (via Phineas) that Arthur was badly hurt and demands details.
- Harry retells the vision to the Weasley children — but deliberately alters the account to omit that he experienced it from inside the snake's body/perspective, instead implying he merely "watched" the attack from nearby — sensing (correctly) that this detail would deeply disturb them. Ron, who knows the true version (having been present at the original telling to McGonagall/Dumbledore), says nothing to contradict him. Fred, George, and Ginny still seem subtly suspicious/uneasy about how Harry could have "seen" this at all.
- The Weasley children (especially Fred and George) are desperate to rush immediately to St. Mungo's; Sirius firmly refuses to let them, arguing it would be impossible to explain how they already know about the attack before the hospital has even notified their mother — risking exposure of Harry's vision-ability to the Ministry, which Sirius fears would be exploited/weaponized if discovered (echoing earlier-established Ministry paranoia about Dumbledore's influence over Harry). A bitter, heated argument erupts: Fred and George furiously accuse Sirius of hypocrisy (safely sheltering at Grimmauld Place while demanding they risk nothing, "risking your neck" rhetoric) when it's their father's life on the line; Sirius, visibly wounded but resolute, insists Arthur "knew what he was getting into" for the Order's cause and that "there are things worth dying for" — explicitly framing this as part of why the twins (and by extension, underage members generally) aren't allowed in the Order themselves.
- Ginny eventually sits down, defusing the standoff; the others reluctantly follow suit. Sirius magically Summons butterbeers for everyone ("Accio Butterbeer!") while they wait anxiously for news from Mrs. Weasley.

**Named characters so far:** Harry Potter, Ron Weasley, Professor McGonagall, Dumbledore, Fawkes (phoenix), Everard and Dilys (portrait Hogwarts Heads-turned-messengers), Armando Dippet (Dumbledore's predecessor as headmaster, portrait), Phineas Nigellus (former Hogwarts headmaster, portrait residing in both Dumbledore's office and Sirius's bedroom at Grimmauld Place; revealed as Sirius's great-great-grandfather), Arthur Weasley (gravely injured victim), Molly Weasley (not yet arrived, referenced via the family clock), Fred, George, and Ginny Weasley, Sirius Black, Kreacher.

**Named places/objects/spells/creatures:**
- St. Mungo's Hospital for Magical Maladies and Injuries (destination for Arthur).
- Dumbledore's office: the gargoyle password system ("Fizzing Whizbee"), portraits of former Heads capable of moving between their own portraits elsewhere, Fawkes the phoenix, a silver smoke-producing instrument (unnamed, produces a serpent that can "divide" on command — implicitly related to detecting or confirming aspects of dark magic/possession).
- The Weasleys' family clock (referenced, tracks members' location/status, e.g. "mortal peril" — from Chamber of Secrets).
- The blackened kettle Portkey, activated via "Portus."
- Butterbeer (Summoned via Accio).

**Specific numbers/quantities:**
- Arthur Weasley's attack occurs in the deep night (Harry wakes well past midnight per earlier reference to Dumbledore's lit office at that hour).

**Small/obscure details:**
- The reveal that the "sniggering" empty portrait in Harry and Ron's Grimmauld Place bedroom (noted repeatedly since Chapter 4) is Phineas Nigellus is a satisfying continuity payoff.
- Phineas Nigellus is confirmed as Sirius's great-great-grandfather, reinforcing the Black family tapestry details from Chapter 6.
- Harry's momentary, involuntary surge of hatred/urge to bite Dumbledore at the exact instant their eyes meet is a significant, unsettling foreshadowing moment regarding Harry's psychic link to Voldemort.
- Dumbledore's cryptic exchange with the silver instrument ("But in essence divided?") is left deliberately mysterious in-text — likely tied to the nature of the snake/Voldemort's possession, a detail attentive readers would flag for later significance.

**Chapter 22 continued — full plot events:**
- Harry privately agonizes over his guilt, oscillating between rationalizing ("you haven't got fangs, you were lying in bed") and renewed horror (his urge to attack Dumbledore at the Portkey moment).
- A phoenix-feather-accompanied scroll arrives via Fawkes mid-vigil: a brief note from Mrs. Weasley ("Dad is still alive. I am setting out for St. Mungo's now. Stay where you are. I will send news as soon as I can. Mum.") — its ambiguous "still alive" phrasing deepens everyone's dread.
- A long, silent overnight vigil follows at Grimmauld Place; at 5:10 a.m., Mrs. Weasley returns with good news: Arthur will be fine, is sleeping, and can be visited later; Bill sat with him and is taking the morning off work. Relief breaks out; Sirius cheerfully organizes breakfast (Kreacher fails to answer his summons, foreshadowing the elf's absence).
- Mrs. Weasley hugs Harry gratefully, crediting him with saving Arthur's life (since he'd have gone unfound for hours otherwise) and noting Dumbledore has fabricated a cover story to protect Arthur from Ministry scrutiny over his presence at the scene (contrasting with Sturgis Podmore's unlucky fate). Sirius happily invites the Weasleys to stay at Grimmauld Place indefinitely, even through Christmas, delighting Mrs. Weasley.
- Harry privately confides the full, unaltered truth to Sirius (that he was the snake) and his terrifying moment of wanting to attack Dumbledore at the Portkey; Sirius reassures him it's likely just emotional aftermath/shock and sends him to bed, dismissing (perhaps too quickly) the possibility that something is seriously wrong.
- Harry, too afraid to sleep (worried he might "become the serpent" again and hurt Ron), stays awake rigidly uncomfortable but eventually must rest; he fakes having slept well to Ron.
- That afternoon, the group changes into Muggle clothes (trunks arrive from Hogwarts) for the trip to St. Mungo's, escorted by Tonks (hair short and bright pink) and Mad-Eye Moody (disguised in a tilted bowler hat hiding his magical eye). On the Underground, Tonks quizzes Harry about whether there's "Seer blood" in his family, correctly intuiting his visions show the present rather than the future — a distinction Harry finds uncomfortable to discuss.
- St. Mungo's entrance is hidden behind a Muggle department-store façade: "Purge and Dowse Ltd.," a shabby, permanently "CLOSED FOR REFURBISHMENT" building with an ugly mannequin in the window that, when addressed correctly ("We're here to see Arthur Weasley"), allows entry by walking directly through the glass.
- Inside: a crowded reception area with bizarre ailments on display (a witch emitting steam-whistles, a man clanging like a bell), staff in lime-green robes (a crossed wand-and-bone emblem, confirmed as "Healers," not "doctors" per Ron's dismissive clarification of the Muggle equivalent), and a portrait of Dilys Derwent (St. Mungo's Healer 1722–1741, later Hogwarts Headmistress 1741–1768 — explaining her earlier appearance as a portrait-messenger in Dumbledore's office).
- The hospital's floor directory is given in full: Ground Floor – Artifact Accidents (cauldron explosions, wand backfiring, broom crashes); First Floor – Creature-Induced Injuries (bites, stings, burns, embedded spines); Second Floor – Magical Bugs (contagious maladies: dragon pox, vanishing sickness, scrofungulus); Third Floor – Potion and Plant Poisoning (rashes, regurgitation, uncontrollable giggling); Fourth Floor – Spell Damage (unliftable jinxes, hexes, incorrectly applied charms); Fifth Floor – Visitors' Tearoom and Hospital Shop.
- Comic interludes at the Welcome Witch's desk: a young wizard with shoes magically eating his feet is directed to Spell Damage; an elderly wizard asks after a "Broderick Bode" on Ward 49, dismissed as "completely addled... thinks he's a teapot" (foreshadowing a later plot-relevant character); a father carries his daughter, who has sprouted large wings from her romper suit, up to the fourth floor.
- Arthur Weasley is found in the "Dangerous" Dai Llewellyn Ward: Serious Bites (First Floor, Creature-Induced Injuries), under Healer-in-Charge Hippocrates Smethwyck and Trainee Healer Augustus Pye (both named). A portrait of the ward's namesake, Urquhart Rackharrow (1612–1697, inventor of the Entrail-Expelling Curse), hangs on the wall.
- Tonks and Moody wait outside to let immediate family go in first (Moody stands guard, magical eye spinning); Mrs. Weasley insists Harry come in too, since "Arthur wants to thank you."
- Arthur, propped up and reading the Daily Prophet, is in good spirits despite his injury; explains the snake's venom contains an unusual poison that keeps wounds from closing (preventing bandage removal), requiring hourly Blood-Replenishing Potion until an antidote is found. Two other patients share the ward: a man bitten by a werewolf (incurable, two weeks from the next full moon, being counseled on managing the condition — Arthur mentions knowing a werewolf personally, i.e., Lupin, without naming him, and gets threatened with "another bite" for his trouble), and a woman (bed by the door) who won't disclose what bit her, implying she was handling something illegal.
- Arthur deflects questions about the circumstances of the attack, citing Ministry secrecy (the Prophet hasn't reported it, since the Ministry doesn't want public knowledge that "a dirty great serpent got" him — he catches himself before finishing the sentence under Molly's glare); he distracts the children with news from the Prophet about Willy Widdershins — the real culprit behind the "regurgitating toilets" prank campaign from earlier chapters — now caught again for selling "biting doorknobs" to Muggles (two of whom lost fingers and are now in St. Mungo's themselves for bone regrowth and memory modification), though he'd escaped the toilet charges, implying bribery.
- George directly asks if Arthur was guarding "the weapon" Voldemort is after — Mrs. Weasley sharply shuts this down. Fred asks if Harry's previously-mentioned giant snake sighting (from the graveyard, Goblet of Fire) is connected; Mrs. Weasley ends the visit, sending the children/Harry to wait outside so Tonks and Moody can visit privately.
- In the corridor, Fred and George produce a full set of five Extendable Ears (previously thought confiscated/banned) to eavesdrop on the adults' conversation through the ward door; Harry, initially reluctant, is persuaded to join in ("you saved Dad's life, if anyone's got the right to eavesdrop on him it's you").
- Via the Extendable Ears, they overhear critical information: the attacking snake was never found at the scene — it seemingly vanished instantly after striking; Moody theorizes Voldemort sent it purely as a reconnaissance "lookout," since his own plans ("he's not had any luck so far") are stalled, and that Arthur's presence cut the snake's exploration time short. Mrs. Weasley reveals, with unease, that Dumbledore seemed to already be expecting Harry to witness "something like this." Moody states flatly, "There's something funny about the Potter kid, we all know that," and reveals the most alarming theory yet: that Harry was "seeing things from inside You-Know-Who's snake" and that, if Voldemort is "possessing him," Harry himself may not even realize what that means. Harry yanks out the Ear in shock just as Moody's sentence is caught mid-thought by the others, who are all staring at him, equally afraid.

**Named characters (ch.22 additions):** Harry Potter, Ron Weasley, Fred & George Weasley, Ginny Weasley, Mrs. (Molly) Weasley, Sirius Black, Kreacher (absent), Tonks, Mad-Eye Moody, Arthur Weasley, Bill Weasley, Hippocrates Smethwyck (Healer-in-Charge), Augustus Pye (Trainee Healer), Urquhart Rackharrow (portrait, 1612-1697, inventor of the Entrail-Expelling Curse), Dilys Derwent (portrait, St. Mungo's Healer 1722-1741, Hogwarts Headmistress 1741-1768), Willy Widdershins (recurring minor criminal, revealed as the "regurgitating toilets" prankster, now caught selling biting doorknobs to Muggles), Broderick Bode (referenced, "completely addled... thinks he's a teapot," Ward 49 — a name worth flagging, since Bode appeared earlier as the sallow wizard sharing Mr. Weasley's lift in Chapter 7), Cedric Diggory (referenced, Harry's prior/earlier mention of Voldemort's snake from the graveyard).

**Named places/objects/spells/creatures:**
- St. Mungo's Hospital for Magical Maladies and Injuries, hidden behind "Purge and Dowse Ltd.," a fake Muggle department store.
- St. Mungo's five-floor directory (fully listed above).
- Dai Llewellyn Ward ("Dangerous... Serious Bites"), First Floor.
- Blood-Replenishing Potion.
- Extendable Ears (Fred and George's invention, used here for the first time explicitly against adult Order members).
- Witch Weekly (magazine, read by waiting patients).

**Specific numbers/quantities:**
- Mrs. Weasley returns with news at 5:10 a.m.
- Five Extendable Ears produced by Fred and George.
- Werewolf patient is two weeks from the next full moon.
- Dilys Derwent's dates: Healer 1722-1741, Headmistress 1741-1768. Rackharrow's dates: 1612-1697.

**Small/obscure details:**
- Broderick Bode's reappearance (now hospitalized and "addled," believing himself a teapot) is a significant plant for a later plot development (he previously appeared as Arthur's lift companion in Chapter 7, heading to the Department of Mysteries).
- The "biting doorknobs" sold to Muggles and the consequential memory modifications performed on injured Muggles is a darkly comic worldbuilding detail.
- Moody's blunt, chilling assessment ("there's something funny about the Potter kid") directly sets up Harry's ensuing crisis of identity in Chapter 23.

## Chapter 23: Christmas on the Closed Ward

**Plot events (in order):**
- Harry, shattered by what he overheard, spirals into fear that he may be possessed by Voldemort, recalling how Voldemort's face once literally emerged from the back of Quirrell's head (Philosopher's/Sorcerer's Stone) and wondering if the same could happen to him. He feels "contaminated" and unworthy to be near "clean" people.
- He connects this fear to Sirius's earlier remark (Chapter 5) that Voldemort seeks "a weapon... something he didn't have last time," concluding with dread that he himself might be that weapon — explaining (in his panicked logic) why he's guarded so heavily, and fearing Voldemort could be "listening to his thoughts" in real time.
- On the Underground and walking back to Grimmauld Place, Mrs. Weasley repeatedly checks on Harry's pallor, sending him to rest. Once alone, Harry resolves — in escalating, panicked logic — that he must remove himself from anyone he could endanger: first considering returning to Hogwarts alone over the holidays, then realizing other students would still be at risk, finally deciding he must return to the Dursleys at Privet Drive to isolate himself completely from the wizarding world. He begins packing his trunk to leave immediately.
- Phineas Nigellus's portrait (confirmed to be present in Harry and Ron's Grimmauld Place bedroom) interrupts him mid-escape, mocking his "noble" flight as more cowardly than brave, and delivers a terse message from Dumbledore: "Stay where you are" — with no further explanation. Harry, furious at being kept in the dark yet again, rages at Phineas; Phineas lectures him condescendingly about the arrogance of youth assuming they alone understand danger, refuses to confirm or deny whether Voldemort's plans involve Harry specifically, and exits his frame.
- Harry unpacks his trunk, physically and emotionally exhausted, and reluctantly obeys Dumbledore's instruction to stay, despite his fear of what might happen if he falls asleep again. He sleeps, dreaming again of the corridor and the locked black door (a recurring dream motif, distinct from the snake-vision), and is woken by Ron, who (Harry assumes, perhaps correctly) seems reluctant to be alone with him now, having overheard Moody's possession theory. Harry skips dinner, isolating himself further, and sleeps through much of the next day, waking to find Phineas Nigellus's portrait watching over him — Harry assumes Dumbledore has stationed Phineas specifically to monitor him in case he "attacks somebody else."
- The next morning, everyone else decorates the house for Christmas while Harry broods alone in the cold drawing room, nursing a bitter satisfaction that his isolation is likely fueling gossip about him; he ignores Mrs. Weasley's calls to come down. That evening, Hermione arrives unexpectedly (having abandoned her family skiing trip, telling her parents she needed to stay for O.W.L. revision, and telling Ron a white lie that skiing was "really good" to avoid his mockery) — she'd learned about Arthur's attack from Dumbledore the previous morning but had to wait for term to officially end before leaving school. She confirms Umbridge is furious that the group vanished from under her nose, though Dumbledore has provided official cover (permission to visit St. Mungo's).
- Hermione, Ron, and Ginny confront Harry together about his self-isolation; Ginny bluntly points out that she, having herself been possessed by Voldemort via Tom Riddle's diary in her first year (Chamber of Secrets), is uniquely positioned to reassure him — and provides the key diagnostic test: genuine possession causes significant memory blackouts (hours of lost time), which Harry has never experienced. Since Harry can account for all his time, Ginny concludes he has never actually been possessed. This brings Harry immense, immediate relief.
- Hermione further reassures him using Hogwarts: A History (her perennial reference), confirming nobody — not even Voldemort — can Apparate or Disapparate within Hogwarts's wards, so Harry could not have been magically transported to London and back; Ron confirms Harry never left his bed during the episode, merely thrashing in his sleep. Harry concludes with relief that he is "not the weapon after all."
- Christmas preparations resume in full swing: Sirius, thrilled to have a full house, is in exceptionally high spirits (singing carols, including a joke rendition of "God Rest Ye Merry, Hippogriffs" for Buckbeak), working tirelessly to transform Grimmauld Place — holly and streamers replace cobwebs on the chandeliers, magical snow covers the carpets, a live-fairy-decorated Christmas tree (obtained by Mundungus) conceals the Black family tapestry, and the mounted house-elf heads in the hall are given Father Christmas hats and beards.
- Christmas morning gift exchange (detailed list): Ron gives Harry a Broom Compass and an enormous box of Every-Flavor Beans; Hermione gives Harry a joke "nagging" diary/homework planner that scolds him about deadlines, and separately gives Ron a perfume (implying a prior gift mix-up/joke) and buys herself New Theory of Numerology; Sirius and Lupin jointly give Harry a set of books, Practical Defensive Magic and Its Use Against the Dark Arts, with moving illustrations — directly useful for his D.A. lesson planning; Hagrid sends a furry, fanged wallet (an "anti-theft device" that bites anyone (including Harry) trying to use it); Tonks gives a small working model Firebolt that flies around the room; Mr. and Mrs. Weasley give their traditional hand-knitted jumper plus mince pies; Dobby sends a crude painting of what's meant to be Harry (mocked by Fred and George as looking like "a gibbon with two black eyes"). Fred and George Apparate in to warn everyone to avoid the kitchen temporarily: Percy has returned his Weasley Christmas jumper with no note and hasn't asked after his injured father at all, reducing Mrs. Weasley to tears again (Lupin is deployed to comfort her).
- Hermione reveals she's bought Kreacher a Christmas present (a patchwork quilt, not clothes, since freeing him remains impossible/dangerous per Sirius's repeated warnings about what he knows of the Order) — the group discovers Kreacher's "bedroom" is actually a filthy nest under the kitchen boiler cupboard, filled with stolen trinkets, stale food, and salvaged family photographs Sirius had discarded — including, prominently and carefully mended with Spellotape, a photo of Bellatrix Lestrange (Sirius's cousin, Voldemort loyalist, imprisoned in Azkaban), apparently Kreacher's favorite. Kreacher himself is noted to be missing/unseen since the night they arrived (when Sirius ordered him "out" of the kitchen); Sirius assumes he's merely sulking somewhere in the house, joking darkly that he might have crawled into an airing cupboard and died (Hermione is visibly unamused). Harry points out (correctly, foreshadowing later plot significance) that house-elves CAN leave a house without being freed if they choose to (citing Dobby's self-punished departure from the Malfoys in Chamber of Secrets to warn Harry) — unsettling Sirius slightly.
- After Christmas lunch, the group (Weasleys, Harry, Hermione, escorted by Moody and Lupin) returns to St. Mungo's via a car "borrowed" by Mundungus (Charmed with the same Enlarging Spell as the old Weasley Ford Anglia, fitting ten people comfortably) since the Underground doesn't run on Christmas Day.
- St. Mungo's is festively decorated (red-and-gold illuminated orbs, holly, magical snow-covered Christmas trees). They find Arthur in good spirits but acting cagey; Mrs. Weasley grows suspicious upon noticing his bandages were changed a day early, and extracts a confession: Trainee Healer Augustus Pye has been experimenting with Muggle medical techniques — specifically "stitches" — on Arthur's wound as "complementary medicine," with poor results (the technique doesn't work well against the snake's magical venom). Mrs. Weasley is outraged; the younger members flee the ensuing argument under the pretext of getting tea.
- En route to the fifth-floor tearoom, portraits of historical Healers on the stairwell shout out absurd medieval diagnoses at passersby; one portrait pursues Ron through six other portraits insisting he has "spattergroit" (a skin affliction with a grotesque folk cure involving toad liver, eels' eyes, and standing naked under the full moon) — Ron is incensed, since the "blemishes" are just his freckles.
- On the fourth floor (Spell Damage), they unexpectedly encounter former Hogwarts Defense Against the Dark Arts professor Gilderoy Lockhart — now a long-term patient, his memory permanently and severely damaged (from the backfired Memory Charm he attempted on Harry and Ron at the end of Chamber of Secrets, inadvertently triggered by Ron's then-broken wand). Lockhart, oblivious to his own condition, remains cheerfully vain, immediately offering autographs and failing to recognize Harry and Ron as former students even when reminded, though he reflexively claims credit ("Taught you everything you know, I expect, did I?"). A motherly Healer, delighted that the perpetually visitor-less Lockhart finally has guests on Christmas Day, fusses over him affectionately.

**Named characters (ch.23 additions):** Harry Potter, Ron Weasley, Hermione Granger, Ginny Weasley, Phineas Nigellus, Dumbledore (referenced, message relayed), Sirius Black, Mrs. (Molly) Weasley, Mr. (Arthur) Weasley, Fred & George Weasley, Bill Weasley, Percy Weasley (returns his jumper, no contact with family), Lupin, Tonks (referenced via gift), Hagrid (referenced via gift), Dobby (referenced via gift), Kreacher (absent, "bedroom"/nest discovered), Bellatrix Lestrange (photo in Kreacher's nest), Mundungus Fletcher (provides the enlarged car), Mad-Eye Moody, Augustus Pye (Trainee Healer), Healer Hippocrates Smethwyck (referenced, Arthur asks after him), Gilderoy Lockhart (patient, memory permanently damaged), an unnamed motherly Healer (Lockhart's caretaker), Viktor Krum (implied, via Hermione's numerology book interest — not explicit here), Tom Riddle (referenced indirectly via Ginny's possession, Chamber of Secrets).

**Named places/objects/spells/creatures:**
- Grimmauld Place's Christmas transformation: holly/streamer-wrapped chandeliers, magical snow, a live-fairy Christmas tree (obscuring the Black family tapestry), Father-Christmas-hatted elf heads.
- Gifts (fully listed above): Broom Compass, Every-Flavor Beans, the nagging homework-diary, New Theory of Numerology, Practical Defensive Magic and Its Use Against the Dark Arts (illustrated book set from Sirius & Lupin), Hagrid's fanged anti-theft wallet, Tonks's model Firebolt, the Weasley jumper/mince pies tradition, Dobby's crude painting.
- Kreacher's hidden "nest" under the kitchen boiler: stolen trinkets, stale food, salvaged (Spellotape-repaired) family photographs, prominently featuring Bellatrix Lestrange.
- Mundungus's "borrowed," Enlarged car (parallel to the Weasleys' old Ford Anglia).
- St. Mungo's Christmas decor: red-and-gold crystal-orb "baubles," magical-snow Christmas trees.
- "Stitches" (Muggle medical technique, failed experiment on Arthur's snakebite wound).
- Spattergroit (a fictional skin affliction, grotesquely described by a portrait Healer).
- Gilderoy Lockhart's damaged memory (Chamber of Secrets callback).

**Specific numbers/quantities:**
- Percy's returned jumper arrives with no note.
- Ron pursued through "six more portraits" by the spattergroit-diagnosing Healer.
- St. Mungo's Christmas Day car trip: ten people fit in Mundungus's enlarged car.

**Small/obscure details:**
- Ginny's possession backstory (Chamber of Secrets) is used here as the direct, personal proof that resolves Harry's fear — a meaningful character callback.
- The detail that house-elves CAN leave a house without being set free (as Dobby proved) is explicitly re-raised by Harry and visibly unsettles Sirius — notable foreshadowing significance regarding Kreacher's eventual fate/actions later in the book.
- Lockhart's failure to recognize Ron and Harry, juxtaposed with his reflexive vanity, is a poignant/comic character beat revisiting a prior book's antagonist-turned-casualty.
- The Weasley family's visible class/financial strain (Percy's cold rejection; Arthur's modest circumstances; the old car's charm echoing the Ford Anglia) recurs as a persistent thematic thread.

**Chapter 23 continued — the Janus Thickey Ward and the Longbottom reveal:**
- The Healer leads them into the Janus Thickey Ward (password/charm: "Alohomora") — the long-term, permanent-spell-damage resident ward. Lockhart's bed is surrounded by self-portraits he's autographed to himself; he mentions a devoted fan, Gladys Gudgeon, who writes weekly, and credits his continued "fan mail" to his good looks.
- Other ward patients glimpsed: a sallow, mournful wizard mumbling blankly at the ceiling; a woman named Agnes whose entire head is covered in fur (she can only bark in response; Harry recalls Hermione's similar temporary affliction from the Polyjuice mishap in Chamber of Secrets) — her son is visiting that night; and "Broderick" (confirmed as Broderick Bode from Chapter 7/22) is seen regaining limited speech, though not in any recognizable language, and is given a potted plant with swaying tentacles and a hippogriff calendar.
- At the ward's far end, flowery curtains are drawn back from two beds as a formidable elderly witch (long green dress, moth-eaten fox fur stole, a pointed hat topped with a stuffed vulture) and a visibly mortified Neville Longbottom emerge — revealed to be Neville's grandmother, Augusta Longbottom (not named explicitly here, referred to as "Mrs. Longbottom").
- Mrs. Longbottom greets Harry, Ron, Ginny, and Hermione graciously by name (confirming she already knows them through Neville's stories) but bluntly notes Neville "hasn't got his father's talent" — prompting Ron to innocently ask if Neville's father is one of the two patients in the end beds, which forces Mrs. Longbottom to realize Neville has never told his friends about his parents.
- Mrs. Longbottom reveals, with blunt pride rather than shame, that her son and daughter-in-law (Frank and Alice Longbottom, former Aurors, "very well respected" and "highly gifted") were tortured into permanent insanity by Voldemort's followers, scolding Neville for seeming ashamed rather than proud of them.
- Neville's mother, Alice Longbottom, approaches in her nightdress — her once plump, happy face (as seen in Moody's old Order photograph, Chapter 9) now thin, worn, prematurely white-haired — and wordlessly presses an empty Drooble's Blowing Gum wrapper into Neville's hand, a small, heartbreaking ritual Neville endures with quiet grace ("Thanks, Mum"), visibly fighting back humiliation in front of his friends; Neville is seen secretly pocketing the wrapper (rather than discarding it as his grandmother instructs), suggesting he treasures these small tokens.
- After the Longbottoms leave, Hermione, Ron, and Ginny are all shaken, having never known about Neville's parents; Harry reveals he already knew (sworn to secrecy by Dumbledore) and confirms the attacker was specifically Bellatrix Lestrange — using the Cruciatus Curse until the Longbottoms lost their minds — directly tying back to the photo Kreacher treasures in his nest (Chapter 23 earlier) and the Black family tapestry reveal (Chapter 6).

**Named characters (ch.23 further additions):** Gilderoy Lockhart, Gladys Gudgeon (referenced, fan), Agnes (furred patient), Broderick Bode, Augusta Longbottom (Neville's grandmother, named via context as "Mrs. Longbottom"), Frank Longbottom and Alice Longbottom (Neville's parents, confirmed tortured into insanity by Bellatrix Lestrange specifically), Neville Longbottom, Bellatrix Lestrange (confirmed as the torturer), Harry, Ron, Hermione, Ginny.

**Named places/objects:**
- The Janus Thickey Ward (permanent spell-damage ward, St. Mungo's).
- Drooble's Blowing Gum (brand, the wrapper Alice gives Neville).
- Gladys Gudgeon's fan letters (referenced).

**Small/obscure details:**
- Neville secretly keeping the gum wrapper (rather than discarding it as told) is an understated, poignant detail.
- Agnes's fur-covered head is an explicit callback to Hermione's cat-hair mishap in Chamber of Secrets (Polyjuice Potion gone wrong).
- Mrs. Longbottom's vulture-topped hat and fox-fur stole are a vivid, specific character detail.

## Chapter 24: Occlumency

**Plot events (in order):**
- Back at Grimmauld Place: Kreacher has reappeared (Sirius assumes he was just hiding/sulking somewhere, hoarding more Black family relics); he's docile but Harry notices him staring oddly/avidly at him before looking away — a detail Harry doesn't share with Sirius, deepening his vague unease.
- As the holidays end, Sirius grows increasingly sullen and withdrawn ("fits of the sullens," per Mrs. Weasley), isolating himself in Buckbeak's room; Harry, for the first time ever, dreads returning to Hogwarts (Umbridge's tyranny, the Quidditch ban, mounting homework, Dumbledore's continued distance) and even considers asking to stay at Grimmauld Place permanently if not for missing the D.A.
- On the last day of the holidays, Snape arrives at Grimmauld Place (on Dumbledore's orders) to inform Harry, in front of a hostile Sirius, that he must take private weekly Occlumency lessons from Snape himself — to be publicly disguised as "Remedial Potions" and kept secret even from Umbridge. Occlumency is defined as "the magical defense of the mind against external penetration."
- Snape and Sirius immediately clash: Snape needles Sirius about his uselessness to the Order while confined to the house; Sirius, in turn, mocks Snape as Lucius Malfoy's "lapdog" and brings up Snape's old nickname "Snivellus"; Snape retaliates by revealing that Lucius Malfoy did recognize Sirius's dog disguise at King's Cross (confirming the trio's earlier fear from Chapter 10/14) and mocks Sirius for being too cowardly to leave his "hidey-hole." The confrontation nearly escalates into a wand duel, broken up only by Harry physically intervening and the sudden arrival of the newly "Cured!" Arthur Weasley and the rest of the family (Healer Smethwyck found an antidote to the snake venom; Arthur has learned his lesson about experimenting with Muggle "stitches").
- Snape explains the stakes of Occlumency to Harry in a private first lesson (after returning to Hogwarts): Voldemort is a master of Legilimency — the ability to extract feelings/memories from another's mind (explicitly distinguished from crude Muggle "mind reading" — minds are "complex and many-layered," not books to be read outright) — and can usually detect lies unless the liar has mastered Occlumency to suppress contradictory memories/emotions. Snape clarifies physical proximity/eye contact matters for Legilimency, and Hogwarts's ancient wards provide some protection, but Harry's unique scar-connection to Voldemort bypasses normal limits: during moments of mental vulnerability (e.g., sleep), Harry has been unknowingly sharing Voldemort's thoughts/emotions.
- Crucially, Snape reveals that Voldemort was previously unaware of this connection but became aware of it specifically because of the intensity of Harry's vision of the Arthur Weasley snake attack — meaning the connection may now work in both directions, and Voldemort might attempt to implant thoughts or exert influence on Harry. Harry realizes (and explicitly figures out, to his own shock and Snape's visible unease) that he witnessed the attack through the snake's perspective because Voldemort himself was possessing the snake at that exact moment, meaning Harry tapped into Voldemort's consciousness indirectly via the snake.
- The actual Occlumency practice: Snape empties some of his own memories into the Pensieve (to keep them private from Harry during the exercise) and repeatedly casts "Legilimens" on Harry, forcing him to try to resist mental invasion. Harry's defenses fail badly in early attempts, with involuntary flashes of deeply personal/embarrassing or painful memories surfacing uncontrollably: age five watching Dudley's new bike with jealousy; age nine being chased up a tree by Aunt Marge's dog Ripper; the Sorting Hat considering Slytherin for him; Hermione's cursed fur-covered face (Chamber of Secrets); a hundred Dementors by the lake (Prisoner of Azkaban); and Cho Chang approaching him under the mistletoe (which Harry consciously, if futilely, tries to mentally block as "private").
- Harry accidentally produces a Stinging Hex on Snape's wrist during one defensive flail; Snape criticizes this as losing control rather than proper resistance, instructing Harry to "repel with your brain," not his wand, and to "clear your mind" of emotion — advice Harry finds impossible given his anger at Snape in the moment.
- Subsequent attempts surface a great black dragon (Goblet of Fire, Triwizard first task), his parents waving from the Mirror of Erised (Philosopher's/Sorcerer's Stone), and devastatingly, Cedric Diggory's dead body in the graveyard — Harry breaks down screaming "NOOOOOOO," collapsing to his knees; Snape (visibly paler and angrier than usual, though not as furious as Harry) scolds him for "allowing access to memories you fear, handing me weapons," framing emotional vulnerability as a fatal weakness against Voldemort specifically ("weak people... stand no chance against his powers").
- On a subsequent attempt, Harry has a breakthrough: amid flashing images (Uncle Vernon nailing the mail slot shut, Dementors over the lake, running with Mr. Weasley through a corridor), he suddenly recognizes the recurring windowless-corridor-and-locked-door dream as a real place — specifically the corridor leading to the Department of Mysteries at the Ministry of Magic, the same route he and Arthur Weasley took en route to his disciplinary hearing on 12 August, and the exact location where Arthur was later attacked by the snake while on guard duty.
- Harry directly asks Snape what's in the Department of Mysteries; Snape is visibly unnerved by the question, deflects ("many things... none of which concern you"), refuses to elaborate, and ends the lesson, scheduling the next for Wednesday. He orders Harry to empty his mind of emotion nightly before sleep as homework and warns he'll "know" if Harry skips practicing.
- Harry reports his deduction to Ron and Hermione in the library: the mysterious "weapon" Voldemort seeks (per Sirius's Chapter 5 hint) must be located within the Department of Mysteries. Hermione connects this to Sturgis Podmore's earlier arrest (Chapter 14) for trying to force his way through a "top-security door" at the Ministry at 1 a.m. — too much of a coincidence not to be the same door, though she can't explain why an Order member would be trying to break in rather than guard it. Ron notes Department of Mysteries staff are called "Unspeakables" since nobody knows what they actually do there; Hermione speculates it's "something top secret the Ministry has been developing."
- That night in the common room, Fred and George debut a new joke-shop product, "Headless Hats" (2 Galleons each) — an Invisibility Charm extended beyond the charmed object's boundary, causing the wearer's entire head to vanish when the hat is donned; Hermione is intellectually fascinated by the magical theory (extended invisibility fields) despite the chaos. Harry, now suffering a severe scar-ache and feeling physically ill (worse than after the earlier snake vision), retreats toward the dormitory, mockingly scolded along the way by his new enchanted homework planner (a running gag item from Hermione's Christmas gift).
- Entering the dormitory, Harry is struck by a second, even more intense psychic episode: total disorientation, loss of self, maniacal laughter, overwhelming euphoria — "jubilant, ecstatic, triumphant," as if "a wonderful, wonderful thing had happened" — before Ron slaps/shakes him back to consciousness, revealing the manic laughter had been coming from Harry's own mouth. Harry, shaking and nauseated (echoing the earlier snake-attack episode), reports that Voldemort is extremely happy about "something he's been hoping for," though he doesn't yet know what. Ron reveals Hermione sent him to check on Harry, theorizing his Occlumency lessons have temporarily weakened his mental defenses, making him more susceptible to these episodes. The chapter ends with Harry fearing his first Occlumency lesson may have made things worse rather than better, and wondering with dread what could have made Voldemort "the happiest he had been in fourteen years."

**Named characters (ch.24 additions):** Harry Potter, Sirius Black, Severus Snape, Ron Weasley, Hermione Granger, Mrs./Mr. Weasley (Arthur, "Cured!"), Kreacher, Lucius Malfoy (referenced), Dumbledore (referenced, orders the lessons), Aunt Marge and her dog Ripper (memory flash), Dudley Dursley (memory flash), Cedric Diggory (memory flash — his death), Cho Chang (memory flash, mistletoe moment), James and Lily Potter (Mirror of Erised memory flash), Hermione (furred-face memory flash, Chamber of Secrets callback), Zacharias Smith (mocks Harry's "Remedial Potions" cover story), Fred & George Weasley (Headless Hats), Stan Shunpike (Knight Bus conductor), Madam Marsh (unwell Knight Bus passenger), Lupin and Tonks (escort the group to Hogwarts via Knight Bus).

**Named places/objects/spells/creatures:**
- Occlumency and Legilimency (both fully defined/explained for the first time).
- The Department of Mysteries (identified as the location of the windowless-corridor dream and, implicitly, Voldemort's "weapon").
- The Pensieve (Snape's use of it to store his own memories during Harry's lessons).
- The Knight Bus (detailed depiction: purple triple-decker, mismatched chairs, abrupt "BANG" teleportation stops, conductor Stan Shunpike).
- Headless Hats (Weasley twins' new product, 2 Galleons each).
- Hermione's enchanted nagging homework planner (recurring comic prop).
- "Remedial Potions" (Harry's cover story for the Occlumency lessons).
- Occlumency practice instructions: clear the mind of emotion nightly before sleep.

**Specific numbers/quantities:**
- Occlumency lessons scheduled weekly, specifically Monday evenings at 6 p.m. in Snape's office (later moved to include Wednesdays as seen).
- Eleven Sickles each paid by Harry and Ron for the Knight Bus fare.
- Snape has been teaching at Hogwarts "fourteen years" (restated, consistent with Chapter 17).
- Voldemort is described as "the happiest he had been in fourteen years" — matching the length of his downfall/weakened state since Harry's infancy.

**Small/obscure details:**
- The Sorting Hat's memory flash reveals it once considered Slytherin for Harry — a Chamber of Secrets/Philosopher's Stone callback made explicit here.
- Snape unconsciously rubs his left forearm (where the Dark Mark is branded) when discussing fear of saying Voldemort's name — a subtle, telling physical tic.
- Cho's reaction and the mistletoe kiss resurface as one of the private memories Harry instinctively tries hardest to shield from Snape.
- Harry privately resolves never to use Sirius's mysterious gift (a "badly wrapped package roughly the size of a paperback book," meant to summon Sirius if Snape mistreats him) — setting up unused/foreshadowed significance for later.
