## Chapter 25: The Beetle at Bay

**Plot events so far (in progress):**
- The Daily Prophet reports a mass breakout from Azkaban: ten high-security prisoners escaped overnight, confirmed by Minister Fudge, who has already alerted the Muggle Prime Minister. Fudge publicly links this breakout to Sirius Black's escape two and a half years earlier, theorizing Sirius (the first person ever to break out of Azkaban) may have helped orchestrate this one as a "rallying point" for old Death Eaters — implying outside assistance was involved.
- The front page displays ten mugshots (nine wizards, one witch) with names and crimes: Antonin Dolohov (convicted of brutally murdering Gideon and Fabian Prewett — Molly Weasley's brothers, per Chapter 9's Order photo); Augustus Rookwood (convicted of leaking Ministry secrets to Voldemort); and most significantly, Bellatrix Lestrange (confirmed by caption: convicted of the torture and permanent incapacitation of Frank and Alice Longbottom) — matching exactly what Harry revealed to his friends in the previous chapter.

**Named characters so far:** Harry Potter, Ron Weasley, Hermione Granger, Cornelius Fudge, Sirius Black (referenced/blamed), Antonin Dolohov, Augustus Rookwood, Bellatrix Lestrange, Gideon and Fabian Prewett (referenced victims), Frank and Alice Longbottom (referenced victims).

**Named places/objects:**
- Azkaban (mass breakout location).
- The Daily Prophet's front-page mugshot spread.

**Specific numbers/quantities:**
- Ten high-security prisoners escape Azkaban.
- The breakout occurs "two and a half years" after Sirius's original escape (Prisoner of Azkaban timeline marker).

**Chapter 25 continued — full plot events:**
- Fudge's official Prophet statement (paraphrased) directly accuses Sirius of rallying the escaped Death Eaters as their "leader," explicitly naming Bellatrix Lestrange as his cousin among the escapees, and urges the public not to approach any of them.
- Hermione bitterly notes Fudge has no politically safer alternative explanation (he can't admit Dumbledore was right that Azkaban's guards defected to Voldemort), having spent six months branding Harry and Dumbledore liars.
- A second, smaller Prophet story (page 10) reports the bizarre death of Ministry worker Broderick Bode (age 49, confirmed from Chapter 22/23): found strangled in his St. Mungo's bed by a potted plant, which turns out to have been a disguised cutting of Devil's Snare (not the harmless Flitterbloom the hospital believed it to be) rather than a genuine gift; Healer Miriam Strout (in charge of his ward) is suspended on full pay pending inquiry. Harry, Ron, and Hermione realize with horror that they personally saw this exact plant delivered as a "Christmas present" during their St. Mungo's visit and failed to recognize it as Devil's Snare (a plant they'd previously encountered and escaped in Philosopher's/Sorcerer's Stone) — Hermione concludes this was deliberate murder, disguised as an accident, precisely because an anonymous poisoned gift is nearly impossible to trace. Harry connects Bode to the sallow-faced wizard ("Bode") who shared the lift with him and Arthur Weasley to the Ministry's Atrium level on the day of his August hearing (Chapter 7) — Ron confirms Bode was a Department of Mysteries "Unspeakable." Hermione abruptly rushes off to send an urgent letter, refusing to explain why ("I'm the only one who can").
- Outside the Great Hall, Harry and Ron encounter Hagrid, still heavily bruised (plus a fresh cut across his nose) — he deflects questions about his condition and reveals, almost as an aside, that he is now officially on probation following Umbridge's thestral-lesson inspection (confirmed as expected, given how poorly the inspection visibly went).
- Over the following days: Hagrid's probation becomes common knowledge, met with more glee (especially from Malfoy) than concern; the Bode murder registers with almost nobody except the trio; the dominant topic of school gossip becomes the ten escaped Death Eaters, with wild rumors (sightings in Hogsmeade, hiding in the Shrieking Shack, planning a Hogwarts break-in like Sirius's in Prisoner of Azkaban).
- Students from established wizarding families grow up fearing these Death Eaters' names nearly as much as Voldemort's; some, like Susan Bones (who lost an uncle, aunt, and cousins to one of the ten), become uncomfortable minor celebrities of grief, with Susan telling Harry during Herbology that she now understands "what it feels like to be" him.
- Harry notices a subtle shift in how he's gossiped about — now curious rather than purely hostile, as growing numbers of students quietly doubt the Prophet's account of the Azkaban breakout and begin leaning toward Harry/Dumbledore's version of events (that Voldemort is back).
- Teachers are now visibly guarded in the corridors, breaking off hushed conversations whenever students approach (since the staffroom itself is compromised by Umbridge's presence).
- A new sign appears: Educational Decree Number Twenty-six, explicitly banning teachers from giving students any information unrelated to their taught subject — widely mocked by students (Lee Jordan cleverly points out it technically bars Umbridge from disciplining Fred and George for playing Exploding Snap in her class, since that's not DADA-related; Lee is later seen with a badly bleeding hand from a subsequent detention/punishment, treated with murtlap essence).
- Rather than being chastened by the Azkaban breakout happening on her watch, Umbridge intensifies her campaign of control, now personally attending every single Divination and Care of Magical Creatures lesson with her clipboard — grilling Trelawney with increasingly obscure divination-method questions (Ornithomancy, Heptomancy) and demanding on-the-spot predictions, visibly destabilizing her (muttering to herself in corridors, smelling of cooking sherry); meanwhile Hagrid, despite following Hermione's advice to teach safe material (crups — Jack-Russell-like dogs with forked tails), has grown uncharacteristically nervous, distracted, and error-prone in lessons, and has started forbidding Harry, Ron, and Hermione from visiting him after dark, fearing further risk to his job.
- With Hagrid's cabin, Sirius's letters, his Firebolt, and Quidditch all effectively taken from him by Umbridge's escalating control, Harry channels his frustration entirely into intensifying the D.A.; Neville in particular is transformed by the news of Bellatrix's escape, becoming grimly, relentlessly focused during meetings (never discussing his parents or Bellatrix directly) and improving dramatically — second only to Hermione in mastering the newly-taught Shield Charm (a jinx-deflecting defensive spell).
- Harry's own Occlumency lessons, by contrast, are worsening rather than improving: his scar now aches almost constantly (not just occasionally as before), and he experiences frequent small emotional "lurches" (unexplained bursts of annoyance or cheerfulness) that he now recognizes as leaked fragments of Voldemort's own moods — he privately dates this heightened sensitivity to the start of his Occlumency training itself. He also now dreams nightly of walking the Department of Mysteries corridor toward the locked black door, always stopping just short of opening it.
- Hermione suggests (optimistically) that the intensifying symptoms might be like an illness getting worse before improving; Ron, more darkly, voices suspicion that Snape might not be trying to help at all — possibly deliberately widening the connection to make it easier for Voldemort to access Harry's mind, given Snape's past as a Death Eater and the lack of concrete proof he ever really changed sides. Hermione shuts this down firmly, trusting Dumbledore's judgment absolutely ("if we can't trust Dumbledore, we can't trust anyone").
- Time skip to February: the second Hogsmeade weekend falls on Valentine's Day, and Harry's much-anticipated date with Cho goes disastrously. Hermione, receiving an urgent-seeming letter from an unfamiliar owl, asks Harry to meet her (and optionally Cho) at the Three Broomsticks at midday — she won't explain why yet, just that "it's really important." Ron can't attend (mandatory full-day Quidditch practice with Angelina, who refuses to let him resign despite his and the team's — Sloper and Kirke's — poor form).
- Harry and Cho's date: initially pleasant, bonding over shared Quidditch memories (their first match against each other, Oliver Wood's advice not to be chivalrous, meeting at the Quidditch World Cup campsite) until Pansy Parkinson and a gang of Slytherin girls mock them ("Potter and Chang!... at least Diggory was good-looking!"), souring the mood. They wander Hogsmeade past a Ministry poster offering a 1,000-Galleon reward for information on any of the ten escaped Death Eaters; Cho notes the conspicuous absence of dementors searching for them this time (unlike Sirius's manhunt in Prisoner of Azkaban) — Harry privately registers this as further proof the dementors have abandoned Ministry control entirely.
- They take shelter from rain in Madam Puddifoot's Tea Shop (frilly, cramped, confetti-throwing cherub decorations Harry finds tacky/Umbridge-office-like); the date grows awkward as Harry notices every other table is a kissing couple (including Ravenclaw Quidditch Captain Roger Davies and his girlfriend), and he agonizes internally over whether to hold Cho's hand. Cho reveals, apropos of nothing, that Roger Davies asked her out and she turned him down — Harry misreads the significance. Harry mentions his planned meeting with Hermione; Cho reacts coldly. Cho then reveals she used to come to this exact tea shop with Cedric, and asks — tearfully — whether Cedric ever mentioned her before he died; Harry (truthfully) says there wasn't time, then tries to deflect to Quidditch, which backfires badly: Cho, devastated, accuses him of being willing to talk about Cedric's death with Hermione but not with her, storms out crying in the rain, ending the date.
- Harry, baffled and frustrated ("Women!"), heads early to the Three Broomsticks, first stopping to check on a visibly battered, morose, and heavily drinking Hagrid (two fresh cuts, "same boat... both orphans" maudlin monologue about the importance of having "decent" family, pointedly deflecting all questions about his ongoing unexplained injuries as "normal bumps and bruises... I got a rough job") before finding Hermione's table.
- The chapter's major reveal: Hermione has arranged a meeting with Rita Skeeter (the disgraced ex-Prophet journalist, now unemployed and visibly decayed — unkempt hair, chipped nail polish, missing glasses jewels) and Luna Lovegood. Hermione is blackmailing Rita (threatening to expose her status as an unregistered Animagus — the secret from Goblet of Fire that allowed Rita to spy as a beetle) into writing and publishing Harry's full, true account of Voldemort's return — specifically in The Quibbler (Luna's father's magazine), since the Daily Prophet (per Rita, confirmed to be under direct pressure from Fudge) won't print anything favorable to Harry's credibility, as it goes against the "public mood" that doesn't want to believe Voldemort is back. Hermione's strategic logic: with gaps already appearing in the official Azkaban breakout story, an alternative account — even in an "unusual" outlet like The Quibbler — may find real traction. Rita is further forced to accept writing for free (Quibbler contributors aren't paid; Luna notes it's considered "an honor"), under continued threat of exposure. The chapter ends with Rita reluctantly readying her Quick-Quotes Quill to interview Harry on the full truth.

**Named characters (ch.25 additions):** Harry Potter, Ron Weasley, Hermione Granger, Cornelius Fudge, Broderick Bode (confirmed dead, murdered via disguised Devil's Snare), Healer Miriam Strout (suspended), Hagrid (on probation, visibly battered, cryptic about his injuries), Susan Bones, Professor Trelawney (destabilized by Umbridge's inspections), Neville Longbottom (intensified focus post-Bellatrix's escape), Draco Malfoy (gleeful about Hagrid's probation), Lee Jordan (injured hand from Umbridge punishment), Cho Chang, Marietta Edgecombe (implied, Cho's friend), Pansy Parkinson, Roger Davies (Ravenclaw Quidditch Captain) and his unnamed girlfriend, Madam Puddifoot (tea shop owner), Rita Skeeter (reappears, unemployed, blackmailed by Hermione), Luna Lovegood, Xenophilius Lovegood (referenced as Quibbler editor, "Daddy"), Oliver Wood (referenced, now playing for Puddlemere United — contradicting/clarifying earlier "Pride of Portree" rumor), Bellatrix Lestrange (escaped convict, referenced), Antonin Dolohov and Augustus Rookwood (named escapees).

**Named places/objects/spells/creatures:**
- Devil's Snare (the disguised murder weapon, Philosopher's/Sorcerer's Stone callback).
- Educational Decree Number Twenty-six (bars teachers from discussing anything outside their subject).
- Ornithomancy and Heptomancy (obscure divination methods Umbridge quizzes Trelawney on).
- Crups (Jack-Russell-like dogs with forked tails, Hagrid's "safe" teaching choice).
- The Shield Charm (new D.A. spell, mastered fastest by Hermione then Neville).
- Madam Puddifoot's Tea Shop, Hogsmeade (frilly décor, confetti-throwing cherubs).
- The Ministry's 1,000-Galleon reward poster for the ten escaped Death Eaters.
- Rita Skeeter's Quick-Quotes Quill and crocodile-skin handbag (Goblet of Fire callbacks).
- The Quibbler (confirmed as the publication venue for Harry's true account).
- Rita's secret status as an unregistered Animagus (Goblet of Fire reveal, used as blackmail leverage here).

**Specific numbers/quantities:**
- Ten high-security Azkaban escapees (restated).
- Broderick Bode was 49 years old.
- 1,000-Galleon reward offered per escaped Death Eater.
- The Azkaban breakout occurs "two and a half years" after Sirius's escape (restated).

**Small/obscure details:**
- Lee Jordan's clever use of Decree 26 against Umbridge (Exploding Snap isn't DADA-related) is a sharp, specific bit of student resistance.
- Hagrid's uncharacteristically maudlin, drunken "both orphans... blood's important" monologue is a notable, specific emotional beat distinct from his usual cheerful demeanor.
- Cho's tea-shop breakdown and the detailed misunderstanding (Hermione's later explanation of Cho's jealousy test) is a thorough, specific piece of relationship trivia.
- The final Quidditch match before this point: Gryffindor loses to Hufflepuff 240-230 despite Ron's dismal performance (14 failed saves) and Sloper/Kirke's incompetence, saved only by Ginny Weasley (new Seeker) catching the Snitch off a lucky sneeze from Hufflepuff Seeker Summerby.

## Chapter 26: Seen and Unforeseen

**Plot events so far (in progress):**
- Luna mentions her father intends to run Harry's interview, but it may be delayed behind a "very important story" about Crumple-Horned Snorkack sightings.
- Harry finds the Rita Skeeter interview process difficult but cathartic — he gives her every detail he can recall about Voldemort's return, driven by a "burning desire to do something" after Bellatrix's escape, despite expecting it will just reinforce most people's view that he's insane (especially given the surrounding Quibbler content).
- At dinner, reactions trickle in: Dean is awed anticipating Umbridge's reaction to Harry "going public"; Neville earnestly validates Harry's choice, noting people need to know what Voldemort and his Death Eaters are capable of, and awkwardly implies he understands what it's like to lose composure discussing hard truths (alluding to, without directly mentioning, his parents) — Seamus listens in silently without directly engaging.
- Cho enters with Marietta, pointedly not looking toward the Gryffindor table; Hermione, having apparently withheld the earlier context from Ron, now asks Harry to recount the Valentine's date disaster — Harry recounts it in full (Madam Puddifoot's, the tea-shop meltdown); Hermione offers her analysis: Cho was testing how much Harry liked her via jealousy, since Harry's mention of meeting Hermione mid-date upset her, and this was compounded by Harry's clumsy handling of the Cedric conversation — Hermione lays out a detailed script for how Harry should have phrased things instead, drawing a baffled, frustrated response from both Harry and Ron ("you should write a book translating mad things girls do").
- Quidditch update: Gryffindor's practice (without Fred/George present, confirmed departed from the team following their ban) is going badly per Ron and Ginny, who return muddy and disgruntled; Fred and George (now spectating rather than playing) report the team "will be slaughtered" without them, though George credits Ginny's natural skill — revealed by Hermione to stem from secretly "borrowing" her brothers' brooms from the shed since age six, unbeknownst to them. Fred confirms the Weasley twins' Skiving Snackboxes product line is now fully functional (the boils problem solved via murtlap essence, per a tip from Lee Jordan) and reiterates their indifference to N.E.W.T.s.
- The actual Gryffindor vs. Hufflepuff match (the following Saturday) is a fiasco: lasting only 22 minutes, marked by Ron's fourteenth failed save, Beater Jack Sloper accidentally hitting Angelina in the mouth with his own bat while missing a Bludger, and Beater/Keeper candidate Andrew Kirke falling off his broom in fright as Hufflepuff's Zacharias Smith bears down on him. Gryffindor loses by only 10 points (240-230) purely because Ginny Weasley snatches the Snitch from Hufflepuff Seeker Summerby (who sneezed and blinked at the critical moment) — confirming Ginny as a genuinely skilled Seeker despite the team's otherwise dismal performance. Slytherin becomes the new favorite to win the Quidditch Cup.
- Ginny reveals to Harry that his Quidditch ban is tied specifically to Umbridge's tenure at Hogwarts, not literally lifelong as stated by the decree's wording — and that she personally intends to try out for Chaser once he's reinstated (since Angelina and Alicia are both graduating, and she prefers scoring goals to Seeking). Fred and George, now too fond of Ron to mock him directly, privately joke about his failures only "for parties."
- That night, Harry dreams again of the Department of Mysteries corridor and the black door — this time finding it slightly ajar with a strip of blue light visible — but is woken just before opening it by Ron's genuine snore, leaving him frustrated and guiltily aware he "shouldn't have" been seeing the door at all (per Occlumency goals), yet consumed by curiosity about what lies behind it.

**Named characters so far:** Harry Potter, Ron Weasley, Hermione Granger, Ginny Weasley, Fred & George Weasley, Dean Thomas, Seamus Finnigan, Neville Longbottom, Cho Chang, Marietta Edgecombe, Luna Lovegood (referenced, Quibbler), Professor Umbridge (referenced, gloating at the match), Zacharias Smith, Andrew Kirke, Jack Sloper, Angelina Johnson, Summerby (Hufflepuff Seeker).

**Named places/objects/spells/creatures:**
- The Gryffindor vs. Hufflepuff Quidditch match (final score 240-230, Hufflepuff wins despite Ginny's Snitch catch).
- The Department of Mysteries corridor dream (now shows the black door ajar with blue light beyond — new detail).
- Skiving Snackboxes (confirmed fully perfected, boils cured via murtlap essence per Lee Jordan's tip).

**Specific numbers/quantities:**
- Final Quidditch score: Hufflepuff 240, Gryffindor 230.
- Ron's fourteenth failed save during the match.
- Ginny has been secretly "borrowing" her brothers' brooms since age six.
- The match lasts only 22 minutes.

**Chapter 26 continued — full plot events:**
- The next morning, Harry is inundated with an avalanche of owls — a dozen or more — delivering reader mail in response to his published Quibbler interview (titled "HARRY POTTER SPEAKS OUT AT LAST: THE TRUTH ABOUT HE-WHO-MUST-NOT-BE-NAMED AND THE NIGHT I SAW HIM RETURN"). Luna reveals she personally requested her father send Harry a free copy; reactions are decidedly mixed — some call him mad or recommend St. Mungo's Shock Spell treatment, but a growing number of letters (including one from a witch in Paisley) say they now believe him, and one includes a photo from a reader who calls him a "real hero."
- Umbridge discovers the letter avalanche and interrogates Harry; he openly admits he gave an interview about "what happened to me last June" and hands her the magazine. She reacts with fury, banning Harry from all future Hogsmeade trips and docking 50 points from Gryffindor plus another week of detentions for "lying."
- By mid-morning, a new sign appears: Educational Decree Number Twenty-seven, banning possession of The Quibbler on pain of expulsion. Hermione is delighted, correctly predicting that banning the article guarantees everyone in school will read it out of curiosity — within a day, this proves true: the whole school is discussing it despite nobody being seen holding a physical copy (students have magically disguised the pages to look like textbook extracts or rendered them blank until wanted).
- Teachers, barred by Decree 26 from directly discussing the interview, express support through loopholes: Professor Sprout awards Gryffindor 20 points for an unrelated small favor; Flitwick gives Harry squeaking sugar mice with a conspiratorial "Shh!"; Trelawney (not yet sacked) has a dramatic emotional outburst in Divination, prophesying — in front of a visibly disapproving Umbridge — that Harry will NOT die young but will become Minister of Magic and father twelve children.
- Cho reconciles with Harry, praising the interview as brave (it made her cry) and kissing him on the cheek; Seamus Finnigan publicly apologizes to Harry, confirms he now believes him, and has sent a copy of the magazine to his mother; Malfoy, Crabbe, Goyle, and a previously-unmentioned Slytherin named Theodore Nott are spotted conspiring and glowering at Harry in the library — since he named their fathers as Death Eaters in the interview, and they can't publicly contest it without admitting to having read a banned magazine. Luna reports the issue became The Quibbler's fastest-selling ever, prompting a reprint.
- That night, Fred and George triumphantly display an Enlargement-Charmed, talking cutout of the Quibbler cover in the Gryffindor common room (Harry's giant head intermittently shouting slogans like "The Ministry are morons" and "Eat dung, Umbridge") — initially a hit, though the charm degrades over the evening into random word-shouting and gives Harry (whose scar is already prickling) a headache; he retires early.
- That night, Harry has his most detailed and important Voldemort-POV dream yet: he is Voldemort himself, in a dark, candlelit room, addressing a cowering Death Eater named Rookwood (one of the ten Azkaban escapees, confirmed to have formerly worked in the Department of Mysteries). Rookwood reveals that Bode (the murdered Ministry worker) could never have successfully stolen "it" (the weapon) even under Avery's Imperius Curse instructions, because Bode would have recognized he was incapable of removing it — implying only certain people/methods can safely extract the weapon. Voldemort expresses renewed hope ("We begin again, from now") and thanks Rookwood, then summons a Death Eater named Avery (implicitly to be punished for the failed Bode operation). Left alone, "Harry" (as Voldemort) looks into a cracked mirror and sees Voldemort's own skull-white, red-eyed, slit-pupiled face reflected — Harry wakes screaming, pulled from his hangings by Ron.
- Harry recounts the dream to Ron, revealing he again experienced it fully as Voldemort (not merely watching), and that a Death Eater named Avery is currently being punished for providing bad information, while Rookwood has put Voldemort "back on the right track." Harry refuses Ron's urging to tell anyone, reasoning that Occlumency is specifically supposed to prevent exactly this kind of vision, so reporting it would be an admission of his own continued failure at the discipline Dumbledore wants him to master.
- The next day, Harry and Ron share the dream with Hermione in a secure courtyard corner; she deduces the full picture: Bode was placed under the Imperius Curse (by Lucius Malfoy, who is "never out of the Ministry") specifically to steal "the weapon" from the Department of Mysteries, but some kind of defensive enchantment on/around the weapon backfired catastrophically on him, scrambling his mind and voice — explaining his hospitalization. When he began recovering his speech (and thus might expose the plot), he was murdered via the disguised Devil's Snare specifically to silence him before the Imperius Curse could fully lift and he could explain what happened.
- Harry connects this to Chapter 7: Bode was in the Department of Mysteries corridor the same day as Harry's hearing, which Mr. Weasley assumed meant Lucius Malfoy was simply trying to overhear the hearing's outcome — Hermione realizes this timeline fits Sturgis Podmore's earlier arrest too (Chapter 14): Podmore, invisible under Moody's borrowed Invisibility Cloak, was likely standing guard at that exact Department of Mysteries door when Malfoy either detected him or placed an opportunistic Imperius Curse "on the off chance" a guard was there — causing Podmore to later attempt, under the curse's compulsion, to break into the department during his own next guard shift, resulting in his arrest and six-month Azkaban sentence (Chapter 14/25). Harry concludes Voldemort will likely now send the newly informed Rookwood (a former Department of Mysteries employee himself) to retrieve the weapon directly.
- Hermione, true to her earlier instinct, insists Harry should NOT have seen this vision at all and that he needs to apply himself much harder to Occlumency going forward — firmly steering the conversation away from further speculation.
- The following weeks grind on with mounting O.W.L. pressure, continued dread about Hagrid's job security, and Harry's private, recurring preoccupation with the Rookwood dream (which he doesn't raise again with Ron/Hermione to avoid another lecture, and can't discuss with Sirius given surveillance concerns).
- Harry's Occlumency lessons continue deteriorating: a session a couple of weeks later surfaces more early Dudley-era childhood humiliation memories, but Snape specifically zeroes in on and demands an explanation for the "man kneeling in a darkened room" memory (the Rookwood dream) — Harry deflects, claiming it was "just a dream"; Snape, visibly suspicious, presses him on how many Voldemort dreams he's had, and Harry lies, saying only one. Snape needles Harry that he might secretly "enjoy" feeling "special" via these visions; Harry snaps back angrily that Snape's own job is precisely to find out what Voldemort tells his Death Eaters — Snape, rather than reacting with pure fury, seems oddly satisfied by this retort, confirming it as his actual function within the Order/for Dumbledore.
- The subsequent Legilimens attempt produces Dementors swarming Harry at the lake — but this time Harry, with Snape visibly straining and muttering nearby, manages to cast "Protego!" (the Shield Charm) defensively, which knocks Snape backward and briefly reverses the flow, giving Harry an unplanned, unwanted glimpse into Snape's own memories: a hook-nosed man shouting at a cowering woman while a dark-haired boy cries in the corner (implied to be young Snape's parents arguing, and Snape himself as a child); a lonely, greasy-haired teenage Snape shooting flies with his wand in a dark bedroom; and a girl laughing at a scrawny boy failing to mount a bucking broomstick. Snape furiously ends the intrusion ("ENOUGH!"), visibly shaken and pale, oddly calling the Shield Charm counterattack "effective" despite not having taught it, and resets the Pensieve (again checking his stored memories are still safely removed).
- The very next attempt, before Harry can even try to clear his mind, Snape's "Legilimens" sends Harry hurtling down the Department of Mysteries corridor in a dream-like vision far more vivid and uncontrolled than before — this time the plain black door actually bursts open, revealing a circular, black-walled room lit by blue-flamed candles with many more doors leading off it, and Harry is left disoriented, searching for which door to take next, when Snape forcibly ends the session ("POTTER!"). Harry is physically exhausted, feverish, and has a lump on his head from falling; Snape is furious, accusing him of laziness, but Harry deflects by needling Snape about his odd habit of calling Voldemort "the Dark Lord" (a term, Harry notes, otherwise used only by Death Eaters) — before Snape can respond, a woman's scream interrupts them from the entrance hall above.
- Harry and Snape rush up to find chaos in the entrance hall: Professor Trelawney, drunk, disheveled, and hysterical, has just been summarily sacked by Umbridge in front of a gathered crowd of students — her belongings (two trunks) already thrown down the stairs after her. Umbridge gloats cruelly over Trelawney's breakdown (mocking her inability to even predict "tomorrow's weather"), revealing Fudge personally countersigned her dismissal order an hour earlier. McGonagall and Sprout rush to comfort the devastated Trelawney (sixteen years at Hogwarts, which she calls her "home"), but Umbridge coldly asserts her Decree-23 authority to fire any underperforming teacher.
- Dumbledore dramatically intervenes, arriving through the front doors from the misty grounds at the crucial moment: he concedes Umbridge's technical right to dismiss staff but firmly asserts that only the Headmaster has authority to banish a dismissed teacher from the castle itself — and declares his explicit wish that Trelawney remain living at Hogwarts regardless of her employment status. Trelawney, overwhelmed, is escorted upstairs by McGonagall and Sprout (with Flitwick levitating her trunks via "Locomotor Trunks").
- Umbridge, furious, demands to know what Dumbledore intends to do with Trelawney's rooms once a replacement Divination teacher needs them — Dumbledore reveals, to her visible shock, that he has already secured a new Divination teacher (invoking Educational Decree Twenty-two's own stated caveat: the Ministry may only appoint a teacher if the headmaster himself cannot find a suitable candidate) — and introduces Firenze, a centaur Harry previously encountered in the Forbidden Forest (Philosopher's/Sorcerer's Stone, where Firenze once rescued Harry from a cloaked, Voldemort-possessed figure drinking unicorn blood), who steps out of the misty night, confirmed suitable for ground-floor lodgings (rather than the Divination tower, inaccessible to his equine half).

**Named characters (ch.26 additions):** Harry Potter, Ron Weasley, Hermione Granger, Luna Lovegood, Professor Umbridge, Cho Chang, Seamus Finnigan, Draco Malfoy, Crabbe, Goyle, Theodore Nott (newly named Slytherin), Fred & George Weasley, Professor Sprout, Professor Flitwick, Professor Trelawney (sacked by Umbridge, defended/retained in residence by Dumbledore), Professor McGonagall, Cornelius Fudge (countersigns Trelawney's dismissal), Dumbledore, Firenze (the centaur, newly introduced as the new Divination teacher), Bane (referenced, hostile centaur from Philosopher's/Sorcerer's Stone), Rookwood (Death Eater, Voldemort's dream-vision subordinate, former Department of Mysteries employee), Avery (Death Eater, implicitly punished for the failed Bode operation), Lucius Malfoy (deduced as Bode's and Podmore's Imperius-Curse caster), Sturgis Podmore (re-examined in light of new theory), Broderick Bode (re-examined, confirmed Imperius-cursed victim), Lavender Brown and Parvati Patil (emotional reactions to Trelawney's sacking).

**Named places/objects/spells/creatures:**
- The Quibbler's March issue (Harry's interview, full title given).
- Educational Decree Number Twenty-seven (bans Quibbler possession, punishable by expulsion).
- Fantastic Beasts and Where to Find Them (referenced, Harry's cover excuse to speak with Hagrid).
- The Department of Mysteries' circular, blue-candlelit inner chamber (new dream detail — "many doors," the black door now confirmed to open).
- The Shield Charm "Protego" (used successfully by Harry against Snape mid-lesson).
- Firenze (centaur, new Divination teacher) — confirmed banished from his herd for "betrayal" by agreeing to work for Dumbledore.
- Locomotor Trunks (levitation spell, used by Flitwick on Trelawney's luggage).
- Sage and mallowsweet (herbs for Firenze's divination-by-smoke method, in the next chapter).

**Specific numbers/quantities:**
- Trelawney had been at Hogwarts for "sixteen years" (restated, matching Chapter 15).
- Fudge countersigned her dismissal "an hour" before the confrontation.
- Umbridge docks 50 points from Gryffindor and gives Harry another week of detentions over the Quibbler interview.

**Small/obscure details:**
- Trelawney's drunken, disheveled breakdown (empty sherry bottle in hand, lopsided glasses) is a vivid, specific visual.
- Dumbledore's clever procedural win (citing the Ministry's own Decree 22 loophole to install Firenze) mirrors his earlier use of legal technicalities at Harry's hearing (Chapter 8).
- Firenze bears a fresh hoof-shaped bruise on his chest, implying a violent confrontation with his own herd (likely Bane) over his banishment.

## Chapter 27: The Centaur and the Sneak

**Plot events (in order):**
- A few days after Trelawney's sacking, the first Divination lesson with Firenze takes place in a new venue: Classroom Eleven on the ground floor (not the North Tower, inaccessible to a centaur), magically transformed into a mossy forest clearing with a leafy canopy, dappled light, and (once Firenze "lowers the light") a twilight sky full of stars projected on the ceiling.
- Firenze greets Harry by name, references a prior meeting ("It was foretold that we would meet again" — Philosopher's/Sorcerer's Stone callback), and bears a visible hoof-shaped bruise on his chest. He explains to the class that he has been banished from his herd for agreeing to work for Dumbledore (seen by centaurs as "a betrayal of our kind"); he rebukes Dean Thomas sharply for carelessly asking if Hagrid "bred" him like the thestrals ("Centaurs are not the servants or playthings of humans").
- Firenze's teaching philosophy radically differs from Trelawney's: he dismisses human "fortune-telling" methods (like Trelawney's specific astrological claims about Mars and Saturn causing "trivial" accidents) as self-flattering nonsense, explaining that centaurs instead watch for "great tides of evil or change" in the stars — slow, impersonal, impartial observations that can take up to ten years to confirm. He points to Mars (bringer of battle) shining brightly as an omen that "the fight must break out again soon," framing the current period as merely "a brief calm between two wars." He has the class attempt (unsuccessfully, as expected and accepted by him) to divine omens in burning sage and mallowsweet smoke, repeatedly stressing that even centaurs' own methods are fallible and shouldn't be trusted blindly.
- After class, Firenze privately asks Harry (confirmed as "a friend of Hagrid's") to relay an urgent, cryptic warning: Hagrid's secretive "attempt" at something is "not working" and he would be wise to abandon it; Firenze won't reveal the specific secret out of respect for Hagrid (who recently did Firenze "a great service"), but confirms it's dangerous enough to risk a "centaurs' battle" if Firenze personally returned to the forest to warn him.
- Harry eventually delivers the warning to Hagrid (using a ruse about a lost textbook to get him alone, since Umbridge still attends all his classes); Hagrid dismisses Firenze's concerns ("nice bloke... but he don' know what he's talkin' abou' on this. The attemp's comin' on fine") and refuses to explain further, insisting "there's things more importan' than keepin' a job" despite visibly shaking hands (he drops a basin of knarl droppings while saying this) — strongly implying continued secret, dangerous activity in the Forbidden Forest (later revealed to involve his giant half-brother Grawp).
- Meanwhile, O.W.L. pressure intensifies school-wide; Hannah Abbott becomes the first fifth-year to require a Calming Draught from Madam Pomfrey after a breakdown in Herbology.
- The D.A. remains Harry's primary source of satisfaction; the group begins working on producing full Patronuses — Cho's takes the form of a swan; Hermione's is an otter; Neville and Lavender struggle (only weak wisps of silver vapor); Seamus (brought by Dean to his very first D.A. meeting) claims a possible success ("something hairy"). Harry cautions that a Patronus conjured calmly in a lit classroom is very different from one produced under genuine threat (as when he learned via a boggart-Dementor in third year).
- The meeting is abruptly interrupted when Dobby the house-elf bursts in, terrified and attempting repeated acts of self-punishment (running into walls, trying to hit himself), struggling to warn Harry that Umbridge has discovered the D.A. and is on her way, despite house-elves being forbidden to tell. Harry orders the room to scatter immediately; everyone flees in different directions, aiming for alibi locations like the library or Owlery rather than their dormitories (too far/suspicious). Harry personally orders Dobby back to the kitchens and commands him to lie if asked whether he gave a warning, and forbids him from further self-harm.
- Fleeing alone, Harry is tripped by a Trip Jinx cast by Draco Malfoy (revealed to have been specifically deployed, along with other Slytherin prefects like Pansy Parkinson, by Umbridge to patrol for fleeing D.A. members) — Malfoy triumphantly summons Umbridge, who awards Slytherin 50 points and personally escorts a resigned-looking Harry straight to Dumbledore's office.
- In Dumbledore's office, Harry finds a tense tableau awaiting him: Dumbledore himself (calm, fingers steepled), a visibly strained Professor McGonagall, a gloating Cornelius Fudge warming himself by the fire, two unfamiliar Ministry enforcers flanking the door (one identified as Kingsley Shacklebolt, undercover, maintaining his cover by acting as a stranger), and Percy Weasley (now working directly for Fudge, visibly excited, poised to transcribe the proceedings officially). All the portraits of former headmasters are awake and alert, whispering urgently to each other — a stark contrast to their usual feigned sleep.
- Fudge, visibly thrilled at the prospect of finally catching Harry out, begins the interrogation; Harry, about to honestly affirm he knows why he's there, catches Dumbledore's subtle headshake signal and reverses course, claiming total ignorance of any broken school rules or Ministry decrees — infuriating Fudge further.
- Umbridge, unable to make headway through Harry's feigned innocence, fetches her "informant" — revealed to be Marietta Edgecombe (Cho's friend), visibly distressed and hiding her face; Umbridge reveals Marietta's mother is Madam Edgecombe of the Floo Network Office (Department of Magical Transportation), explaining her access/motivation to inform and her mother's complicity in helping police/monitor Hogwarts fireplace communications — tying back to the ongoing surveillance concerns raised since Chapter 17. When Fudge cheerfully asks Marietta to speak up, she reveals her face: something has gone horribly, visibly wrong with it (the chapter cuts off mid-reveal, with Fudge leaping back from the fire in shock and Marietta wailing and hiding again) — setting up the reveal of Hermione's jinxed parchment (from Chapter 17/18) finally activating against the informant who broke her oath of secrecy.

**Named characters so far:** Harry Potter, Ron Weasley, Hermione Granger, Parvati Patil, Lavender Brown, Firenze, Dean Thomas, Hagrid, Hannah Abbott, Madam Pomfrey, Cho Chang, Neville Longbottom, Seamus Finnigan, Dobby, Draco Malfoy, Pansy Parkinson, Dolores Umbridge, Dumbledore, Professor McGonagall, Cornelius Fudge, Kingsley Shacklebolt (undercover, unacknowledged), Percy Weasley, Marietta Edgecombe (confirmed by full name, revealed as the informant), Madam Edgecombe (Marietta's mother, Floo Network Office).

**Named places/objects/spells/creatures:**
- Classroom Eleven (ground floor), transformed into Firenze's forest-clearing classroom.
- Patronus forms revealed: Cho's is a swan; Hermione's is an otter (both now canon-confirmed in-text).
- The Trip Jinx (used by Malfoy against Harry).
- Fizzing Whizbee (Dumbledore's office password, restated).
- The Department of Magical Transportation / Floo Network Office (Marietta's mother's workplace, tied to Hogwarts fireplace surveillance).

**Specific numbers/quantities:**
- 50 points awarded to Slytherin for Malfoy's capture of Harry.
- It was "ten to nine" when the D.A. meeting was raided — i.e., before curfew, giving some members a plausible alibi window.

**Small/obscure details:**
- Firenze's teaching philosophy (skepticism even of centaur methods, emphasis on humility about fallibility) is a deliberate contrast to Trelawney's self-aggrandizing mysticism — notable thematic/characterization material.
- Dobby's renewed self-punishment habits (running into walls, hitting himself) directly recall his Chamber of Secrets characterization.
- The reveal that Marietta's mother works in the Floo Network Office retroactively explains how Umbridge/the Ministry have been monitoring Hogwarts-connected fireplaces all year (tying back to the Sirius fire-call near-miss in Chapter 17).

**Chapter 27 continued — the climax of the chapter (Dumbledore's dramatic escape):**
- Marietta's face is revealed: disfigured by purple pustules spelling out the word "SNEAK" across her nose and cheeks — the activation of Hermione's jinx on the D.A. sign-up parchment from Chapter 18, confirming Marietta broke her oath.
- Umbridge recounts how Marietta came to her office after dinner to report the meeting location; the jinx triggered before she could give further details. Fudge, oblivious to the irony, praises Marietta as "brave" for informing (his attempt at a "counterjinx" fails since Umbridge hasn't found one — Harry feels pride in Hermione's work).
- Umbridge reveals she already had prior intelligence: Willy Widdershins (the recurring minor criminal from earlier chapters) was secretly in the Hog's Head back in October and reported overhearing the original D.A. recruitment meeting to her directly — explaining why he escaped prosecution for the regurgitating toilets scandal (implied bribery/informant deal), which McGonagall sharply calls out as "blatant corruption" (echoed by an indignant portrait, Fortescue, a past headmaster).
- Dumbledore calmly corrects the record: the October Hog's Head meeting occurred two days BEFORE Educational Decree 24 (banning clubs) took effect, meaning it was not illegal at the time — a fact that visibly stuns Fudge and Percy. Umbridge pivots to the six months since, insisting continued meetings would be illegal — Dumbledore challenges her to prove meetings continued (rather than just the one caught that night); Umbridge has Marietta confirm via nod/shake whether meetings happened regularly — but to everyone's shock, the catatonic, blank-eyed Marietta shakes her head "no" each time, confirming (falsely, per the actual facts) that there were no regular meetings — the first hint that something unseen is manipulating the scene in Harry's favor (later explained as Kingsley Shacklebolt discreetly modifying Marietta's memory via a quick, concealed spell during the commotion).
- Umbridge, furious, grabs and shakes Marietta physically; Dumbledore immediately rises with wand drawn in anger — the only time in the book he's shown overtly furious — and Kingsley (maintaining his undercover role as a stranger) warns Umbridge to calm herself "before you get yourself in trouble," causing her to publicly back down in front of the Minister.
- With her human witness neutralized, Umbridge produces hard physical evidence instead: the Room of Requirement's wall-posted sign-up list (retrieved by Pansy Parkinson), bearing all members' names including Harry's — and crucially, the title Hermione wrote at the top: "DUMBLEDORE'S ARMY." Fudge, delighted, reads this as proof Dumbledore has been personally building a private army to move against the Ministry.
- Dumbledore, rather than denying it, immediately and calmly confesses in full — confirming he personally organized the group, recruited the students, that the raided meeting was meant to be its first, and that inviting Marietta was his own mistake — fully and deliberately taking all blame onto himself to shield Harry and the other students from expulsion/prosecution. Harry desperately tries to interject/object, but Dumbledore silences him.
- Fudge, ecstatic, orders Percy to transcribe Dumbledore's "confession" verbatim for immediate publication in the Daily Prophet's morning edition, and announces Dumbledore will be arrested, charged, and sent to Azkaban to await trial.
- Dumbledore calmly refuses to "come quietly," stating he has no intention of being taken to Azkaban and could easily escape but would rather not waste the time. He politely warns the Auror "Dawlish" (newly named) against trying to physically restrain him by force, acknowledging Dawlish's "Outstanding" N.E.W.T.s while promising to "hurt" him if forced; he also stops McGonagall from drawing her wand to help him, insisting "Hogwarts needs you."
- Fudge orders Dawlish and Kingsley to "take him"; a spectacular silent magical battle ensues — streaks of silver light, bangs, the office destroyed (desk overturned, silver instruments smashed) — Harry is forced to the ground by McGonagall for protection. When the dust clears, Fudge, Umbridge, Kingsley, and Dawlish all lie stunned/unconscious on the floor; Fawkes the phoenix circles overhead singing.
- In the brief window before the others regain consciousness, Dumbledore reveals to McGonagall (and Harry) that he deliberately stunned Kingsley too (to maintain his cover as a Ministry loyalist, since Kingsley had secretly and swiftly modified Marietta's memory mid-chaos to make her answer the "no regular meetings" question favorably) and asks McGonagall to relay his thanks. He instructs everyone to act as though no time passed and they simply don't remember being knocked out.
- McGonagall asks if Dumbledore will go into hiding at Grimmauld Place; he refuses, declaring (ominously, confidently) that he isn't fleeing and that Fudge "will soon wish he'd never dislodged me from Hogwarts."
- Before escaping, Dumbledore urgently instructs Harry to study Occlumency as hard as possible, to do everything Snape says, and specifically to practice emptying his mind every night before sleep to close himself off from "bad dreams" — promising Harry will understand why "soon enough." As Dumbledore grips Harry's wrist urgently, Harry's scar sears with pain and he again feels an overwhelming, uncontrollable "snakelike" urge to bite/attack Dumbledore — a third occurrence of this specific, unexplained compulsion (following Chapters 21/22). Dumbledore releases him, grasps Fawkes's tail, and both vanish in a flash of flame — escaping Hogwarts entirely (confirmed impossible to Apparate from within the school, per Umbridge's own panicked assertion) via phoenix travel.
- Fudge, furious and humiliated, declares this "the end" of Dumbledore to a openly scornful McGonagall, then orders her to send Harry and Marietta to bed. As they leave, Phineas Nigellus's portrait wryly remarks (even while disagreeing with Dumbledore "on many counts") that "you cannot deny he's got style."

**Named characters (ch.27 further additions):** Marietta Edgecombe (jinx-afflicted, later revealed to have had her memory discreetly altered by Kingsley), Willy Widdershins (confirmed as Umbridge's original October informant, explaining his earlier lucky toilet-charge acquittal), Fortescue (an indignant portrait, former headmaster), Dawlish (newly named Ministry Auror), Kingsley Shacklebolt (undercover, secretly aids Harry/Dumbledore by altering Marietta's memory), Percy Weasley (eagerly transcribes Dumbledore's "confession" for publication), Phineas Nigellus (closing wry remark), Dumbledore, Cornelius Fudge, Dolores Umbridge, Professor McGonagall, Fawkes, Pansy Parkinson (retrieved the incriminating list).

**Named places/objects/spells/creatures:**
- The jinxed D.A. sign-up parchment (payoff of Hermione's Chapter 18 precaution) — causes the word "SNEAK" to erupt in pustules across the face of anyone who betrays the group's secrecy.
- Fawkes the phoenix (Dumbledore's escape method).
- Educational Decree Number Twenty-four (the club-ban date is now precisely established as having taken effect two days after the original Hog's Head meeting).
- "Dumbledore's Army" — the name itself becomes the pivotal piece of evidence/confession trigger.

**Specific numbers/quantities:**
- The original Hog's Head D.A. meeting preceded Decree 24's enactment by exactly two days.
- The confrontation spans roughly six months of alleged (but legally unprovable, per Dumbledore's maneuvering) meetings.

**Small/obscure details:**
- Dawlish is noted to have scored "Outstanding" in all his N.E.W.T.s, per Dumbledore's own knowledge — a small, specific trivia detail about a minor antagonist.
- The destruyed office (overturned desk, smashed silver instruments) sets up its rebuilding/redecoration under Umbridge in the next chapter.
- Percy Weasley's eager, ink-splattered note-taking and enthusiastic service to Fudge is a continued, damning characterization beat.

---

## Chapter 28: Snape's Worst Memory

**Plot events (in order):**
- Overnight, school-wide notices announce: Educational Decree Number Twenty-eight — Dolores Jane Umbridge (High Inquisitor) has officially replaced Dumbledore as Head of Hogwarts. Despite no official explanation, the entire school somehow knows accurate details of Dumbledore's dramatic escape (overcoming two Aurors, the High Inquisitor, and the Minister himself) by the next day, through rumor — though some details get garbled in the retelling (one second-year claims Fudge now has "a pumpkin for a head" at St. Mungo's). Since Marietta is in the hospital wing, Harry becomes the sole eyewitness source and is besieged with requests to recount the story.
- Ernie Macmillan confidently predicts Dumbledore's swift return (as happened after being removed once in "second year" — referencing the implication that this isn't the first time he's been forced out, likely alluding to past book/series lore), and relays gossip from the Fat Friar (Hufflepuff ghost) that Umbridge tried and failed to access the Headmaster's office overnight — it has magically sealed itself against her, causing her a "tantrum."
- Draco Malfoy confronts Harry, Ron, Hermione, and Ernie in the entrance hall, revealing he's now a member of the newly formed "Inquisitorial Squad" — a hand-picked group of pro-Ministry students personally selected by Umbridge, identifiable by a small silver "I" pin worn beneath their regular badges, with the explicit power to deduct House points (a privilege normally reserved for prefects/staff). Malfoy vindictively docks points from Hermione (for insulting Umbridge, and separately for being Muggle-born — "Mudblood"), Ernie (for contradicting him), Harry (pure spite), and Ron (untucked shirt) — totaling roughly 50 points from Gryffindor, visibly draining the house-points hourglasses in real time while Slytherin's remains untouched.
- Fred and George reveal they similarly thwarted a point-deduction attempt by Slytherin Quidditch Captain Montague (a fellow Inquisitorial Squad member) by forcing him headfirst into a Vanishing Cabinet on the first floor — Montague vanishes entirely, location unknown, expected to remain missing for "weeks." They declare, now that Dumbledore is gone, they've abandoned all restraint about getting in trouble, having "always known where to draw the line" before but now intending deliberate "mayhem" as a parting gesture of loyalty to Dumbledore before they eventually leave Hogwarts (hinting at their eventual dropping out, foreshadowed). They cryptically announce "phase one" is imminent and advise everyone to be visibly at lunch for an alibi.
- Filch summons Harry to see the new "headmistress" — visibly thrilled about increased future authority once Decree 29 (not yet detailed beyond implication) grants him corporal-punishment-style powers over students, and reveals Umbridge has petitioned Fudge to have Peeves the Poltergeist formally expelled.
- In Umbridge's newly redecorated office (now bearing a "HEADMISTRESS" nameplate, Harry's confiscated Firebolt and the twins' Cleansweeps chained to the wall as trophies), she interrogates Harry under the pretense of hospitality — pressing him (after a calculated offer of tea, which Harry refuses to actually drink, suspicious of poisoning/Veritaserum, prompted by imagining what Mad-Eye Moody would say) about Dumbledore's whereabouts and, more alarmingly, Sirius Black's location — directly confirming she recognizes Sirius as the fire-caller she almost caught in Chapter 17 ("I know perfectly well it was you he was meeting"). Harry stonewalls both questions. Umbridge warns him the Ministry now monitors all Hogwarts communication channels — every fireplace except her own is watched by a "Floo Network Regulator," all owl post is being opened/read by her Inquisitorial Squad, and Filch monitors all secret passages — before a loud BOOM interrupts her mid-threat, allowing Harry to dump his undrunk tea into a vase and escape to investigate.
- Fred and George's "phase one": an enormous crate of enchanted fireworks ("Weasleys' Wildfire Whiz-Bangs") unleashed throughout the castle — fire-spark dragons, giant Catherine wheels, ricocheting silver-tailed rockets, profanity-writing sparklers, and ever-strengthening firecrackers that resist being Stunned or Vanished (Vanishing attempts reportedly cause them to multiply tenfold) wreak joyful havoc for the rest of the day; Filch (a Squib, unable to use offensive magic) is reduced to swatting them with a broom that catches fire; Umbridge is humiliated repeatedly (soot-blackened by day's end), forced to personally respond to every teacher's summons since none of them can (or will) remove the fireworks themselves (McGonagall and Flitwick both play along with barely concealed amusement/sarcasm). Fred and George become instant common-room heroes that night, even winning over Hermione's grudging admiration ("They were wonderful fireworks") — though they reveal they used their entire stock and will have to restart production. Hermione, in an uncharacteristic display of "feeling rebellious," suggests skipping homework for the night.
- That night, Harry's recurring Department of Mysteries dream advances significantly further than ever before: he passes through the circular room of doors, then through a second room (filled with inexplicable mechanical clicking and dancing light-flecks on the walls — not investigated), then into a third, vast, church-sized room lined with towering shelves of small dusty glass spheres — he senses overwhelming excitement and that something in this room is deeply desired (by himself, or by someone else) — before being abruptly woken by fireworks-related commotion from his dormitory-mates (a Catherine wheel/rocket hybrid "mating," per Seamus), leaving him sick with disappointment at having been snatched away just before reaching his goal again.
- The next day, Harry dreads his rescheduled Occlumency lesson, guiltily aware he hasn't practiced at all since Dumbledore's departure (too much chaos) and worried about explaining how much deeper into the Department of Mysteries he's now penetrating in his dreams. Before the lesson, Cho catches him to apologize for Marietta's betrayal, defending her friend sympathetically (her mother works for the Ministry, making informing "really difficult" for her) — Harry, still bitter, angrily rejects this excuse, pointing out Ron's father also works for the Ministry without becoming a "sneak"; Cho criticizes Hermione's secret jinx as a "horrible trick"; the argument escalates into a full row, ending with Cho storming off after Harry warns her not to cry again.
- Arriving at Snape's office still fuming over Cho, Harry is interrupted almost immediately: Draco Malfoy bursts in (now visibly delighting in exposing Harry's "Remedial Potions" cover story to confirm his suspicions) to report that Umbridge urgently needs Snape's help — Slytherin Quidditch Captain Montague has been found, confused and incoherent, jammed inside a fourth-floor toilet (the Vanishing Cabinet mystery resolved, at least partially — he reappeared randomly). Snape postpones Harry's lesson to the next evening and rushes off with Malfoy, leaving Harry alone in the office — with an unsupervised, unguarded Pensieve sitting in plain view.
- Harry notices a dancing, shivering light pattern on the office door frame, identical to the strange flickering lights he saw in the second Department-of-Mysteries dream-room — tracing it to the Pensieve's glowing contents (Snape's extracted memories, deliberately hidden from Harry during their lessons). Overwhelmed by curiosity about whether these memories might relate to the Department of Mysteries, and still raw from his fights with both Cho and Malfoy, Harry succumbs to reckless temptation: he pokes the Pensieve's contents with his wand (causing them to swirl and become transparent, revealing an aerial view of what appears to be the Great Hall), and then — despite knowing it's "insane" — deliberately plunges his face into the memory, pulled bodily into Snape's own past.
- Inside the memory: Harry finds himself in the Great Hall transformed for exam conditions (individual desks replacing House tables), observing a Defense Against the Dark Arts O.W.L. exam in progress. He locates a teenage Severus Snape (age 15-16, pallid, greasy-haired, writing furiously and at great length in cramped handwriting) sitting near the back. Exploring further, Harry spots his own fifteen-year-old father, James Potter, sitting nearby — a startling, almost-mirror image of Harry himself (hazel eyes instead of green, no scar, slightly longer nose, but otherwise nearly identical, including identically messy hair) — along with a young, strikingly handsome, casually elegant Sirius Black (lounging, tipping his chair back, unnoticed admirer nearby), Remus Lupin (pale, likely nearing a full moon, seriously absorbed in reviewing his exam answers), and — completing the group — Peter Pettigrew ("Wormtail"), small, mousy-haired, anxious, and visibly trying to copy his neighbor's answers.
- Professor Flitwick (much younger) ends the exam ("Quills down... Accio!" — sweeping over a hundred parchment rolls into his arms, comically knocking him off his feet). James hastily hides a doodle he'd been drawing (a Snitch with the initials "L.E." traced repeatedly — presumably Lily Evans, Harry's mother, not yet identified explicitly in-text but strongly implied) before joining Sirius.
- Following the group outside (trailing discreetly behind the oblivious, still exam-paper-absorbed teenage Snape to stay within his memory's "range"), Harry overhears Lupin and Sirius joking about an exam question concerning identifying werewolf traits (Lupin self-deprecatingly jokes the key signs are "he's sitting on my chair... wearing my clothes... his name's Remus Lupin" — the other boys laugh except an anxious Wormtail, who worries aloud he only identified three of the physical signs and is harshly mocked by James ("How thick are you, Wormtail? You run round with a werewolf once a month—") before Lupin nervously shushes him, confirming to the reader (if it wasn't already clear from Prisoner of Azkaban) that James, Sirius, and Wormtail were already Animagi at this point to accompany werewolf-Lupin during the full moon.
- The group settles under the very same lakeside beech tree Harry, Ron, and Hermione often use, with Snape nearby, sprawled in bushes still absorbed in reviewing his exam paper, allowing Harry to watch unnoticed. James is shown casually, arrogantly playing with a stolen ("nicked") Golden Snitch, catching it with excellent reflexes purely to show off for an admiring Wormtail and a bored, unimpressed Sirius, while repeatedly glancing at a group of girls sunbathing by the lake and compulsively ruffling his own hair to keep it messy. Sirius eventually tells James to put the Snitch away before Wormtail "wets himself from excitement" — Harry notes James seems to only perform for Sirius's approval, not caring about anyone else's opinion, including Wormtail's open adoration.
- Sirius, bored, notices Snape rising to leave and gleefully alerts James ("Look who it is... Snivellus"). As Snape gathers his things to walk away across the lawn, James and Sirius stand up with clear predatory intent; Lupin stares fixedly at his book without really reading, visibly uncomfortable; Wormtail watches eagerly. James calls out "All right, Snivellus?" — and Snape, clearly anticipating exactly this kind of attack (reflexively going for his wand the instant he's addressed), is disarmed before he can react: James casts "Expelliarmus," sending Snape's wand flying twelve feet away, and Sirius immediately follows up with "Impedimenta," knocking Snape off his feet mid-dive for his wand — a crowd of onlooking students begins to gather, some apprehensive, others entertained, as the humiliation (title-confirmed as "Snape's Worst Memory") begins in earnest.

**Named characters (ch.28 additions):** Harry Potter, Ron Weasley, Hermione Granger, Ernie Macmillan, the Fat Friar (Hufflepuff ghost, referenced), Draco Malfoy, Crabbe, Goyle, Filch, Dolores Umbridge, Fred & George Weasley, Montague (Slytherin Quidditch Captain, vanished into/reappears from a Vanishing Cabinet), Professor McGonagall, Professor Flitwick (present day and in memory, much younger), Cho Chang, Marietta Edgecombe (hospitalized, unhealed pustules), Severus Snape (both present-day and as a teenager in the memory), James Potter (teenage, vividly detailed), Sirius Black (teenage), Remus Lupin (teenage), Peter Pettigrew/Wormtail (teenage), Lily Evans (implied via "L.E." doodle, not yet named explicitly), Stebbins (minor named classmate in the exam scene), Mad-Eye Moody (referenced, Harry's imagined caution about drinking Umbridge's tea), Peeves (referenced, Umbridge seeking his formal expulsion via Fudge), Weasleys' Wildfire Whiz-Bangs (named product).

**Named places/objects/spells/creatures:**
- Educational Decree Number Twenty-eight (installs Umbridge as Head of Hogwarts).
- The Inquisitorial Squad (newly formed, Umbridge's hand-picked pro-Ministry student enforcers, marked by a small silver "I" pin; empowered to dock House points).
- The giant House-points hourglasses in the entrance hall (visually track points in real time).
- A Vanishing Cabinet on the first floor (used by Fred/George to banish Montague — a significant item that recurs in later books, though not elaborated here).
- Weasleys' Wildfire Whiz-Bangs (the fireworks product; pricing mentioned: 5 Galleons for a "Basic Blaze" box, 20 Galleons for "Deflagration Deluxe").
- Umbridge's redecorated office (HEADMISTRESS nameplate; Harry's Firebolt and the twins' Cleansweeps chained/padlocked on display).
- The Floo Network Regulator (new Ministry surveillance role monitoring Hogwarts fireplaces, confirming earlier fears).
- Dumbledore's Pensieve (now in Snape's office, used for private memory storage during Occlumency lessons) — the vessel for this chapter's central memory-dive.
- The O.W.L. Defense Against the Dark Arts exam (Snape and the Marauders' own fifth-year exam, the memory's setting).

**Specific numbers/quantities:**
- Malfoy docks points totaling roughly 50 from Gryffindor in one encounter (5+5+5+5+10+... exact breakdown given: 5 from Hermione for rudeness, 5 from Ernie for contradicting him, 5 from Harry "because I don't like you," 5 from Ron for an untucked shirt, 10 more from Hermione for being Muggle-born).
- Weasleys' Wildfire Whiz-Bangs pricing: 5 Galleons (Basic Blaze), 20 Galleons (Deflagration Deluxe).
- Snape, James, Sirius, Lupin, and Wormtail are all confirmed to be fifteen or sixteen years old in the memory (matching Harry's own age) — this O.W.L. exam year directly mirrors Harry's current year.

**Small/obscure details:**
- The "L.E." initials James doodles around a Snitch are a strong, specific hint at Lily Evans (not yet confirmed by name in text) — notable foreshadowing/trivia detail.
- Lupin's self-deprecating werewolf-identification joke ("he's sitting on my chair... wearing my clothes... his name's Remus Lupin") is a now-famous, specific quotable bit (paraphrased here per copyright guidance).
- James's habit of "ruffling up his hair to keep it messy" directly mirrors a trait Harry shares/dislikes about himself.
- The memory confirms Snape was already deeply unpopular and targeted specifically by James and Sirius while Lupin looked away uncomfortably and Wormtail eagerly watched — a formative, canon-important scene re James/Snape's lifelong enmity.
- The exact wording of the chapter's central memory title ("Snape's Worst Memory") is the chapter title itself — a direct, important thematic marker.

**Chapter 28 continued — the memory's conclusion and aftermath:**
- The bullying escalates: James and Sirius mock Snape's academic effort ("nose touching the parchment... grease marks"); Snape, still immobilized by the jinx, swears venomous threats; James casts "Scourgify" on him, filling his mouth with soap bubbles.
- Lily Evans (confirmed by name, description: thick dark red hair, startlingly green almond-shaped eyes — explicitly "Harry's eyes") intervenes, shouting at James to leave Snape alone; James immediately shifts to a more "mature," eager tone addressing her as "Evans." Lily calls James an "arrogant, bullying toerag" and refuses his quid-pro-quo offer to leave Snape alone forever if she'll date him ("I wouldn't go out with you if it was a choice between you and the giant squid").
- As the Impediment Jinx wears off, Snape curses James from behind, slashing his face; James retaliates by hanging Snape upside down by the ankles via a spell, exposing his underwear to the laughing crowd. Lily demands he be let down; James complies, but when Sirius re-petrifies Snape ("Petrificus Totalus") the moment he tries to retaliate, Lily draws her own wand and threatens James directly, forcing him to lift the curse.
- The pivotal line: Snape, humiliated and furious even at his rescuer, snarls "I don't need help from filthy little Mudbloods like her!" — Lily coolly disowns any further sympathy for him and walks away. James, stung, demands Snape apologize to Lily; Lily rebukes James instead, declaring him "as bad as he is," listing his specific faults (messing up his hair for effect, showing off with a stolen Snitch, hexing people "just because you can") and declares he makes her "sick" before storming off, unmoved by his calling after her.
- James, humiliated in turn, immediately re-hexes the now-passive Snape (hanging him upside down again) out of wounded pride, about to publicly strip him further — at which point adult, present-day Snape forcibly yanks Harry out of the memory by the arm before Harry can see how it ends.
- Back in Snape's office, Snape is in a cold, shaking rage ("Having fun?... Amusing man, your father, wasn't he?"), physically throws Harry to the ground, and furiously bans him from ever returning to his office, declaring the Occlumency lessons over — hurling a jar of dead cockroaches at Harry's head as he flees.
- Harry retreats, badly shaken — not by Snape's anger, but by the uncomfortable realization that his idealized image of his father has been shattered: James was, per what Harry directly witnessed, genuinely "every bit as arrogant" as Snape always claimed, and Harry recognizes with horror the specific humiliation of being mocked in a circle of onlookers (a feeling he knows intimately from his own life).

**Named characters (ch.28 further additions):** Lily Evans (confirmed by full name and vivid description — Harry's mother), James Potter (confirmed as "Prongs," his Marauder nickname, used by Sirius), Severus Snape (present-day reaction), Harry Potter.

**Named spells:** Scourgify (soap-bubble mouth curse), Petrificus Totalus (Full Body-Bind, used by Sirius on Snape), Expelliarmus and Impedimenta (restated from earlier in the memory).

**Small/obscure details:**
- Lily's exact insult list against James (messy hair for effect, showing off with the Snitch, casual hexing) is a precise, quotable character indictment — paraphrased here per copyright guidance.
- Snape's slur against Lily ("Mudblood") is the single word that destroys her sympathy for him — a hugely significant, specific plot/character beat (this moment is later revealed across the series as the breaking point of Snape and Lily's lifelong friendship).
- The memory cuts off before Harry can see whether James actually removed Snape's pants a second time — deliberately left unresolved in-text.
- Sirius's nickname for James, "Prongs," appears here (Marauders' Map/Animagus nicknames: Prongs=James/stag, Padfoot=Sirius/dog, Moony=Lupin/werewolf, Wormtail=Pettigrew/rat — all four nicknames are used across this memory sequence).
