## Chapter 29: Career Advice

**Plot events (in order):**
- Harry lies to Hermione that Snape has simply decided he's learned "the basics" of Occlumency and can continue alone — concealing the real reason (Snape's furious refusal to continue teaching him after the Pensieve incident). Hermione remains skeptical and presses him to resume lessons; Harry refuses to discuss it further.
- First day of the Easter holidays: Hermione produces detailed color-coded study schedules for Harry and Ron (O.W.L.s are six weeks away); Ron is startled by how close exams have crept up given "a lot going on" this year. Ron notes gloomily that Gryffindor has little hope of winning the Quidditch Cup this year (comparing their odds to his dad becoming Minister of Magic).
- Harry is visibly distracted and uncommunicative, privately consumed by guilt and disillusionment over what he saw in Snape's memory; he deflects Hermione's concern by blaming a (real but secondary) renewed row with Cho over Marietta, letting Ron's anti-Marietta rant fill the silence while his mind dwells on the Pensieve memory.
- Harry's internal crisis: for nearly five years, the idea of his father (as described admiringly by Hagrid and Sirius) had been a source of pride and comfort; now he's forced to reconcile this with direct evidence that James was "arrogant," casually cruel to Snape for no real provocation beyond boredom, and that his own mother, Lily, visibly "loathed" James at the time — Harry even briefly wonders if James somehow "forced" Lily into eventually marrying him. He recalls McGonagall once comparing James and Sirius to the Weasley twins as fellow "troublemakers," but can't picture Fred/George casually tormenting someone the way James did Snape. He notes that Lupin, made a prefect specifically (per his own Chapter 9 admission) to help rein in James and Sirius, nonetheless sat passively by during the bullying in the Pensieve memory.
- Late one evening in the library, Ginny Weasley (having just left Quidditch practice early after Jack Sloper accidentally knocked himself unconscious with his own Beater's bat) finds Harry alone and brooding; she delivers an Easter care package from Mrs. Weasley (chocolate eggs, now officially "INSPECTED AND PASSED BY THE HOGWARTS HIGH INQUISITOR" per a stamped note — confirming Umbridge is now screening even confectionery mail). Harry is unexpectedly overcome with emotion unwrapping his egg (Fizzing Whizbees inside) — not fully understanding why.
- Ginny gently presses about his low mood, correctly guessing it's not really about Cho; Harry finally admits, after checking no one's listening, that what he really wants is to talk to Sirius — impossible given Umbridge's total surveillance of owls and fireplaces. Ginny, drawing on her upbringing with Fred and George, muses that "anything's possible if you've got enough nerve," sparking a flicker of hope in Harry — interrupted immediately by Madam Pince catching them eating chocolate in the library and literally chasing them out with self-propelled, wand-whacking books and bags.
- Career-advice pamphlets and notices appear in Gryffindor Tower: all fifth-years must attend a short individual meeting with their Head of House in the first week of summer term to discuss future careers; Harry's slot is Monday at 2:30 p.m. with McGonagall (clashing with most of Divination). The trio spends the last weekend of the Easter break reading career leaflets: Ron rules out Healing (impossibly high N.E.W.T. requirements: Exceeds Expectations in Potions, Herbology, Transfiguration, Charms, and DADA); Hermione considers "Muggle Relations" work (low bar: just one Muggle Studies O.W.L. plus "enthusiasm, patience, and a good sense of fun") and also Gringotts Curse-Breaking (requires Arithmancy — Harry suggests this suits Hermione) before settling on neither, instead browsing a leaflet for "training security trolls." Fred and George tease Harry (relaying Ginny's tip) about wanting to talk to Sirius; Hermione is horrified at the idea given Umbridge's total surveillance, but Fred and George reveal their Easter-holiday "quiet" period (no pranks, out of unusual consideration for everyone's exam revision) is about to end — they offer to stage a deliberate, large-scale diversion the next day specifically timed to let Harry sneak into Umbridge's office (the one fireplace confirmed NOT monitored, per Umbridge's own earlier boast in Chapter 28) using Sirius's old enchanted knife (a Christmas-before-last gift that can open any lock, even Umbridge's warded door) to access the Floo Network undetected. They promise the diversion (location: "Gregory the Smarmy's corridor," 5 p.m. the next day) will buy roughly twenty minutes.
- The next day: Harry is anxious about both the planned office break-in and his first post-Pensieve encounter with Snape in Potions class. He watches Hagrid limping back from the Forbidden Forest that morning, apparently injured again, disappearing into his cabin (smoke from the chimney confirms he's at least capable of tending a fire).
- Hermione spends the whole day urgently, repeatedly trying to talk Harry out of the plan (warning of expulsion risk and potential forced Veritaserum interrogation if caught), to Ron's visible irritation; Snape, by contrast, pointedly ignores Harry entirely in Potions (a relief compared to his usual taunting) until, just as Harry finishes a genuinely successful potion (expecting an E), Snape deliberately smashes his sample flask with a gloating "Whoops... another zero" — and Hermione, trying to help, accidentally vanishes the remaining potion in Harry's cauldron before he can refill a sample, compounding his frustration (sincere accident, but poorly timed).
- Harry's rescheduled Career Advice meeting (forgotten amid the day's stress, remembered late) takes place in McGonagall's office with Umbridge pointedly present, clipboard in hand, auditing the meeting. Harry states his ambition to become an Auror; McGonagall warns of the exceptionally high bar (minimum five N.E.W.T.s at Exceeds Expectations or higher, rigorous character/aptitude testing, and the fact that nobody has been accepted into Auror training in three years) but commits to recommending Transfiguration, Charms, Potions, and DADA as necessary subjects — noting she personally requires E-or-higher at O.W.L. to accept N.E.W.T. students, and that Snape demands "Outstanding" specifically.
- Umbridge repeatedly interrupts with escalating fake coughs, eventually directly challenging whether Harry has "the temperament" for Auror work and asserting (via a "note" she claims to have left in Harry's file) that his actual DADA marks under her personally have been "very poor" — McGonagall coldly clarifies that Harry's high marks were earned under "a competent teacher" (i.e., not Umbridge), causing Umbridge's smile to vanish instantly. Umbridge then plays her trump card: Harry's "criminal record" (his cleared Ministry hearing) disqualifies him from ever being hired as an Auror, since the Ministry vets applicants' records — McGonagall retorts that Harry was "cleared of all charges." The exchange escalates into a full shouting match: McGonagall vows to personally coach Harry nightly if necessary to secure his required grades; Umbridge insists the Minister will never hire him; McGonagall counters there "may well be a new Minister of Magic" by the time Harry's ready — provoking Umbridge into openly, furiously accusing McGonagall of secretly wanting Dumbledore reinstated as Minister so she herself could become Hogwarts headmistress and "Senior Undersecretary" simultaneously — McGonagall dismisses this as "raving" and ends the meeting.
- That afternoon's DADA lesson finds a visibly still-fuming Umbridge glowering at Harry throughout. Harry spends the lesson agonizing over whether to proceed with the risky Sirius plan, oscillating between practical caution (McGonagall just publicly vouched for him) and emotional need (he cannot simply let the memory go unaddressed) — he recalls Sirius's earlier bitter remark that Harry is "less like your father" in avoiding unnecessary risk, prompting him to question whether he still wants to emulate James at all. Hermione continues pleading against the plan right up to the bell, invoking Dumbledore's sacrifice to protect Harry from expulsion; Ron pointedly tells her to let Harry decide for himself.
- At the critical moment, Fred and George's diversion erupts (screams/noise from the east wing); Umbridge rushes to investigate, leaving her office corridor empty. Harry commits to the plan: dashes to her office, uses Sirius's knife to pick the warded lock, dons his father's Invisibility Cloak, and successfully breaks in — finding the office empty (his and the twins' confiscated broomsticks still chained on the wall).
- Using Floo powder from a box by the fireplace, Harry performs his first-ever "head-only" Floo call (physically kneeling in Umbridge's office while his head travels to Grimmauld Place) — reaching the kitchen, where he finds not Sirius but Lupin, who fetches Sirius (searching for the hiding Kreacher in the attic) and kneels alongside him by the fire.
- Harry recounts the full Pensieve memory to both men. Lupin and Sirius offer context and partial defense: Lupin cautions against judging James based on a single moment at age fifteen (Harry retorts he's currently fifteen himself); Sirius explains James and Snape's mutual hatred was instant and visceral — James represented everything Snape wanted to be (popular, talented, good at Quidditch) while Snape was fixated on the Dark Arts, which James always genuinely despised. Both concede the incident reflects real arrogance ("I'm not proud of it," says Sirius) but contextualize it as part of general teenage showing-off common to "the best in school at whatever they did." Both find Harry's specific complaints (James messing up his hair for effect, showing off with a stolen Snitch) fondly amusing/nostalgic rather than shameful. Sirius notes Lupin, despite being made a prefect specifically to help control them, never actually confronted them about the bullying, though his quiet disapproval "made us feel ashamed of ourselves sometimes."
- Sirius and Lupin confirm Lily did NOT actually hate James as much as the scene suggested — she began dating him in seventh year, after he'd "deflated his head a bit" and genuinely stopped hexing people for fun — with the notable, telling exception of continuing to duel Snape specifically (since Snape, per Lupin, "never lost an opportunity to curse James" in turn, making it harder to frame as pure unprovoked bullying by that later point). Sirius assures Harry that James was "the best friend I ever had" and "a good person" who simply "grew out of" typical fifteen-year-old idiocy.
- Lupin, probing further, asks how Snape reacted to Harry seeing the memory; learning Snape has flatly refused to continue Occlumency lessons, both Sirius and Lupin react with alarm disproportionate to Harry's own nonchalance — Lupin insists with uncharacteristic sternness that "there is nothing so important as you learning Occlumency" and orders Harry to go back and insist Snape resume the lessons (invoking Dumbledore's authority); Sirius furiously threatens to personally confront Snape himself, restrained physically by Lupin. Harry reluctantly agrees to try, though skeptical Snape will listen.
- The call is cut short by the sound of approaching footsteps (at the Grimmauld Place end); Harry hastily withdraws his head from the fire just before Filch bursts into Umbridge's actual office (Harry's own end), gleefully searching her desk for an "Approval for Whipping" form he's been seeking for ages — confirming per Chapter 28's Decree hints that corporal punishment has now been formally authorized. Harry, cloaked and unnoticed, slips out behind Filch and follows him down to the entrance hall.
- There, in a dramatic set-piece mirroring Trelawney's public sacking (Chapter 26), Harry finds the whole school assembled in the entrance hall: Fred and George have been cornered by Umbridge and the Inquisitorial Squad after flooding a school corridor into a full swamp (their actual, much more spectacular diversion than initially described — several students are shown covered in Stinksap from an earlier, related prank). Filch arrives triumphantly bearing the signed "Approval for Whipping" form, eager to finally punish the twins corporally.
- Before Umbridge or Filch can act, Fred and George — utterly fearless — declare they've "outgrown full-time education," publicly summon their chained-and-padlocked broomsticks (which smash straight through Umbridge's office door to reach them, confirming/completing the earlier plan), and depart Hogwarts entirely and permanently in a blaze of glory: announcing to the assembled crowd that they've secretly established "Weasleys' Wizard Wheezes" at number 93, Diagon Alley (their joke shop, long-planned and secretly funded in part by Harry's Triwizard winnings since Goblet of Fire), offering special discounts to any student who promises to use their products against Umbridge specifically. As Umbridge screams for them to be stopped, they rocket fifteen feet into the air (trailing the iron wall-peg and chain still attached to one broom), instruct Peeves the Poltergeist (who has never before been seen obeying a student) to "give her hell" on their behalf — Peeves salutes with his belled hat — and fly out through the open front doors into a glorious sunset, to the entire student body's applause.

**Named characters (ch.29 additions):** Harry Potter, Ron Weasley, Hermione Granger, Ginny Weasley, Fred & George Weasley, Professor McGonagall, Dolores Umbridge, Jack Sloper (Quidditch mishap), Madam Pince, Remus Lupin, Sirius Black, Filch, James Potter and Lily Evans (discussed/defended), Snape (discussed; present-day reaction to the Pensieve incident), Peeves, Montague (still recovering, not yet resolved), Warrington and Pansy Parkinson (foreshadowed next chapter's pranks, not yet detailed here).

**Named places/objects/spells/creatures:**
- The Career Advice notice and process (fifth-year Head-of-House meetings).
- St. Mungo's Healer career leaflet requirements (E or higher in five N.E.W.T. subjects).
- Gringotts Curse-Breaker recruitment leaflet (requires Arithmancy).
- "So You Think You'd Like to Work in Muggle Relations?" and "Have You Got What It Takes to Train Security Trolls?" (career pamphlets, named).
- Sirius's magical knife (opens any lock, gifted "Christmas before last").
- James Potter's old Invisibility Cloak (used by Harry for the break-in).
- Floo powder (used for a "head-only" fire-call for the first time in the series' depiction).
- Weasleys' Wizard Wheezes, 93 Diagon Alley (the twins' joke shop, formally named and located for the first time).
- The "Approval for Whipping" form (confirms corporal punishment has been authorized for Filch under a new, unspecified Decree).
- Portable Swamp (the twins' prank product, publicly advertised as available for purchase at their new shop).

**Specific numbers/quantities:**
- Six weeks remain until O.W.L.s at the start of this chapter.
- Auror requirements: minimum five N.E.W.T.s at Exceeds Expectations or higher; nobody accepted into Auror training in the past three years.
- Fred and George guarantee Harry roughly twenty minutes of diversion.
- Fred and George's broomsticks fly fifteen feet into the air during their final exit.

**Small/obscure details:**
- Ginny's package of Easter eggs stamped "INSPECTED AND PASSED BY THE HOGWARTS HIGH INQUISITOR" is a small but vivid detail of the surveillance state Umbridge has built.
- Madam Pince's wand-animated books literally chasing Harry and Ginny out of the library for eating chocolate is a distinctly comic beat.
- The detail that Lupin was made prefect specifically to restrain James/Sirius yet never actually confronted them is an important, morally complex piece of characterization.
- Montague's unresolved, worsening condition (still "confused and disoriented" from the Vanishing Cabinet) foreshadows later significance (the Vanishing Cabinet network becomes major in a later book).

## Chapter 30: Grawp

**Plot events so far (in progress):**
- In the days following Fred and George's dramatic departure, their exit becomes instant Hogwarts legend, with exaggerated retellings already circulating (some students misremembering them as having dive-bombed Umbridge with Dungbombs). Their prank legacy persists physically: the corridor-swamp remains unremovable (Umbridge and Filch fail repeatedly; the area is roped off, and Filch is reduced to literally punting students across it on a boat to reach classes) — Harry suspects teachers like McGonagall and Flitwick could easily clear it but deliberately choose not to help Umbridge. Two broom-shaped holes remain punched through Umbridge's office door (from the twins' broomsticks smashing free); Harry's own confiscated Firebolt is relocated to a dungeon under armed security-troll guard per rumor.
- Copycat mayhem spreads: a hairy-snouted Niffler is smuggled into Umbridge's office, trashing it hunting for shiny objects and attacking Umbridge's jeweled fingers; Dungbombs and Stinkpellets proliferate corridor-wide, prompting students to adopt Bubble-Head Charms (giving them a comic upside-down-goldfish-bowl look) just to breathe clean air between classes; Filch patrols with an authorized horsewhip but is overwhelmed by sheer volume of troublemakers. Inquisitorial Squad members suffer mysterious magical mishaps while attempting to help enforce order: Warrington develops a cornflake-like skin condition; Pansy Parkinson sprouts antlers (to Hermione's open delight).
- The true scale of Fred and George's pre-departure Skiving Snackbox sales becomes apparent: merely entering her own classroom now causes Umbridge's students to spontaneously faint, vomit, develop fevers, or nosebleed — diagnosed collectively (and untraceably) by students as "Umbridge-itis"; mass detentions fail to uncover the cause, forcing her to let the afflicted leave class in droves.
- Peeves the Poltergeist, having taken Fred's parting instruction to heart, escalates into his most destructive campaign yet: upending furniture, bursting through blackboards, toppling statues, trapping Mrs. Norris inside suits of armor (twice), extinguishing lights, juggling flaming torches over screaming students, flooding the second floor, dropping a sack of live tarantulas into the Great Hall during breakfast, and relentlessly tailing Umbridge blowing raspberries. Notably, no staff member except Filch makes any real effort to stop him — Harry personally witnesses McGonagall walk past Peeves (who is dismantling a chandelier) and murmur unhelpful technical advice ("it unscrews the other way") rather than intervening.
- Montague, still mentally damaged from his unexplained ordeal in the Vanishing Cabinet (confusion, disorientation persisting weeks later), prompts his furious parents to storm up to the school demanding answers; Hermione worries about potential permanent injury and whether more information might help Madam Pomfrey treat him, while Ron and Harry are unsympathetic (Montague "shouldn't have tried to take all those points" in the first place) and view it as simply more trouble for Umbridge.
- Domestic Weasley-family subplot: Ron anxiously anticipates backlash once Mrs. Weasley's delayed letter finally clears Umbridge's mail-screening process, fearing he'll be unfairly blamed for failing to physically stop Fred and George from leaving; he's also suspicious about how the twins secured the funds for Diagon Alley premises so quickly, wondering if Mundungus Fletcher helped them fence stolen goods for capital — Hermione, who (per Harry's private knowledge, withheld from Ron throughout the book) knows the real funding source was Harry's Triwizard winnings, deflects by noting the twins "must have been planning this for ages."

**Named characters so far:** Harry Potter, Ron Weasley, Hermione Granger, Umbridge, Filch, Mrs. Norris, Peeves, Montague and his parents (Mr. and Mrs. Montague), Warrington, Pansy Parkinson, Professor McGonagall, Madam Pomfrey, Mundungus Fletcher (suspected funding source, speculated by Ron), Fred & George Weasley (referenced, departed).

**Named places/objects/spells/creatures:**
- The unremovable corridor-swamp (east wing, fifth floor) — Filch punts students across it.
- The hairy-snouted Niffler (smuggled prank creature).
- Bubble-Head Charms (adopted school-wide as a fashion/necessity against Dungbombs/Stinkpellets).
- "Umbridge-itis" (student-coined term for mass Skiving Snackbox-induced symptoms).
- The security-troll-guarded dungeon (rumored location of Harry's confiscated Firebolt).

**Specific numbers/quantities:**
- Mrs. Norris is shut inside suits of armor by Peeves twice.
- A full week passes with no staff intervention against Peeves's escalating chaos.

**Small/obscure details:**
- McGonagall's quiet, deliberate non-cooperation with Umbridge (walking past Peeves destroying a chandelier and merely offering technical advice rather than stopping him) is a pointed, specific act of staff-side resistance.
- The students' adoption of Bubble-Head Charms as everyday protective wear is a vivid, specific worldbuilding detail of the chaos's scale.

**Chapter 30 continued — full plot events:**
- Harry finally confesses to Ron and Hermione the true source of Fred and George's startup capital: his own 1,000-Galleon Triwizard Tournament winnings, given to them the previous June. Hermione is shocked/disapproving; Ron is thrilled, since it exonerates him from any blame his mother might assign for failing to stop the twins leaving.
- Hermione pivots to badgering Harry again about resuming Occlumency with Snape — Harry had told her/Ron (misleadingly) that Sirius wanted him to continue lessons, without revealing the real reason (the Pensieve incident) he'd gone to see Sirius in the first place. Ron accidentally reveals Harry has been muttering "just a bit farther" in his sleep; Harry lies that he dreamed about urging the Quidditch team to stretch further for the Quaffle, privately covering for his continued, deliberate nightly indulgence of the Department of Mysteries dream — he has now navigated past the circular room and the clicking/light-flecked room into the vast room of shelved glass orbs, specifically reaching row ninety-seven before waking. Harry admits to himself he's not actually trying hard at Occlumency anymore, since he's intensely curious what's hidden in that room and privately wants the dreams to continue.
- Final Quidditch match of the season: Gryffindor vs. Ravenclaw. Despite Ron's abysmal season record, optimism rises since Slytherin was narrowly upset by Hufflepuff in the second-to-last match — meaning a Gryffindor win could still take the Cup. Mid-match (Ravenclaw's Roger Davies scores immediately, prompting another round of "Weasley Is Our King"), Hagrid interrupts Harry and Hermione from the stands, visibly distressed, freshly injured (bleeding nose, two black eyes), and begs them to leave immediately and follow him into the Forbidden Forest while everyone's attention is on the match — this is revealed to be deliberate timing, since Hagrid wants witnesses minimized.
- Hagrid leads Harry and Hermione off the Quidditch-match path and deep into the Forbidden Forest, now carrying a crossbow as a "precaution" (unlike his earlier, unarmed thestral-viewing trip) — explained by the fact that the centaurs, previously neutral/aloof but not hostile toward him, have turned against him specifically since he personally intervened to save Firenze from being beaten to death by roughly half the herd (confirmed: Firenze was physically attacked for his "betrayal" in serving Dumbledore, and Hagrid single-handedly broke it up, for which he now resentfully feels Firenze should be more grateful rather than sending him cryptic warnings).
- Before revealing the forest's secret, Hagrid confesses he expects to be sacked "any day now" — Umbridge suspects (wrongly, he insists) that he smuggled the Niffler into her office, and has been looking for any pretext to remove him since his return. He says he'd leave voluntarily right now (to go help Dumbledore directly, being more useful outside Hogwarts) if not for needing to arrange care for a secret dependent first — extracting a promise from Harry and Hermione (and hopefully Ron) to help him with this unspecified task once he's gone.
- The reveal: deep in the forest, in a cleared, barricaded area surrounded by uprooted trees, lies a sleeping giant roughly sixteen feet tall, restrained by thick ropes tied to surrounding tree trunks. Hagrid reveals this is Grawp, his giant half-brother (same giantess mother, different giant father) — brought back from the giant expedition (explaining the two-month delay and his unhealed injuries, since Grawp doesn't know his own strength and has struck Hagrid repeatedly). Grawp is considered unusually small/runty by giant standards (only sixteen feet — giantesses value producing large offspring) and was being bullied/beaten by the other giants for his size; Hagrid couldn't bear to leave him, despite Grawp himself not wanting to come. Madame Maxime tolerated the plan out of sympathy for Hagrid but eventually grew tired of Grawp and the two split up partway through the journey home (she promised secrecy). They traveled only by night through wild country to avoid detection, slowed further by Grawp's frequent attempts to turn back.
- Hagrid's request: once he's sacked, he wants Harry, Ron, and Hermione to continue visiting Grawp regularly (using the Invisibility Cloak) to keep him company and continue teaching him rudimentary English (not for food — Grawp can hunt birds/deer himself) — reasoning that if Grawp can communicate, he'll understand that people care about him and want him to stay, making him less dangerous/more "civilized." Harry, having already promised unconditional help earlier, reluctantly commits Harry and Hermione (Ron's willingness still pending) to the task, despite recognizing — via the "Firenze" warning payoff — that this is almost certainly the doomed "attempt" the centaur urged Hagrid to abandon back in Chapter 27.
- Hagrid wakes Grawp by poking him with a bough (Hermione's protests notwithstanding) to formally introduce Harry and "Hermy" (Grawp can't manage "Hermione"); Grawp is enormous, misshapen (disproportionately large round head, stubby nose, half-brick-sized teeth, small muddy eyes), destructive (idly uproots a huge pine tree for amusement, flings eggs from a bird's nest in annoyance when empty), and nearly grabs/attacks Hermione on sight before Harry pulls her clear — Hagrid is struck in the nose for scolding him.
- On the way out of the forest, the group is confronted by a group of centaurs led by Magorian (newly introduced, proud, high-cheekboned, chestnut-bodied) alongside the previously-established hostile Bane (from Philosopher's/Sorcerer's Stone and Chapter 21) and other centaurs — they formally ban Hagrid from the forest going forward, citing his role in helping the "traitor" Firenze escape their earlier attempted killing, and his continuing to harbor an unspecified secret creature in the forest (confirmed the centaurs already know about Grawp, even if not by name) as further insult; they allow passage only this once because Hagrid is accompanied by "foals" (children/students) and centaurs "do not touch the innocent." Hagrid defiantly refuses to accept being barred and nearly re-escalates the conflict before Harry and Hermione physically push him onward.
- Walking back, a shaken, scratched-up Hermione despairs over the sheer scale of what's been asked of them (teaching English to a homicidally strong, unwilling giant, while dodging a hostile centaur herd just to reach him) — Ron, informed afterward, is similarly aghast, invoking past Hagrid-monster disasters (Norbert the dragon from Philosopher's/Sorcerer's Stone, Aragog the giant spider from Chamber of Secrets) as proof that "mixing with Hagrid's monster mates" never ends well; the trio agrees to quietly hope Hagrid simply isn't sacked before term ends, obviating the need to ever visit Grawp at all.
- The chapter ends on a triumphant note undercutting the day's dark revelations: Gryffindor has won the Quidditch Cup after all (Ginny snatched the Snitch from right under Cho Chang's nose, provoking Cho to angrily throw her broom down after landing) — Ron is carried back to the castle in triumph on his teammates' shoulders amid a joyful (now positive) rendition of "Weasley Is Our King," having redeemed his season with a strong final match performance.

**Named characters (ch.30 additions):** Harry Potter, Ron Weasley, Hermione Granger, Hagrid, Grawp (Hagrid's giant half-brother, newly introduced and named), Firenze (referenced, attacked by the centaur herd for "betrayal"), Bane (returning hostile centaur, confirmed from Philosopher's/Sorcerer's Stone), Magorian (newly introduced centaur leader figure), an unnamed gray centaur, Madame Maxime (referenced, split from Hagrid partway through the giant-expedition return journey), Ginny Weasley (wins the match for Gryffindor), Cho Chang (loses the Snitch race, throws her broom in frustration), Roger Davies and other named Ravenclaw players: Bradley, Chambers (mentioned by Ron recounting his saves).

**Named places/objects/spells/creatures:**
- Grawp (named: Hagrid's half-giant brother, ~16 feet tall, restrained by ropes in a forest clearing).
- The centaur herd's territory/laws (formally established: centaurs "do not touch the innocent"/foals; Firenze's "servitude to humans" considered an unforgivable betrayal; centaurs claim ownership/authority over the forest).
- Norbert (the dragon, Philosopher's/Sorcerer's Stone) and Aragog (the giant spider, Chamber of Secrets) — referenced by Ron as cautionary precedent for "Hagrid's monster mates."
- The Quidditch Cup (won by Gryffindor this match).

**Specific numbers/quantities:**
- Grawp is approximately sixteen feet tall (confirmed multiple times, emphasized as unusually small for a giant).
- The group travels roughly 15+ minutes deeper into the forest before reaching Grawp's clearing.
- Madame Maxime and Hagrid split up partway through their roughly month-long return journey from the giants' territory.

**Small/obscure details:**
- Grawp calls Hermione "Hermy" since he struggles with her full name — a small but specific, memorable detail.
- Hagrid's blind faith that Grawp is "harmless" and merely needs "manners" mirrors his past pattern of self-delusion about dangerous creatures (explicitly invoked by Ron via Norbert/Aragog comparisons).
- The detail that giantesses value producing large offspring (explaining why Grawp, despite his towering size by human standards, was bullied as "runty" by actual giants) is a specific, quotable worldbuilding fact.
- Hagrid's indignant claim that the centaurs' aggression toward Firenze is unjustified, set against his own admission that "angry doesn't cover it... ruddy livid," underscores his persistent selective blindness to danger.

---

## Chapter 31: O.W.L.s

**Plot events so far (in progress):**
- The next day, Ron is still euphoric over the Quidditch win and uninterested in anything else; Harry and Hermione struggle to find a moment to tell him about Grawp, eventually doing so under the lakeside beech tree (chosen for privacy) after Ron recounts his match saves at length (Harry privately notes Ron's hair-ruffling and crowd-awareness mannerisms eerily mirror James Potter's from the Pensieve memory). Ron is initially hurt that Harry and Hermione missed his heroics, but once told about Grawp, reacts with blunt, practical alarm — invoking Norbert and Aragog as proof that "mixing with Hagrid's monster mates" never ends well, and urging Hermione to simply break her promise to Hagrid given the stakes (looming O.W.L.s, near-constant risk of expulsion). Hermione reluctantly agrees in principle but feels bound by her word; the group settles on hoping Hagrid simply isn't sacked before term ends, so the issue becomes moot.
- June arrives, bringing the O.W.L. exams. Lessons shift entirely to review; Harry notices Snape has apparently never resumed Occlumency lessons despite Lupin's instruction (conveyed via Harry, per Chapter 29) — Snape ignores Harry as thoroughly as Harry ignores Snape, which Harry finds a relief given his already overloaded schedule. Hermione, similarly preoccupied with exam stress, has stopped nagging Harry about Occlumency and has not knitted any elf clothes "for days."
- Exam-season behavioral quirks proliferate: Ernie Macmillan obsessively tracks and compares daily study hours (claiming 8-9 hours average, up to 10 on good days); Draco Malfoy spreads intimidation rumors that his father is personally friendly with Griselda Marchbanks, head of the Wizarding Examinations Authority, implying favorable treatment — Neville quietly debunks this (Marchbanks is a friend of his own grandmother's and has never mentioned the Malfoys), while revealing, in a notably vulnerable moment (the first time he's acknowledged the St. Mungo's encounter from Chapter 23 to the trio), that his grandmother constantly unfavorably compares him to his father in front of Marchbanks, and that he doesn't expect the connection to help him at all.
- A black-market trade in magical study aids springs up among fifth and seventh years: Ravenclaw sixth-year Eddie Carmichael offers Harry and Ron a pint of "Baruffio's Brain Elixir" (credited with his own nine Outstanding O.W.L.s) for 12 Galleons; Hermione intervenes, confiscating and destroying it before the sale completes, and separately confiscates classmate Harold Dingle's "powdered dragon claw" (revealed to actually be dried doxy droppings) — thoroughly souring Harry and Ron on black-market remedies.
- McGonagall delivers the official O.W.L. logistics: exams span two consecutive weeks (written/theory exams each morning, practical exams each afternoon; the Astronomy practical exam held at night); strict Anti-Cheating Charms apply to all papers, with Auto-Answer Quills, Remembralls, Detachable Cribbing Cuffs, and Self-Correcting Ink all explicitly banned. McGonagall pointedly notes, with visible distaste, that Umbridge ("our new — headmistress") has mandated that Heads of House warn students that cheating will be severely punished, since exam results will reflect on Umbridge's "new regime." Results will be owled out in July — meaning Harry anticipates learning his fate while isolated at Privet Drive over the summer.
- The actual examiners arrive the evening before exams begin: a delegation of elderly Wizarding Examinations Authority officials, including the very deaf, heavily lined Professor Griselda Marchbanks (previously referenced in Chapter 26 as one of the Wizengamot elders who resigned in protest over Umbridge's Inquisitor appointment) — Umbridge is visibly nervous/deferential toward them. Marchbanks loudly (due to her deafness) asks Umbridge whether there's any news of Dumbledore's whereabouts, pointedly noting she doubts the Ministry will ever find him if he doesn't want to be found, and personally recalls examining Dumbledore's own N.E.W.T.s in Transfiguration and Charms decades earlier, remarking he did things with a wand she'd "never seen before."
- Exam week begins: written Theory of Charms first (Monday morning), held in the Great Hall reconfigured with individual desks (explicitly mirroring the exact arrangement Harry saw in Snape's O.W.L.-era Pensieve memory from Chapter 28) under McGonagall's supervision with a giant hourglass timer. Harry recalls, while answering a flying-charm question, a specific memory of successfully using the spell against a mountain troll (Philosopher's/Sorcerer's Stone callback). The afternoon features alphabetically-ordered small-group practical exams; Hermione is tested alongside Anthony Goldstein, Gregory Goyle, and Daphne Greengrass; students who've been tested do not return to the waiting chamber, leaving Harry and Ron without any sense of how she performed, though they jokingly reassure each other she'll be "fine" (Hermione is noted to have once scored "a hundred and twelve percent" on something, the exact context cut off at the chapter boundary).

**Named characters so far:** Harry Potter, Ron Weasley, Hermione Granger, Hagrid, Ernie Macmillan, Draco Malfoy, Crabbe, Goyle, Neville Longbottom, Griselda Marchbanks (Wizarding Examinations Authority head, fully introduced), Eddie Carmichael (Ravenclaw sixth-year), Harold Dingle (classmate, confiscated "dragon claw"), Professor McGonagall, Dolores Umbridge, Dean Thomas, Seamus Finnigan, Parvati Patil, Lavender Brown, Anthony Goldstein, Gregory Goyle, Daphne Greengrass (named, Slytherin).

**Named places/objects/spells/creatures:**
- Baruffio's Brain Elixir (fake/dubious study aid).
- Powdered dragon claw (revealed to be dried doxy droppings).
- Anti-Cheating Charms, Auto-Answer Quills, Remembralls (as cheating devices here), Detachable Cribbing Cuffs, Self-Correcting Ink (all explicitly banned exam items).
- The Wizarding Examinations Authority (governing body for O.W.L.s/N.E.W.T.s).
- Achievements in Charming and Intermediate Transfiguration (study textbooks referenced).

**Specific numbers/quantities:**
- O.W.L.s span two consecutive weeks: theory exams each morning, practicals each afternoon, Astronomy practical at night.
- Baruffio's Brain Elixir offered for 12 Galleons a pint.
- Ernie Macmillan studies 8-9 hours a day, up to 10 on weekends (9.5 hours one Monday, only 7.25 on a Tuesday).
- Results to be delivered by owl "some time in July."
- Eddie Carmichael reportedly earned nine "Outstanding" O.W.L.s the previous summer (credited, dubiously, to the Brain Elixir).

**Small/obscure details:**
- Neville's quiet admission about his grandmother unfavorably comparing him to his father is a significant, vulnerable character beat — the first time he's directly referenced the St. Mungo's encounter to his friends.
- Professor Marchbanks' anecdote about examining Dumbledore's own N.E.W.T.s is a nice piece of Dumbledore backstory trivia.
- The Great Hall's O.W.L. exam setup is explicitly confirmed to mirror exactly what Harry saw in Snape's memory (Chapter 28), reinforcing the generational parallel between Harry's cohort and the Marauders'.

**Chapter 31 continued — full plot events:**
- Transfiguration practical examiner Professor Tofty (elderly, bald) tests Harry; recognizes him as "the famous Potter," causing Malfoy (being tested nearby by Marchbanks) to lose concentration and drop/smash a levitated wine glass. Harry's Levitation Charm outperforms Malfoy's, though he mixes up Color-Change and Growth Charm incantations, causing his rat to swell to badger-size before correcting it (Ron separately turns a dinner plate into a mushroom by accident).
- Subsequent exams: Transfiguration written (Harry forgets the Switching Spell definition but manages a full iguana vanishment; Hannah Abbott multiplies her ferret into a flock of flamingos, halting the exam 10 minutes for bird wrangling); Herbology (minor Fanged Geranium bite); Defense Against the Dark Arts — Harry's strongest showing, performing confidently in front of a coolly watching Umbridge, earning Professor Tofty's enthusiastic praise for a perfect boggart-banishing spell; prompted by a tip from "dear friend Tiberius Ogden" (a Wizengamot elder who resigned alongside Marchbanks in Chapter 26) that Harry can produce a Patronus, Tofty asks for a bonus-point demonstration — Harry casts a full stag Patronus while deliberately imagining Umbridge being sacked, earning Tofty's delighted applause; Harry privately believes (but tells no one, superstitiously) that he's achieved an "Outstanding."
- Friday: day off for Harry/Ron while Hermione sits Ancient Runes (she returns furious, having mixed up "ehwaz" and "eihwaz" translations) and reports a second Niffler incident in Umbridge's office (tried to bite her leg) — worried this will be blamed on Hagrid despite his being visibly occupied teaching outside at the time; later revealed (next chapter) that Lee Jordan, not Hagrid, has been levitating Nifflers through her window using leftover stock from Fred and George.
- Weekend: Potions exam (Harry answers the Polyjuice Potion question with real-world authority from his illegal second-year use of it; practical goes more smoothly than usual without Snape present, administered instead by Professor Marchbanks); Care of Magical Creatures practical (held outdoors near the forest specifically so Hagrid can watch anxiously from his cabin window) — tasks include identifying a knarl hidden among hedgehogs via a milk test (knarls are paranoid and react aggressively, assuming poisoning), correct bowtruckle handling, cleaning/feeding a fire-crab without burns, and selecting proper diet items for a sick unicorn; Harry gives Hagrid a reassuring thumbs-up afterward.
- Astronomy theory exam (uncertain about Jupiter's moons but confident none is inhabited by mice — a callback/joke re: Ron's earlier Chapter 14 essay mix-up); Divination practical goes disastrously (Harry sees nothing in the crystal ball, garbles a tea-leaf reading into predicting Professor Marchbanks's imminent death, confuses life/head lines on her palm; Ron separately describes his own examiner's reflection as a stranger's face in his crystal ball) — both boys are relieved to be dropping the subject permanently after O.W.L.s. Hermione reports cautious confidence in her Arithmancy exam.
- The climactic Astronomy practical exam (held at 11 p.m. atop the Astronomy Tower under Marchbanks and Tofty's supervision) is dramatically interrupted: Harry spots Umbridge leading five companions out across the grounds toward Hagrid's cabin around midnight; Fang barks, lights appear in Hagrid's windows, then an enormous commotion erupts — Hagrid is confronted by a squad including a named Ministry Auror, Dawlish (from Chapter 27), attempting to Stun and forcibly arrest him. Hagrid fights back ferociously and effectively (Stunning Spells visibly bounce off him, attributed by Hermione to his giant blood making him as hard to Stun as a troll), hurling one attacker roughly ten feet, defending himself with his fists while Fang is Stunned trying to help. Professor McGonagall rushes out from the castle to intervene, shouting that Hagrid has done nothing to warrant the attack — she is blasted by four simultaneous Stunning Spells to the chest and collapses, unmoving, to horrified screams from the watching students (several, including Professor Tofty, abandon all pretense of examination protocol to watch). Hagrid, enraged, knocks out two more attackers, scoops up the unconscious Fang, and flees into the night toward the gates, escaping successfully despite Umbridge's final failed Stunning Spell.
- Aftermath: the whole school is abuzz; Hermione furiously brands Umbridge's ambush "despicable cowardice" (echoed by Madam Pomfrey's reaction later); Lee Jordan privately confesses to Harry/Ron/Hermione that HE (not Hagrid) has been the one levitating Nifflers into Umbridge's office window using stock Fred and George left behind — too late to save Hagrid's job, since (as Dean Thomas notes) Umbridge was always going to force him out specifically for being "too close to Dumbledore," regardless of actual guilt. The common room buzzes past 4 a.m.; Harry, furious and sleepless, contemplates revenge fantasies (Ron suggests feeding Umbridge to Blast-Ended Skrewts) before getting only three hours' sleep ahead of the final O.W.L. (History of Magic).
- The next afternoon's History of Magic written exam (the final O.W.L.): Harry, exhausted, struggles to focus (skips the goblin-riot wand-legislation essay question, fumbles the 1749 Statute of Secrecy question involving vampires) but manages a reasonably solid answer on the Formation of the International Confederation of Wizards and the Liechtenstein warlocks' objection (first Supreme Mugwump Pierre Bonaccord's attempt to grant trolls legal rights conflicted with Liechtenstein's ongoing troll-hunting problem) — drawing partly on memorized notes and partly on genuine historical interest. Mid-exam, his exhaustion tips into a full, vivid, uncontrolled vision: he relives walking the Department of Mysteries corridor all the way to row ninety-seven of the glass-orb room, where he finds a black-robed figure lying injured on the floor — Harry, speaking and acting entirely as Voldemort, orders the figure to retrieve an object ("Lift it down... I cannot touch it... but you can") since Voldemort himself apparently cannot physically handle it; when the figure resists, Voldemort casts the Cruciatus Curse on him. The tortured figure finally raises his bloodied, gaunt, defiant face: it is Sirius Black, who croaks "You'll have to kill me" — Voldemort coldly promises to do so eventually but insists Sirius retrieve "it" first, threatening prolonged torture ("we have hours ahead of us and nobody to hear you scream"). Harry is jolted awake mid-vision by his own scream, falling off his exam desk, scar burning, as the Great Hall erupts in chaos around him.

**Named characters (ch.31 further additions):** Harry Potter, Ron Weasley, Hermione Granger, Professor Tofty (elderly examiner), Professor Marchbanks, Draco Malfoy, Hannah Abbott, Lee Jordan (confirmed as the actual Niffler-smuggler, not Hagrid), Dean Thomas, Angelina Johnson, Katie Bell, Colin Creevey, Alicia Spinnet, Lavender Brown, Ernie Macmillan, Hagrid, Fang, Dawlish (Auror, named attacker), Professor McGonagall (Stunned, hospitalized), Dolores Umbridge, Madam Pomfrey, Montague (still recovering, seen being treated by Pomfrey), Tiberius Ogden (referenced, Wizengamot elder who tipped off Tofty about Harry's Patronus), Sirius Black (revealed as Voldemort's torture victim in the vision), Lord Voldemort (POV of the climactic vision).

**Named places/objects/spells/creatures:**
- The Formation of the International Confederation of Wizards (History of Magic exam topic): first Supreme Mugwump Pierre Bonaccord, contested by Liechtenstein's wizarding community over troll rights/troll-hunting conflict.
- Statute of Secrecy breach of 1749 (exam question, involving vampires — details fumbled by Harry).
- The glass-orb room's row ninety-seven (confirmed as the specific location of the vision's events).
- The Cruciatus Curse (used by Voldemort on Sirius in the vision).
- Knarls, bowtruckles, fire-crabs, unicorns (all featured in the Care of Magical Creatures practical exam).
- Polyjuice Potion (Potions exam question, Chamber of Secrets callback).

**Specific numbers/quantities:**
- Four Stunning Spells hit McGonagall simultaneously, square in the chest.
- Harry gets roughly three hours of sleep before his final exam.
- The History of Magic exam question numbering reaches at least 10 (goblin riots = Q4, Statute of Secrecy 1749 = Q5, International Confederation of Wizards = Q10).

**Small/obscure details:**
- The detail that Hagrid's attackers' Stunning Spells visibly "bounce off" him (explained by his half-giant blood/troll-like resilience) is a specific, quotable magical-biology fact.
- McGonagall's exact fate (transferred to St. Mungo's) is confirmed in the next chapter via Madam Pomfrey.
- Harry's vision explicitly confirms that Voldemort cannot personally touch/retrieve "it" (the weapon/prophecy, not yet identified by name) — a crucial, specific plot mechanic requiring him to use Sirius as a proxy.
- The nickname "Padfoot" (Sirius's Animagus alias) is used by Voldemort/Harry's vision-self only implicitly — Harry uses it explicitly moments later in Chapter 32 when trying to signal Snape.

## Chapter 32: Out of the Fire

**Plot events (in order):**
- Harry is led out of the exam hall by the sympathetic Professor Tofty, who assumes it's exam-stress-induced collapse; Harry fakes composure, then sprints to the hospital wing seeking McGonagall — Madam Pomfrey (treating Montague with a blue potion) reveals McGonagall was transferred to St. Mungo's that very morning, having taken four point-blank Stunning Spells to the chest at her age — confirming the Chapter 31 cliffhanger's outcome and expressing furious, uncharacteristic disgust at the "despicable cowardice" of the attack.
- Realizing there's now no Order member left at Hogwarts to turn to (Dumbledore and Hagrid both gone, McGonagall hospitalized), Harry finds Ron and Hermione and pulls them into an empty classroom to reveal: "Voldemort's got Sirius" — describing the vision in full (the glass-orb room, row 97, Voldemort torturing Sirius to force him to retrieve something he can't touch himself, threatening to eventually kill him).
- Harry immediately wants to rush to the Department of Mysteries to rescue Sirius; Hermione raises urgent, reasonable objections: how could Voldemort and Sirius have entered a Ministry building full of workers/Aurors at 5 p.m. undetected, being two of the most wanted wizards alive? She points out Harry has never actually physically been to the Department of Mysteries — only dreamed of it. Harry insists the visions are proven real (citing the accurate Arthur Weasley attack from Chapter 21); Ron backs him up on this point.
- Hermione, struggling to reconcile the evidence, voices a new theory: Sirius's own late brother Regulus was a Death Eater (Chapter 6) who might have known/told Sirius the secret of retrieving "the weapon" — possibly explaining both why Voldemort wants Sirius specifically and why Dumbledore has kept Sirius confined to Grimmauld Place all year. Hermione remains skeptical of the entire premise absent proof, and gently raises the sensitive point that Harry has a documented "saving-people-thing" — citing his unnecessary over-dedication to rescuing Fleur Delacour's sister in the Triwizard Tournament's second task (Goblet of Fire) as he nearly didn't need to. Harry reacts with fury at the comparison; Hermione clarifies she's worried specifically that Voldemort knows and is deliberately exploiting this exact impulse (as he did luring Harry to the Chamber of Secrets via Ginny in book 2) to draw him to the Department of Mysteries for some other purpose entirely.
- Harry refuses to accept inaction given the stakes and the total absence of anyone else to alert; he explicitly connects his dot: this is exactly why Dumbledore wanted him to master Occlumency — to prevent precisely this kind of vision — confirming (in his own mind) their reality and urgency.
- Compromise reached (proposed by Hermione): verify first whether Sirius has actually left Grimmauld Place by using Umbridge's fireplace again to check — if he's confirmed absent, Hermione will fully commit to helping storm the Ministry; if he's found safely at home, the vision will be exposed as a trick.
- Ginny and Luna wander in, drawn by the shouting, and are recruited as lookouts/diversion-runners despite the others' reluctance to fully explain the situation; Ginny proves remarkably prepared, suggesting a fake "Garroting Gas" warning (a prank Fred and George had been planning before leaving) to clear Umbridge's corridor of students. Ron volunteers to personally lure Umbridge away by reporting a fake Peeves rampage in the Transfiguration department.
- The plan executes: Ron draws Umbridge off; Ginny and Luna clear/guard the corridor with the gas lie; Harry and Hermione (under the Invisibility Cloak) pick Umbridge's office lock again with Sirius's knife and find it undisturbed. Harry Floos (head-only) to Grimmauld Place again — finding the kitchen empty except for Kreacher, now sporting bandaged hands and in an unusually gleeful, smug mood. Kreacher confirms Sirius "has gone out" but refuses to say where — until Harry directly asks if he's gone to the Department of Mysteries, at which point Kreacher gloatingly confirms "Master will not come back from the Department of Mysteries!" exulting that he and his "Mistress" (the portrait of his late mistress, Mrs. Black) will finally be alone again — strongly implying Kreacher himself engineered or enabled Sirius's departure/entrapment.
- Before Harry can react further, Umbridge violently yanks him bodily out of the fire by his hair — revealed to have placed "Stealth Sensoring Spells" around her office door specifically after the second Niffler incident, catching Harry and Hermione (whose wands are both confiscated) red-handed. Harry claims he was retrieving his Firebolt; Umbridge correctly calls this a lie (the broom is confirmed under guard in the dungeons) and demands to know who he was contacting.
- Reinforcements arrive: the Inquisitorial Squad has captured Ron, Ginny, and Luna (all gagged) plus — unexpectedly — Neville (who tried to stop Ginny's capture and was nearly strangled by Crabbe in retaliation); Slytherin student Warrington reports the roundup; Millicent Bulstrode pins Hermione against the wall; Malfoy gleefully confiscates/toys with Harry's wand. Umbridge gloats about Hogwarts soon being "a Weasley-free zone."
- Umbridge, having failed to extract answers, sends Malfoy to fetch Snape — hoping for Veritaserum to force a confession. Harry, realizing Snape is the last remaining Order contact at Hogwarts, tries desperately to communicate telepathically/visually with him when he arrives (coldly indifferent on the surface) — Snape deflects Umbridge's Veritaserum request, revealing he has none left (used his last bottle on an earlier, unspecified interrogation of Harry — implying Umbridge drugged Harry with Veritaserum at some prior unseen point) and that brewing more takes a full lunar cycle (about a month) — infuriating Umbridge, who threatens him for being "deliberately unhelpful" despite Lucius Malfoy's praise, and orders him out of her office.
- As Snape turns to leave — Harry's last hope for alerting the Order — Harry blurts out, in coded language only Snape would understand: "He's got Padfoot! He's got Padfoot at the place where it's hidden!" Umbridge, confused, demands clarification; Snape, giving nothing away, claims total ignorance, dismisses Harry's outburst as "nonsense," and pointedly warns Crabbe to loosen his death-grip on a suffocating Neville (citing potential paperwork and reference-letter consequences) before leaving — Harry is left devastated, uncertain whether Snape even understood the coded message, with his last avenue of help seemingly exhausted.
- With Snape gone, Umbridge — framing this explicitly as a matter of "Ministry security" beyond mere school discipline, and visibly working up her own nerve/justification — draws her wand on the wandless, powerless Harry and announces her intent to use the Cruciatus Curse on him to force the truth out, rationalizing that the Minister will understand "I had no choice." Malfoy watches with open, hungry anticipation as the chapter ends on this cliffhanger.

**Named characters (ch.32 additions):** Harry Potter, Ron Weasley, Hermione Granger, Ginny Weasley, Luna Lovegood, Neville Longbottom, Professor Tofty, Madam Pomfrey, Montague, Kreacher, Sirius Black (referenced, confirmed missing from Grimmauld Place), Dolores Umbridge, Draco Malfoy, Millicent Bulstrode, Warrington, Crabbe, Severus Snape, Fleur Delacour's sister (referenced, Goblet of Fire second task callback — implicitly Gabrielle Delacour, not named here), Regulus Black (referenced, Hermione's theory).

**Named places/objects/spells/creatures:**
- Stealth Sensoring Spells (Umbridge's new office security measure).
- Veritaserum (confirmed used on Harry at some earlier, unshown point by Umbridge; Snape's stock exhausted, takes a full lunar cycle to brew more).
- The Cruciatus Curse (Umbridge's threatened weapon against Harry — a shocking escalation for a Hogwarts professor).
- "Garroting Gas" (fictional/prank gas invented as a crowd-clearing lie, originally planned by Fred and George).
- "Padfoot" (Sirius's Animagus codename, used by Harry as a coded signal to Snape).
- "Babbling Beverage" (a potion Snape sarcastically threatens to give Harry for "nonsense").

**Specific numbers/quantities:**
- Veritaserum takes a full moon cycle (~1 month) to brew.
- Harry and Hermione are allotted roughly 5 minutes for their office break-in, per Hermione's estimate.

**Small/obscure details:**
- Kreacher's bandaged hands and gleeful, "Mistress" (portrait)-obsessed demeanor strongly foreshadow his active betrayal of Sirius — a major, specific plot-mechanics reveal (Kreacher is later confirmed to have manipulated Sirius into leaving via a lie, exploiting Sirius's love for Harry).
- Umbridge's explicit admission that she previously used Veritaserum on Harry (per Snape's dialogue) is a significant, easily-missed confirmation of an off-page interrogation/violation.
- The detail that Lee Jordan (not Hagrid) was behind the Niffler incidents is explicitly resolved here, closing a minor mystery thread from Chapter 30/31.

**Chapter 32 continued — the Cruciatus cliffhanger resolves:**
- As Umbridge begins casting the Cruciatus Curse on Harry, Hermione suddenly breaks down "crying" (faking it — Harry notices no actual tears) and announces she'll tell Umbridge everything. This stuns everyone, including Ron (gagged, protesting "Mnee—no!") and Ginny/Neville.
- Hermione spins an elaborate, improvised lie: she and Harry were trying to contact Dumbledore (checking the Leaky Cauldron, Three Broomsticks, Hog's Head — none of which worked) to tell him "it" (a secret "weapon" Dumbledore ordered them to build against the Ministry) is finally ready. Umbridge, consumed by excitement at the prospect of a literal weapon to seize, demands to be shown it, sends away her Slytherin helpers (refusing Malfoy's offer to accompany her, boasting she's a "fully qualified Ministry official" who can manage "two wandless teenagers alone" — a fatal error), and orders Harry and Hermione to lead her there alone.
- Crucially, Umbridge's own admission during this scene: she personally sent the dementors that attacked Harry and Dudley in Little Whinging the previous summer (Chapter 1), confirming she, not just the Ministry in general, orchestrated Harry's near-expulsion specifically to discredit/silence him — Fudge never knew, but was happy to use the resulting hearing anyway.

---

## Chapter 33: Fight and Flight

**Plot events (in order):**
- Hermione, improvising confidently, leads Umbridge out of the castle and into the Forbidden Forest (not toward Hagrid's cabin, since "he might have set it off accidentally") — deliberately following the path toward Aragog's lair (from Chamber of Secrets) rather than Grawp's clearing, and deliberately making excessive noise, explaining quietly to Harry that she "wants [them] heard."
- The ambush: roughly fifty centaurs emerge with bows drawn, led by Magorian (from Chapter 30) and including the hostile Bane and an unnamed hard-faced gray centaur. Umbridge, terrified, identifies herself by her full Ministry title and cites "Law Fifteen B" (regarding magical creatures of "near-human intelligence") — a phrase that enrages the centaurs (Bane: "near-human intelligence... we consider that a great insult"). She further inflames them by calling them "filthy half-breeds... beasts... uncontrolled animals" after a warning arrow grazes her hair, then illegally casts "Incarcerous" on Magorian, binding him with conjured ropes — triggering a full centaur charge. Harry pulls Hermione to the ground as the herd tramples past them; Umbridge is seized by Bane, lifted into the air screaming, and carried off into the forest; her wand falls and is deliberately snapped by a centaur's hoof before Harry can retrieve it.
- Harry and Hermione are then seized themselves by centaurs (the gray one grips Harry, noting he is "nearing manhood" and thus not exempt as a "foal"); a tense standoff follows in which Hermione's attempt to clarify "we only came in here because we hoped you'd drive her off for us" backfires badly, read by the centaurs as confirmation that humans see them as servants/attack dogs to be commanded — nearly getting Harry and Hermione "joined" to Umbridge's fate as punishment for trespassing.
- They are saved by the sudden arrival of Grawp, who bursts into the clearing calling "Hagger" (Hagrid) repeatedly, then recognizing and naming "Hermy" (Hermione) — confirming he remembers her from their earlier introduction (Chapter 30). When Grawp reaches for them (searching for Hagrid, not attacking out of malice), the centaurs seize the opening and fire some fifty arrows into his face; he roars in pain/fury and charges after the fleeing herd, allowing Harry and Hermione to escape into the trees.
- Now wandless (Harry's wand was left behind, retrieved later) and far from help, they're found by Ron, Ginny, Neville, and Luna, who freed themselves from the Inquisitorial Squad's custody (using a Disarming Charm, Stunners, Neville's Impediment Jinx, and — most notably — Ginny's signature Bat-Bogey Hex, used devastatingly on Draco Malfoy) and tracked Harry/Hermione's path into the forest. Ron returns both confiscated wands.
- Group debate over transport to London: Luna calmly suggests thestrals (confirmed by Hagrid to be excellent navigators, able to find destinations their rider has in mind) — initially only two are visible (drawn by the smell of Grawp's blood on Harry and Hermione's robes/sleeves), but Ginny correctly predicts more will arrive once the bloodstains are noticed, and six or seven duly emerge from the trees. Harry reluctantly accepts that all six of them (despite his strong wish to exclude Ginny, Neville, and Luna from the danger) will make the journey, following Neville's pointed reminder that the D.A. was always meant to be about real resistance to Voldemort, not just practice.

**Named characters (ch.33 additions):** Harry Potter, Hermione Granger, Ron Weasley, Ginny Weasley, Neville Longbottom, Luna Lovegood, Dolores Umbridge, Magorian, Bane, an unnamed gray centaur, Grawp, Draco Malfoy (hexed by Ginny), Hagrid (referenced, thestral-luring knowledge).

**Named places/objects/spells/creatures:**
- The path toward Aragog's lair (Chamber of Secrets callback, deliberately chosen by Hermione as a decoy route).
- "Incarcerous" (rope-conjuring spell, used illegally by Umbridge on Magorian).
- Law Fifteen B (fictional Ministry regulation re: "near-human intelligence" creatures, cited by Umbridge).
- The Bat-Bogey Hex (Ginny's signature spell, used devastatingly on Malfoy — first full demonstration in the series).
- Thestrals (confirmed able to track riders' intended destinations; drawn specifically by the scent of blood).
- Sirius's magical knife (destroyed/melted later in Chapter 34, foreshadowed as still carried here).

**Specific numbers/quantities:**
- Roughly fifty centaurs ambush the group.
- Fifty arrows are fired into Grawp's face by the retreating centaurs.
- Six thestrals are needed/found for the full group of six students.

**Small/obscure details:**
- Hermione's deliberately noisy, decoy route and "I want us heard" strategy is a clever, specific piece of tactical planning.
- Umbridge's citing of "Law Fifteen B" and her overt contempt for "half-breeds" is a direct, textual confirmation of her prejudice (consistent with her anti-werewolf legislation against Lupin, Chapter 14, and her general disdain for "part-humans").
- The detail that thestrals are drawn to the smell of blood (explaining the group's sudden "transport" solution) is a neat, specific piece of worldbuilding payoff.

## Chapter 34: The Department of Mysteries

**Plot events (in order):**
- The six fly to London by thestral — a long, cold, high-speed night flight over Hogsmeade, mountains, Muggle towns, and motorways, with Harry mentally tracking his own emotional state as a proxy for Sirius's condition (reasoning that if Voldemort killed Sirius or Sirius broke and complied, Harry would feel it via his scar — the connection hasn't flared, so he assumes Sirius is still alive and resisting).
- They land near the visitors' entrance (the same vandalized red telephone box/overflowing dumpster from Chapter 7); enter, dial 62442, and identify themselves to the recorded voice as "Harry Potter, Ron Weasley, Hermione Granger, Ginny Weasley, Neville Longbottom, Luna Lovegood... here to save someone, unless your Ministry can do it first" — receiving badges reading "HARRY POTTER — RESCUE MISSION" (implying the others' badges are similarly labeled).
- They descend into an eerily empty, nighttime Atrium (no fires lit, dimmer light, golden ceiling symbols still twisting) — the security desk is unattended, heightening Harry's unease. They take the lift to Level 9 (Department of Mysteries), finding the corridor equally deserted.
- Inside the Department: a large circular black room with identical, unmarked, handle-less black doors at intervals, lit by blue-flamed candles reflected in a mirror-like black marble floor. After Neville shuts the entrance door, the room's wall mechanically rotates (a security/disorientation measure, correctly identified by Ginny as designed to prevent anyone from tracking which door they entered through) — confirming the dozen doors are now indistinguishable and that finding the correct one, and later their way back out, will require trial and error.
- Room-by-room search (Hermione marks each tried door with a fiery "X" via "Flagrate" so they don't repeat it, cleverly noting this mark persists through the wall's rotations):
  1. A long, bright room containing a huge tank of green water full of drifting, glimmering brains (correctly identified by Hermione, not "Aquavirius maggots" as Luna guesses per Quibbler lore) — quickly abandoned as the wrong room.
  2. A deep, sunken, amphitheater-like chamber (resembling Harry's Wizengamot hearing room) containing a raised stone dais with an ancient, crumbling stone archway hung with a tattered, gently swaying black veil/curtain despite no air movement — Harry hears faint whispering voices from behind the veil and feels a powerful, almost hypnotic urge to walk through it; Luna also hears the voices ("There are people in there!"); Hermione, visibly frightened (more than at the brain room), forcibly pulls Harry and then Ginny and Neville (also found "entranced" by it) away and insists they leave — this room/archway (later identified in fan-canon as "the Veil," central to Arch Chapter 35) is left deliberately mysterious and unexplained in-text.
  3. A locked door that resists both "Alohomora" and Sirius's enchanted knife — the knife's blade melts on contact, destroying it permanently; the group abandons this room too (Hermione reasoning it can't be the right one since Harry's dreams always let him pass through every door).
  4. The correct room at last: a brilliantly lit space full of ticking clocks of every kind (grandfather, carriage, etc.) and bookshelves, centered on a tall crystal bell jar containing a shimmering, swirling wind in which a tiny jeweled egg endlessly hatches into a hummingbird, ages, and reverts back into an egg in an eternal cycle (a visual metaphor for manipulated/reversed time, not explained further) — Ginny is briefly transfixed by it but hurried onward by Harry.
  5. Through a final door beyond the clock room: the long-sought Hall of Prophecy — a church-sized, blue-candlelit room with towering shelves of small dusty glass orbs, organized by numbered rows. The group finds row 97 but discovers Sirius is NOT there, nor any sign of a struggle — Harry's dread mounts as he searches adjacent rows in increasing desperation and growing dawning horror that the vision may not have reflected reality as directly as he assumed.
  6. Ron discovers a dusty glowing orb specifically labeled with Harry's name: a yellowed label beneath it reads (in full, as directly quoted in-text): a date roughly sixteen years prior, then "S.P.T. to A.P.W.B.D." / "Dark Lord" / "and (?) Harry Potter" — strongly implying this is a recorded prophecy (S.P.T. = the Seer who made it; A.P.W.B.D. = Albus Percival Wulfric Brian Dumbledore, per his full name from Chapter 8) concerning Harry and Voldemort specifically. Against Hermione's and Neville's explicit warnings not to touch it, Harry picks it up — it's warm, not cold, "as though it had been lying in the sun for hours" — but nothing dramatic happens upon handling it.
- The chapter ends on a cliffhanger: a drawling voice from directly behind them — revealed as Lucius Malfoy — orders Harry to turn around slowly and hand over the orb.

**Named characters (ch.34 additions):** Harry Potter, Ron Weasley, Hermione Granger, Ginny Weasley, Neville Longbottom, Luna Lovegood, Lucius Malfoy (revealed at chapter's end).

**Named places/objects/spells/creatures:**
- The Department of Mysteries, fully detailed: the rotating circular antechamber (black doors, blue candles, black marble floor); the Brain Room (tank of drifting brains, confused by Luna for "Aquavirius maggots"); the Death Chamber/amphitheater with the mysterious Veil and whispering archway (left deliberately unexplained); a permanently locked room (destroys Sirius's knife); the Time Room (ticking clocks, the hummingbird-egg crystal bell jar); the Hall of Prophecy (towering shelves of numbered, dusty glass orbs/prophecy spheres).
- "Flagrate" (Hermione's fiery door-marking spell, new to the book).
- Sirius's magical lock-picking knife — destroyed (melted) in this chapter, a significant, permanent loss of a Sirius-gifted item.
- The prophecy orb's label: "S.P.T. to A.P.W.B.D. / Dark Lord / and (?) Harry Potter" — the central MacGuffin/plot device of the book's climax.
- 62442 (the Ministry's visitor phone code, restated from Chapter 7).

**Specific numbers/quantities:**
- The prophecy orb's label date is "some sixteen years previously" (consistent with Harry's age/the timing of Voldemort's first downfall).
- Row 97 in the Hall of Prophecy is the specific target row (established across Chapters 28/29/31 dream sequences).
- The Department of Mysteries antechamber has "around a dozen" identical black doors.

**Small/obscure details:**
- The crystal bell jar's eternally cycling hummingbird/egg is a vivid, symbolic, unexplained Department of Mysteries exhibit (time manipulation).
- The Veil/archway's whispering voices and hypnotic pull are left completely unexplained in this chapter — a deliberately mysterious, significant object (pays off tragically in Chapter 35).
- The fact that Harry's dreams only ever showed him doors he could pass through (never the locked one that destroys the knife) is a subtle piece of internal logic Hermione uses to reason about which rooms matter.
- Luna's calm, matter-of-fact demeanor throughout (noting "we're flying," observing "You two must really smell" about the thestrals, politely asking "Where do we go from here?") provides a steady comic/character throughline amid escalating tension.
