## Chapter 35: Beyond the Veil

**Plot events so far (in progress):**
- Harry and his five friends are surrounded by a dozen or so hooded, wand-lit Death Eaters led by Lucius Malfoy, who demands Harry hand over "the prophecy." Harry demands to know where Sirius is; a harsh-voiced female Death Eater (soon revealed as Bellatrix Lestrange) mockingly confirms "the Dark Lord always knows" and taunts Harry in a cruel, mocking baby-voice, implying his vision of Sirius being tortured was a deliberately implanted false dream — "the difference between life and dreams."
- Malfoy explains to Bellatrix that he/Voldemort understand Harry has "a great weakness for heroics," confirming (as Hermione feared in Chapter 32) that the entire vision of Sirius's capture and torture was a deliberate trap designed specifically to lure Harry to the Hall of Prophecy to physically retrieve the orb himself (since, per the Chapter 31 vision, Voldemort/his followers apparently cannot take it directly).
- Harry stalls for time, refusing to hand it over and raising his wand; all five of his companions raise theirs in solidarity, though Harry is wracked with guilt at having potentially led them to their deaths for nothing. When the Death Eaters hold back from attacking rather than risk damaging the orb, Bellatrix attempts to Summon it directly ("Accio Proph—") but Harry blocks with "Protego" just in time, retaining his grip.
- Bellatrix, enraged, pulls back her hood — revealed as gaunt, skull-like, and "fanatical" in appearance after her time in Azkaban (confirming her as one of the ten escaped convicts from Chapter 25) — and threatens to torture Ginny (identified as "the smallest") in front of Harry to force compliance, personally volunteering to do it. Harry steps protectively in front of Ginny, holding the prophecy up as a human shield/bargaining chip, correctly calculating that the Death Eaters cannot risk damaging it to attack them. He stalls further by asking what the prophecy actually says, buying time while hoping his friends are thinking of an escape plan — his own mind is blank.

**Named characters so far:** Harry Potter, Ron Weasley, Hermione Granger, Ginny Weasley, Neville Longbottom, Luna Lovegood, Lucius Malfoy, Bellatrix Lestrange (revealed by name/unmasking).

**Named places/objects/spells:**
- The Hall of Prophecy (continued setting).
- "Accio" (Summoning Charm, attempted by Bellatrix on the prophecy).
- "Protego" (Shield Charm, used successfully by Harry to block the Summons).
- The prophecy orb (central MacGuffin, confirmed by Malfoy's dialogue as "the prophecy").

**Specific numbers/quantities:**
- A dozen Death Eaters surround the group (restated: "outnumbered two to one").

**Small/obscure details:**
- Bellatrix's post-Azkaban appearance (gaunt, skull-like, feverish/fanatical) visually confirms her as one of the escapees and foreshadows her unstable, zealous characterization.
- Malfoy's blunt assessment of Harry's psychology ("a great weakness for heroics") is a direct, textual confirmation of the trap's design and Hermione's earlier "saving-people-thing" warning (Chapter 32).

**Chapter 35 continued — the full climax (Hall of Prophecy battle through Sirius's death):**
- Harry stalls Bellatrix by revealing (recklessly, to Hermione's alarm) that Voldemort is a half-blood (mother a witch, father a Muggle) — enraging her ("filthy half-blood's tongue"); she attempts "Stupef—" on Harry but Malfoy deflects her spell into a nearby shelf, shattering several prophecy orbs and releasing two ghostly Seer figures who recite fragmentary prophecy lines (paraphrased: one about a new arrival "at the Solstice," one about "none will come after") before dissolving — giving Harry the idea to destroy the shelves deliberately as an escape tactic.
- Malfoy reveals (taunting Harry) the core mechanics of the plot: only the person(s) a prophecy concerns may remove it from the Department of Mysteries (explaining why Voldemort couldn't simply send proxies like Sturgis Podmore or Bode to steal it, and why he needed to lure Harry personally); the prophecy concerns "both of you" — Harry and Voldemort — and Malfoy implies it explains why Voldemort tried to kill baby Harry in the first place. Bellatrix mocks the idea of Voldemort entering the Ministry himself, given the Aurors are currently "wasting their time" hunting Sirius (her "cousin") instead of searching for Voldemort.
- On Harry's signal ("NOW!"), all five of his companions simultaneously cast "Reducto" at the shelves, triggering a cascading shelf collapse that releases countless more prophecy-Seer ghosts and showers the Death Eaters with glass and debris, allowing the group to flee. Hermione seals the door behind them with "Colloportus." They become separated from Ron, Ginny, and Luna in the chaos.
- Overheard through the sealed door: Lucius Malfoy organizing the Death Eaters into search pairs by name — confirming additional named Death Eaters beyond those already seen: Nott (injured, left behind), Jugson, Rabastan (Lestrange), Macnair, Avery, Rookwood, Mulciber, Dolohov, Crabbe (an adult Death Eater, presumably Draco's father's associate, distinct from the student) — and Malfoy's explicit order to keep Harry alive until the prophecy is recovered but to kill the others "if necessary."
- Harry, Hermione, and Neville battle through the Time Room: Harry Stuns one Death Eater; blocks an "Avada Kedavra" aimed at Hermione by tackling the caster; Neville's wild Disarming Charm accidentally knocks away both Harry's and the Death Eater's wands; a mis-aimed Stunning Spell from Neville destroys and magically self-repairs a Time-Turner cabinet; Hermione finally Stuns the unmasked Death Eater, who topples backward with his head passing straight through the crystal bell jar — comically but unsettlingly aging backward into a baby and forward again on a loop (confirmed by Hermione as a visible demonstration of "Time" itself) — Hermione refuses to let Harry attack the now-literally-infantilized man further ("You can't hurt a baby!").
- Catastrophe: a masked Death Eater casts a silent, unidentified purple-flame curse at Hermione, who collapses unconscious (but alive, confirmed by Neville finding a pulse) — this attacker is unmasked as Antonin Dolohov (the murderer of Gideon and Fabian Prewett, per his Chapter 25 Prophet mugshot). Neville's inherited wand (his father's old wand) is broken in the ensuing scuffle when a Death Eater kicks him in the face, breaking his nose. Harry Petrifies Dolohov just as a second "baby-headed" Death Eater (the earlier bell-jar victim, now wandering the room in a bizarre, uncontrollable rage-filled toddler tantrum, his adult body topped by a cycling infant/adult head) blunders into view.
- Neville insists on helping rather than fleeing to safety, carrying the unconscious Hermione (given her own wand to hold, since his is broken) while Harry fights; they navigate back toward the circular antechamber, where Hermione's fiery door-marks have faded (the wall having rotated again), forcing another blind guess at doors.
- They're reunited with Ron, Ginny, and Luna, who tumble out of a side door: Ron has been struck by an unknown curse causing him to behave drunk/concussed, giggling deliriously and making repeated "Uranus" puns, having fought off a room full of floating planets (Ginny's ankle is broken from a Death Eater's grab during the escape; Luna, uninjured, reports they were chased through "a dark room full of planets"). The group (now six, several badly hurt) attempts to flee together.
- Bellatrix Lestrange and other Death Eaters corner them again in the circular hall; the group flees back into the Brain Room, sealing doors with "Colloportus" as they go, but Luna is struck down (flung across a desk, knocked unconscious) before sealing her door, and five Death Eaters flood in. Ron, still disoriented/giddy, impulsively Summons a brain from the tank ("Accio Brain!") out of drunken curiosity — it wraps him in choking, rope-like mental tendrils; Ginny is Stunned in the face trying to help and knocked out; Neville's Stunning attempts (mispronounced "Stubefy" due to his broken/bleeding nose) all miss.
- Harry draws the remaining Death Eaters (including Bellatrix) away from his incapacitated friends by running with the prophecy held aloft, diving through the only open door — which turns out to be a trapdoor-like floor opening, dropping him down a long flight of steps directly into the sunken amphitheater room containing the mysterious archway/Veil from Chapter 34. Roughly ten Death Eaters converge on him there; Lucius Malfoy (unmasked) gloats that Harry's "race is run," outnumbering him decisively and mocking Dumbledore's teaching of "how to count."
- Neville reappears (having left Ron fighting the brain) to fight alongside Harry despite being seized/restrained by a Death Eater; Malfoy and Bellatrix taunt him specifically about his parents (Malfoy: his grandmother is "used to losing family members"; Bellatrix reveals with sadistic glee that she personally tortured the Longbottoms — "I have had the pleasure of meeting your parents, boy"). When Harry still won't surrender the prophecy, Bellatrix casts the Cruciatus Curse on Neville as a "taster," then threatens to kill him "the hard way" unless Harry complies — Harry, with no real choice, is about to hand over the prophecy when the Order of the Phoenix arrives: Sirius Black, Remus Lupin, Mad-Eye Moody, Tonks, and Kingsley Shacklebolt burst in through two doors, immediately engaging the Death Eaters in a full magical battle.
- Pitched battle ensues across the amphitheater: Harry is nearly strangled/disarmed by an unnamed Death Eater until Neville stabs the man's mask eyehole with Hermione's wand, forcing him to release Harry — unmasked as Walden Macnair (Buckbeak's would-be executioner, Prisoner of Azkaban callback). Mad-Eye Moody is injured, his magical eye knocked loose and rolling across the floor; his attacker, Dolohov, turns on Harry and Neville next, hitting Neville with a Leg-Locker-like "Tarantallegra" dancing curse and attempting the same purple-flame curse on Harry, who partially blocks it with "Protego." Sirius intervenes, physically ramming Dolohov aside, then duels him directly; Harry finishes Dolohov off with a second "Petrificus Totalus."
- Sirius, mid-duel with Bellatrix (taunting her: "Come on, you can do better than that!"), orders Harry to take the prophecy and get Neville out. Harry, fleeing with a leg-dancing Neville, is tackled by Lucius Malfoy demanding the prophecy at wandpoint — Harry throws the orb to Neville, blasts Malfoy away with "Impedimenta," and Lupin intervenes to protect them, urging Harry to "round up the others and GO."
- Climactic tragedy: as Harry and Neville struggle up the stone steps (a stray spell destroys the step under Harry's foot), Neville's robe tears and the prophecy orb falls from his pocket — kicked accidentally by his still-dancing leg, it flies ten feet and smashes on the stone steps. A ghostly prophecy-figure rises and speaks, but its words are lost entirely in the chaos and noise — the prophecy's content is never heard by anyone who needed to hear it, and it dissolves into nothing. Neville is devastated ("I'b so sorry, Harry") but Harry dismisses it as unimportant in the moment.
- Neville spots Dumbledore arriving, framed in the doorway, wand raised, expression "white and furious" — his entrance instantly turns the tide: he effortlessly recalls a fleeing Death Eater with an invisible magical "hook," and the Death Eaters realize they're beaten.
- THE central tragedy: Sirius, still dueling Bellatrix and laughing, taunting her ("Come on, you can do better than that!"), is struck by her second jet of red light squarely in the chest. His expression shifts from laughter to shock; his body arcs backward in slow motion and falls through the ragged Veil hanging from the ancient archway — the Veil flutters "as though in a high wind" and settles back into stillness. Harry, certain this is reversible (believing Sirius is merely standing just behind the curtain, as voices had seemed to be earlier), screams his name repeatedly, convinced he'll reappear — but he does not. Lupin physically restrains Harry from running to the archway, repeating with breaking voice that "there's nothing you can do... he's gone," while Bellatrix lets out a triumphant scream.

**Named characters (ch.35 further additions — full Death Eater/Order rosters established):** Harry Potter, Hermione Granger, Ron Weasley, Ginny Weasley, Neville Longbottom, Luna Lovegood, Lucius Malfoy, Bellatrix Lestrange, Antonin Dolohov, Walden Macnair, Rookwood, Nott, Jugson, Rabastan Lestrange, Avery, Mulciber, Crabbe Sr. (adult Death Eater, distinct from student Crabbe), Sirius Black, Remus Lupin, Mad-Eye Moody, Nymphadora Tonks, Kingsley Shacklebolt, Albus Dumbledore (not yet arrived at this point — arrives at the very end of the chapter), Alice and Frank Longbottom (referenced, tortured by Bellatrix per her own gloating confirmation).

**Named places/objects/spells/creatures:**
- "Reducto" (Reductor Curse, used in unison by five characters to destroy the prophecy shelves).
- "Colloportus" (door-sealing charm, used repeatedly by Hermione).
- "Avada Kedavra" (the Killing Curse, attempted on Hermione, blocked by Harry's tackle).
- "Petrificus Totalus," "Impedimenta," "Protego," "Tarantallegra" (Dancing Feet Curse, used by Dolohov on Neville), "Stupefy," "Accio" (restated/used throughout the battle).
- The Time-Turner cabinet (confirmed by Hermione's reaction, "It's time... Time..." — a glass cabinet of hourglasses that perpetually shatters and self-repairs when struck).
- The Veil and archway (site of Sirius's death — left permanently mysterious/unexplained).
- The prophecy orb (destroyed/smashed, its contents lost forever — a crucial plot point: nobody present (not even Harry or Dumbledore) ever learns its exact wording from this copy).

**Specific numbers/quantities:**
- Malfoy explicitly states "there are ten of us and only one of you" at the standoff on the dais (before the Order's arrival evens the odds).
- Harry's prior prophecy-vision in Chapter 31 showed "sixteen years previously" as the orb's creation date (restated context).

**Small/obscure details:**
- The detail that only the subject(s) of a prophecy can remove it from the Ministry (revealed by Malfoy) is the central, crucial piece of previously-hidden lore explaining the entire book's plot mechanism.
- Bellatrix's direct, gloating confirmation that she tortured Frank and Alice Longbottom (first suggested via the Daily Prophet mugshot caption in Chapter 25, now confirmed in her own words) is a major character/plot beat.
- The prophecy's actual content is permanently lost to the smashing — a deliberate narrative choice meaning neither Harry nor the reader learns it from this physical copy (the content is later revealed via Dumbledore's own memory/testimony, in the next chapter).
- Ron's "Uranus" joke and his brain-induced delirium provide a notable tonal contrast of dark comedy amid the chapter's tragedy.

---

## Chapter 36: The Only One He Ever Feared

**Plot events (in order):**
- In the immediate aftermath of Sirius's fall, Harry refuses to accept his death, fighting Lupin's restraining grip and insisting Sirius is merely hiding behind the Veil. Lupin, visibly grief-stricken himself, uses "Finite" to end the Dancing Feet Curse still afflicting Neville's legs and gently asks where the others are; Neville reports Ron (recovering from the brain attack), Hermione (unconscious but with a pulse), Ginny, and Luna are all back in the previous room.
- Bellatrix Lestrange, having fled the fight, is pursued single-mindedly by an enraged Harry (breaking free of Lupin, screaming "SHE KILLED SIRIUS! I'LL KILL HER!") through the Brain Room (deluging himself in the spilled brain-tank liquid, using "Wingardium Leviosa" to fling the clinging brains off himself) and into the circular antechamber, finally cornering her in the Atrium upstairs near the Fountain of Magical Brethren.
- A one-on-one duel ensues: Bellatrix taunts Harry in her mock-baby voice, goading him into using an Unforgivable Curse; Harry, consumed by grief and rage, casts "Crucio" on her — it knocks her down but doesn't truly torture her, since (as she mockingly explains once she recovers) effective Unforgivable Curses require genuine malicious intent/enjoyment of causing pain, not just righteous fury ("You need to mean them, Potter!... I'll show you how it is done"). The duel continues around the fountain's golden statues (wizard, witch, centaur, goblin, house-elf), with stray spells blasting off body parts of the statues (a head, an ear, an arm).
- Harry, his scar searing increasingly as Voldemort's attention is drawn in, goads Bellatrix by falsely/truthfully (it's true) revealing the prophecy was destroyed and its contents never heard by anyone — causing her to panic and beg forgiveness aloud to her absent "Master," not realizing Voldemort can actually hear this since he has just arrived.
- Voldemort appears in person in the Atrium — described in full, definitive detail: tall, thin, black-hooded, with a "terrible snakelike" white, gaunt face and scarlet, slit-pupiled eyes — confirming Bellatrix's failure directly to her face, dismissing her panicked apologies/warnings about Dumbledore being present, and casting "Avada Kedavra" directly at Harry.
- Harry is saved when the previously-animated, headless golden wizard statue (animated earlier by Dumbledore to defend Harry) leaps in front of him, absorbing the Killing Curse on its chest. Voldemort, realizing Dumbledore is present, immediately attacks him instead.
- An extended, spectacular wizard duel between Dumbledore and Voldemort unfolds across the Atrium: Dumbledore animates all the remaining fountain statues (witch pins Bellatrix to the floor; goblin and house-elf flee toward the fireplaces; a one-armed centaur statue charges Voldemort) while dodging/deflecting repeated Killing Curses; Voldemort conjures a silver shield to block one of Dumbledore's unnamed, powerful spells (which produces an eerie "gonglike" resonance on impact); the two exchange taunting philosophical dialogue about death (Voldemort: "There is nothing worse than death"; Dumbledore: "You are quite wrong... there are things much worse than death... your failure to understand [this] has always been your greatest weakness"); Dumbledore conjures a fire-whip that becomes a serpent and nearly bites him before Voldemort teleports away to the fountain's central plinth; Fawkes the phoenix swoops in and swallows a final Killing Curse whole (bursting into flame and falling to the floor, small and featherless, in the process of regeneration), allowing Dumbledore to banish the conjured snake and trap Voldemort in a rising, encasing sphere of water from the fountain's pool.
- Voldemort escapes the water-trap and vanishes entirely (implied to have Disapparated, confirmed moments later by an arriving Auror, Williamson, who witnessed him grab a woman — Bellatrix — and Disapparate) just as Ministry reinforcements (led by a pajama-clad, badly shaken Cornelius Fudge) flood into the Atrium via the fireplaces.
- Final, devastating beat: as Dumbledore shouts a warning ("Stay where you are, Harry!" — visibly frightened for the first time), Harry's scar "bursts open" in agony, and he experiences a direct, forced psychic merger/possession by Voldemort himself — bound together "by pain," speaking with Harry's own mouth, Voldemort goads Dumbledore: "Kill me now, Dumbledore... If death is nothing, Dumbledore, kill the boy." Harry, in his own despairing thoughts, actually welcomes the idea of death in that moment (reasoning he'd be reunited with Sirius) — and it's this emotional response (implicitly, love/acceptance rather than fear or hatred) that breaks Voldemort's hold; the possession ends, and Harry finds himself lying on the cold floor, disoriented but alive, his glasses knocked off.
- Dumbledore confirms Harry is physically fine; the Atrium fills with arriving Ministry witches and wizards pouring from the fireplaces, including Fudge, who witnesses firsthand (via Auror Williamson's testimony and his own eyes) that Voldemort was really there — finally, irrefutably confirming Harry and Dumbledore's year-long claims. Dumbledore, composed and commanding even in victory, directs Fudge to the Department of Mysteries to collect the captured, Anti-Disapparation-Jinxed Death Eaters in the Death Chamber, demands Umbridge's removal from Hogwarts and Hagrid's manhunt called off as the price of his cooperation, grants Fudge "half an hour" of explanation time that same night, and creates an emergency Portkey (the detached golden statue head, charmed with "Portus") to send Harry directly back to his office at Hogwarts — ending the Ministry confrontation on Dumbledore's unambiguous terms.

**Named characters (ch.36 additions):** Harry Potter, Remus Lupin, Neville Longbottom, Bellatrix Lestrange, Lord Voldemort (first full, direct on-page appearance in the Ministry, described in definitive detail), Albus Dumbledore, Fawkes, Cornelius Fudge, Williamson (named Auror witness), Dawlish (named again, sent to the Department of Mysteries).

**Named places/objects/spells/creatures:**
- The Fountain of Magical Brethren (site of the Harry/Bellatrix duel and the Dumbledore/Voldemort duel — its statues destroyed/animated in the process).
- The Cruciatus Curse ("Crucio," cast by Harry on Bellatrix for the first time — confirmed ineffective without genuine sadistic intent).
- "Avada Kedavra" (used multiple times by Voldemort against Harry and Dumbledore).
- Fawkes's phoenix regeneration cycle (swallows a Killing Curse, bursts into flame, is reborn small/featherless — consistent with established phoenix lore).
- "Portus" (used again to create an emergency Portkey from the golden statue's head).
- The twelve-handed watch Dumbledore carries (a specific, memorable personal item, used to track time precisely).

**Specific numbers/quantities:**
- Dumbledore grants Fudge "half an hour" of his time that night for explanations.
- The duel/battle takes place just before dawn (a "cool line of pale green" visible on the horizon by the end).

**Small/obscure details:**
- The specific mechanic that Unforgivable Curses (at least Cruciatus) require genuine malicious intent to be fully effective is a significant, quotable piece of magical theory revealed here by Bellatrix herself.
- Voldemort's "there is nothing worse than death" vs. Dumbledore's "things much worse than death" exchange is a central thematic statement for the whole series, delivered in paraphrase here per copyright guidance.
- Harry's brief, explicit moment of welcoming death to be reunited with Sirius — and this being the emotional key that frees him from Voldemort's possession — is a critical, specific plot mechanism (the "power the Dark Lord knows not," i.e., love, foreshadowed here without being named outright).
- Fudge is notably still in pajamas under his cloak, having been roused from bed for the emergency — a small, humanizing/comic detail amid the devastation.

## Chapter 37: The Lost Prophecy

**Plot events so far (in progress):**
- Harry arrives via Portkey alone in Dumbledore's office, finding it fully restored (silver instruments humming normally, portraits asleep) with dawn breaking outside. Alone in the silence, he begins to process his guilt — believing Sirius's death is entirely his own fault for failing to consider that the vision might be a trick exploiting his "saving-people-thing," exactly as Hermione had warned in Chapter 32.

**Named characters so far:** Harry Potter, Dumbledore (office, not yet present), Sirius Black (mourned), Hermione Granger (referenced, her warning recalled).

**Named places:** Dumbledore's office (restored to its pre-battle state).

**Chapter 37 continued — full plot events (Dumbledore's confession and the prophecy revealed):**
- Phineas Nigellus wakes and chats obliviously, not yet knowing of Sirius's death; Harry, unable to voice it, finds the office door magically locked, trapping him. Dumbledore returns via Floo powder, carrying the tiny, featherless baby Fawkes (reborn from his regeneration in the Atrium battle) and confirms none of Harry's fellow students suffered lasting harm; Tonks will need brief St. Mungo's treatment but will fully recover.
- Harry, consumed by grief and rage, lashes out verbally and physically — smashing several of Dumbledore's delicate silver instruments and a lunascope against the walls/into the fire — while Dumbledore remains calm, refusing to stop him, insisting Harry's capacity to feel such pain is his "greatest strength," not weakness. Harry demands to leave; Dumbledore refuses, locking the door and insisting on explaining everything first, stating plainly: "It is my fault that Sirius died... That blame lies with me, and with me alone" — because Dumbledore's secrecy prevented Harry from understanding Voldemort might try to lure him to the Department of Mysteries.
- Phineas Nigellus, told of Sirius's death, refuses to believe it and leaves his frame (to check Sirius's other portrait at Grimmauld Place, implicitly to "call for Sirius through the house").
- Dumbledore's full confession begins: he reveals that fifteen years ago, upon seeing Harry's scar, he correctly guessed it signified a magical connection to Voldemort — one that intensified as Voldemort regained his body, allowing Harry to sense Voldemort's proximity/emotions, and (crucially, revealed for the first time) to risk the connection working in reverse, letting Voldemort access/manipulate Harry's mind. Dumbledore explains his year of calculated emotional distance from Harry was a deliberate (and, he now admits, mistaken) protective measure: he feared that if Voldemort realized how close Harry and Dumbledore's relationship truly was, he would exploit the connection to spy on Dumbledore through Harry, or worse, possess Harry outright to force Dumbledore into killing him. Dumbledore reveals he sometimes glimpsed "a shadow" of Voldemort stirring behind Harry's eyes during close eye contact — explaining Harry's own unexplained urges to attack Dumbledore (Chapters 21/22/27).
- Dumbledore confirms Voldemort's possession of Harry during the final Atrium confrontation was deliberately engineered — hoping Dumbledore would be forced to kill Harry to stop Voldemort, which Dumbledore refused to do; the possession failed/ended specifically because Voldemort "could not bear to reside in a body so full of" the emotion he detests (love — not yet named outright at this point but clearly implied).
- Dumbledore reveals the full Kreacher betrayal mechanism: Kreacher, ordered by Sirius months earlier (around Christmas, Chapter 23) to simply "get out" of a room, interpreted this (deliberately/opportunistically) as permission to leave Grimmauld Place entirely, and went to the one remaining Black family member he respected — Narcissa Malfoy (Bellatrix's sister, Lucius's wife) — thereby indirectly serving Voldemort's interests for months. Bound by house-elf magic, Kreacher couldn't reveal Order secrets directly (not being Secret-Keeper), but freely shared "trivial"-seeming information Sirius never thought to forbid: namely, that Harry was the person Sirius cared about most in the world, and that Harry regarded Sirius as a father/brother figure — giving Voldemort the precise psychological lever needed to lure Harry to the Ministry via a vision of Sirius in danger. Dumbledore confirms he extracted this full confession from Kreacher via Legilimency the previous night, just before departing for the Ministry — explaining why Kreacher was "laughing fit to burst" when revealing Sirius's whereabouts to Dumbledore.
- Mechanism of the false "Sirius isn't home" deception, fully explained: per Malfoy/Voldemort's prior instructions (anticipating Harry might try to verify Sirius's safety), Kreacher was prepared to lie that Sirius was away; conveniently, Kreacher himself had injured Buckbeak the hippogriff that same day, giving Sirius a genuine reason to be upstairs tending to the animal (and thus unreachable) at the exact moment Harry called via Umbridge's fire.
- Harry, consumed by misplaced guilt and rage, blames Kreacher viciously; Dumbledore gently pushes back, framing Kreacher as a victim of generations of wizard mistreatment (comparing his misery to Dobby's) who felt no true loyalty to Sirius, who in turn treated him with "indifference and neglect" rather than active cruelty — which Dumbledore argues did real harm nonetheless ("The fountain we destroyed tonight told a lie. We wizards have mistreated and abused our fellows for too long, and we are now reaping our reward" — a direct callback to the Fountain of Magical Brethren's propagandistic imagery). Harry furiously rejects any criticism of Sirius, redirecting blame onto Snape instead — citing Snape's needling of Sirius's "cowardice" (Chapter 24) and his abrupt termination of Occlumency lessons after the Pensieve incident (Chapter 28).
- Dumbledore defends Snape: confirms Snape alerted the Order the moment Harry gave his coded "Padfoot" warning (Chapter 32), deduced Harry's forest trip and subsequent Ministry departure, and crucially gave Umbridge a FAKE Veritaserum potion rather than a real one when she demanded it to interrogate Harry — protecting him from forced confession. Dumbledore admits his own error in not personally teaching Occlumency (fearing closer proximity to Harry while Voldemort's influence was active would have been even more dangerous) and in trusting Snape to overcome his decades-old animosity toward James Potter, which he now concedes was "another old man's mistake."
- Full Order emergency response sequence detailed: when Harry failed to return from the forest with Umbridge, Snape alerted Moody, Tonks, Kingsley, and Lupin (all present at Grimmauld Place), who departed immediately for the Ministry; Snape asked Sirius to stay behind to brief Dumbledore (expected imminently) while Snape himself searched the Forbidden Forest for Harry. Sirius refused to stay behind, delegating the task of informing Dumbledore to Kreacher instead and joining the rescue party — meaning Dumbledore only learned what had happened, and where Sirius had gone, from Kreacher himself upon arriving at the now-empty headquarters.
- Dumbledore then delivers the long-withheld, central explanation Harry has wanted since age eleven: he confirms he deliberately left baby Harry on the Dursleys' doorstep specifically to invoke an ancient blood-protection charm — Lily's sacrificial death created a magical protection that persists as long as Harry can still call his mother's blood-relative's home (Privet Drive) "home," renewable via at least one yearly visit; this is why Harry must return to the Dursleys every summer regardless of how unhappy he is there. Dumbledore confirms he personally sent the Howler to Petunia (Chapter 2) specifically to remind her of this unspoken "pact," fearing the dementor attack had shaken her resolve to keep Harry.
- Dumbledore then confesses the titular "flaw" in his broader plan: his own excessive personal affection for Harry repeatedly stopped him from telling Harry about the prophecy at each opportune moment across the years (age eleven, after the Stone; age twelve, after the Chamber; age thirteen, after rescuing Sirius; age fourteen, after Cedric's death) — prioritizing Harry's happiness/peace of mind over necessary but painful truth, which Dumbledore now recognizes as "exactly" the weakness Voldemort expects from "fools who love."
- Dumbledore reveals the prophecy's existence and origin: made nearly sixteen years ago by Sybill Trelawney herself, during her original job interview for the Divination post, in a room above the bar at the Hog's Head Inn — witnessed personally and solely by Dumbledore (who initially found her unimpressive/unqualified and was about to reject her for the position). Using his own Pensieve, Dumbledore shows Harry his memory of the event: a trance-voiced, altered Trelawney delivers the prophecy in full (quoted directly in the text; summarized/paraphrased here per copyright guidance): it foretells the birth, at the end of July, of someone with "the power to vanquish the Dark Lord," born to parents who had three times defied Voldemort; states Voldemort will "mark him as his equal" yet the boy will possess "power the Dark Lord knows not"; and concludes that neither can live while the other survives, meaning one must eventually kill the other.
- Dumbledore reveals the prophecy could have applied equally to two boys born at the end of July that year to Order-of-the-Phoenix parents who'd thrice evaded Voldemort: Harry Potter or Neville Longbottom. The deciding factor was Voldemort's own choice: by personally attacking and marking Harry (not Neville) with the scar, Voldemort inadvertently fulfilled the "marked as his equal" clause specifically for Harry, cementing the prophecy's application to him and no one else. Dumbledore notes the significance that Voldemort chose the half-blood (Harry) over the pureblood (Neville), "seeing himself" in Harry before ever meeting him.
- Dumbledore explains why Voldemort attacked as a baby rather than waiting: his spy at the Hog's Head (an eavesdropper, thrown out partway through) only overheard the prophecy's first half — the birth conditions — and was ejected before hearing the full text, meaning Voldemort never learned the crucial "power the Dark Lord knows not" clause or the mutual-destruction warning, leaving him ignorant of the risk that attacking the boy might transfer power to him rather than eliminate the threat.
- Dumbledore identifies the mysterious "power" as something housed in a permanently locked room within the Department of Mysteries (not further specified, deliberately left as the series' ongoing central theme) — identified unambiguously, if unnamed directly as "love," as the force that drove Harry to attempt Sirius's rescue, and the very thing that made Voldemort's possession of him in the Atrium unbearable and brief ("he could not bear to reside in a body so full of the force he detests... It was your heart that saved you").
- Harry confirms the prophecy's grim final meaning explicitly with Dumbledore: "neither can live while the other survives" means one of them must eventually kill the other. Dumbledore confirms this bluntly: "Yes."
- In a final, quieter, more personal beat, Dumbledore reveals (almost as an aside) that he deliberately never made Harry a prefect, believing he already carried enough responsibility — visibly moved to tears as he says this, the first time Harry has ever seen him cry.

**Named characters (ch.37 further additions):** Harry Potter, Albus Dumbledore, Phineas Nigellus, Fawkes, Armando Dippet (portrait, brief interjection), Sybill Trelawney (as the original, true Seer of the prophecy — a major reveal contradicting her portrayal as a "fraud" all book), Kreacher, Narcissa Malfoy (confirmed as Kreacher's new "mistress"/contact), Lucius Malfoy, Bellatrix Lestrange, Severus Snape (defended in detail by Dumbledore), Sirius Black (mourned, defended, and gently critiqued by Dumbledore), Neville Longbottom (revealed as the prophecy's "other" possible subject), James and Lily Potter (Lily's sacrificial protection explained in full), Petunia Dursley (the Howler's true meaning revealed), Dobby (referenced, comparison to Kreacher's misery), Alastor Moody, Tonks, Kingsley Shacklebolt, Remus Lupin (full Order emergency-response roles detailed).

**Named places/objects/spells/creatures:**
- The Pensieve (used again, this time by Dumbledore to show Harry his own memory of the original prophecy).
- The Hog's Head Inn (confirmed as the exact location of the original prophecy, explaining the pub's recurring significance all book).
- The sword of Godric Gryffindor (glimpsed in a glass case in Dumbledore's office, background detail).
- Dr. Ubbly's Oblivious Unction (not yet introduced — appears next chapter).
- Lily Potter's blood protection charm (the "ancient magic" underpinning Harry's required yearly return to Privet Drive) — fully explained for the first time in the series.
- The locked Department of Mysteries room housing "the power" (love) — left deliberately unnamed/mysterious.

**Specific numbers/quantities:**
- The prophecy was made "sixteen years ago" / "nearly sixteen years ago," at the Hog's Head Inn, during Trelawney's job interview.
- Two boys (Harry and Neville) matched the prophecy's initial birth criteria — both born at the end of July.
- Dumbledore guessed the scar's significance "fifteen years ago."

**Small/obscure details:**
- Dumbledore's admission that he never made Harry a prefect specifically because he felt Harry already had enough responsibility (paying off the Chapter 9 mystery of why Ron, not Harry, got the badge) is a quietly devastating, specific reveal — delivered with Dumbledore visibly crying, the only time in the book he does so.
- The detail that Trelawney's prophecy-trance voice is "harsh, hoarse" rather than her usual "ethereal, mystic" one directly parallels her identical trance state from Prisoner of Azkaban (not explicitly cross-referenced in text but a notable continuity point).
- Kreacher's exploitation of Sirius's throwaway "get out" command (not meant literally as dismissal from the house) is a precise, critical plot mechanism worth flagging for trivia.
- Dumbledore's single tear, trickling into his "long silver beard," closes the chapter on a rare note of visible headmaster vulnerability.

---

## Chapter 38: The Second War Begins

**Plot events (in order):**
- The Daily Prophet runs a front-page story (headline: "HE-WHO-MUST-NOT-BE-NAMED RETURNS") in which Fudge publicly, officially confirms Voldemort's return and the mass defection of Azkaban's dementors to his service, promising free home-defense guides to all wizarding households; the piece notes the Ministry's abrupt reversal from its "no truth whatsoever" denials of just days earlier, and confirms Dumbledore has been fully reinstated as Hogwarts Headmaster, member of the International Confederation of Wizards, and Chief Warlock of the Wizengamot (all three positions Umbridge/Fudge had stripped from him over the course of the book).
- In the hospital wing: Ron (recovering from the brain-tentacle attack, deep arm welts treated with "Dr. Ubbly's Oblivious Unction"), Hermione (recovering from Dolohov's curse, on ten different potions daily, bored and restless), Ginny (ankle mended instantly by Madam Pomfrey), Neville (nose restored), and visiting Luna (reading the newest Quibbler upside down) discuss the Prophet's coverage — now flatteringly recasting Harry as "the Boy Who Lived" and a heroic "lone voice of truth," conveniently omitting the Prophet's own prior role in mocking him. Luna reveals her father sold Harry's original interview to the Prophet for a good price, funding a summer expedition to Sweden to hunt the (fictional) Crumple-Horned Snorkack.
- Domestic/school updates: Flitwick removed Fred and George's magical swamp in seconds but deliberately preserved a small patch under a window as a "monument" to their magical skill; Filch is despondent now that order has returned, having preferred Umbridge's regime; Umbridge herself remains hospitalized, catatonic/unspeaking since Dumbledore personally rescued her from the centaurs (details of the rescue never explained), flinching awake only when Ron mimics centaur hoofbeat sounds with his tongue — comic relief confirming her trauma is specifically centaur-related.
- Firenze and Trelawney will both continue teaching Divination jointly next year (Firenze cannot return to the forest, permanently banished by his herd).
- Harry, still withholding the prophecy's true content from Ron and Hermione (who still believe, per Neville's account, that it was destroyed/never heard by anyone — including Voldemort), deflects their questions by claiming he needs to visit Hagrid.
- Harry visits Hagrid (recently returned from hiding in a mountain cave, deliberately echoing Sirius's own Prisoner-of-Azkaban-era fugitive experience, a parallel Hagrid catches himself before finishing); Hagrid reports Grawp has become much better-behaved since his return, seemed "pleased" to see him, and Hagrid is now contemplating finding Grawp a "lady friend" (a prospect Harry can't muster energy to argue against, despite real concern). Hagrid gently tries to comfort Harry about Sirius's death, calling it an honorable death "in battle" that Sirius wouldn't have wanted any other way (since he "couldn't have lived with himself" sitting idle while others fought) — Harry reacts angrily and flees the conversation.
- Confrontation with Malfoy, Crabbe, and Goyle in the entrance hall: Malfoy threatens Harry ("You're dead, Potter... I'm going to make you pay for what you've done to my father"), revealing Lucius Malfoy has been imprisoned (implicitly captured among the Death Eaters at the Ministry) but noting the dementors' defection from Azkaban means the imprisoned Death Eaters, including his father, will likely escape "in no time." A wand-drawing standoff is interrupted by Snape, who tries to dock points from Gryffindor (discovering, to his visible annoyance, that Gryffindor's hourglass is already completely empty of points, having been fully drained by Malfoy's Inquisitorial Squad abuses earlier in the book) — before Professor McGonagall arrives, freshly released from St. Mungo's (leaning on a cane but otherwise recovered), overrides Snape, and awards 50 points each to Harry, Ron, Hermione, Ginny, and Neville (and 50 to Luna, credited to Ravenclaw) for "alerting the world to the return of You-Know-Who" — symbolically and decisively restoring Gryffindor's points and publicly validating the D.A. members' actions.
- Harry spends his final days at Hogwarts in a melancholy, isolated state, alternately wanting company and solitude, haunted by grief for Sirius and dread of both his looming responsibility (the prophecy) and his mandatory return to Privet Drive — now fully, painfully understanding why he must go back each summer but liking it no better for the explanation.
- Umbridge is forced to flee Hogwarts in disgrace the day before term ends, sneaking out during dinner — only to be caught and gleefully chased off the grounds by Peeves (fulfilling Fred's parting instruction from Chapter 29 to "give her hell"), beaten with a walking stick and a sock full of chalk, to a cheering audience of students and only half-hearted attempts by staff (including McGonagall, who openly regrets being unable to join in since Peeves had borrowed her own walking stick for the assault) to intervene.
- On their final evening, packing his trunk, Harry discovers the small, badly-wrapped package Sirius gave him before term began (Chapter 29) — still unopened, having never been used despite Harry's earlier private vow never to rely on it.

**Named characters (ch.38 additions):** Harry Potter, Ron Weasley, Hermione Granger, Ginny Weasley, Neville Longbottom, Luna Lovegood, Xenophilius Lovegood (referenced, sold Harry's interview to the Prophet), Cornelius Fudge (Prophet statement), Dolores Umbridge (hospitalized, traumatized, fled/expelled from Hogwarts), Professor Flitwick, Filch, Hagrid, Grawp, Draco Malfoy, Crabbe, Goyle, Lucius Malfoy (imprisoned, implicitly among the captured Death Eaters), Severus Snape, Professor McGonagall (returned from St. Mungo's), Peeves, Madam Pomfrey, Firenze and Sybill Trelawney (both confirmed continuing as Divination teachers next year).

**Named places/objects/spells/creatures:**
- Dr. Ubbly's Oblivious Unction (treatment for Ron's brain-tentacle scarring).
- The Daily Prophet's front page: "HE-WHO-MUST-NOT-BE-NAMED RETURNS," plus internal coverage pages ("You-Know-Who's Last Attempt to Take Over," "What the Ministry Should Have Told Us," "Why Nobody Listened to Albus Dumbledore," and a reprint of Harry's Quibbler interview).
- The Crumple-Horned Snorkack (Luna's family's planned Sweden expedition target).
- Gryffindor's house-points hourglass (shown completely drained, then replenished with 250 points total: 50 each to Harry, Ron, Hermione, Ginny, Neville).
- Ravenclaw's hourglass (50 points awarded to Luna).
- Fred and George's preserved "monument" swamp patch under a Hogwarts window.

**Specific numbers/quantities:**
- 50 points each awarded to Harry, Ron, Hermione, Ginny, Neville (Gryffindor) and Luna (Ravenclaw) by McGonagall — 300 points total restored/awarded.
- Hermione takes ten different potions daily during recovery.
- Ron eats his "fourteenth" Chocolate Frog during the hospital-wing scene (a specific, comic quantified detail).

**Small/obscure details:**
- Umbridge's selective startle-response to centaur hoofbeat sounds (mimicked by Ron) is a vivid, specific, comic trauma detail confirming (without explanation) what happened to her in the forest.
- Flitwick's deliberate preservation of a small swamp patch as a "monument" to Fred and George is a charming, specific staff-side tribute to the twins.
- Malfoy's threat and reference to his imprisoned father, paired with the dementors' defection implying an eventual Death Eater Azkaban breakout, sets up direct plot stakes for the following book.
- The chapter/book ends with Harry rediscovering Sirius's unused gift — the specific detail of the still-unopened package is the final, poignant image before the book's true ending (to be confirmed in the remaining excerpt).

**Chapter 38 continued — the book's final scenes:**
- Harry unwraps Sirius's earlier gift (from Chapter 29): a small, dirty, two-way mirror, with a note explaining Sirius owns the matching other half (a pair James and Sirius used to communicate during separate detentions at school) — Harry, briefly electrified with hope of speaking to Sirius again, says his name into it repeatedly, but nothing happens; he realizes, devastated, that Sirius didn't have his own mirror on him when he died, so it can never work. Harry smashes the mirror in grief/frustration, then immediately has a second idea.
- Harry rushes to find Nearly Headless Nick (Sir Nicholas de Mimsy-Porpington), skipping the end-of-term feast, to ask whether dead wizards can return as ghosts — hoping Sirius might do the same. Nick gently explains only wizards can become ghosts, but that it's an exceptionally rare choice ("very few wizards choose that path") driven by fear of death and a refusal to "go on" to whatever lies beyond; Nick confirms Sirius will not return this way, having essentially already "gone on." When pressed about what lies beyond death, Nick admits he doesn't know, having chosen his "feeble imitation of life" out of fear rather than facing it, and vaguely suggests "learned wizards study the matter in the Department of Mysteries" (Harry reacts with fierce refusal to discuss that place again).
- Walking back, Harry encounters Luna Lovegood posting notices seeking the return of her belongings (routinely hidden by unkind classmates who consider her "a bit odd" — the "Loony Lovegood" nickname's origin confirmed directly by her); she's serenely unbothered, confident her things "always" turn up eventually. Harry, newly sympathetic, learns Luna's mother (an "extraordinary witch" who liked to experiment) died in a spell accident when Luna was nine. Luna reveals she doesn't consider her mother's death final, firmly asserting she believes she heard her mother's voice (among others) whispering from behind the Veil in the Department of Mysteries — the same voices Harry heard — offering a quietly comforting, unresolved ambiguity about the Veil's true nature that the book deliberately leaves unexplained. Harry finds, after this conversation, that his grief has "lessened slightly."
- The train journey home: Malfoy, Crabbe, and Goyle attempt to ambush Harry alone in the train corridor but are thwarted by a compartment full of D.A. members (named: Ernie Macmillan, Hannah Abbott, Susan Bones, Justin Finch-Fletchley, Anthony Goldstein, Terry Boot), who hex all three into slug-like, barely mobile states and stuff them into the luggage rack to "ooze." In the compartment, Hermione reads ongoing Prophet coverage of escalating Voldemort-related incidents (dementor-repelling guides, Death Eater manhunts, panicked eyewitness letters); Neville's Mimbulus mimbletonia has grown and now makes crooning noises when touched. Cho Chang (now paired with Michael Corner, per Ginny) passes by with Marietta Edgecombe (still wearing a balaclava to hide her lingering "SNEAK" pustule scars). Ginny reveals she's dumped Michael Corner (sulked over Ravenclaw losing to Gryffindor at Quidditch) and casually announces she's now dating Dean Thomas, prompting Ron to furiously upend the chess board.
- Arrival at King's Cross: Harry finds an unexpected welcome party waiting beyond the platform barrier — Mad-Eye Moody, Tonks (bubble-gum pink hair, a Weird Sisters T-shirt), Lupin, and Mr. and Mrs. Weasley (with Fred and George in flashy new dragon-skin jackets, funded by their thriving joke shop) — assembled specifically to intimidate the Dursleys into treating Harry well over the summer. Moody and Mr. Weasley confront Vernon, Petunia, and Dudley directly: Moody bluntly threatens consequences if Harry is mistreated, flashing his magical eye to frighten Vernon into visible terror; the group collectively warns that if they don't hear from Harry for three consecutive days, someone will be sent to check on him. Mrs. Weasley promises to get Harry away from Privet Drive "as soon as we can"; Ron and Hermione promise to see him "really soon." The book ends with Harry leading the Dursleys out of the station, newly armed (for the first time) with visible, organized backup protecting him from his relatives — but privately still devastated by Sirius's death and burdened by the prophecy's weight.

**Named characters (final ch.38 additions):** Harry Potter, Sirius Black (posthumous gift/mirror), James Potter (referenced, mirror's shared history), Nearly Headless Nick/Sir Nicholas de Mimsy-Porpington, Luna Lovegood, Luna's unnamed mother (deceased, died in a magical accident when Luna was nine), Draco Malfoy, Crabbe, Goyle, Ernie Macmillan, Hannah Abbott, Susan Bones, Justin Finch-Fletchley, Anthony Goldstein, Terry Boot, Hermione Granger, Ron Weasley, Ginny Weasley, Neville Longbottom, Cho Chang, Marietta Edgecombe, Michael Corner, Dean Thomas (Ginny's new boyfriend), Mad-Eye Moody, Nymphadora Tonks, Remus Lupin, Arthur and Molly Weasley, Fred and George Weasley, Vernon, Petunia, and Dudley Dursley.

**Named places/objects/spells/creatures:**
- The two-way mirror (Sirius's final gift, destroyed by Harry) — a direct callback/parallel to the Mirror of Erised (Philosopher's/Sorcerer's Stone).
- The Department of Mysteries (referenced again regarding the study of death — Harry refuses to discuss it).
- Cauldron Cakes and Pumpkin Pasties (train snacks).
- Mimbulus mimbletonia (Neville's plant, now grown, makes crooning sounds).
- The Weird Sisters (band, referenced via Tonks's T-shirt).
- Dragon-skin jackets (Fred and George's new, joke-shop-funded luxury item).

**Specific numbers/quantities:**
- Luna's mother died when Luna was nine years old.
- The Order's ultimatum to the Dursleys: contact within three consecutive days, or "someone" will be sent to check on Harry.

**Small/obscure details:**
- Nick's confirmation that becoming a ghost is a conscious, fear-driven "choice" (not automatic) is a significant piece of series-wide death lore.
- Luna's quiet, unresolved claim to have heard her dead mother's voice behind the Veil (paralleling Harry's own experience) is one of the book's most delicately ambiguous, unexplained supernatural elements — deliberately left open rather than confirmed or debunked.
- The reveal that "Loony Lovegood" is a real, known taunt (not just something Harry/Ron said idly) and that Luna's belongings are routinely stolen/hidden by classmates adds meaningful pathos to her characterization.
- The chapter/book ends on the Platform 9¾ confrontation, with Moody's magical eye-flash physically terrifying Vernon Dursley into submission — a visually memorable final beat.

*(Note: book5.txt's text continues after this point with the series title list and a preview excerpt of Chapter One of Harry Potter and the Half-Blood Prince ["The Other Minister"] — this is bonus/promotional content appended to the file, NOT part of Order of the Phoenix itself, and is excluded from these notes accordingly. The novel proper ends at the King's Cross platform scene above, covering all 38 chapters in full.)*

## CROSS-CHAPTER FACTS (Franchise-Wide Ledger Material)

**Central antagonist / plot device of this book:**
Two interlocking threats drive the book: (1) Dolores Umbridge's bureaucratic takeover of Hogwarts on the Ministry's behalf (as Defense teacher, then High Inquisitor, then Headmistress), which suppresses practical magic education and persecutes anyone loyal to Dumbledore, while (2) Voldemort, newly returned and determined to avoid public exposure, works to obtain a prophecy foretelling that only Harry Potter has the power to defeat him — manipulating Harry's psychic scar-connection and his own "saving-people-thing" to lure him to the Ministry's Department of Mysteries. The book culminates in the Battle of the Department of Mysteries, where the prophecy is destroyed (never fully heard by anyone but Dumbledore, who already knew it) and Sirius Black is killed by Bellatrix Lestrange, falling through the mysterious Veil archway.

**Defense Against the Dark Arts teacher this year:** Professor Dolores Jane Umbridge (Senior Undersecretary to the Minister), who later also becomes Hogwarts High Inquisitor (Educational Decree 23) and finally Headmistress (Educational Decree 28) after Dumbledore's ousting. She is removed by Ministry order at the book's climax; Dumbledore is reinstated as Headmistress/Headmaster.

**Death(s) in this book:** Sirius Black — killed by Bellatrix Lestrange's Stunning Spell in the Death Chamber of the Department of Mysteries, falling through the ancient stone archway's fluttering black Veil and never reappearing (Chapter 35, "Beyond the Veil"). This is the major, named death flagged for the cross-book ledger.

**Dumbledore's Army (D.A.) — confirmed members (28 signed the original roster):** Harry Potter (leader/founder, voted in), Hermione Granger, Ron Weasley, Ginny Weasley, Neville Longbottom, Luna Lovegood, Fred Weasley, George Weasley, Lee Jordan, Angelina Johnson, Katie Bell, Alicia Spinnet, Cho Chang, Marietta Edgecombe (later the group's "sneak" informant), Michael Corner, Terry Boot, Anthony Goldstein, Padma Patil, Parvati Patil, Lavender Brown, Dean Thomas, Seamus Finnigan (joined later, not an original signer), Colin Creevey, Dennis Creevey, Ernie Macmillan, Justin Finch-Fletchley, Hannah Abbott, Susan Bones, Zacharias Smith (reluctant signer). Name chosen: "Dumbledore's Army" (Ginny's suggestion, originally proposed as "D.A." for "Defense Association"). Spells taught: Expelliarmus (Disarming Charm, first taught), Impediment Jinx, Stunning Spells, the Shield Charm (Protego), and finally Patronuses (begun just before the year's end); communication via Hermione's Protean-Charmed fake Galleons.

**Order of the Phoenix — members/associates named this book:** Albus Dumbledore (founder/leader), Sirius Black, Remus Lupin, Alastor "Mad-Eye" Moody, Nymphadora Tonks, Kingsley Shacklebolt, Arthur Weasley, Molly Weasley, Bill Weasley, Mundungus Fletcher, Emmeline Vance, Elphias Doge, Dedalus Diggle, Sturgis Podmore (arrested/imprisoned mid-book), Hestia Jones, Hagrid, Minerva McGonagall (implied member/ally), Severus Snape (spy, deeply distrusted by Harry/Ron but defended by Dumbledore). Original (First War) Order members seen in Moody's old photograph: Dumbledore, Moody, Dedalus Diggle, Marlene McKinnon (killed), Frank & Alice Longbottom (tortured insane), Emmeline Vance, Lupin, Benjy Fenwick (killed), Edgar Bones (killed, Amelia's brother), Sturgis Podmore, Caradoc Dearborn (vanished), Hagrid, Elphias Doge, Gideon & Fabian Prewett (killed, Molly's brothers), Aberforth Dumbledore, Dorcas Meadowes (killed by Voldemort personally), Sirius Black, James & Lily Potter, Peter Pettigrew/Wormtail (betrayer).

**Quidditch / House Cup outcomes:** Harry is banned from Quidditch for life by Umbridge (Educational Decree 25) after attacking Malfoy post-match, along with Fred and George (also banned) — ban effectively lifted once Umbridge leaves. Ginny Weasley becomes the replacement Seeker (very skilled); Andrew Kirke and Jack Sloper become replacement Beaters (mediocre). Ron Weasley becomes Gryffindor's new Keeper (rocky season, haunted by the "Weasley Is Our King" taunt song, but redeems himself in the final match). Match results: Gryffindor loses badly to Slytherin early (Harry's ban triggered here after the brawl), Slytherin is later upset by Hufflepuff, Gryffindor loses narrowly to Hufflepuff (230–240) despite Ginny's Snitch catch, and Gryffindor wins the final match against Ravenclaw, securing the Quidditch Cup for Gryffindor overall for the year.

**New Weasley-family details:** Percy Weasley remains estranged from the family all book (took a Ministry promotion under Fudge, accused his parents of being "traitors," sends back his Christmas jumper with no note, never visits injured Arthur). Fred and George Weasley are funded in their joke-shop ambitions by Harry's secret gift of his 1,000-Galleon Triwizard winnings; they dramatically quit Hogwarts mid-year (Chapter 29) and open Weasleys' Wizard Wheezes at 93 Diagon Alley, which proves commercially successful. Arthur Weasley is attacked and badly injured by Voldemort's snake while guarding the Department of Mysteries (Chapter 21-22), nearly dies, recovers at St. Mungo's. Ginny Weasley is revealed to be an accomplished flier/Quidditch player (secretly practiced on her brothers' brooms since age six) and a powerful dueler (signature Bat-Bogey Hex); she dates Michael Corner then Dean Thomas over the course of the book. Bill Weasley works a desk job for the Order (left Gringotts Egypt post) and is courting Fleur Delacour (now working at Gringotts London). Charlie Weasley remains in Romania, recruiting foreign wizards for the Order.

**Prefects / Head Boy-Girl:** Ron Weasley and Hermione Granger are named Gryffindor's fifth-year prefects (shocking Harry, who is passed over — later explained by Dumbledore as a deliberate choice not to burden Harry further). Other named prefects: Draco Malfoy and Pansy Parkinson (Slytherin), Ernie Macmillan and Hannah Abbott (Hufflepuff), Anthony Goldstein and Padma Patil (Ravenclaw). No Head Boy/Girl explicitly named this book (Percy was Head Boy in a previous year, referenced).

**Named spells introduced/featured this book:** Occlumency and Legilimency (mental defense/intrusion magic, central mechanic), Flagrate (fiery marking charm), Colloportus (door-sealing charm), Incarcerous (conjures binding ropes), Reducto (blasting curse), the Patronus Charm (reinforced), the Cruciatus Curse (Unforgivable, used by Bellatrix on Neville and Harry on Bellatrix), the Imperius Curse (referenced, used by Lucius Malfoy on Broderick Bode and Sturgis Podmore), Avada Kedavra (the Killing Curse, used multiple times by Voldemort), the Bat-Bogey Hex (Ginny's signature spell), Petrificus Totalus, Impedimenta, Protego, Stupefy, Expelliarmus, Tarantallegra (Dancing Feet curse), Scourgify, Alohomora, Accio, Wingardium Leviosa, Finite (Incantatem).

**O.W.L. exam results:** Not revealed within the book itself (results are sent by owl in July, after the book's narrative ends) — Harry believes he scraped an "Outstanding" in Defense Against the Dark Arts specifically (casting a full Patronus for the examiners) but the actual full results for any character are never shown on-page.

**The book's central plot device / antagonist one-liner for the cross-book ledger:**
*Order of the Phoenix's antagonist/plot device: Dolores Umbridge's Ministry-backed authoritarian takeover of Hogwarts (suppressing real Defense education and persecuting Dumbledore's allies) runs in parallel with Voldemort's secret scheme to retrieve a prophecy — using his psychic link to Harry's scar to lure him into a trap at the Department of Mysteries — climaxing in the Battle of the Department of Mysteries and the death of Sirius Black.*
